# Pitfalls

Technical mistakes that already happened in Brim and were fixed. Each entry says what went wrong, why, and how to avoid it next time. Read the section for the area you change before you start. Add a new entry when you fix a bug whose cause could happen again.

**When you fix a bug, search the whole codebase for the same mistake and for similar ones, and fix every place, not only the one that was reported.** Name in the entry where else it was found. (owner decision, 2026-11-10)

Dashboard UI (Leptos) details are in [crates/dashboard/ui/PITFALLS.md](crates/dashboard/ui/PITFALLS.md).

## 1. Discord API and permissions

**Three lists said which Discord permissions are dangerous, and they disagreed.** (one list, owner decision 2026-11-20)
Anti-nuke, verification, quarantine, backup restore and the security score read `DANGEROUS_PERMS` (9 permissions), the role lock had its own list of 15, and `info` had its own names. A role with View Audit Log, Manage Nicknames, Manage Expressions, Manage Events, Manage Threads and Posts or Mention @everyone was taken by a lock but not guarded by anti-nuke. *Avoid:* `brim_antinuke::DANGEROUS_PERMS` is the one list and `PERM_NAMES` its names; the role lock's `DANGEROUS` is that constant, and `/tag dangerous-permissions` lists it. A new feature that judges a Discord permission dangerous reads it, never a list of its own. The one exception is Mention @everyone on a staff permit role (Auto Setup gives it): use `STAFF_DANGEROUS` for those roles (security score, giving a role).

**A case's `active` flag was changed with a weaker permit than the effect it has.** (builder report on the undo buttons, 11-19)
`closecase` and `opencase` (and the dashboard and MCP case edit) set `cases.active` with only the `cases` permit perm, which `timeout`, `kick` and `ban` also imply. On a warn case that flag is the warn points (`cases::warn_points`), so a member without the `warn` permit perm could remove a warn, or put one back, which `unwarn` and `warn` refuse them. Root cause: the flag was treated as case bookkeeping, while one kind of case reads it as the punishment itself. *Avoid:* an action never needs less than the permit perm of the effect it has. `ModCmd::CaseSet` on a warn case also asks `actor.has("warn")`; the dashboard `case_edit` refuses a change of `active` on a warn case inside the update itself (`c.kind <> 'warn' or c.active = $5 or $6`), so the check and the write see the same row. The text of a warn case stays editable with `cases`. Searched: `active` on other kinds of case changes no punishment (a ban, timeout or quarantine stays); `clearwarns` and `unwarn` already ask `warn`; `case_delete` needs dashboard Admin. Also fixed there: "Left While Timed Out" in the user info reply read `expires_at`, which only a warn case has, so it always said no; it now reads `created_at + duration_s` and no later `untimeout` case.

**An undo of an old case lifted a punishment the case did not make.** (builder report on the undo buttons, 11-19)
A ban row in the dashboard's revert list (and an undo button on an old mod log) undoes by member: it unbans whoever is banned now, also when another moderator banned them again later for another reason. Root cause: the stored inverse names the member, not the punishment. *Avoid:* an undo that starts from a case asks `cases::not_current` first. It answers why the case is no longer the member's current punishment: its ban or timeout ran out, a later case banned, timed out, quarantined or released the member, or the quarantine row is newer than the case. The undo button (`undo::press`) refuses with that reason and lifts nothing. The dashboard revert leaves such a row under "Skipped", as it does a server renamed again, and also when a later `action_log` row bans or unbans the member (anti-nuke's ban rows have no case). The typed `unban`, `untimeout` and `unquarantine` name a member, not a case, and lift what the member has now, on purpose. A ban made by hand in Discord opens no case, so only the run-out check can catch it.

**A Trusted Admin could give a quarantined nuker their roles back.** (security review, 11-17)
The quarantine hold exempted every level that `whitelisted("quarantine")` accepts, and Owner, Extra Owner and Trusted Admin accept every flag. `unquarantine` let any immune level release a member whose saved roles hold a permit or dangerous permissions. *Avoid:* the hold asks `hold_exempt`: Owner, Extra Owner, a permit with the `quarantine` flag or the quarantine whitelist; a Trusted Admin only for a join quarantine (an unverified joiner). Their change is undone; they stay immune to punishment (the nuke run logs them). `unquarantine` of staff needs Owner or Extra Owner. A level-wide `whitelisted` is never the right check for a bar that is narrower than "immune". No dashboard or MCP route releases a quarantine.

**Panic mode started by a Trusted Admin loaded the latest backup.** (security review, 11-17)
A direct load needs Owner or Extra Owner (`t!backups load`, the dashboard `load`), but the dashboard's panic start needs only Admin, and panic's `LoadLatestBackup` step loaded whatever the trip settings said. *Avoid:* the `panic_start` job carries `by`; `panic::start` takes it, and `may_restore` reads that member's level when the job runs: Owner or Extra Owner loads, anything else (or an unread level, or an old job without `by`) skips the load with a report line that says why. A trip (no person) loads as set up. A second way to reach an action asks the bar of the first, also through a queued job.

**Mass role removal from members was not counted by anti-nuke.** (security review, 11-17)
`watched` had no action for a `MemberRoleUpdate` that takes roles, so a staff member could strip every member's roles. *Avoid:* `Action::RoleRemove` (opt-in card, heat 15 as a timeout) counts by heat only (`heat_only`: left out of `watched`, so it never trips a minute or hour cap) and is undone by `Inverse::AddRoles`, which adds each role back one by one (`RestoreRoles` replaces the whole list and would drop roles given since). A new action needs `watched`, `inverse`, `undo`, `undo_text`, `run_inverse`, the dashboard's `run_inverse`, `NUKE_CARDS` and the coverage note.

**A taken-over guard job kept its lease, heat panic ignored immunity, and the rank guard still stopped silently on an unread owner or victim.** (final review of audit-p, 11-17)
A live run reused a pending `nuke_revert` job as it was, so a job a failed run left leased (heavy lease, 900 s) retried 15 minutes late. With a failed whitelist lookup, `count_at` added heat and called `panic::note_heat` for an immune actor. A retry that contained a nuker opened no case. `guard_rank` still used `?` on the owner and the victim's level. *Avoid:* the takeover is one `UPDATE .. SET run_at = now + 120 s, locked_until = NULL .. RETURNING id` (safe: a job runs only under the run claim); `count_at` asks `is_immune` before heat panic (immune: no panic, unread: counts); the late containment opens a case and names it; any unread level in `guard_rank` undoes as if the victim were the owner and the actor had no level, and logs. Discord logs say only "not saved"; the database error goes to tracing. `first_in` prunes past 10 000 keys.

**A nuke run reverted and reported an immune actor, a late containment was silent, and each unread run kept its own guard job.** (review of audit-p, 11-17)
An Owner, Extra Owner or Trusted Admin reaches a nuke run only when a whitelist lookup failed, but a level read of Ok(true) still undid their work, opened a quarantine case and counted towards panic. `REPORTED` took "stopped" only from a run that reports, so a retry that contained the actor sent no log and left "left free" set, and a second nuke in the hour was not reported. Each live run queued its own `nuke_revert` job, and an unread error ended the run before the actions that came in meanwhile were reverted. *Avoid:* `nuke_run` reads the level once per run: immune ends the run with one log and no revert, case or panic count; unread reverts, opens no quarantine case (`UNREAD`), and fails at the end (`LevelUnread`) after the revert-only passes. A non-reporting run that stops the actor logs it and sets `mark_stopped`. A run takes over a pending `nuke_revert` job of the same actor. The case line says "not saved" or "none" instead of a case 0, and `quarantine_actor` sends its log when the case insert fails.

**A live nuke run quarantined an immune member whose level could not be read, and a rank breach with an unread level was left in place.** (audit, 11-17)
`count_audit` counts an action when the whitelist lookup fails, but the live run then contained without asking the level, so a Discord 5xx made an Owner, Extra Owner or Trusted Admin a quarantine target. `resume_nuke` folded the level into `contain = false`, which `revert_and_report` reads as "contained earlier", so the report said "Quarantined" and hid channels from a free actor. `guard_rank` returned on an unread actor level, so a ban of a permit holder stayed with no log. *Avoid:* the Contain step asks `is_immune` itself on every run: Ok(false) contains, Ok(true) and Err contain nobody and say why, and Err fails the run so the `nuke_revert` guard job asks again. `guard_rank` undoes as for the lowest level and logs "their level could not be read". Searched: `voice_nuke::apply` used `?` on the whitelist lookup and dropped the count; it now counts as `count_audit` does.
**A failed case insert dropped the nuke report, and a nuker the bot could not contain got a case per action.** (audit, 11-17)
`report` used `?` on `open_case` and `record_nuke` before the log and the owner DM, and was then marked reported. The live path had no `reported` check, so each run against an uncontainable nuker opened a case and a full report. *Avoid:* the case and the record are best effort (the log says what was not saved); a live run reports only when the last report in the hour did not leave the actor free (`reported_free`), so a released nuker who nukes again is reported again.
**Enforce permits stopped at the first role it could not remove.** (audit, 11-17)
The loop used `?` on each removal, so one 5xx left the other dangerous roles on with no log. *Avoid:* a loop over independent items logs a failed one and goes on; the log names what was not removed (`outcome`).

**An embed-only message was logged as an empty "-" line.** (owner decision, 11-17)
The message cache kept only text and file names, so a deleted bot or embed-only message showed an empty content diff, and an edit or bulk delete file gave no sign it had an embed. *Avoid:* the cache keeps the embed count, and every log that shows cached content goes through `CachedMessage::text` (or `cache::shown` for a live message): text, then "(1 embed)". Delete, edit and bulk delete all use it.
**A failed temp ban kept its unban job, and a softban's failed unban left a lasting ban.** (audit, 11-17)
The temp ban queued its `unban` job before `create_ban` and left it when Discord refused the ban, so the job could later lift a ban made by hand. A softban ran `delete_ban(..)?`, so an outage on the unban returned an error with no case and kept the member banned. *Avoid:* a job queued before an action is finished when Discord refused the action (a 4xx, `http_err::refused`; after an outage the ban may stand, so its unban stays) (`unban_job` in `apply_dm`); a second step of an action that already happened is queued as a job on failure, never dropped with `?`.

**The report Delete button skipped immunity, and chat showed settings panels to every permit.** (audit, 11-17)
`reports::act` deleted the reported message of an Owner, TA or any permit holder, while `purge` of one member refuses them; every failure said "it may be gone". `settings::try_run` showed `Op::Show` panels (for example the anti-nuke caps) to anyone with a permit, while the dashboard needs `dashboard_view`. *Avoid:* a second way to do the same thing runs the same check as the main way: the Delete button runs `permits::check` on the author and maps errors with `http_err::gone_any` and `why`; a settings panel needs Dashboard Access or Dashboard Edit, as the dashboard does.
**A failed confirmed command left "Working on it..." on screen.** (owner decision, 11-17)
After the question became "Working on it..." / "Purging messages...", an error from `guild_info`, `cfg` or `Pending::run` returned through `?`, so nothing replaced that line. *Avoid:* `handle_confirm` catches every error of the run and an empty result and writes "The command did not finish: <reason>" in its place.

**A second Yes press showed "This interaction failed".** (owner decision, 11-17)
`confirm::handle_confirm` acknowledged Yes with `DeferredUpdateMessage`, which changes nothing on screen, so during a slow run (a `t!purge 100`) the question and its live buttons stayed and the moderator pressed again. That press found the row already taken, was treated as "expired" and tried to rewrite the question to "Timed out, nothing was done." while the first run was still busy and the purge deleted that very message. *Avoid:* the first answer is an `UpdateMessage` that removes the buttons and shows what runs ("Purging messages..."); a press that finds no row gets an ephemeral reason from the command's snowflake age in milliseconds (`gone_text`: already answered, or timed out after 10 s) and never touches the message. A late press leaves the row, so the expiry job closes the question. One handler serves every caller (ban, kick, timeout, warn by id, hackban, unban, quarantine, purge, note sweep, closeall, slash and prefix).

**A purge's result went to a message the purge deleted.** (owner decision, 11-17)
After a confirmed `t!purge` the result edited the question, which was among the newest messages and so deleted; the moderator saw nothing, and the mod log had no channel, filter or skipped count. *Avoid:* a purge posts its own note in the channel (`modcmds::post_briefly`, removed after 10 s, owner decision 11-17), sets `Applied::posted` so no reply repeats it, and its mod log entry adds Channel, Deleted, Not deleted (older than 14 days), Filter and time (`purge_log`). `t!purge N` and `/purge` count N messages from before the command (`ModCmd::Purge::before`: the command message or interaction id); the command message (`own`, real message ids only) goes in the batch, uncounted and best effort, and the question is deleted after the note. When the mod log and the Message Delete Bulk log share a channel, the bulk embed is held back and its file goes under the case (`purge::on_bulk`, one entry, owner decision 11-17); a bulk delete is matched to its purge by message id, so two purges in one channel keep their own files, and a failed purge sends what it held. In different channels both stay. A refused purge says "I lack Manage Messages or Read Message History", not the role-order text of `http_err::why`.

**A kick in one server hid a leave log in another, and a self-delete was blamed on a moderator.** (audit, 11-17)
The logger kept kick and ban audit entries by user id only, so a kick of X in server A within 10 s dropped X's "Member Leave" log in server B. It joined a message delete to a moderator's audit entry on author and channel for 60 s, so X deleting their own message there after a moderator deleted one was logged with that moderator as Responsible. *Avoid:* removals are kept per (guild, user); an audit delete names one gateway delete only, seen before it or within `HOLD` after, and is removed once it matched. A later delete then shows no Responsible line instead of a wrong one.
**Commands, joins and the dashboard skipped immunity for the member they acted on.** (audit, 11-17)
`note remove ID` (without `@user`), `note modify`, a note sweep by author and `purge N @member` had no target, so `permits::check` never ran: a moderator could rewrite notes about themselves or an Owner, wipe the Owner's notes, or purge an Owner's messages. The dashboard's case edit and note edit, delete and sweep had no check at all. Verification's `on_join` and `deadline` never asked `is_immune`, so "quarantine on join" stripped a rejoining EO or TA, and the time limit kicked them. *Avoid:* every action on a member's record or messages finds that member first (the note's or case's `user_id`) and refuses self and an immune member unless the actor is immune (`modcmds::run`, `records::subject_guard`). `purge N @member` goes through `ModCmd::target` and `permits::check` like ban, so it refuses self and every permit holder (owner decision 11-17); only the role order check is skipped, since deleting messages needs none. An automatic action on a joiner asks `is_immune` first; a failed read keeps the action at join (contain first) and retries the job at the deadline. Web mode releases an EO who is still in a join quarantine instead of refusing them.

**`t!tshoot` reset anti-nuke and automod heat.** (audit, 11-17)
Anyone with a permit could clear `nuke_heat`, `heat`, `pings` and `mentions` for the whole server between bursts, so mixed actions never reached the heat trip and raiders restarted at 0. *Avoid:* a cache reset clears caches only (`invalidate_guild`, `forget_cfg`); detection state is never reset by a command.

**Verify answered after the grant, and errors left no answer.** (audit, 11-17)
Verify Start and the solved captcha ran `self_grant` (reads, a member PATCH in the busy member bucket, logs) before `create_response`, and every `?` went to `events::handle`, which only logs. During a raid, or for a member in another quarantine, Discord showed "This interaction failed". The same kind of hidden reason: a failed ticket close always said "check that the bot can send files", and `note add` with closed DMs said only "Could not DM you" although the note was saved. *Avoid:* slow work after a click runs after a defer and edits the answer; a failure is mapped to text that names the cause (`verify::grant_failed`, the `send` refusal or `http_err::why`). Also a DM challenge waits at most `DM_WAIT`.

**A message menu purge skipped "Use slash commands" and Confirm.** (audit, 11-17)
`slash::run` sent message context menus to `reports::message_menu`, which called `modcmds::run` before the `use_slash` check and without `ask`. *Avoid:* a menu builds the same `Cmd` as its slash command (`Cmd::Ext`) and takes the same path; only user menus skip Confirm.

**"Quarantine on join" replaced the roles of members who were not held.** (audit, 11-17)
`grant` replaced the member's role list when `quarantine_on_join` was on, so a joiner who was never quarantined, an old member, or one just released by `t!verify` lost every role. *Avoid:* only `replace_roles` or a real join quarantine (`quarantine_kind == Some(true)`) replaces roles; otherwise roles are added.

**The raid alert DM linked the panic log.** (audit, 11-17)
`alert_owner` always took `LogKind::Panic`, but join and voice raid logs go to `JoinRaid`. *Avoid:* the alert takes the kind of the log it points to.

**A remade log channel took old backup overwrites without saying so.** (audit, 11-17)
`copy_overwrites` uses the newest backup (a nuker may have added a grant first), which also undoes a tightening made after the backup. *Avoid:* the "Log Channel Deleted" alert names every role or member whose overwrite differs between the backup and the deleted channel (`protected::differing`).

**Lookups ran once per item.** (audit, 11-17)
`unban a b c` read the whole ban list (up to 50 pages) for each name, and a purge with `role`/`norole` read one member at a time, also for webhook authors. *Avoid:* read a list once per command (`targets::ban_names`) and read members a few at a time (`buffer_unordered(5)`), once each. An author who left counts as having no role, so `norole` matches them (owner decision 11-17); a failed read is unknown and matches no role filter (`purge::known_roles`).

**An unsaved verification channel was made again for every joiner.** (audit, 11-17)
`adopt` only logged a refused save and posted the panel anyway, so `channel` stayed empty and the next joiner made another channel, up to the channel cap in a raid. *Avoid:* `ensure_channel` deletes a channel it could not save, waits 10 minutes per server before it tries again, and tells the owner why.
**An unlock lifted invite and DM pauses the lock did not set.** (audit, 11-17)
The unlock sent `pause_invites(None)`, which clears both pauses, so the owner's own 24 h pause (or one a Discord AutoMod rule set) ended with a 10-minute lock. *Avoid:* before a lock pauses invites, `plan_now` reads the guild's `incidents_data` into the snapshot's `prior_pauses`, and the unlock puts back each end still ahead (`set_pauses`). A failed read, or one slower than 2 s, does not stop the lock; that unlock lifts both, as before.

**A softban of an id that was already banned unbanned it.** (audit, 11-17)
A softban is a ban and an unban, with no check that the target is in the server. *Avoid:* `apply_dm` refuses a softban of someone who is not a member ("use ban").

**A timeout with the server default length had no duration in its case and mod log.** (audit, 11-17)
Only the DM got the default; the case and log got the typed `?t`, which was None. *Avoid:* `apply_dm` sets `duration_s` to the real timeout length once, for the DM, case and log.

**Every prefixed message fetched its channel from Discord.** (audit, 11-17)
`commands::ignored` asked for the channel kind over HTTP on each message, and a bad body skipped automod for it. *Avoid:* `GuildInfo.voice` holds the cached voice and stage channels; HTTP only for a channel the cache does not know (not a known thread), and any failure counts as not voice.

**The adder of an unauthorized bot got the harshest action of all the bot's filters.** (audit, 11-17)
`adder_action` was the harshest action over every filter the bot matched, so a No Avatar ban banned the member who added it. *Avoid:* the adder gets the Unauthorized Bot filter's own action.

**The security score missed a main role with Mention @everyone.** (audit, 11-17)
The role loop and `stripped` used `DANGEROUS` for every role but @everyone, while the overwrite check used `EVERYONE_DANGEROUS` for main roles too. *Avoid:* `role_mask` gives @everyone and the main roles `EVERYONE_DANGEROUS` in the role row, `strip_targets` and `stripped`. Staff roles stay out (`staff_mention_note`).

**A nuke retry quarantined a member made immune since, and a config failure reported a member as contained while a role it could not take kept them armed.** (audit, 11-17)
`resume_nuke` contained whenever the actor was free, without asking their level, and `strip_dangerous` only looked at dangerous roles below the bot. *Avoid:* a retry contains only when `is_immune` answers Ok(false) (an unread level does not punish, and the next retry asks again), and the config-failure strip reports success only when no dangerous role is left at all, including roles above the bot and managed ones.

**A lock set-back used the audit entry's old value.** (audit, 11-17)
`guard_lock` set a locked role or overwrite back to the entry's `old` value. Two quick edits (L -> L+Send -> L+Send+Embed) set the second one back to L+Send, so members could write during a lock. *Avoid:* `lock_target` takes the target from the lock snapshots only: a role gets its saved value minus every holding lock's `takes`, an overwrite gets what `lockdown::exec` wrote (`prior` allow minus SILENCE, `prior` deny plus SILENCE). No live read is needed. Searched: the role remap after a restore already strips `takes`. On purpose (owner decision, 11-17): under an All lock a non-main staff role is set back without SILENCE too, as the restore path does. A set-back never adds a `DANGEROUS` bit the entry's old value lacked (`& !(DANGEROUS & !old)`): a channel-only lock takes none, and its saved value can hold Administrator that staff removed since (security pass). The same holds for an overwrite's allow, against the entry's old allow (empty when the entry has none) (security pass 2).

**One refused channel stopped hiding the rest from a quarantined member.** (audit, 11-17)
`hide_channels` used `?` on each channel's deny, so a 404 (a channel deleted in the nuke) or a 403 left every later channel open, with only a tracing warning. *Avoid:* the loop logs each failure and goes on, returns an error naming the failed channels at the end, and `hide_channels_best_effort` posts it to the Anti Nuke log. Containment first (owner decision, 11-17): when `deny_member` cannot save the member's own overwrite, the deny still goes on, and the Anti Nuke log names that overwrite's allow and deny bits, since it will not come back on release.

**Anti-nuke logs said "undone" or gave a wrong reason.** (audit, 11-17)
The vanity guard only warned on a failed PATCH (and undid nothing when there was no earlier code), `hold_overwrite` only warned on a failed undo and then logged "undone where it can be", and every failed nuke quarantine said "check that the bot's role is on top", also when the nuker had left or only the `action_log` row failed (which also made `contain_bot` kick a quarantined bot). *Avoid:* a failed undo goes into `undo` or its own failure log, and no "undone" log follows it. `Applied::Failed` carries the reason (left the server, missing permissions, see logs). `record_quarantine` is best effort once the role change worked. The hold's `unsure` branch uses `undo_role_change` (retry and log), not `?`.
**A restore said "Restored" when Discord refused steps.** (audit, 11-17)
`backup::execute` only traced each refused step (and the role order call), so `/backups load` and the panic report counted the successes and named no failure. *Avoid:* every step goes through `Summary::count`, which counts the failure and keeps the first reason; the load reply and the panic report show "N steps failed, first: <reason>". Searched: webhook deletes and both position calls use it too.

**A restore dropped every member overwrite.** (audit, 11-17)
`map_overwrites` kept only targets in `id_map`, which holds roles, channels and the guild, so a recreated or updated channel lost its member overwrites (ticket access, member mutes, Brim's own allow). *Avoid:* only role targets are remapped; a member overwrite keeps its user id. But an image can hold a grant a nuker gave himself, and a member overwrite beats the quarantine role's deny (security pass, 11-17). So `execute` runs `guard_members`: a quarantined member's or a departed user's overwrite is dropped, an Update keeps a quarantined member's live overwrite (the quarantine deny), and every member who is not immune (or Brim) loses `DANGEROUS_PERMS` from the allow. `protected::copy_overwrites` drops member overwrites for the same reason.

**Auto backups reached only the first 200 servers.** (audit, 11-17)
`current_user_guilds` returns at most 200 rows, and a parse error read as "no servers". *Avoid:* `backup::all_guilds` pages with `after(last id)` until a page has fewer than 200 rows, and a failed page is logged. Any other job over every server must page the same way.

**Restore kept every channel named "server-locked" and put recreated channels at the bottom.** (audit, 11-17)
A nuker could name spam channels `server-locked` to keep them through a panic restore, and created channels got no position. *Avoid:* keep the notice channel by the id the lock saved (`Snapshot.locked_channel`), never by name. After the channel loop, one `update_guild_channel_positions` call puts created channels at their image positions, as roles already were.

**Restore could delete the catch channel, and an older image gave back dangerous overwrites.** (audit, 11-17)
`KEEP_FIELDS` lacked `catch.channel` and `catch.category`, so a restore before the next backup deleted the honeypot and its Information category. `newest_with` stripped `DANGEROUS_PERMS` from roles of an older image but not from channel overwrites. *Avoid:* every channel or role Auto Setup makes goes into `KEEP_FIELDS` and its test. A channel from an older image loses dangerous overwrite allows and counts in `stripped`, as a role does.
**Automod, NSFW scan, invites and raiders: eight small gaps.** (audit, 11-17)
"Not NSFW" was allowed "from now on", but the 30-day sweep (and the failed-punishment cleanup in `act`) deleted its `feedback = 'fp'` row, the only memory of it. A failed NSFW catch-up sweep marked the server swept and never retried, one unreadable config ended the pass for all servers, and the enabled-servers query read every stored config version. The raid detector redid the ID-farm analysis per joiner per bucket under a blocking mutex, with no cap on the window. `is_public` let 192.0.0.0/24, 198.18.0.0/15, 240.0.0.0/4, NAT64, 6to4 and 2001:db8::/32 through. Any non-Brim unban counted as staff allowing a raider, a bot's too. `LockKind::mask` always held View Channel, so unlocking a plain Server lock gave it back although only Blind takes it. The invite tracker credited a code that was one use short and also expired. *Avoid:* a row that is a user decision is exempt from every retention sweep and cleanup; a "done" set is filled only after the work succeeds; a trust record from an audit actor needs the actor's permit and a human check (`has("ban")` covers Owner, EO, TA); an unlock mask lists only what the lock took; a per-joiner analysis is computed per bucket and its window capped (`MAX_JOINS`). Not fixed: a code deleted by the invite-delete event while one use short still counts as a last use, since Discord sends that event for a last use too.
**A failed level lookup quarantined whoever deleted the verification channel.** (audit, 11-17)
`verify::deleted::on_audit` read a failed owner or level lookup as `Level::None`, so a Discord 5xx made the owner, an Extra Owner or a Trusted Admin who deleted the channel a quarantine target. *Avoid:* the D12b rule: an unread level means make the channel again and punish nobody (`Deleter::Unread`), and the alert says the level could not be read. Searched: no other `unwrap_or(Level::None)` is left in the bot.

**Ticket commands skipped the gates every other command has.** (audit, 11-17)
`tickets::on_message` runs before `commands::ignored`, and answered "no access" with no cooldown to quarantined members, in voice-channel chats and with "Ignore Permit 1 Commands" on. Its admin commands were listed as Manage Tickets in `t!commands`, `-h` and the dashboard, but need Owner, Extra Owner or Trusted Admin. Ticket DM and panel answers of Owner, EO and TA also went through the word and invite filters. *Avoid:* a command path of its own repeats `commands::ignored`, `ignore_no_permit` and `cooled_down`; the help table names the real bar (`"immune"` in `general::CMDS`, `access::command` on the dashboard) and the refusal names it too; a filter on member text skips immune levels (`dm::bypasses`, a failed lookup filters).

**A deleted panel category stopped every ticket of that panel.** (audit, 11-17)
`panel::open` and `public::ensure` used the saved category id as is, so after staff deleted it every create failed with a vague error. *Avoid:* the same as "A saved id can point at something deleted": `tickets::category_live` (cache, then Discord, only 10003 is gone) before using a panel category, else the ticket category through `ensure_category`, which `public::ensure` now calls too.

**The dashboard knew only the ticket category, not the panel categories.** (ticket types, 11-22)
Channel Setup (`fixed_by`, `own_access`), the Message Builder's create refusal, `embeds::bot_channel` and lockdown read only `tickets.category`, so a channel in a panel's own category could get its access reset or a bot post. *Avoid:* every check for "this holds ticket channels" uses `tickets::categories` (raw config, so a panel that does not parse keeps its category). A full category (50 channels) made every panel ticket fail: `panel::open` counts the children first and goes on to the ticket category, else `reply.ticket.categories_full` and one owner DM a day (not `alert_owner`: that shares the attack alert slot). `dm::send_mail` does the same check, and asks the type before it makes the channel.

**A click or a DM paid REST calls per server or per member page with no cache.** (audit, 11-17)
The DM server menu asked Discord about every ticket server on each open and each "Other server" press, and a message menu's `role.member_count` paged up to 10 000 members on every click. *Avoid:* cache such answers per user or per server for a minute (`dm::shared`, `msgmenu::role_counts`), give every button that starts the work the same cooldown (`tk:other`), and never cache an answer built while a lookup failed. A cap on the lookups (first try: 50 servers) is wrong here: it locked out members of every newer server (review).

**A verified-member cache outlived the membership.** (audit, 11-17)
`verify::given` remembered a verified member for 10 minutes, also across a leave and rejoin, so a bot's role grant to the rejoined unverified member was not taken back. *Avoid:* state about a member's stay is forgotten on `MemberRemove` (`given::forget_member`, before the event's first await).

**A channel overwrite gave dangerous permissions past the grant guard.** (owner, 11-15)
After the role grants were guarded for everyone, a non-immune member (or a bot with the `antinuke` whitelist flag) could still give a staff role or a single member Manage Messages, Manage Channels or Administrator-like rights in a channel: the overwrite guard watched only @everyone and the main roles, only with `monitor_channel_perms` on, and exempted the whitelist flag. Root cause: the same rule was built once per Discord object (role, member, overwrite) and only the role half was moved to `is_immune`. *Avoid:* `ChannelOverwriteCreate`/`Update` are grants in `guard_perms`: any role or member overwrite (`options.type` "0"/"1", member ones undone with `PermissionOverwriteType::Member`) that gains a `DANGEROUS_PERMS` allow bit is undone unless `is_immune`, always on, with the live re-read and the current denies kept. `strict_mode`, `strict_member_roles` and `monitor_channel_perms` were removed (serde ignores the keys in old configs and exports; `AntinukeCfg` has no `deny_unknown_fields`). Brim's own entries (Channel Setup, lockdown, panic, backup restore and the dashboard, which uses the same bot token) are skipped by `actor == app.me` in `on_audit_as`. Also fixed in the same pass: a failed undo is logged as failed, not as undone; a looping MemberRoleUpdate take-back (a reaction-role bot) is logged through `take_back_log_due`; a dangerous role that also carries a permit is handled only by `guard_perms`; an unreadable lock state falls back to the live roles and then takes the roles back unpunished; a replayed RoleCreate removes only the dangerous bits the role still holds; BotAdd reads the bot's role a second time before it gives up. Searched: `hold_overwrite` (quarantined members, quarantine whitelist, may act on the same entry), `guard_lock` (lock snapshots), `verify::given` (verified channel access) and the role guards; no other overwrite path gives permissions.

**Dangerous grants were guarded only behind strict mode and the whitelist flag.** (owner, 11-15)
`guard_perms` undid a role created with Administrator (or another `DANGEROUS_PERMS` bit), a role given one, or a member given such a role only with `strict_mode` or `strict_member_roles` on (both off by default), and a permit with the `antinuke` whitelist flag (a bot's too) exempted the actor. A bot added with Administrator on its own role was not checked at all. Root cause: a rule from "trust comes from Brim, never from Discord rights" was built as an opt-in toggle with the module's general whitelist. *Avoid:* RoleCreate, RoleUpdate, MemberRoleUpdate and BotAdd grants are always guarded and exempt only `is_immune` (Owner, Extra Owner, Trusted Admin); a failed lookup undoes without punishment. A bot's own role is judged once, on the BotAdd entry with the adder as actor: the RoleCreate of a role with `tags.bot_id` is skipped (Discord's docs do not say whom that entry names), and a MemberRoleUpdate never judges a managed role. The toggles were removed later the same day (see the next entry). Searched: `guard_permit_roles`, the join gate bot filter and the hold already ask `is_immune`/the quarantine whitelist; the vanity guard keeps its toggle and whitelist on purpose.

**Another Discord bot was named in Brim text.** (bot profile PR, owner decision 11-15)
A PR body, commit message and code comments said "as in <other bot>". Rule: never name another Discord bot anywhere Brim text lands: code, comments, docs, commit messages, PR text, issues. Say what the rule is, not where it comes from. *Avoid:* before a commit or PR, check `git diff origin/main` and `git log origin/main..HEAD` for the names of other bots.

**An Extra Owner was asked about as if it were less than the owner.** (bot profile, owner decision 11-15)
The bot profile plan gave a new owner-only page to the physical Discord owner only, and the owner had to answer again what an Extra Owner may do. Rule (owner decision 11-15): an Extra Owner is the same as the physical owner and can do all the owner can, and is fully immune, except two things: only the physical owner adds or removes Extra Owners, and only the physical owner holds the rescue key. *Avoid:* a new owner feature uses `Level::Owner` (owner or Extra Owner) with 2FA for writes; use `api::is_discord_owner` only for the Extra Owner list, the rescue key and the other physical-owner things that already use it (handover Keep, purge). Ask owner decision only for a new physical-owner-only case.

**With anti-nuke off, the hold logged an undo that never happened.** (review of permits-only-2, 11-14)
A kick, ban or member edit of a quarantined member cannot be undone, but with `punish` false `hold` sent "undone where it can be" for them. *Avoid:* with anti-nuke off, `hold` returns without a log for `MemberUpdate`, `MemberKick` and `MemberBanAdd`. A log line must say only what the code did.

**A join-quarantine undo was silent with anti-nuke off.** (review of permits-only-2, 11-14)
Roles given to an unverified (join-quarantined) member were taken back with no log, so with anti-nuke off staff never learned who gave them. *Avoid:* the `on_join` branch of `hold` logs who gave which roles and that nobody was punished when `punish` is false. Who is exempt did not change (a verify permit exemption is asked separately).

**A take-back log could flood the anti-nuke channel.** (review of permits-only-2, 11-14)
A reaction-role or level bot that gives a permit role on every click made one take-back log per click. *Avoid:* `guard_permit_roles` logs one message per (guild, actor, role set) per 10 minutes (`take_back_log_due`); the next log says how many were taken back since. The take-back itself always runs. For a staff (guarded) permit the owner also gets one DM at the fifth take-back of the same (guild, actor, role set) (`owner_dm_due`, in memory, owner decision); weak permits never DM.

**A late undo set back a newer change.** (review of permits-only-2, 11-14)
`guard_perms` restored the entry's old permissions on a role or channel overwrite without looking at the current value. A replayed or slow entry could so undo a later, correct change too. *Avoid:* before the undo it reads the role (`http.roles`) or the overwrite (the channel) live; when the current value is no longer the entry's new value it skips the undo and logs that. Anti-nuke on or off. `guard_lock` sets back to the lock's snapshot, which is meant to win, so it has no such check.

**A verify permit could not verify a join-quarantined member by hand.** (owner decision, 11-14)
With quarantine on join, the hold took back every role a held joiner got from someone without the quarantine whitelist, also the verification role a `verify` permit gave, and `verify::given` left it to the hold. *Avoid:* `hold`'s `on_join` branch calls `verify::grant` (the same path as the button: roles set, join quarantine row deleted) when a verification role is among the added roles and `given::may_verify` says yes (a person, not a bot, with `verify` or `quarantine`, or Owner, EO, TA). `given::hold_takes_it` returns false for such an actor on a join quarantine, so the two places agree. Any other role is still taken back.

**A listed bot kept user trust at runtime.** (owner decision, 11-14)
GuildCreate removes bots from the EO, TA and quarantine whitelist lists, but until then (or after a failed check) `permits::resolve` and the hold still read them as listed. *Avoid:* `resolve` takes `bot` and skips the EO and TA tables for a bot (`App::level` passes `member.user.bot`), and `App::quarantine_listed` ignores a bot on the quarantine whitelist. The bot's role permits still count. Callers without a member at hand pass `false`; none of them grants trust to the actor.

**Discord can hand out a permit role by itself.** (owner decision, 11-14)
Onboarding answers, linked roles and server subscription or purchase roles are given by Discord, with no actor in the audit log for the take-back guard to judge. A permit on such a role is a permit for anyone. *Avoid:* `api::permit_roles_ok` refuses newly added permit roles with tags `guild_connections`, `subscription_listing_id` or `available_for_purchase`, or listed in `GET /guilds/{id}/onboarding` prompt options (404 is no Onboarding; any other error refuses). A bot's own role (`bot_id`) stays allowed. At runtime `App::level` drops linked and subscription roles (`GuildInfo::unpermittable`) when the guild info is cached; Onboarding roles are not marked in the role tags, so they are caught at save only.

**A permit guard ran only with anti-nuke on.** (live test 11-13)
`guard_permit_roles` took back a permit role given by someone without the right to give it, but `on_audit_as` returned before it when anti-nuke was off, so with anti-nuke off a Discord Manage Roles holder could hand out a permit. *Avoid:* with anti-nuke off the guard still runs, takes the role back and punishes nobody (`punish = false`). Rules that come from "only permits act" (owner decision 11-13) must not hang on a module switch.

**The other undo guards also ran only with anti-nuke on.** (plan permits-only-2, 11-13)
`hold`, `hold_overwrite`, `guard_lock` and `guard_perms` sat after the same early return, so with anti-nuke off a quarantined member could be given roles back and a lock or strict mode was not enforced. A weak permit role was only logged, never taken back. *Avoid:* `on_audit_as` runs every guard with `punish = cfg.antinuke.enabled`; with `punish` false a guard undoes and logs "Anti-nuke is off, so nobody was punished" instead of `quarantine_and_revert`, and only the counting stops. `handled_audit` now records entries with anti-nuke off too, so a catch-up does not undo an entry twice. `verify::given::hold_takes_it` dropped its anti-nuke check with it, or verification and the hold would both act on one grant. Still gated on anti-nuke, correctly: `voice_nuke::fetch` (counting only).

**A command with no valid target said it succeeded.** (live test 11-13)
`t!warn @role` resolved no member and still replied "All members were warned!". *Avoid:* the shared target loop in `commands.rs` returns early with no targets and asks for a member when no name failed (a failed name was already answered). It is the one loop every mod command uses.

**A bot's managed role cannot be removed.** (#58, #133, #142)
Quarantining a nuking bot failed, because Discord does not let anyone take a bot's own managed role away, and that role kept Administrator. #142 found the same hole again in `quarantine_actor` (verification channel delete, command rate limit), which skipped the shared path.
*Avoid:* every place that contains an actor goes through `antinuke::contain`. It sets the managed role's permissions to 0, then quarantines, and kicks only as a fallback. Never write a second quarantine path. The overlap deep dive (11-12) found two more: the mod `/quarantine` command and the Raider List's Quarantine action called the plain quarantine, so a bot kept its managed role's Administrator. Both now call `contain` and say "Kicked" when the fallback ran. Verification's join and time-limit quarantines call `quarantine_as` directly because `verify::on_join` skips bots.

**A role list without the member's managed roles is refused.** (review, 11-12)
Discord refuses a role list that leaves out a managed role (Server Booster, an integration's role), and refuses to remove one. The quarantine hold set a held member to `[quarantine]` and verification replaced roles with the verification roles, so both failed for a booster. Because the hold used `?`, the actor who touched the member was then never quarantined. "Enforce permits" tried to remove a dangerous managed role and stopped at the error, so the member's other dangerous roles stayed and no log was sent.
*Avoid:* build every full role list with `antinuke::quarantine_roles` (it keeps the managed roles) or add the member's managed roles yourself, and never remove a managed role on purpose: skip `GuildInfo::managed`. Searched: `hold` (now `back_to_quarantine`), `verify::grant`, `enforce_permits`. `Inverse::RestoreRoles` and Unquarantine restore saved lists, which held the managed roles when they were saved.

**Role order decides everything.** (#57, #133)
The first live nuke test got 403 because Brim's role was tied with the attacker's. A role between the bot and the quarantine role also breaks quarantine.
*Avoid:* the bot role must sit above every role it acts on, and the quarantine role right below the bot. The security score checks the second one (Move role fix). When a live test gets 403, check the role order first.

**Tied role positions were compared as equal.**
On a server whose roles were never reordered, every role sits at position 1 (test server: both bots, Quarantine, Member, Admins, Moderators). Discord breaks the tie by id, with the older role ranking higher, but Brim compared only the numbers. So `/kick` and `/ban` refused any target with a role, anti-nuke would not strip Admins from a nuker, lockdown and `tshoot lockdown` skipped every role, the security score read them all as level with the bot, and Auto Setup never moved the quarantine role. `security.rs` already ranked ties by id, but other code called it with raw positions.
*Avoid:* rank roles once where they are read from Discord: `hierarchy::ranked` in the bot, `ops::roles_of` and `recovery::live_image` in the dashboard, all built on `brim_core::roles::ranks`. Every role list that is compared or written back goes through one of them. Never compare `Role::position` straight from Discord.

**Leaving and rejoining ended a quarantine.** (sticky roles plan, 11-11)
The `quarantines` row survived a leave, but no join code read it, so a quarantined member who left and came back was a normal member again. Only verification with "quarantine on join" put the role back, and only because it quarantines every unverified joiner. Checked for the same gap in other state: timeouts survive a rejoin in Discord itself, strikes and warn points are stored per user, and join lock and join raid act on every join.
*Avoid:* state that holds a member back must be re-applied on `MemberAdd`, not only while they stay. `antinuke::requarantine_rejoin` runs first in `joingate::on_join` and goes through `contain`, so a re-added bot loses its managed role's permissions too.
A rejoin while the bot was down sent no `MemberAdd` at all, so the member stayed free (11-13). *Avoid:* an event-driven re-apply needs a catch-up at startup. `joingate::requarantine_missed` runs at GuildCreate: a member with a row who joined after the row and after the bot's last `quarantine` record goes through `requarantine_rejoin`; one who joined before is the same membership (held, or released by hand as `still_contained` allows) and is left alone. It reads GuildCreate's member list when complete and one member per row otherwise.

**The post-join username filter punished immune staff.** (Join Gate check, 11-11)
Join Gate's username filter checks names again after join (the "POST join" switch) and called `punish` without a level check, so an Extra Owner or Trusted Admin who picked a nickname on the word list could be kicked or banned. At join time nobody is staff yet, so the gap only showed after join. The other automatic paths on existing members (automod, Discord AutoMod hits, reactions, threads) already go through `whitelisted`.
*Avoid:* every automatic action against a member who is already in the server checks `is_immune` or `whitelisted` first. The one deliberate exception is auto-dehoist (and Join Gate's Rename through `dehoist::rename`): the owner's D6 skips only the owner and Extra Owners, so Trusted Admins and mods are renamed too.
Join Raid had the same gap (review, 11-12): when a raid triggers, it punishes every joiner it flagged in the window, and an earlier joiner may have been made staff since. The loop now skips immune ids other than the joiner. A failed lookup punished anyway, so a Discord error could ban a Trusted Admin (11-13): it now queues the `raid_punish` retry, which checks immunity again and fails (so it runs again) while the lookup still fails. *Avoid:* when an immunity check fails, wait and check again; never read an error as "not immune" before a removal. Voice raid had no later check at all: members flagged earlier in the window were punished on the trigger even if made staff since. It now checks them the same way, and disconnects a member whose check failed at once (round 2): only the punishment waits for the retry.

**Member edits share one rate-limit bucket.** (auto-dehoist, 11-11)
Nick changes, role adds, timeouts and kicks of members all use Discord's member bucket, so a burst of automatic renames or role restores slows the punishments of a raid.
*Avoid:* bulk automatic member edits are capped per guild (`dehoist::rename`, 10 a minute through `RateLimiter`) and auto-dehoist is off during a join raid or lockdown.

**Not every member lookup error means "not a member".** (#97)
All `guild_member` errors (5xx, timeouts) were read as "left the server". Staff then lost their permit level for that event, so automod or anti-nuke could act on them.
*Avoid:* use `http_err::member`. Only a 404 means not a member. Other errors propagate.

**An automatic timeout shortened a longer one.** (review, 11-12)
Automod, warn thresholds, Join Gate and heat panic set `communication_disabled_until` to now plus their own length, so a 5-minute strike timeout replaced a 7-day staff timeout, and a long timeout's renewal lost to the next spam message. Root cause: "time out for X" was written as "the timeout ends in X", whatever ran before.
*Avoid:* every automatic timeout goes through `commands::timeout`, which reads the member first and leaves a timeout that ends later (`outlasts`); a failed read times out as before. A mod's own `/timeout` still sets exactly what they typed, shorter too.

**Swallowed errors hide missing permissions.** (#97)
`.await.ok` dropped real failures, so a punishment Discord refused left no trace.
*Avoid:* use `http_err::best_effort`. It stays quiet only for 404 and closed DMs (50007) and logs everything else. Failed automod actions go to the log with `action_failed`.

**Bulk delete takes at most 100 messages.** (#97)
More than 100 gave a 400, nothing was deleted, and the error was swallowed.
*Avoid:* split with `reply::batches` (100 per call, 14-day cutoff, plain delete for one message).

**Write state after the Discord call, or roll it back.** (#97)
The quarantine row was written before the role change. When the change failed, the bot believed a member was quarantined and refused a correct save later.
*Avoid:* write the row after the call succeeds, or delete the row you created when the call fails.

**Outgoing HTTP needs a timeout.** (#97)
The raw reqwest client had none, so one hung call could hold a lockdown or rule sync forever.
The dashboard's and the MCP server's Discord clients had none either, found 11-11.
*Avoid:* every client gets a timeout (15 s in `automod/src/discord_api.rs`, the dashboard's and the MCP's Discord client). Twilight already handles 429.
Ticket attachment copies still used a bare `reqwest::get` (no timeout), found 11-12 while adding the icon revert: one hung download held the ticket reply. They now use the bot's shared client (`Api::client`). Never call `reqwest::get` or `Client::new` outside tests.

**twilight 0.18 has no soundboard audit types.** (anti-nuke triggers, 11-12)
Types 130-132 arrive as `AuditLogEventType::Unknown(n)`. Matching `Unknown(132)` would silently stop matching once a later twilight names the variant.
*Avoid:* match a type twilight does not name by its number (`t if u16::from(t) == 132`), as `antinuke::watched` does.

**Voice disconnects and moves are merged into one audit entry.** (anti-nuke voice, 11-12)
Discord merges `MEMBER_DISCONNECT` (27) and `MEMBER_MOVE` (26) by the same actor in quick succession into the first entry and raises its `options.count`, and those updates fire no gateway event. Counting gateway entries sees 1 for a mass disconnect of 20.
*Avoid:* count the growth of `count`, not entries. `voice_nuke` watches a guild for 10 minutes after the first entry and rereads the audit log on voice updates (one fetch at a time, at most one per 3 s, plus a trailing fetch so the end of a burst counts). The last seen count per entry is read and stored under one lock, entries older than the bot are seeded at their count (a restart does not recount them), `count` is a string parsed to u32 and capped at 500, and 26/27 are counted only there, never also through `watched`. Message deletes merge the same way (see "Discord's own rendering quirks").
The review (11-12) found an entry first seen through a fetch counted in full: growth from while the guild was unwatched (a moderator moving 12 members over minutes) landed as one lump and quarantined them. Root cause: "first sight" was treated as "new", but a fetch can be the first sight of an old entry. A fetch now seeds an unseen entry made before the current watch began (minus 5 s slack, `State::seed_before`); a gateway entry (`note`) is new by definition and counts from its count.

**A server icon is downloaded from a stored hash.** (anti-nuke triggers, 11-12)
The icon revert fetches the old icon by the hash in `action_log.inverse`, which anyone with database or revert access could shape into another path.
*Avoid:* build the URL only with `brim_antinuke::icon_url` (`a_` + 32 lowercase hex, fixed CDN host) and fetch through `discord_api::icon_data_uri`: client with a timeout, no token, refuse a redirect off the CDN, `image/*` only, 10 MB cap while reading. Bot and dashboard share it.

**The old server icon is gone from the CDN.** (live test, 11-15)
The icon revert never worked live: Discord's CDN answers 404 for an icon hash the moment the server icon changes, so the download of the old icon always failed and the report said "Could not undo: Put the server icon back". Root cause: the design assumed the CDN keeps old hashes (unverified in the 11-12 plan). *Avoid:* Brim keeps its own copy (`guild_icons`, migration 3929): `welcome::save_icon` saves the current icon at GuildCreate and on every GuildUpdate, and a backup saves the icon it names. The anti-nuke revert (bot and dashboard) and the backup restore read the copy first through `discord_api::stored_icon` and fall back to the CDN only for the live icon. Kept per server: the newest 3, every icon a backup names and every icon an anti-nuke revert row (`action_log`) names, so an attacker's many changes cannot push out the icon from before the attack (review); the purge removes the rest with the guild. The save runs in the background so a slow CDN does not hold up GuildCreate, and a restore whose icon copy is gone says so. A backup without an icon never removes the live one. Searched: the bot revert, the dashboard revert center and the backup restore are the only places that set a server icon.

**A bot cannot delete another app's integration.** (live test, 11-15)
The anti-nuke undo "Remove the new app" never worked: `DELETE /guilds/{id}/integrations/{id}` with a bot token answers 403 Missing Access (50001), even with Administrator, so the report said "Could not undo" while the app stayed. Root cause: the undo used an endpoint Discord keeps for people. *Avoid:* `brim_antinuke::app_removal` looks the integration up and kicks the app's bot user, which removes the app with it (checked live); an app gone already counts as undone; an app with no bot user can only be removed by a person, and the report says where (Server Settings > Integrations). Searched: the bot revert (`antinuke::remove_app`) and the dashboard revert center (`ops::run_inverse`) are the only places that remove an app.

**A REST call before an interaction's answer can miss the 3 seconds.** (spammer-flag review, 11-11)
The verify Start button asked Discord for the spammer flag on every click before it answered, through `Api::send`, which waits out a 429 up to 30 s and retries; the 15 s client timeout was the only limit. Discord drops an interaction answer after 3 s, so a slow or rate-limited lookup left the member with "This interaction failed", and every click cost another request on one bucket. `verify::guild_name` (before the "verified" answer) had no limit either.
*Avoid:* do the work that needs no REST first and call only when the answer changes the result (`flagged_step`), cache the answer (`App::spam_flags`, 10 minutes, bounded), and bound the call: one try with no 429 wait (`Api::send_tries(.., 1)`) inside `tokio::time::timeout(1.5 s)`. Decide what a timeout means (here: a captcha, fail closed). Anything slower than that defers the interaction first.

**A DM can be slow or refused.** (#136, #142)
A DM before a punishment must not hold the punishment back, and a DM to a bot is refused.
*Avoid:* send DMs through the shared helper with the `DM_WAIT` limit (3 s). The punishment runs anyway. A DM that times out is logged.

**Discord's own rendering quirks.** (5ec560a, #92)
A mention of the @everyone role renders as `@@everyone`, so write plain `@everyone`. Discord lists each mentioned user once per message, so mention spam counts one ping per user per message. Discord merges audit log entries, so the bot's deletion of someone else's message can show no Responsible.

**A saved id can point at something deleted in Discord.** (#147)
Auto Setup answered "internal error" because the saved Quarantine role had been deleted in Discord: the PATCH got 404 and the whole setup stopped. Config keeps ids after the role or channel is gone (deleted by hand, or a backup restore).
*Avoid:* treat a 404 on a saved id as "missing" and make it again (`sections::create` checks an existing id first). Never let a 404 on reuse fail a whole setup.
The deep dive (11-12) found the same in every quarantine: with the Quarantine role deleted, each quarantine PATCHed a list holding the dead id, Discord refused it, and no human could be quarantined any more. `quarantine_as` now reads the live roles, leaves a missing quarantine role out (the member is stripped to their managed roles) and posts "Quarantine role missing" in the anti-nuke log. The hold and the live containment check use the same `quarantine_parts`.
The other half (11-13): when a revert, panic restore or backup load made the object again, it got a new id and the config still named the old one, and a deleted log channel had already been dropped from `logging` (`forget_log_channel`), so logs, catch, tickets and the quarantine role stayed broken after the cleanup. *Avoid:* every restore passes its old-to-new id map to `relink::relink` (`backup::recreate` and `backup::restore`), which swaps the id everywhere in the config and in `permit_roles` as one config version, and puts back the log routes the version just before the deletion sent to it: the version before the bot's `forget_log_channel` version, never an older one, so a route staff removed on purpose months ago does not come back (R-h3 7), and a slot that version had empty stays empty. The lookup is per deleted channel (R-h3 round 2, 1): `forget_log_channel` writes one version per channel, so a single newest lookup found only the channel deleted last. It also ran that lookup for every recreated role (R-h3 round 3): `relink` takes the image and looks up only the ids that were channels in it (`relink::channels`); the `permit_roles` swap still covers every id.

**A saved role id must be one the bot can use.** (review, 11-12)
The quarantine role and the main roles took any role id: @everyone, a managed role, or one above the bot. Auto Setup then set the picked "quarantine role" to no permissions, which for @everyone strips every member, and `t!setup` denied it in every channel.
*Avoid:* a role the bot edits or hands out passes `brim_core::roles::unfit` (not @everyone, not managed, below the bot's top role) wherever it is saved: the dashboard's `put_section` (`check_held_roles`, only ids the save adds), the bot's settings panel for main roles (`GuildInfo::unfit`), and Auto Setup and `t!setup`, which make a new Quarantine role instead of using an unfit saved one. Not covered: config import and History rollback write the stored JSON as is.

**For bans the bot makes on staff's behalf, the audit actor is the bot, so checks must use the case moderator.** (Ban Sync review, 11-11)
Ban Sync held a ban for 60 s and dropped it when anti-nuke contained the audit actor. A `t!ban` or a dashboard ban is made with the bot's token, so the actor was always the bot, never the hacked moderator, and the check could never fire for staff commands. Root cause: the audit entry names who called Discord, not who asked the bot to.
*Avoid:* when the actor is the bot, take the moderator from the matching case (`cases.mod_id`, written by `apply_dm` within seconds) and check that person; with no case (an automatic ban), stop only for a nuke case or the quarantine of someone whose permit can ban, kick or quarantine since the ban. Any quarantine at all is too wide: Join Gate and verification quarantine raiders on join, so raiders could switch sharing off by getting themselves quarantined. Store the moderator with anything that acts later (`ban_sync_links.mod_id`), so the later step checks again. Searched for the same mistake: `antinuke::on_audit` skips the bot's own entries on purpose (staff commands are limited by the permit rate limit in `commands::gated` instead), and `verify::deleted::on_audit` treats the bot as the actor only for its own deletes; neither reads a staff member from a bot entry.

**Never mark a row done before the Discord call.** (Ban Sync review, 11-11)
The first Ban Sync copy set its row to `banned` before the ban, so a job that crashed or failed in between left a "banned" row with no ban, and the retry saw `banned` and stopped. Root cause: the claim that makes a step run once was used as the record that it happened.
*Avoid:* keep the row in its working state (`pending`) while the call runs, so a failure leaves it for the retry; on the retry, recognise your own earlier result in Discord (a ban whose reason starts with `Ban Sync from` counts as ours) and only then move the row on with `UPDATE … WHERE state = 'pending' RETURNING`; write the case only if the row has none yet. Mark a row skipped only for a refusal, never while the action exists. The Ban here button does the same and gives the row back to `alerted` when the ban does not happen. Searched for the same mistake: `reports::report` deletes its row when the log message fails, `joingate::punish` records after the action, lockdown uses `locking` → `locked` (section 2), and the quarantine row is written after the role change (above).

**A Discord outage is not a refusal.** (Ban Sync review, 11-11)
`commands::apply_dm` turned every failed Discord call into a refusal reply, a 502 too. A job that bans through it (a Ban Sync copy) then marked the row skipped and never retried, although the same request would have worked a minute later. Root cause: the error arm did not tell "Discord said no" (4xx) from "Discord did not answer" (5xx, timeout), and the doc comment ("Err only for infrastructure failures") already promised the second as `Err`.
*Avoid:* only a 4xx is a refusal (`http_err::refused`); anything else goes back as `Err`, so jobs retry and commands show "Something went wrong". Searched the other places that turn an error into reply text with `http_err::why`: `confirm`, `reports`, `appeals`, `slash` and `tickets::dm` answer a person, who can press again; `verify::deleted` sends the failure to the owner as its alert; and `joingate::punish` returns the error after its log, so the job retries.
Join and voice raid punishments ran in a loop that only logged the error, so a 5xx or timeout during a raid left the raider in (review, 11-12). They go through `joingate::punish_or_retry`, which queues a `raid_punish` job for anything that is not a refusal. A retry is only safe when the error means the action did not happen: `joingate::finish` returned its Join Gate log's error, so a ban that worked plus a failed log was punished again (review, 11-12). `finish` only warns now, Warn (its case is written first) is never retried, and the job skips whoever is immune or allowed by then; `raiders::allow` (every unban, ban appeal and Allow) drops a pending retry.

**The dashboard acts as the bot.** (Raider List review, 11-11)
An unban from the dashboard (an accepted appeal, a revert) is made with the bot's token, so the audit entry names the bot. The Raider List read a bot-made unban as one of its own lifts and did not record that staff let the user back in, so the next removal or join acted on them again. Root cause: the same as "the audit actor is the bot" above, on the unban side.
*Avoid:* when the dashboard lifts something the bot may have done, record the staff choice in the same transaction (`brim_core::raiders::allow` in `appeals::accept_appeal` and `ops::revert`), and on the bot side take a bot-made unban's moderator from the newest unban case or action-log row by someone other than the bot in the last 5 minutes (`raiders::on_unban`). Searched for the same mistake: Ban Sync's `on_unban` takes any actor on purpose (a lifted copy is lifted whoever did it); anti-nuke skips the bot's own entries (above); MCP has no unban tool.

**Raw requests need a real User-Agent.** (live tests, 11-09)
Without `User-Agent: DiscordBot (...)` Cloudflare answers 403 / error 1010. Until 11-11 none of the bot's, dashboard's or MCP's raw Discord clients set one.
*Avoid:* every reqwest client that talks to Discord sets `DiscordBot (<repo url>, <version>)`.

**Twilight drops permission and user flag bits it does not know.** (#133; live test 11-13)
Twilight 0.18 deserialized `Permissions` with `from_bits_truncate`, so every role and overwrite the bot read (HTTP models, gateway events, audit log changes) lost bits it does not know, and every read-modify-write sent them back without them. Live, one role lock and unlock took 1<<47 and 1<<48 (Set Voice Channel Status) off @everyone, Member, Admins and Moderators (2248473465835073 -> 1826261000769089), with nothing logged. Root cause: the snapshot was already truncated at the read, so `from_bits_retain` at the write alone would not have helped. The same loss hit panic's own lock, channel locks, backups restore, anti-nuke `Inverse::RolePerms` and the bot-role record, the permission undo, `general` role tweaks and panic's category exempt.
*Avoid:* the workspace patches twilight-model (`vendor/twilight-model`, `[patch.crates-io]` in `Cargo.toml`) so `Permissions` deserializes with `from_bits_retain`; re-apply that one line on every twilight upgrade. `exclude = ["vendor"]` keeps the vendored crate's own tests out of the workspace run, and a `[patch]` left at an old version is ignored with only a warning, so the guard is `lockdown::tests::twilight_keeps_unknown_permission_bits` (no DB needed): it fails when the bits are dropped again. Turn a stored u64 back into `Permissions` with `from_bits_retain` before writing it to Discord; `from_bits_truncate` is only for reading names (`render::perm_names`) or checking known bits (`setup`, `events::public`) and for constants. Exact shape checks on u64 (`verification_shape`) now see unknown bits too, which is right: an unknown allow rides along. The dashboard reads and writes raw strings and was not affected. Test: `a_lock_and_unlock_keep_unknown_permission_bits`.
User flags too: `UserFlags` is read with `from_bits_truncate`, so `public_flags` never holds Discord's undocumented spammer bit (`1 << 20`).
*Avoid:* read such a bit from the raw gateway JSON before twilight parses it, like `spam_flag` in `crates/bot/src/main.rs` (the loop reads raw messages instead of `next_event`).

**A download without a size cap could fill the bot's memory.** (review, 11-12)
Ticket and modmail relays copied every attachment with `.bytes` and then `to_vec`, so a few large files held twice their size in memory, and an out-of-memory restart wipes the in-memory protection state. `Sites::fetch` read the whole body when Content-Length was missing, and the phishing/NSFW list refresh read any size and took an empty or garbled 200 as the new list, which switched those filters off for 12 hours. Root cause: three hand-written readers, only the IP list one capped while reading and refused a list that shrank.
*Avoid:* read a body from outside through `sites::read_capped` (Content-Length and the running total), with a cap above the real size of the source (the NSFW list is about 18 MB, so domain lists get 64 MB, IP lists 20 MB); a first load that fails is logged as an error, since the filter is off. Refuse a refreshed list that lost over half its entries unless the next refresh brings a non-empty count within 10% of it (`sites::ShrinkGuard`, shared with the IP lists), so a source that really shrank is not refused forever. A list that is on but still empty (a failed first load) is retried after 1, 5 and 15 minutes, not after the 12-hour refresh (`sites::next_refresh`). Ticket files are copied up to Discord's 25 MB per message in total; the rest is linked, and the staff message holding it is kept. Searched: the icon revert already capped while reading; the NSFW scan fetch goes through `Sites::fetch`.

**A REST call on the join path had no time limit.** (11-12) `invites::used_invite` runs before raid detection on every join and waited out Discord's rate limit, so during a raid each joiner's detection queued behind the invites bucket. *Avoid:* a lookup on a protection path gets `tokio::time::timeout` (1.5 s here) and a timeout just means "unknown". Since 11-13 raid detection runs before every REST call of the join (invite lookup, bot adder, verification quarantine); the invite is added to the raid's record and `raid_log` afterwards (`note_invite`). Since Invite Tracking (11-15) the join path makes no invite call at all: the per-guild tracker (`invites.rs`) fetches in its own task and Join Gate only reads its answer in a spawned task. The answer can come after the raid triggered (and got a new code), so `note_invite` always notes the invite in the detector and updates rows under the join-time and the current code (review, 11-15). A raider the raid removed gets no verification quarantine and no second Join Gate punishment.

**A Raider List hit was invisible to Join Raid.** (11-13) `joingate::on_join` returned right after the list acted, so listed raiders never counted and a raid made only partly of listed accounts did not trigger. *Avoid:* an early return on a protection path must not skip the counters of the next check. The hit now counts (`RaidDetector::note(.., listed)`) and the raid skips whoever the list already punished.

**A command that can end a quarantine skipped every target check.** (11-13)
`t!verify` ends a quarantine, but `ModCmd::target` did not list it, so the release guard (staff saved in the quarantine go free only through an owner or TA), the hierarchy check and the self check never ran: the verify permit freed any quarantine, a nuking admin's too. The guard also looked only at permit roles, so a saved role with Administrator but no permit counted as a plain member. Root cause: the per-command checks hang off `target`, and a new command that acts on a member must be added there by hand.
*Avoid:* every command that acts on a member returns it from `target`. `verify` ends only verification's join quarantine (`on_join`); any other needs the `quarantine` permit, and saved roles holding `DANGEROUS_PERMS` count as staff. Searched: the dashboard's revert skips Unquarantine, the Raider List releases only its own quarantines, verification's buttons lift only `on_join`.

**Another bot's role skipped verification.** (11-13)
An autorole or reaction-role bot handing out the verification role verified members without Brim. The quarantine hold took such a role back only for a join quarantine and only with anti-nuke on (#170), so verification without "quarantine on join" had no check at all. Root cause: the rule lived in the anti-nuke hold, not in verification.
*Avoid:* a rule about who may hand out a role sits with the feature that owns the role. `verify::given` takes back a verification role a bot (not Brim) gives a member who held none, logs it in the verification log and punishes nobody; staff giving it by hand is fine, and turning verification off lets another bot verify. A held member is left to the hold.

**A nuke case counted failed undo steps as undone.** (Fase G gap list, 11-15)
`revert_actor` returned every planned step as `done`, also the ones Discord refused, and `report` stored `done` in `nuke_incidents.data.reverted`. The MCP `nuke_incidents` view and the case so showed more undone than really was. Root cause: the plan list was reused as the result list. *Avoid:* a result list holds only steps that succeeded; `revert_actor` drops the failed ones from `done` (test: `a_failed_revert_step_stays_for_the_next_run`). Searched: the dashboard revert counts `done` only after a step succeeds.

**A re-invite list had no length cap.** (Fase G, 11-15)
The anti-nuke breach log and the `/revert` reply listed every kicked member, so a large nuke could pass Discord's embed or message limit and the log or reply was refused. *Avoid:* lists of users in a log or reply go through `antinuke::capped` (10, then "and N more"). Searched: the nuke report and its owner DM already use it.
**The slash form of the general commands skipped two gates.** (Invite Tracking review, owner decision 11-15)
`general::slash` checked only the permit, so `/about`, `/info`, `/invites` and the rest answered with "Use slash commands" off and had no cooldown for members without a permit, while the prefix form and the moderation slash commands had both. Root cause: each entry point repeated its own gates. *Avoid:* when a command has a prefix and a slash entry, give both the same gates (use_slash with the Owner/Extra Owner exception, `App::cooled_down` for no permit). Searched: the moderation slash path already checks `use_slash`; tickets have their own staff gate.

**Discord answers 200 and keeps the old image.** (bot profile live check, 11-15)
`PATCH /guilds/{g}/members/@me` with avatar data Discord cannot use (`data:image/png;base64,AAAA`) answers 200 with the old avatar, so a status check alone would record a change that never happened. *Avoid:* after setting an image, check that the answer carries an image hash; no hash is "rejected" (`profile::apply`). Discord's member object has no `bio` either (only the PATCH answer has it), so the dashboard shows the bio from its own last accepted change, unless the bot rejoined since or the last change got no answer.

**Any moderator could purge a log channel.** (owner decision 11-15)
`purge` needs only a timeout, kick or ban permit and ran in any channel, so a staff member could erase the logs of what they did. *Avoid:* every purge entry (prefix, slash, context menu, report button) goes through the `ModCmd::Purge` arm, which refuses a channel in `GuildCfg::log_channels` (logging routes, appeals channel, ticket log) for everyone, Owner included. A deleted log channel is made again by `protected::log_channel_deleted` and the deleter logged; `forget_log_channel` runs only when no new one was made. Messages deleted by hand in a log channel are reported (`protected::on_audit`); Discord cannot stop the server owner or an Administrator, and folds later deletes by one person into the first audit entry. Log channels are locked (`security::LOG_LOCK`, `lock_overwrites`): @everyone is denied Manage Messages and Manage Channels there (that takes a role's grant away in the channel) and only Brim's member overwrite allows them. The bot puts the lock back on every ChannelUpdate and at GuildCreate (`protected::lock`, `lock_all`), Overview flags an unlocked one with a Lock fix, Auto Setup makes log categories and #ticket-logs locked, and Channel Setup pushes a changed log channel locked. Review round 1: the copy took the deleted channel's overwrites (a nuker's grant came back, a deleted role made Discord refuse it), so it takes the newest backup's overwrites, drops gone roles and members, and is locked; the anti-nuke revert made a second channel when the category was also deleted, so `backup::recreate` skips a channel with a copy (`protected::copy_of`) and moves the copy back into the made-again category; a copy whose config swap changed nothing (a restore came first) is deleted; `/appeals/channel` was missing from `KEEP_FIELDS`, so a restore deleted the appeals channel. Security round: the deleter lookup found the bot's own relock first (it skips the bot's entries now); a restore or revert made a log channel from an unlocked image with no event to lock it (`protected::lock_guild` runs after both); the lock put the @everyone deny before the bot's allow and never repaired a missing bot allow, so the bot could lock itself out (the bot's allow goes first and counts on its own); each relock PUT fired a ChannelUpdate that relocked from a stale payload (one lock at a time, read from the live channel) and every flip sent an owner DM (one per channel per 10 minutes); the report Delete button deleted a reported log message (it refuses now); the revert planned while a copy was still being made (a pending marker it waits on). Known limit: a holder of Manage Roles (only Owner/EO/TA) can remove the @everyone deny and act in the second before the bot relocks; denying Manage Roles too was left out because the bot would need that allow itself, which Discord may refuse.

**An edit in place keeps the text it does not name.** (Message Builder phase 1, review)
The post sent no `content` when the text was empty, so an edit of a message posted with text left the old text on Discord while the dashboard said "edited". *Avoid:* a PATCH of a message names every part the saved version can clear (`content: ""`); a new post may leave empty parts out. Searched: the appeal log edit keeps the text on purpose (it only adds the decision and removes the buttons); the other dashboard PATCH calls edit channels, roles and rules, not messages.

**A saved link could run script in the preview.** (Message Builder phase 2a, review)
Drafts may hold a link still being typed, and the preview opens a link after "Open this link?". A button or title link `javascript:...` saved through the API or MCP would have run with the session of whoever opened the preview (an Editor's draft acting as the Owner). *Avoid:* the save refuses any link scheme other than http(s), even in a draft (`embeds::link_ok`), and the preview opens only http(s) links (`open_link`).
