Searched: preview markdown links were already http(s) only (`md::inline`); image slots show only https images.

**A new key in a saved row broke the previous image.** (Message Builder phase 2a, review)
Saving `"components": []` on every message made the previous image (after a rollback) refuse to save or duplicate any of them, since its check allowed only `content` and `embeds`. *Avoid:* a new key is written only when it holds something; messages that use a new feature (buttons, "when sent" footer time) are the only ones a rollback cannot handle, and the PR says so.

**Hiding channels from @everyone can open read-only channels and shut out bots.** (Auto Setup channels, 11-17, found in review)
The first standard set for open channels (@everyone denied View, the main role allowed View, Send, History) looked only at the main role's own bits. A role allow beats an @everyone deny, so a channel made read-only with "@everyone: no Send" became writable for every member. Denying @everyone View also hid every open channel from other bots (and from Brim itself without Administrator), and a member count that skips bots said nobody loses access. A lock's unlock deletes the main-role overwrite it made, so a change during a lock is half undone.
*Avoid:* when a role is let in where @everyone was, add only the bits @everyone does not deny (View apart). Give managed (bot) roles the same bits as the main roles. Run it only when verification gives a main role, nobody without one is left out, and no lockdown is active, and check that on the server side of a Fix too (`channel_access::blocked`). Searched: Auto Setup's Information category and catch channel use the same check before hiding anything from members without a main role.

**A role id saved in the config can point at a role deleted in Discord.** (Auto Setup main roles, 11-17, the test server)
Auto Setup and Create Member role made a Member role only when the main-role list was empty. A server whose saved main role had been deleted in Discord kept the dead id, so Auto Setup made no Member role, the Overview "Main roles" check passed, and "Move to main roles" stopped with "none of the main roles exist any more". The same dead id was the verification role, so a member who passed the captcha got no role.
*Avoid:* a check or a "make it if missing" step tests that the saved role still exists in Discord, not only that the list is not empty. `checks` counts only live main and verification roles. `member_role_named` drops dead main and verification role ids before it decides; verification whose roles were all deleted gets the main role, because verification with no role does nothing. Searched: the quarantine role and staff roles already check this (`quarantine_missing`, `staff_roles` `alive`); EVERYONE_TO_MAIN skips dead main roles; the channel gates (`channel_access::blocked`) count members without a live main role, so a dead id blocks them.

**A new Information category hid itself from members who could not verify yet.** (Auto Setup layout, 11-17)
Information was always made with @everyone denied View and the main roles let in, even when verification was off or gave no main role, so members without one lost #announcements and #rules. The access gate (`config_gate`) reads the saved main-role ids, so a main role deleted in Discord passes it. And every hiding plan wrote @everyone's deny first, so a refused role write after it (Brim without Administrator) left the channel to staff only.
*Avoid:* a step that hides a channel from @everyone checks [`blocked`] first and, when blocked, leaves it readable (Information then denies only Send) until the standard set may run; the Overview row and its Fix then make it verified-only. The Overview row is hidden, and its Fix refused, while a saved main role no longer exists. Every plan writes the main and bot roles first and @everyone's deny last (`standard_overwrites`, `info_overwrites`), and the bot roles always keep their access. Searched: the catch channel follows the same rule (`move_catch` moves it with its own overwrites when blocked); new "Text Channels"/"Voice Channels" categories get no overwrites and wait for the standard set.

**A one-time pass at bot start must not repeat, and must not stop for good on a failed read.** (staff role hoist, 11-17)
The pass that shows staff roles separately runs at GuildCreate. If it ran on every start, it would undo an owner who turned the grouping off later; if it marked itself done before its reads, one failed read would skip the server forever.
*Avoid:* read what the pass needs first, then claim the server's row (`INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING RETURNING`), then edit. A server with no staff roles yet is not claimed, and the row is released when every edit was refused, so the next start tries again. Searched: no other start-up pass changes Discord objects once per server.

**A bot cannot change its own role, and a role between the bot and quarantine trips a check.** (Brim shown separately, 11-17)
Discord refuses any edit of the bot's highest role ("Missing Permissions", even with Administrator), so hoist and colour cannot go on it. A second role right under the bot would also sit between the bot and the quarantine role, and Overview would then warn "Quarantine role not right below the bot".
*Avoid:* the bot gets its own display role with no permissions (`sections::show_bot`), placed right under the quarantine role, not right under the bot. Auto Setup then sets the whole order below the bot in one move (`order_roles`): Quarantine, the display role, staff, other roles, Member; before, it left a new Quarantine role at the bottom. A display role a member also holds is never reused: it sits above staff, who could then not moderate that member. Only a role the bot holds, with its name and no permissions, is reused; a made role the bot could not be given is deleted again, so a retry makes no second one. Searched: `channel_access` bot roles are the managed roles only, so the display role gets no channel access.

**A channel set up for an old main role kept current members out, and nothing saw it.** (Information and verification channel, 11-17)
Information was made at 11:14 on the test server, while the saved main role was one deleted in Discord; the Member role came at 13:06. Information denied @everyone View and let in no current role, and `info_overwrites` returned nothing once @everyone was denied, so the Overview row, its Fix and Auto Setup all left it hidden from members. The verification channel had the same gap the other way: it was set up before the Member role existed, so verified members kept seeing it, and the wizard's "Setup channel" reused it without its overwrites.
*Avoid:* a check of a channel Brim owns tests every role it must hold, not only whether @everyone is kept out; a reused channel gets the overwrites a new one would. Fixed in `info_overwrites` (main roles get View and History, and lose Send and threads unless allowed) and `verify_overwrites` (each role verification gives is denied View), used by Auto Setup, the wizard, the Overview row and its Fix. Searched: the catch channel already merges the current main roles (`catch_overwrites`); open channels stay skipped when @everyone is denied and no main role is let in, since that is how a private channel looks. The ticket log channel now moves into Logs when it sits elsewhere (`ticket_log_to_logs`, Overview row "Ticket log not with the other logs").

**Auto Setup took two minutes, because each channel move was its own call.** (Auto Setup, 11-17)
Information, the loose channels, the catch channel and the ticket log each sent their own `PATCH /guilds/{g}/channels`. That route has a strict rate limit, the client waits at most 10 s for a 429 and tries 3 times, so a run with about 10 channels took two minutes and the catch move failed with 429 on the test server.
*Avoid:* collect every move of one run and send it once (`channel_access::send_moves`). A step only pushes its entries (`information`, `sort_loose`, `move_catch`, `ticket_log_to_logs`, `verification_first`). Discord refuses the whole list for one bad entry (a category holds at most 50 channels), so after a refusal each move is tried alone. A refused move is named in the result, and the channel is never listed twice in one call.

**Channel access waited for every member to have the Member role.** (Verification, 11-17)
Auto Setup and the Overview Fix skipped locking normal channels while any member had no Member role, so on a server with old members the channels stayed open to everyone and the Overview row came back after each Fix. Owner decision: a member who has not verified must not have access.
*Avoid:* the guard is gone. The first time verification is turned on (`verify_started`, one row per server, migration 4011), the bot gives the roles verification gives to every human member who is not quarantined (`verify::on_saved`); later joiners verify as usual. A server where verification was already on is in `verify_started` from the migration, so its members without the role lose the channels: the lock queues `verify_sweep`, which gives each of them a DM and the normal time limit, then the kick with a DM and a rejoin link (`verify::sweep`, `verify::deadline`). The sweep runs only once the first-time grant finished for every member (`verify_started.granted`; a failed or running grant never counts, so a failed step can never remove members who should have had the role), and the roles given pass the same filter as a normal verify (`verify::safe_roles`: no permit roles, no dangerous permissions). Left out: bots, quarantined members, anyone with a permit or Owner/Extra Owner/Trusted Admin (also at the deadline), members with a running time limit, and servers where only suspicious joiners must verify. The sweep is queued only after the channels really became Member-only.

**A stored staff role deleted in Discord broke the channel fixes, and Fix All called it fixed.** (Overview, 11-18, the test server)
The Overview row "Staff cannot use their channels" only looked at staff roles Discord still has, but its Fix and the Information part of "Channels without the standard permissions" wrote overwrites for every stored staff role (permit and level roles). One of them was deleted in Discord, so Discord answered 404, the toast said "internal error", and `info_access` stopped each Information channel at that write (staff go first), so members never got Information. That Fix answered ok with a `failed` count the page ignored, so Fix All counted it as fixed and the row stayed.
*Avoid:* every write of a stored role id checks it against Discord's role list first (`channel_access::alive`, `live_staff`, shared by the row and the fixes); a refused write says which channel and role and why (`channel_access::put`), and an unexpected error names the issue instead of "internal error" (`internal_text`, never the URL). A refused staff write in Information no longer stops the member writes there (only a member or bot write does, as @everyone's deny comes last). A fix that answers ok with `failed` or `catch_stays` is shown as partly fixed (`refused` in the Overview page). The quarantine channel fix refuses with a plain reason when the stored quarantine role was deleted. Searched: `staff_access`, `information`, `info_access`, `verify_access` now filter; the standard set and catch channel already stop when a main role is gone; Auto Setup's own staff roles are made in the same run.

**A captcha decoy could be drawn cut off at the image edge.** (captcha, 11-17)
Spread decoys were centred anywhere in the image, so some were cut at an edge, and a test that asserted a clear corner failed at random (left 150, right 0) on other threads' PRs.
*Avoid:* keep every drawn shape whole inside the image (`captcha::glyph` clamps the turned box 1 px inside). A test of random output asserts only what holds for every draw, and checks it over many draws.

**A captcha answer could be guessed without limit.** (Captcha rebuild, 2026-11-17)
A wrong answer only said "Wrong answer" and kept the code alive until it expired, so a script could try many readings of one image. The new image also uses look-alike-free characters: a real font draws `l` as a bar like `I`, and the answer ignores case, so `i` and `l` left the answer set with `I`, `L`, `O`, `o` and `0`.
*Avoid:* every answer check counts wrong answers per code and drops the code after `verify::MAX_TRIES` (3); the member presses Verify for a new image. When a font changes, check which characters look alike in it before they can be an answer. Searched: the image captcha and the simple sum share one answer path (`app.captchas`, the code modal), so both get the limit; web verification uses Turnstile.

**A Discord file link was stored, and it stopped working after about a day.** (2026-11-18)
Slash punishments saved the `proof:` attachment's CDN URL in `cases.proof`. Discord signs file links and they expire after about 24 hours, so proof on any case older than a day no longer opened (dashboard Records, mod log). The ticket relay's "Attachment N" fields had the same kind of text link. Root cause: a file link looks like a lasting address, but it is not one.
*Avoid:* never store or post a Discord file URL as the lasting place of a file. Put the file on a message Brim owns and keep that message's jump link (`commands::send_case_log`: the proof goes onto the case's mod-log message). A file that is already on the same message needs no link. When a file cannot be kept (no Mod Logs channel, too large, the download failed), the moderator is told that the link expires; it is never dropped silently.

**A button on a saved message opens what a panel opens, so it needs the panel's proof.** (review, 11-19, found while building)
The ticket button's check accepts only messages recorded in `ticket_panel_posts`, and the panel sync edits or deletes every message in that table, so a saved message listed there would have been rewritten into a panel. An "I accept the rules" button that gave the verification roles itself would have been a way past the captcha, a raid's raised mode and the suspicious-account check.
*Avoid:* a ready-made button is stored as an `action` (`rules`, `ticket:<type>`), never as a `custom_id`; the dashboard makes the id when it sends and drops such buttons from a DM copy. `tickets::panel::posted` accepts a saved message's copy only by its Sent log row in the same server whose button names that ticket type. The rules button runs the Verify button's own decision (`verify::decide`) and gives roles only where that is a plain grant (`accept_step`); otherwise it sends the member to Verify. Posting a ticket panel takes a Trusted Admin, so saving, sending or editing a copy of a message with a ticket button does too (`embeds::opens_tickets`, found in review). An undo button on a mod log builds the typed command from the stored case (`undo::command`) and runs `modcmds::run` as the presser, so it has no checks of its own to forget; a presser with no permit is refused before any lookup or claim (security pass).

**A free permission grid could undo the shapes Brim keeps.** (Channel Setup, Layout Guard L3, 11-19, found in review before merge)
The first Advanced grid took any role of the server and ran after the quarantine and verification steps, so it could let the quarantine role or @everyone (past verification) into a channel, edit the verification channel, or hide a channel from a staff role without the owner check. "Read-only" and Public also read and changed Send denies during a lock, so Public could unlock a locked channel.
*Avoid:* a free editor of overwrites is applied before every shape Brim owns (quarantine, verification), which always win; it takes only roles the server may set (`Ctx::grid_role`: no quarantine, bot or managed roles), refuses @everyone View while main roles exist and the verification channel, and a staff role's View deny counts in `unstaffed` like hiding the channel from staff. Admins only and Owner only count there too. Nothing in Channel Setup reads or changes Send while `recovery::no_backup_now` says a lock is on (`Ctx.locked`).

- **A proof read after the ban found nothing.** (Case proofs review, 2026-11-19) A punishment typed as a reply stored the replied message as verified proof, but read it after the ban, and the ban deletes the member's last messages (7 days by default). *Avoid:* read what a punishment is about before the punishment runs (`proof::replied` in `confirm::Pending::run`), store it after the case exists.

**Layout Guard took open ticket channels as part of the server layout.** (Layout Guard, 11-23)
Each open ticket showed as "Made in Discord, not in the layout", and "Save again" wrote it into the layout, so the guard reported it as deleted once the ticket closed. Root cause: a layout image was the whole live channel list, and temporary channels Brim makes for a ticket were not told apart. *Avoid:* `layout::temp_channels` reads Brim's own ticket records and the held lock's `locked_channel` (the lockdown's `#server-locked`, same problem) by channel id (one query, never the name or the category, so nobody can hide a channel from the guard with a ticket-like name or by moving it into the ticket category). The open ones are left out of the live side of the compare, of every save, push and roll back; the saved side drops any channel a ticket record names, so an old layout that holds a ticket channel shows nothing when it closes. A closed ticket whose channel still exists counts like any other channel. Channel Setup marks the row "Ticket" or "Lockdown": it has no drag, no rename, delete or move, and `hold_fixed` drops those draft fields on the server. A new feature that makes temporary channels joins this list.

**A cleanup of Brim's webhooks knew only one of their users.** (feeds F1, 11-20, found while building)
`message_webhooks` held only Message Builder webhooks, so the 30-day sweep (`msghooks::sweep`) and the dashboard's `forget_unused` deleted a webhook no saved message used, which would have taken a feed's webhook with it. *Avoid:* every query that deletes rows of `message_webhooks` (or the webhooks) also keeps a channel that a feed posts in (`not exists (select 1 from feeds ...)`). A new user of that table adds itself to both queries.

## 2. Concurrency and restarts

- **A push's server layout version was built on a version read after the push.** (Layout Guard L2 review, 11-19) `after_push` read the newest layout version once Discord was done, so an Owner's "Save again" during the push became the base: the push's new channel was added a second time, and the "layout changed during the push" guard could never fire. *Avoid:* read the version a change builds on at the start, inside the lock, and write with `HAVING max(version) = <that version>` (`layout::record`); a mismatch skips the write and says so. A push also saves no version that brings a serious problem the saved layout did not have (`blocking` on both, compared by check id).
- **One server's big job delayed another server's panic start.** (security review, 11-18) `panic_start` and `nuke_revert` shared the heavy worker with `backup_load` and `raider_scan`, which runs one job at a time, so a member scan of a 30,000-member server held another server's panic start for 3.25 s (minutes for bigger servers or a backup load). *Avoid:* protection kinds that must start at once are in `jobs::URGENT`, with their own worker (`scheduler::run_urgent`) that runs each job in its own task; any other kind that can run for minutes goes in `HEAVY` (test: `a_big_job_in_one_server_does_not_delay_another_servers_panic_start`). Running beside the heavy worker let a panic start in the middle of a backup load, whose later steps wrote the locked permissions back: a full load now checks for a lock, panic or running nuke revert before every step (a revert and a load would both recreate the same channels), and a stopped load writes no server settings or role order (`backup::halt`, test: `a_full_load_stops_when_panic_or_a_nuke_revert_starts`). Heat panic could do the same before.
- **Two dashboard-queued job kinds were left out of the run-time re-check.** (security review, 11-18) #312 re-checked the asker of lock, unlock, lock update, panic start and backup jobs, but not panic mode's early end (`panic_end` moved to now with `ended_by`) or the Raider List decisions (`raider_check`, `raider_lift` at server 0), which named no asker at all. Root cause: the list was made from the jobs that carried `by`, not from every place the dashboard queues or moves a job. *Avoid:* when a check covers "jobs a person queues", grep the dashboard (and MCP) for every `jobs::enqueue`, `insert into scheduled_jobs` and `update scheduled_jobs`, and decide for each kind. An early `panic_end` is re-checked as panic start (Owner, Extra Owner, Trusted Admin); a refused one must not delete panic mode's only end and the lock state it holds, so `panic::keep_timed_end` puts the job back at `ends_at` (the time the dashboard moved it from) without the asker. `panic::end_payload` keeps `ends_at` with `ended_by`, and the put-back adds no second end on a retry. A Raider List decision carries `by` and fans out only while that person is the bot owner; a dropped one DMs the bot owner (there is no server log for a list-wide job). Checked and left: `appeal_accept` (the bot's `decide` already checks the asker's permits at run time and logs "Appeal Not Accepted"), the timed `unban`/`timeout_renew` of a mod action (the action is done; its end is the bot's own timer), the member's own `verify_*` jobs, `permit_sync`, `verify_sweep`, `note_log` (housekeeping), and `raider_notify` DMs (a security alert must never be dropped).

**Every event posted its logs before raid detection, anti-nuke and automod ran.** (audit, 11-17)
`events::run` awaited each log post first, and a post waits on the log channel's rate limit (about 5 per 5 s), so in a raid the 300th joiner's detection ran minutes late; `Logger::handle` also returns the due held logs of every server, so one server's delete flood slowed protection in all others. A failed config read there (`?`) ended the whole event. *Avoid:* the event path never awaits a log post: `events::run` sends them in a spawned task with the event's span and an `InFlight` drop guard (a panic must not keep shutdown waiting), and a failed config read skips only the logs. One task per event, not one sender for all servers (review, 11-17): a single sender would make one raid's log channel delay the logs of every server; events had no order between them before either.

**A failed command reply or lookup skipped automod.** (audit, 11-17)
The ticket relay and command steps before `automod::on_message` used `?`, so a 403 on the reply (no Send Messages) or a DB error in `commands::ignored` left `t!ban x <scam link>` unchecked. *Avoid:* the command block runs in its own `async` block whose error is only logged; automod always runs after it.

**A failed join lock read skipped the whole join.** (audit, 11-17)
`lockdown::join_lock(..).await?` returned from `on_join` before raid counting, verification and the Join Gate, so a pool timeout in a raid let every joiner in unchecked. *Avoid:* an unread lock is logged and skips only the lock's kick or ban; the rest of the join runs. Not queued as a retry: `raid_punish` does not read the lock again, so it would kick joiners when no lock is on. The first fix still returned after a lock kick that did not remove the joiner (a refusal, or a retry due in 30 s), so they were not counted, held or gated (review, 11-17): `on_join` returns only when `punish_or_retry` removed them.

**A raid's log, owner DM and lockdown came before its punishments, and the punishments ran one at a time.** (audit, 11-17)
`join_raid` awaited the trigger log, `alert_owner` (up to 3 s) and `lockdown::lock` (one Discord call per role or channel) before punishing the first wave, and each punishment awaited its DM first. The mention spam lock did the same in automod. *Avoid:* punish first: the raid spawns the log, DM and lock task (not awaited) and punishes the flagged list at once (`join_all`); the lock must not wait for the whole first wave either (review, 11-17). The mention spam lock runs in `automod::check` after the punishment, as auto slowmode does. An earlier flagged joiner's level is read from the roles of their join (`RaidDetector::set_roles`, a DB read): a member fetch for each of hundreds of flagged joiners queued before the bans. A member update forgets those roles, so that member is fetched again; a TA or EO added by id is still seen, because the level is resolved again.

**A Join Gate, bot adder or Raider List ban refused by an outage was never retried.** (audit, 11-17)
These paths called plain `punish`, so a 503 left the account in the server with nothing queued, and the gate counted it as removed. Same mistake as "A join lock's failed kick let the joiner stay". *Avoid:* the Join Gate, the bot adder and the Raider List use `punish_or_retry` (it takes the gate's `Detail`), and "removed" comes from its result. Each of the join lock, the Raider List, the raid and the gate could then queue its own retry for one joiner, and each run punishes (review, 11-17): `queue_retry` keeps one pending `raid_punish` per (guild, user) with the harshest action, in its enqueue SQL. A Raider List outage counts as "the list acted" (the raid and the gate skip the joiner), and a refusal does not (the join goes on, verification holds them). The retry of a list kick or ban writes its case, `raider_actions.case_no` and mod log (`raiders::join::retried`), so staff can undo it.

**Verification fetched the joiner's level on every join.** (audit, 11-17)
`verify::on_join` called `is_immune` (a member GET) though `on_join` had already read the level from the join's roles, so a raid with verification on made one extra GET per joiner in the bucket the bans use. *Avoid:* `verify::on_join` takes the `immune` that `joiner_immune` read; unread (None) holds the member.

**A guild_info refill put back what an invalidation removed.** (audit, 11-17)
A cache miss made four REST reads and then inserted with no check, so an owner transfer or a role edit that invalidated during the reads was lost for 5 minutes (old owner treated as Owner, a new dangerous permission seen as harmless). *Avoid:* the same generation rule as `lockdown::LOCKS`, but per guild: invalidations bump that guild's entry in `hierarchy::GENERATION` under the `guilds` lock, and a fill is kept only when it did not change. A global counter made every event in one server (a nuke, a lockdown) throw away the fills of all other servers (review, 11-17).

**Replayed voice audit entries counted at "now".** (audit, 11-17)
`voice_nuke::note` counted through `antinuke::count`, so moves made during a gateway gap landed in the live window. *Avoid:* `note` takes `replayed` and counts through `count_at` with `replay_age`, as the other cards do.

**One failed step stopped the rest of ChannelDelete.** (audit, 11-17)
`tickets::on_channel_delete(..).await?` returned before the log channel and verification channel remakes, so a slow pool in a nuke left a deleted log channel gone. *Avoid:* in a handler with several independent steps, each step logs its error and the next one runs.

**Background sweeps stuck and kept state for good.** (audit, 11-17)
The message webhook sweep took 50 rows with no order, kept every row Discord refused (so the same 50 came back each hour), and ran inside the scheduler tick. `MENTION_HITS` kept one entry per member who ever hit a scoped rule. *Avoid:* a sweep orders by age, moves a refused row forward (4xx; an outage is retried), and runs in its own task; a map keyed by member drops entries past the longest window (an hour for scopes) once it grows.
**The join lock, the join gate filters and the raid kicked or banned an immune joiner, and a bot's adder was punished on an unread level.** (audit, 11-17)
None of these join steps asked the joiner's level, so an Extra Owner or Trusted Admin who rejoined during a join lock was removed; a failed adder lookup counted as "not immune" for the adder's punishment too. *Avoid:* `on_join` reads the joiner's level once from the join's own roles (`joiner_immune`, no member fetch per join in a raid). Immune: every punishing join step is skipped. Unread: nothing punishes now, `hold_back` queues the `raid_punish` retry for a kick, ban or timeout, which reads the level again, and the rest of the join still runs (verification holds them; never return early on an unread level). A bot is never immune, so it is not read (review). The bot adder uses the same rule. Searched: the Raider List (`exempt` with `?`) and the post-join username filter (`is_immune` with `?`) already refuse on an unread level.

**A join lock's failed kick let the joiner stay, and skipped the re-quarantine.** (audit, 11-17)
The join lock ran before `requarantine_rejoin` with plain `punish` and returned its error, so a 5xx left a quarantined nuker in the server with no role and no retry. *Avoid:* `on_join` re-quarantines first, then the join lock uses `punish_or_retry`. A security step that comes first in a handler must not return early before a step that contains.

**A Join Gate retry sent the punishment DM again.** (audit, 11-17)
`punish` sends the DM before the action, and each `raid_punish` retry called `punish`, so a member could get six DMs. *Avoid:* `punish` is `tell` then `act`; `punish_job` calls `act` only. Not changed: the first try still DMs before a kick or ban that Discord may refuse.

**A DB error at GuildCreate skipped the missed-rejoin quarantine.** (audit, 11-17)
The `guild_left` delete and `owner_seen` used `?` before `requarantine_missed`, which queues its retry only once it starts. *Avoid:* in `on_guild_create` each step logs its error and the next one runs.
**The message cache logged content older than 24 hours.** (audit, 11-17)
`MessageCache::remove` ignored `TTL` and `prune` ran only on an insert into the same guild, so a quiet guild (or one the bot left) kept message text past the 24 hours PRIVACY.md promises, and a late delete logged it. *Avoid:* `remove` answers like `get` (expired is missing), and `flush` (every event and the timer) runs `prune_all`, which also drops guilds with nothing left.

**A DB error before or in a nuke run skipped containment, the report or the retry.** (audit, 11-17)
`quarantine_and_revert` queued its guard job and read `still_contained` with `?`, so a DB error left the nuker free. A failed `revert_actor` returned with `?`, skipping the report and panic, and the guard job was deleted anyway. *Avoid:* a failed guard write runs without a guard; `still_contained` returns a bool and any failed read counts as not contained. An unreadable config in `revert_and_report` and `quarantine_as` runs the defaults (auto quarantine on, no Quarantine role, so the roles are cleared), and logs the config error to Anti Nuke; the run is then done (no retry for the config error alone). Without the config the Quarantine role is unknown and counts as "other", so `row_flags` keeps the row: no `released` (the saved roles stay) and the row's `on_join` (security pass). A member who already has a quarantine row keeps their roles then (owner decision, 11-17): stripping would take the Quarantine role that holds them; the revert and report still run. Such a member still loses every role with dangerous permissions (`strip_dangerous`: a row can outlive a release by hand), and counts as quarantined only while at most one non-managed role is left (security pass 2). A retry does only what is left (security pass 2): `resume_nuke` contains whenever `still_contained` is false, and reports and runs panic only when no run reported yet; `mark_reported` (in memory and as `reported` on the guard job) runs right after every report step. `revert_actor` reads the deleted-object rows before any inverse runs, so a failed read cannot leave run inverses unmarked. A failed final `UPDATE action_log` is a warning, and its rows are kept in memory (`UNMARKED`), skipped by the next revert and marked with it, so no deleted channel or role is recreated twice. Accepted (owner decision, 11-17): when the `reported` mark cannot be saved and the bot restarts, the retry reports a second time. A failed revert logs, reports with an empty summary plus a failure log, counts towards panic, and returns the error at the end; the guard job is finished only on `Ok`, so it runs the revert again.

**A failed panic end log retried the end, and start dropped a dashboard end.** (audit, 11-17)
`panic::end` returned the end log's result, so a refused log made the job retry and write the saved category overwrites again. `end_payload` replaced the whole payload (losing `ended_by`) and pushed `run_at` 60 s ahead, so a dashboard end during start was delayed. The backup step read the config with `?` after the locks were taken. *Avoid:* the end log is best effort. `end_payload` keeps `ended_by` and leaves `run_at` alone when it is set (`ends_later` handles the early end). A failed config read in the backup step reports `Backup::Failed` and goes on.
**Command rate limits were shared across servers.** (audit, 11-17)
`RateLimiter` and `command_uses` were keyed by (user, action), so a mod's uses in server A counted in server B and could quarantine them there and undo their work. *Avoid:* the key is (guild, user, action), and `command_uses` has `guild_id` (migration 4005, nullable for a rollback). Rows without a guild are skipped at the restart seed. Any per-user in-memory state must hold the guild in its key.

**A permit sync gave a role to a member quarantined during the run.** (audit, 11-17)
`permit_sync::roles` read the quarantine list once and then made one REST call per change for minutes. *Avoid:* right before each give, check the quarantine again and skip the member as held. After a give, check again and take the role back if a quarantine began during the call (its saved roles miss it). A long loop of REST calls re-reads any state that a guard depends on.

**The first permit migration ran one query per member under the config lock.** (audit, 11-17)
`migrate_members` ran a SELECT for every member inside `settings::locked`, so on a big server every config save waited. *Avoid:* read the permit roles once and query only members who hold one. Keep the work under `settings::locked` small.
**Two presses of one button both acted.** (audit, 11-17)
Two moderators pressing Ban on one report at once both banned, and a later press overwrote `handled_by`; two quick presses of a ticket DM's Send relayed the message twice. *Avoid:* claim before acting: an in-memory claim per report (`reports::Acting`, freed on drop) and `WHERE status='open'` on the close; the ticket prompt's delete is the claim (10008 means a press already took it). Build a drop guard only for a won claim (`then(|| ..)`, never `then_some(..)`): an eager guard for a lost claim drops at once, under the lock it re-takes (a deadlock), or removes the winner's entry. `t!enable`/`t!disable` also flipped the state instead of setting it, so a repeated `disable` turned tickets back on: a command named after a state sets that state.

**`closeall` said "all closed" when closes failed, and the metrics endpoint died on one error.** (audit, 11-17)
Failed closes were only logged, and `metrics::serve` returned on the first `accept` error (EMFILE). *Avoid:* a bulk action reports "closed X of Y" (`tickets::close_all`, after a Yes/No through `confirm`) and does not delete the channel that holds its own answer (the ticket the question is in stays open, review); an answer to a message that may be gone is best effort, never `?`; a long-lived listener logs an accept error and goes on, with a cap on parallel answers.

**Two clicks could decide one appeal twice.** (audit, 11-17)
`appeals::decide` read the appeal row without a lock and opened its transaction later, so two staff clicking at once both passed the "still open" check. Its timeout end also read the member with `let Ok(..)`, so a Discord error looked like "no timeout". *Avoid:* the transaction starts first and the row is read `FOR UPDATE`; the member is read with `http_err::member`.

**Two tickets at once made two ticket categories.** (audit, 11-17)
`tickets::ensure_category` looked for the category and created it when missing, with nothing between the look and the create. *Avoid:* a per-server `tokio::sync::Mutex` holds the look and the create together (one bot process per database). Per server, because it is held across Discord calls.

**A panicking event handler left the in-flight count up.** (audit, 11-17)
`events.rs` decremented `IN_FLIGHT` at the end of the handler, so a panic skipped it and shutdown waited for an event that would never finish. *Avoid:* a drop guard decrements it on every exit.

**A timed thread lock never ended.** (audit, 11-17)
`lock_marked` queued an `unlock` job for timed server locks but not for a thread lock, so `t!lock 1h` in a thread stayed locked. *Avoid:* the thread branch queues the same `unlock` job with its `lock` kind, and a thread lock or unlock first drops older timers of that thread (`forget_timers`), so an old timer cannot lift a newer lock. The dashboard's "unlock all" no longer deletes every `unlock` job: it does not lift threads, so their timers stay, and the bot drops the timer of each lock it lifts.

**The message cache counted messages, not bytes.** (audit, 11-17)
`MessageCache` kept up to 50k messages per server, so a flood of 4,000-character messages held about 200 MB per server. *Avoid:* a per-server byte budget (`MAX_BYTES`, 16 MB) also evicts the oldest; the byte count is kept on insert, edit, remove and prune, and a test checks it against the map.

**Verification jobs treated any Discord error as "member left".** (audit, 11-17)
`verify::deadline` and `verify::web_grant` began with `let Ok(member) = guild_member(..) else { return Ok() }`, so a 5xx or timeout ended the job: an unverified member was never removed, or a member who passed web verification never got the roles. *Avoid:* use `http_err::member`, which gives `None` only for a member who is gone and an error (the job retries) otherwise. Searched: `purge.rs` and `nsfw.rs` use the same form only for best-effort details, not to end a job.

**A backup restore merged two same-named roles or channels.** (audit, 11-17)
`backup::plan` matched each image object by id, else by name among live objects not yet used. With two "ticket" channels and one deleted, the deleted one name-matched the survivor before the survivor's own id match ran, so both mapped to one live channel and nothing was recreated. *Avoid:* a name fallback skips every live object that some image object matches by id (`role_hits`, `chan_hits`). Test: `a_deleted_twin_is_recreated_not_merged_into_the_survivor`.

**An early timeout lift could lose the member's main roles.** (audit, 11-17)
`timeout_roles::on_member_update` deleted the `restore_main_roles` job (DELETE ... RETURNING) and then called `restore`; a Discord error there left no job and no roles. *Avoid:* when a claim deletes the job first, a failed run queues the payload again. Searched: the scheduler path keeps the row until the run succeeds.

**A failed `action_log` insert skipped the anti-nuke count.** (audit, 11-17)
`count_at` returned on the insert error before `on_actions_at`, so under a slow database a nuker's actions went uncounted. *Avoid:* the insert error is logged and counting goes on; only that action's revert row, and its count after a restart (`seed_windows`), are lost.

**A second heat panic replaced the first wave of raiders.** (audit, 11-17)
`panic::note` inserted a fresh raider set and end time, so wave 1 was released early when wave 2 tripped it. *Avoid:* while heat panic is on, a new trip adds to the live set and keeps the later end.

**Two in-memory maps grew until restart.** (audit, 11-17)
Unanswered captchas (`app.captchas`) and emptied anti-nuke windows (`AntiNuke::windows`) were never removed, so memory grew after every raid. *Avoid:* a map keyed by member or actor drops its stale entries once it passes a size cap, as `cool`, `remember_flag`, `remember_code` and `on_actions_at` do. `App::nuke_heat` (per actor) had the same gap and now drops decayed entries. Spot-checked the other `Mutex<HashMap>` fields on `App` and the statics.

**Unlock deleted its snapshot first.** (#97, #111)
Unlock deleted the snapshot, then aborted on the first error (a deleted channel or role). The server stayed half locked with nothing to retry from. A restart in the middle had the same effect.
*Avoid:* lock state is a row with `locking` → `locked` → `unlocking`, deleted only when the permissions are back. `lockdown::resume` finishes cut-off work at startup. A failed step is counted and the rest continue.

**Queue the undo before the action.** (#111)
A restart between a temp ban and its unban job made the ban permanent. Same for removing main roles on timeout and for timed locks.
*Avoid:* queue the undo job first, then act. A failed action leaves an undo that finds nothing and is dropped. Nuke reverts get a guard job that reruns after a crash.

**Events run concurrently.** (#100)
`main.rs` spawns a task per event, so messages already in flight after a trigger each added a strike. One burst could reach the strike cap and send several DMs, cases and logs.
*Avoid:* claim the punishment (`HeatState::claim`, one per 10 s). Anything that must happen once needs an atomic claim: `DELETE … RETURNING`, `WHERE status='open'`, `SKIP LOCKED`.

**Anti-nuke tripped once per event, not once per nuke.** (race review, 11-11)
Past the limit every audit entry returned `Quarantine` again, and each one is its own task, so a 20-channel nuke started about 16 quarantine-and-revert runs at once. They read the same unreverted rows, so the backup recreated the same deleted channel several times (the copy has a new id, so the next run still saw the original as missing), and opened as many cases and owner alerts.
*Avoid:* one run per (guild, actor) (`App::nuke_runs`). A later event only logs its row and asks the running run to revert once more; the run frees its claim in the same step that finds nothing new, so no action falls between two runs. The `nuke_revert` guard job is due after 120 s, and a run under rate limits can take longer: the scheduler skips a guard whose run is still alive, and the job path takes the same claim. An actor an earlier run already quarantined only gets the revert, with no second case or alert. `panic::start` undoes its claim and its category exemptions when its `panic_end` job cannot be saved; otherwise mod commands stay locked with nothing to end them. The same applies to panic mode: `panic::start` checks and inserts `panic_runs` in one step, or a second start saves the already exempted overwrites as the old ones.

**A revert applied same-second actions in any order.** (anti-nuke triggers, 11-12)
`revert_actor` read the actor's rows without an `ORDER BY`, and `plan_revert` sorts by whole seconds. Two server renames in one second could be put back in the wrong order, leaving the attacker's first name instead of the original. Root cause: a sort key coarser than the order of events, with the tie left to whatever order Postgres returned. Earlier inverses (deletes, unbans) did not depend on order, so it only showed with name and icon reverts.
*Avoid:* read rows `ORDER BY id DESC` and keep the sort stable, so ties stay newest first. Checked: the dashboard revert already orders by `created_at desc, id desc`.

**A late rerun put back a name from a rename in between.** (anti-nuke review, 11-12)
O → X → Y → Z: the first run read two renames and restored O, then the row of Y → Z reached the rerun and set Y, the attacker's name. Compare-and-set on the value the entry set (`from`) alone was not enough: when the first run read while the last row was not logged yet (live already Z), it skipped both rows and the rerun still set Y. Root cause: a "set to value" inverse was applied per row, but each run sees a different subset of the rows, and the right target is not in any one row but in the oldest one of the incident. *Avoid:* the bot revert (`revert_actor`, also `/revert` and the guard job) replaces all name and icon ops with one op each holding the value of the actor's oldest row since `since`, reverted or not (`settle_guild`), applies it only when the live value differs, and marks the newer rows reverted. The dashboard keeps compare-and-set on `from` (`still_set`, after a GET of the guild): staff pick rows that are already logged and it reverts them newest first, and a row whose change is no longer live is listed under Skipped and left unreverted, so selecting the rest later still walks back to the original. Rows without `from` (written before) apply as before.

**A claim freed only on the normal return stayed taken after a panic.** (anti-nuke review, 11-12)
`voice_nuke` set `running = true` and cleared it only when the fetch loop returned, so a panic in a fetch left the guild claimed and its voice updates were never counted again. `nuke_runs` had the same hole: a panicking nuke run kept the actor claimed, so later events only logged rows and the guard job skipped the "running" run. Freeing it in a guard kept a second bug: `rerun` frees the claim when nothing new came in, then the run awaits `jobs::finish`, and its final remove could delete the claim a newer run took meanwhile, so a third run started next to the second. *Avoid:* free an in-memory claim in a `Drop` guard (`voice_nuke::Claim`, `antinuke::RunClaim`), taken right after the claim succeeds, and when anything else can free the claim first, let the guard remove only its own (a per-claim token, `antinuke::release`).

**Replacing a job dropped a report that was due but not yet claimed.** (voice raid review, 11-12)
A raid trigger called `jobs::replace`, which deletes every pending job of that kind in the guild. A raid starting right after the last one ended deleted the last one's report job before the scheduler took it. Join Raid's `raid_end` had the same pattern. Also an extended voice raid's end was only in memory, so `restore` after a restart ended it early. *Avoid:* `replace` only where one job per guild is the meaning (`panic_end`, which cannot start again until its end job ran). A job per event uses `enqueue`; a job that re-queues itself enqueues too (it is finished after it returns). Write a moved deadline into its job (`voice_raid::extend`), and read `jobs::pending` in `run_at` order so a restore keeps the latest per guild.

**A run marked rows it never read.** (race review, 11-11)
`revert_actor` read the actor's rows, worked for seconds, then marked every row since `since` as reverted. What the nuker did meanwhile was marked too and never undone, not even by `/revert`.
*Avoid:* mark only the ids you read (`WHERE id = ANY($1)`). Never close work with a range condition when rows can arrive while it runs.
Marking every id read was still too much (review, 11-12): a failed inverse, an op the restore filters dropped and a deleted channel the backup did not recreate were all marked reverted, so neither the next run nor `/revert` tried them again. Mark a row only when its op ran (or its target was already gone): `antinuke::undone` for logged inverses, and `backup::recreate` returns the ids that exist again. The dashboard revert already marked per success.

**Two locks at once saved a locked server as the snapshot.** (race review, 11-11)
`lock_for` checked for an active lock, then planned with several HTTP calls, then saved with `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE`. Mention-spam lock calls it for every message past the limit, so a second call could plan after the first began applying, save locked permissions and overwrite the first snapshot. Unlock then restored the locked state.
*Avoid:* lock and unlock of a guild run one at a time (`lockdown::exclusive`). An in-process lock is enough here only because the dashboard queues jobs and never writes `lock_snapshots`; a second writer process needs an advisory lock (see the Channel Setup entry below).

**An undo outlived what it undoes.** (review, 11-12)
Jobs queued to end something (a temp ban's `unban`, a long timeout's `timeout_renew`, a timed lock's `unlock`, panic's `panic_end` unlock) were never removed when it ended another way, so they hit whatever came later: a permanent ban after a temp ban was lifted by the old job, an untimeout or accepted appeal was timed out again by the renewal, a manual unlock and a new lock was lifted by the first lock's timer, and panic's end lifted a role lock it did not make (its `lock` had returned false). Root cause: "queue the undo first" (above) without the other half, "drop the undo when the thing is gone or replaced".
*Avoid:* every ban, unban, lift and new timeout drops the user's older pending job (`jobs::cancel_user`, `scheduler::forget_jobs` in the bot; the dashboard's appeal accept and revert call `cancel_user` in their own transaction), keeping only the job it just queued (`keep`). A lifted lock drops its own timer and clears panic's claim on the role lock (`lockdown::forget_timers`); `panic_end` carries `locked` (set by `panic::own_lock` only when panic's own lock call saved a snapshot, `lockdown::lock_saving`) and unlocks only then. Ban Sync no longer skips a member who only has a local temp ban that ends before the copy would. The renewal is dropped only after the new timeout's PATCH worked (inside `timeout_until`, keeping its own job), so a failed new timeout keeps the old one's chain, and a ban drops renewals too. A renewal that finds the member gone (10007) tries again hourly until the timeout's end, since Discord keeps the timeout for a rejoin. Searched: nuke reverts re-ban (`Inverse::Ban`) on purpose without dropping anything, as that restores the earlier state.

**An undo kept less than it undid.** (11-13)
An accepted appeal stored `{"op":"ban"}` and the member's visible timeout end, so a revert gave a temp ban back as permanent and a 90-day timeout as at most 28 days (and the dashboard's PATCH past 28 days was refused). Root cause: the real end lived only in the job that was about to be dropped (`unban`, `timeout_renew`). *Avoid:* read the end from that job before dropping it and store it in the inverse (`Inverse::Ban.until`, `Inverse::Timeout.until` from `until_secs`); a revert queues the matching job again (bot `antinuke::run_inverse`, dashboard `ops::run_inverse`, 28-day chunk plus renewal) and skips a punishment that would have ended by now. Round 2 (R-h3 3, 4): the bot's revert queued the `unban` and then returned on a failed ban, leaving the job to lift a later permanent ban, and `decide` dropped the jobs before it marked the appeal and wrote the action-log row, so a crash in between made the retry record a permanent ban as the undo, or answer "already handled" with no verdict DM and the log buttons left on. *Avoid:* a revert deletes the job it queued when the Discord call fails (both `run_inverse`); the appeal's status, the action-log row with the end and the job drop are one transaction, with the Raider List allow of an accepted ban appeal (`raiders::allow` on the transaction; it ran after the commit and was skipped on a resume, R-h3 round 2), and a retry by the same staff member that finds the appeal already accepted only sends the verdict DM it missed (`appeals.verdict_sent_at`, migration 3922) and closes the log.

**A second ban keeps the first ban's reason, so a reason cannot prove who owns a ban.** (live test and g4, 11-13)
Ban Sync laid a copy over a shorter local temp ban  and took the ban's reason as proof the copy was its own. Discord keeps the first reason and writes no audit entry when a banned user is banned again, so the copy still read as the temp ban: the source's lift called it "banned again by hand" and left it, so the temp ban became permanent, and a retry skipped it as "already banned". The same blindness made a staff ban over a plain copy look like the copy. Root cause: ownership read from a field Discord does not overwrite.
*Avoid:* A copy laid over a local temp ban records the reason Discord shows and the temp ban's end on its row before the ban (`over_reason`, `temp_end`, migration 3902), and `bansync::owned` counts that ban as the copy once the temp ban's unban job is gone, at `lift`, `banned_by_us` and the copy's retry. A lift before `temp_end` keeps the ban and queues its unban at the old end (then drops the copy's own unban job); after it, the copy is unbanned as usual (option B, pending the owner's answer). A staff ban over a done copy also leaves the old reason, so `owned` first asks `hand_ban_since`: a ban or tempban case by someone other than the bot newer than the row's `done_at` means banned again by hand, for plain copies too (a Discord-app ban cannot be made on a user who is already banned, and an unban first lifts the row). `temp_end` counts leased unban jobs, a copy fails and retries while the temp ban's unban is due or running (`temp_ending`), and the lift's new unban job, the drop of the copy's job and the row mark run in one transaction. Searched: Ban here goes through `banned_by_us`; nothing else reads a copy's ban reason. The copy path deletes the temp ban's unban job itself with `?` before the row becomes banned (`drop_temp_job` in `finish_ban`), since the ban's own `forget_jobs` is best effort and a job left behind would disown the copy and lift it at the old end.

**A deleted object came back only from the newest backup.** (review, 11-12)
`backup::recreate` read only `latest`, so a backup that missed the object (taken mid-nuke, or after a delete that was not logged in time) made it unrecoverable, and the auto backup could run during a nuke or panic and save the damage as the newest image.
*Avoid:* recreate each id from the newest image that has it (`backup::newest_with`), and skip the auto backup while a nuke run or panic mode is on (`backup::under_attack`). A role taken from an older image may predate staff removing a permission, so it comes back without `DANGEROUS_PERMS` and the anti-nuke report says how many.

**A gateway event and its audit entry run in their own tasks.** (race review, 11-11)
The ticket-category exemption read the category that the `ChannelCreate` task stores. When the audit task ran first, the lookup missed and a ticket bot's channels counted towards its limit.
*Avoid:* do not rely on the order of two events. `note_parent` stores every channel (also "no category"), so a miss means "not seen yet", and `parent_of` waits up to 0.5 s for it.

**An invite diff per join picked a code by luck.** (Invite Tracking, 11-15)
`used_invite` fetched invites in each join's own task, so two joins' fetches could finish in either order and the older list overwrote the newer one; it had no baseline after a restart, took the first grown code in HashMap order, guessed a vanished code as used and read a missing `uses` as 0. Root cause: a diff of shared state done in parallel tasks, with a guess where the data was unsure.
*Avoid:* events that must be diffed in order go to one actor per guild before `tokio::spawn` (`invites::observe` in `main.rs`), and an unsure match is stored as unknown (`invites::resolve`). A join that arrives within 1 s after a fetch may already be in that list, so it joins the batch and the list is read again (up to 5 rounds); dropping the batch instead made every busy server and every raid unknown and emptied `raid_log.invite_code` (review, 11-15). A 403 on the list waits 10 minutes before joins ask again, since 403s count towards Discord's invalid request limit, which blocks the whole bot; GuildCreate seeds are spread over 10 s. A code that vanished counts as its last use only when it was one use short of its limit; expired or event-deleted codes count as no use. A guild's actor checks it still owns the guild before each event, so a GuildDelete and quick GuildCreate never run two writers. Searched: no other state is diffed from REST in per-event tasks.

**Join-time steps race each other.** (sticky roles plan, 11-11)
A `MemberUpdate` (from verification, another bot or the bot's own role adds) can run before or alongside the `MemberAdd` task, and a late update can come from the membership before. Saving roles "per user id" would let such an update overwrite the saved roles before the rejoin reads them.
*Avoid:* key per-member state by membership: `member_roles` rows carry `joined_at`, an update writes only when its `joined_at` matches the row, and the rejoin claims the older row in one statement (`sticky::claim`), so a replayed `MemberAdd` gets nothing.

**Membership was checked when roles were claimed, not when they were added.** (sticky roles review, 11-11)
Sticky roles checked the member once, when the rejoin claimed the saved list, and then added roles later: from a job a minute on, after verification, or when a timeout ended. By then the member could have left and rejoined, been banned, quarantined or sent back to verification, and still got the old roles. Errors after the claim (a failed read, a refused add) dropped the claimed list, and a join where any step acted (even a Log hit) froze the row, so the next rejoin got roles from two memberships ago. Found the same gap in `timeout_roles::restore`, which gave main roles back to a quarantined member.
*Avoid:* every delayed add carries the membership it was claimed for (`joined_at`) and checks again right before the add: same `joined_at`, setting on, no quarantine row, not pending (`sticky::due`). Do fallible reads before a claim, and put whatever a claim took and could not finish into a job. A ban leaves a tombstone row instead of deleting, so a late event of the old membership cannot write it back.

**Member role adds share Discord's rate-limit bucket with punishments.** (sticky roles, 11-11)
A mass rejoin (after a raid, or a re-invite) giving every member's roles back would fill the per-guild member bucket that timeouts, kicks and bans need right then.
*Avoid:* bulk role or nickname work on members gets a per-guild cap (`sticky::take`, 20 adds a minute) and queues the rest as a job instead of dropping it.

**A switch checked in only one of two call sites.** (race review item 5, 11-12)
`on_audit` calls the quarantine hold from two places. Kicks, bans and timeouts checked `quarantine_hold`, role changes did not, so staff were still quarantined with the switch off.
*Avoid:* when a feature has a switch, grep every caller of its entry point and check the switch in each, or check it once inside the function. `hold` now checks it inside, after the join-quarantine branch: an unverified joiner's role change is always undone (that punishes nobody), and only the punishment follows the switch.

**A join quarantine counted as a nuke target.** (race review follow-up, 11-11)
Verification's "quarantine on join" writes a quarantine row with no saved roles. The quarantine hold treated it like an anti-nuke quarantine, so an autorole bot giving a joiner its role was quarantined and lost its role permissions on every join.
*Avoid:* the hold undoes the role change on a join quarantine but punishes nobody. Code that reads `quarantines` must tell the two kinds apart, by the `on_join` column (next entry).
The same happened on a rejoin into a real quarantine (11-13): `requarantine_rejoin` put the quarantine back and the autorole bot's role, a second later, made the hold quarantine the bot. A bot's role change within 60 s of a rejoin's quarantine is now undone without punishment (`note_rejoin`, in memory: after a restart the bot is punished as before, which fails safe); a human doing the same is still quarantined. The join branch also undid with `?`, so a Discord error skipped `guard_perms` for that entry and left the role on without a word: `undo_role_change` tries once more after 2 s, then posts "Could not take back roles" in the anti-nuke log, and never punishes.
*Avoid:* a step whose failure must not stop the rest of an audit handler does not use `?`; retry or report it where it fails.

**"Nothing saved" was used to mean "join quarantine".** (review, 11-12)
The fix above told the kinds apart by an empty `saved_roles`. But a staff, Raider List or time-limit quarantine of a member with no roles also saves nothing, so such a member could verify out of it on the web page; and an autorole landing before the join quarantine filled the list, which made the join quarantine a real one: the joiner could never verify and the autorole bot was punished. Verification also lifted the join quarantine only while "quarantine on join" was still on, so switching it off left members held with a stale row, and a rejoin then logged an anti-nuke "rejoined while quarantined" for a joiner who had only not verified yet.
*Avoid:* never infer a kind from what a field happens to hold; store it. `quarantines.on_join` (migration 3900) is set only by verification (`antinuke::Kind`), a real quarantine always wins over a join one (`on_join AND EXCLUDED.on_join`), and `self_grant`, `grant`, `hold` and `requarantine_rejoin` read the flag. `grant` ends a join quarantine whatever the setting is now, and a rejoin into a join quarantine counts as verifying for sticky roles. A join raid hit that keeps the member (Log, Warn, Timeout, Rename) drops their saved sticky roles, as the sticky-roles plan decided: kept pending, a raider got them back by verifying (web, staff `/verify`) or by leaving and rejoining. `restore_pending` reads the member's live timeout before it claims, because web verification and staff `/verify` work during a timeout, which holds the main roles. Rows from before the column with nothing saved were marked join, as they were treated until then. The time limit's Quarantine is still a join one only when the member had no roles, as before, until owner decision decides whether verifying should lift it.

**Long work under a global lock.** (#100)
Similarity compared whole 4000-character messages (quadratic) while holding the global heat lock.
*Avoid:* bound the input (first 500 characters) and keep work under shared locks short.
The same holds for a claim: `voice_nuke` held the guild's fetch claim while counting, and counting can start a nuke run of tens of seconds, so no other voice update of the guild was fetched meanwhile (review, 11-12). The claim now covers only the fetch (`run` takes `fetch` and `apply`); the one-run-per-actor claim of the nuke run is separate and unchanged.

**Listeners must survive a reconnect.** (#100)
The config listener died after one DB hiccup, so dashboard changes never applied until a bot restart.
*Avoid:* long-lived listeners restart in a loop and reload their caches after reconnecting.

**A failed log must not stop protection.** (#100, #133)
The timeout DM went out before the timeout worked, and a failed anti-nuke report stopped panic mode.
*Avoid:* fixed order Contain → Revert → Report → Panic. Logs and DMs are side effects; their failure never skips the next protective step.
The race review (11-11) found the same in Join Raid (a failed raid log or report job skipped the lockdown and every punishment, and one raider who had already left stopped the punish loop), in automod (the mention-lock log and lock came before the strike, the timeout log before the strike-cap lock) and in `warn_actions`. A loop that punishes several members logs a failure and goes on with the next.
`voice_nuke::apply` broke that with `?`: the audit deltas were already stored as seen, so one actor's failed whitelist read or count dropped every later actor's delta for good (review, 11-12). Root cause: `?` inside a loop whose input was already marked done. It now logs per actor and goes on.

The sticky roles review (11-11) found it in Join Gate too: a refused gate punishment (403 on kick) ended `on_join`, so the join raid check never ran for that joiner. Gate punishments now log and go on.
`panic::note` broke it too (review, 11-12): a failed heat panic log returned `Err` after heat panic was on, and automod (`automod.rs` twice), `activity::heat_up` and anti-nuke's heat path passed it on with `?`, so the strike, timeout or `on_actions` count that tripped it never ran. The panic report and lockdown logs in `panic::start` did the same and ended the nuke run that called it. Those logs only warn now, and every heat caller goes through `panic::note_heat`, which logs a failure and returns. Same search found `catch::on_message` (a failed log handed a caught message on to automod as uncaught) and `verify::deadline` (a failed log failed the job, which would punish again); both warn now. `native_action`'s Report log was left: its caller already logs per action and goes on.

**A gateway event's value can be older than the bot's own write.** (auto slowmode review, 11-11)
Events run in parallel tasks and Discord sends a channel update for every edit (a rename too), so a ChannelUpdate handled after the bot's own PATCH can still carry the old slowmode. Auto slowmode read that value as "staff changed it" and put it back, which undid its own slowmode.
*Avoid:* don't decide from a field of the event when the bot itself writes that field. Read the live value (`GET channel`) and compare it with both the old and the bot's value (`slowmode::staff_value`), or skip the event when a later compare-and-set already handles it (the off job reverts only its own value). Also checked: `enforce_quarantine` and `catch::keep_top` act on event values too, but their writes are idempotent, so a stale event only repeats them.

**A claim row without its job blocked the channel for good.** (auto slowmode review, 11-11)
An `auto_slowmode` row is claimed with `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`. When its `slowmode_off` job gave up after its retries (Discord down, a missing permission retried until the last attempt), the row stayed and every later start found "already owned". Also: a failed start deleted row and job even when the PATCH might have landed, the not-calm re-queue made a job for a row staff had already removed, and a 403 was retried until the job gave up.
*Avoid:* a claim takes over a row with no pending job (`DELETE … WHERE NOT EXISTS (job)` in the claim's transaction, so a parallel claim conflicts instead of doubling it). Treat 50001/50013 as final (`http_err::forbidden_any`): end the claim and log it. Re-queue only while the row exists, under `FOR UPDATE`. Scope a job's rows to its own guild (`AND guild_id = $2`). A start that stops early because the channel already shows the auto value also gives a stuck row a new off job (`requeue_stuck`), since the takeover in the claim never runs then.

**A data request whose job was dropped stayed "approved" for good.** (batch 9 builder, 11-19)
`datareq::run_job` returned an error when the bot owner was unknown (the Discord application lookup at start failed), and any other failing try errored too. After the last attempt the scheduler dropped the job, the row stayed `approved` without a job, `/mydata` refused a new request ("already asked") and the dashboard answers only `pending` ones. *Avoid:* a status row that waits for one job goes back to a state a person can act on when that job is dropped: an unknown bot owner (it cannot become known in this run) reopens the request at once instead of retrying, and the scheduler's give-up branch calls `datareq::gave_up`, which puts an undone request back to `pending` (test: `datareq::tests::gave_up_reopens`). Searched: `auto_slowmode` (entry above), `raider_lift` (logs for a by-hand fix), `appeal_accept` (the appeal stays open; a dropped job unblocks a new accept); the Raider List decisions change no row that the job must finish.

**State built from gateway events starts empty after a restart.** (voice raid, 11-12)
The logger tells voice joins from moves by the channel it last saw each member in, and that map started empty, so after every restart or new gateway session the first move of each member already in voice was a "join". It was only a wrong log line (marked `shortcut:`) until the voice raid counted joins from it: a restart during a busy event could have started a false raid. Root cause: the old value came only from earlier events, and Discord does not replay those.
*Avoid:* seed such state from `GuildCreate`, which carries the whole snapshot (`welcome::on_guild_create` seeds `Logger::seed_voice` from `voice_states`, replacing what the guild had), and read the old value under the same lock right before the event overwrites it (`events.rs`). Both must happen before the task's first await (review, 11-12): the seed ran after `app.cfg.await` and a DB write, wiping voice updates handled meanwhile, and the old channel was read after `app.cfg.await`, so the second update of a member could take the lock first and both read the same old channel. `events::run` now seeds a `GuildCreate` and records a voice update (`Logger::voice_update`) first, and logs the update afterwards with `handle_voice`. Searched: `thread_parents` is filled from `ThreadListSync` and thread events, the ticket-category lookup (`parent_of`) only pairs an event with its own audit entry, and Join Raid and heat start empty on purpose (a restart counts from zero, it never counts twice).

**Heat lives in memory.** (#111, accepted)
A restart resets running heat. Strikes and timeouts survive. This was left as is on purpose.

**A restart reset every limit window.** (overlap deep dive N3, 13-11)
Anti-nuke's per-minute/hour windows, the permit command limits and the heat panic list lived only in memory, and D51 showed a restart can be forced: a nuker at 4/5 bans who crashed the bot started again at 0. Root cause: the counted actions were already in `action_log`, but nothing read them back.
*Avoid:* a limit that protects against an attacker is rebuilt at startup before the gateway (`antinuke::seed_windows`): anti-nuke from `action_log` rows of the last hour (`counted` holds how many actions a row adds: a merged voice entry its growth, a second inverse row 0), command limits from `command_uses`, heat panic from `heat_panic`. Both new tables are pruned hourly in `welcome::purge_left`. Personal heat (above) and Join Raid stay in memory on purpose.

**A permit without its own limit had none.** (review, 13-11)
Auto Setup gives Admins and Moderators ban and kick without limits, and anti-nuke skips the bot's own audit entries, so a hacked mod could mass-ban with `t!ban id1 id2 …` where the same account in Discord's own UI would be quarantined at the anti-nuke limit. A breach of a permit limit then quarantined them but left the bans already made.
*Avoid:* `permits::command_limit` falls back to anti-nuke's limit for the same action (`commands::nuke_fallback`: ban, kick and timeout, one less, as a permit limit trips past its value and anti-nuke at it; only with anti-nuke on). Under that fallback bans and kicks share one rate limit key (`commands::limit_key`), as they share anti-nuke's card: per-action keys let a mod do the card's limit in bans and again in kicks. On a breach `gated` quarantines and runs `antinuke::revert_breach` (the actor's last hour, case bans included). Every staff command goes through `gated`; the dashboard's own rate limit is D24.

**A nuker under every limit, or with quarantine off, never reached panic.** (review, 13-11)
Anti-nuke heat at 100 % only counted towards panic, which needs 3 distinct accounts, so one nuker who spread actions over several cards below each limit was never stopped; and Quarantine On Limit off dropped the Panic step with the quarantine. Root cause: one switch or signal fed only the step next to it.
*Avoid:* 100 % anti-nuke heat contains and reverts like a limit (`antinuke::breach`), and `nuke_plan(false)` still ends with Panic. When a switch turns one step off, check the steps after it still run.

**A category exemption covered anyone who moved a channel there.** (review, 13-11)
Ticket categories and panic's Whitelisted Categories exempted every channel create and delete in them, for any actor, so a nuker moved channels into the category and deleted them uncounted and never recreated. The panic list was only ever documented as "left unlocked by panic". Root cause: an exemption meant for one kind of actor (ticket bots) was keyed only on the place.
*Avoid:* an exemption names who it is for: ticket categories exempt only a bot (`antinuke::is_bot`, a failed lookup counts), never a channel that moved in from outside them in the last hour (`App::note_category` before the first await of the gateway task, `moved_in`), and panic's list is panic-only. Searched: no other module reads `whitelisted_categories`.

**Panic's "lockdown" let everyone keep writing.** (live 13-11, after the overlap deep dive)
Panic locked with `LockKind::Role` only, which strips dangerous perms (Mention @everyone and the like) and never Send Messages, so during panic @everyone wrote everywhere and the Whitelisted Categories exemption changed nothing. The exemption also gave @everyone Send by removing a Send deny the category already had, so a read-only category opened during panic, and it only ever wrote the @everyone overwrite, also when the lock silences other main roles. Root cause: the step was named "lock the server" but used the lock that only covers roles; nothing tested what members could still do.
*Avoid:* panic takes two locks, `Role` and `Server` (main roles, or @everyone, lose `SILENCE` and voice when the lockdown voice option is on), each with its own overlap check, so a lock staff made first leaves the other part to panic. `panic_end` keeps one owner flag per lock (`locked`, `locked_server`), written before the lock, again after it (a staff unlock in between cleared it, round 2), and cleared when no lock was saved (a crash between lock and flag left everyone muted for good); a new panic does not start while an old `panic_end` job still waits for its retry, or its replace drops the claim on the old locks; `lockdown::forget_timers` clears the one whose lock staff lifted, and `panic::end` reads the flags from the job as it is now and tries both unlocks. A whitelisted category gets, per main role, an allow for exactly the bits the server lock takes from that role (`panic::taken`), never a bit the role lock takes too (Mention @everyone, review round 1), never a bit the category already allows or denies the role, and for a role other than @everyone never a bit another role's overwrite denies there (a role allow beats every role deny, so a Muted role would write; the Quarantine role's deny is left out for a role quarantine takes, never for a managed role, which quarantine keeps). Each exemption is saved in the job before it is put; if panic's server lock was not made, the categories close again at once. A category id from another server is refused. A combined lock (`Mixed`, `All`) now takes the parts no active lock covers (`LockKind::rest`), so a join raid during panic still locks joins. With that, `t!unlock <kind>` checks the permits of every active lock it lifts, not only of the kind typed (`unlock` lifts every overlapping lock, a combined one too), and `t!lock` names what it took. Round 3 (owner decision 13-11): a part a lock from before panic held when panic started is marked in `panic_end` (`waits_role`, `waits_server`), and panic takes it once an unlock lifts a lock overlapping it (its timer ran, or staff lifted it), once only, and then owns it (`panic::held` before `lockdown::unlock`, `panic::relock` with the lifted kinds after). An owner flag alone could not tell "a lock from before panic" from "staff lifted panic's own lock" (review round 3), so every unlock re-muted the server. Not while `start` runs (`PanicRun::starting`, a per-start token so an old start's guard never clears a newer one); a relock that panic's end missed is lifted again at once (`unlock_now`). A failed relock keeps waiting (unless panic's own lock holds the part already, so two relocks at once do not leave a stale wait), and the end of `start` takes a waited part whose lock ran out while it ran (`catch_up_waits`, after `start` marks itself done, so nothing falls between them; round 4-5). Relock and catch-up share `take_waited`, which lifts again a lock that an end of panic missed. Panic marks a lock as its own inside the guild's lock mutex, right after the save (`lockdown::lock_marked`), so a second take or an unlock always sees the mark (round 6). Cleanups that lift "panic's own" lock after an early end know it by its saved time (`store::lock_created`), not by its kind: the end may have lifted it and staff made a new lock of the same kind. `relink_roles` rewrites a snapshot with `store::update_lock`, which keeps its state and age. A role a restore made again under an active lock is locked like the old one and its snapshot entries move to the new id (`lockdown::relink_roles`, after `relink` in both restore paths), or it was the one main role still writing. Entries are saved before the PATCH, and a role in two locks gets one PATCH with what both take (one PATCH per lock from the same read gave the first lock's bits back). An early end from the dashboard that ran, or is running, while `start` took its locks is caught at the end of `start` (`panic::ends_later`), which lifts its own locks (only a lock of exactly its kind still active, so a staff `All` stays) and closes the categories. Not covered: the lock's time is read a few ms after the save, outside the guild's lock mutex, so an end plus a new staff lock of the same kind in those ms can still be mistaken for panic's; a failed time read leaves panic's lock after an early end (fails toward locked); a part whose lock failed with nothing holding it waits for an unlock that may not come. A role with Send that is not a main role keeps writing, the same as `t!lock` (owner decision 13-11); a non-synced child channel of a whitelisted category stays locked; a crash after the flag but before the lock lets panic's end lift a server lock staff made later.

**Manage Roles handed out a whitelist.** (review, 13-11)
Anyone with Manage Roles could give themselves a role that carries a permit, and with it the permit's anti-nuke whitelist, then nuke uncounted. Root cause: the permit tables decide trust, but who may hand out a permit's role was left to Discord's hierarchy.
*Avoid:* giving a member a permit role is a guarded action (`antinuke::guard_permit_roles`): the role is taken back and the actor quarantined unless they are Owner, Extra Owner or Trusted Admin. Runs with anti-nuke on, after `guard_perms`, also for a self-grant. Only a guarded permit counts (owner decision): every permit except the weak ones listed in `permits::WEAK_PERMS` and `WEAK_WHITELIST` (cases, notes, mod stats, reports, tickets; word, link and report immunity; automod immunity is guarded, owner decision). Reaction-role, level and autorole bots hand those out all day, so they are only logged. List the weak ones, never the strong ones: round 2 listed the strong ones and missed the per-action `wl_*` flags, which are the original D16 attack, and revert, backups, appeals and `lock_*`. A new permit is then guarded by default.

**A failed whitelist lookup dropped the action.** (review, 13-11)
`on_audit` used `app.whitelisted(..)?`, so a 5xx or timeout on the member lookup returned before the action was logged or counted: a nuke during a Discord hiccup was never stopped or reverted. Root cause: an error in the exemption check was handled like "exempt".
*Avoid:* on a protection path a failed exemption check means "not exempt" for counting (the limits apply). The instant tripwires (`guard_perms`, the hold) still stop on the error, as #97 asks for staff.
