During a nuke the level is reused for 5 s (`App::whitelisted`), cleared on every member update or leave before the event's first await, and a lookup that raced a clear is not kept (as D66).

**The unauthorized-bot filter let a bot in when the audit lookup failed.** (review, 13-11)
A bot whose adder could not be read (5xx, 429, the audit entry not written yet) counted as authorised, on purpose, and a failed immunity check of the adder did too. Root cause: "unknown" was read as "allowed" on a protection path.
*Avoid:* `welcome::bot_adder_retry` tries once more after 1.5 s (each try bounded to 3 s) and Join Gate blocks the bot when that fails too, finds no entry, or the adder's level cannot be read. The mock Discord answers `audit-logs` (`Mock::audit`, `fail_audit`).

**During a Role lock the stripped permissions were read as the real ones.** (review, 13-11)
A Role lock saves each role's permissions and strips them, so sticky roles and verification saw a stripped Admin role as harmless and handed it out, and `strict_member_roles` and "enforce permits" did not see it either; when the lock ended the member held Administrator. Root cause: live role permissions are not the role's permissions while a lock holds them.
*Avoid:* a check that judges a role dangerous reads `lockdown::unlocked_info` (live permissions OR the active locks' saved ones). Used by `sticky` (3 places), `verify::grant`, `antinuke::guard_perms` and `enforce_permits`, and the public-log-channel check (`events::public`). The active locks are kept in memory (`lockdown::active_locks`, also read by `guard_lock`), forgotten on every lock write in `lockdown.rs`: these checks run per join and per audit entry, during raids. Searched: `info` shows the live permissions on purpose, `setup` checks the bot's own role (never locked); the dashboard's security score is not changed.

**A role's Discord permissions made its members staff.** (review, owner decision 13-11)
Automod, activity heat and auto slowmode exempted anyone whose roles had Manage Messages, Administrator and the like (`App::staff_by_perms`, `slowmode::exempt`), and during a Role lock by the saved permissions too. A raider or nuker given such a role just before a panic stayed exempt for the whole lock, and anyone with Manage Roles could hand out exemptions. Root cause: two sources of trust, Brim's permits and Discord's permission bits.
*Avoid:* use our permits, not Discord permissions. Exemptions, staff status and immunity come only from permits, whitelists and Owner/Extra Owner/Trusted Admin (`App::whitelisted`). Discord permissions only judge whether a role or channel is dangerous or visible (protection). A failed permit lookup counts the message (`slowmode::exempt`): per-author lookups 429 during a raid, and failing open exempted every raider. `misc.staff_only_permitted` has no effect now: still parsed so old configs load, stored values kept, hidden from the dashboard. Searched bot, automod, dashboard and mcp: no other place gives an exemption from permission bits (slash commands set no default_member_permissions; dashboard access is by permits).

**A new delete type was not watched.** (review, 13-11)
Deleting threads and forum posts (audit type 112) was not an anti-nuke action at all, so a forum could be wiped without a count. *Avoid:* the "Thread & forum post deletions" card (`Action::ThreadDelete`, opt-in like the other later cards, nothing to restore). A new action needs the enum, `ALL`, `watched`, the dashboard's `NUKE_CARDS` and the UI's `NUKE_WORDS`.

**A lost gateway session lost its audit entries.** (review, 13-11)
Discord replays missed events only on a resume. After a new session (`Ready`) the audit entries made in the gap never arrived, so a nuke during a reconnect was never counted. *Avoid:* `welcome::saw_audit` keeps the newest audit id per guild, and `on_ready` runs anti-nuke on the entries after it (`catch_up_audit`). The id handled last is saved in `audit_seen` and loaded before the gateway (`load_audit_seen`), so the first `Ready` after a restart reads the entries made while the bot was down too.
A replay is not a live event (round 2): (1) a replayed entry counts at its own time (snowflake), never at now (`antinuke::replay_age`, `AntiNuke::on_actions_at`, `action_log.created_at` backdated, no heat); older than the hour window it is not counted; a burst in the gap still trips. (2) It has no gateway event, so its category comes from the channel itself or the latest backup (`replayed_parent`); a ticket made and closed in the gap is counted. (3) The start point is taken in the gateway loop before the `Ready` task is spawned (`note_ready`): a live entry right after `Ready` must not raise it past the gap. (4) Live and catch-up bring the same entries: `saw_audit` returns whether an entry is new (last 1000 ids per guild) and both run anti-nuke only then. (5) Pages back with `before` (newest first) up to 1000 entries; past that, and when every retry of a failed read fails, the Anti Nuke log says the gap was not checked. (6) The `audit_seen` write is batched every 2 s after handling (`done_audit`, `flush_audit`): an UPSERT on one row per guild in the event path serialised a nuke's entries on its row lock.
Round 3: (7) "Replayed" is a flag from the catch-up (`antinuke::on_replayed`), never inferred from the host clock: a clock an hour fast made every live entry too old to count. (8) A replayed entry re-judges every window that holds it, with the live entries already counted after it (`on_actions_at`): a nuke split across a reconnect tripped nothing. (9) Past the hour window an entry is still logged with `counted` 0 (revertable), and the Anti Nuke log says how many were not counted. (10) Crash and restart edges: the floor is taken before `on_ready`'s first await and a second `Ready` keeps the older start (`note_ready`); the saved id never passes an entry still being handled (`audit_inflight`) or a gap whose catch-up has not succeeded (`audit_pinned`); `action_log.audit_id` lets a catch-up skip entries counted before a crash lost the batched id. (11) The dedupe ring (2500) is larger than a whole catch-up plus the live entries meanwhile. Round 4: (12) every handled entry is recorded in `audit_handled` (tripwires, lock set-backs, voice and exempt entries too, not only counted ones; not when anti-nuke is off or for the bot's own entries; pruned after a day, since the saved id trails by seconds), and a catch-up skips them and every id in `action_log.audit_id` (counted by a handling that crashed before it was recorded): replaying a tripwire entry from before a restart took back a role an Owner had since re-granted. (13) A gap whose catch-up failed every retry is declared unchecked by the alert and unpinned, and an in-flight id leaves when its `InFlight` guard drops, at the end of its handling or on a panic (a 30 min expiry only bounds a leak). (14) Replayed tripwire entries have no age limit, on purpose: `guard_perms`, `guard_permit_roles` and the holds judge a grant that is still in force (the role is still held), so an old one is undone and punished like a new one; only counting has the hour window.

**Staff could take a lock apart one channel at a time.** (review, 13-11)
During an active lock, deleting a locked overwrite, removing its deny or giving a stripped role its permissions back went unwatched: overwrite changes are not a watched action, a role's permission change counted only as a rename, and nothing reapplied the lock. *Avoid:* `antinuke::guard_lock` checks every role update and overwrite update or delete against the active locks' snapshots (`role_perms`, `channel_denies`, `prior`): the change is set back to its old values from the audit entry, logged, and counted on the Channel Updates or Role Updates card (when that card is on). An Owner, Extra Owner or Trusted Admin is only logged. Every check in `on_audit` runs whatever an earlier one returned and the first error is returned at the end (`keep`): a `?` on `guard_perms` or `hold_overwrite` skipped the set-back, and one on the kick/ban hold skipped the count, the D21 pattern again.

**Panic's backup load deleted what the bot made after the backup.** (overlap deep dive N2, 13-11)
A restore deletes every live role and channel missing from the image, and protected only the Quarantine role and the log channels. Open ticket channels, a remade verification channel and Auto Setup's roles made after the last backup were deleted by panic mode. *Avoid:* `sections::restore_keep` keeps a fixed list of config fields (`KEEP_FIELDS`: quarantine role, log channels, ticket category and log channel, anti-nuke's ticket categories, verification channel and roles), the permit roles and open tickets' channels; the bot's restore (panic, `/backup load`) and the dashboard's preview both use it. Not every id in the config (round 2): most fields are Editor level, so an Editor could list a nuker's Administrator role in an automod list and panic would leave it. A keep or exempt list built from config names the fields it trusts.

**A quarantine left channel access from overwrites.** (review, 13-11)
Quarantine swapped roles only. A member's own allow overwrite, or one for a managed role they keep (booster, a bot's own role), still let them read and act in that channel, since a member or role allow beats the Quarantine role's deny; and a new overwrite for a quarantined member was never checked. *Avoid:* after a real quarantine `contain` calls `hide_channels` (its own step, after the role change and the `quarantines` row, so `save_row`/`restore_row` are untouched): in each channel that grants them access it puts a member overwrite denying those bits and saves the old one in `quarantine_overwrites`; `Inverse::Unquarantine` puts them back. `hold_overwrite` undoes a member overwrite that gives a quarantined member new access and quarantines the actor, as the role hold does. A managed role's overwrite that later grants more (`hold_role_overwrite`) gets the same member deny for every really quarantined member who holds the role, without punishing the actor (staff set up booster channels). Join quarantines are left alone. Taking access back works both ways (round 2): a lost deny is a grant like a new allow, so clearing the quarantine's own member deny or deleting that overwrite (`ChannelOverwriteDelete` goes to `hold_overwrite` too) is put back and punished, and a managed role's lost deny gets the member deny like its new allow. A check for "more access" reads both the allow and the deny change. When the actor's quarantine whitelist cannot be read, both holds (and `guard_perms`, `guard_permit_roles`; `guard_lock` sets back without counting) undo the grant but quarantine nobody and say so in the Anti Nuke log (`hold_whitelisted`; owner decision, undo not punish): returning on the error left the quarantined member what they were given. In a nuke the hiding runs after the revert (`contain_with(.., false)` in `revert_and_report`): one overwrite write per channel must not hold up recreating the deleted channels.

**The bot's own rows were undone like anyone's.** (review, 13-11)
`/revert actor:@Brim` lifted every quarantine the bot made, gave a nuking bot its managed role's Administrator back, and unbanned automatic bans, for anyone with the revert permit.
*Avoid:* the bot counts as an immune target (Owner or Extra Owner with `include_owners`, as for an owner's actions), and a revert of the bot's rows never runs Unquarantine or RolePerms (`antinuke::bot_contain_op`; the dashboard's Revert Center skips both and lists the bot as immune too).

**A resumable run read its intent from the state it was changing.** (Channel Setup review, 11-11)
Push decided "this channel follows its category" from live Discord (synced with the category). Once the category's overwrites were pushed and a child's step failed, the child was no longer synced in live, read as an exception on the next push, and stayed public.
*Avoid:* a run that is resumed by recomputing a diff stores every decision derived from the old state before its first step (Push writes `reset` into the draft for each follower), and stops at the first failed step instead of running the rest (deletes included) against a half-changed server.

**A per-process flag does not stop a second writer.** (Channel Setup review, 11-11)
Push guarded itself with a static `HashSet` of guilds, so a second dashboard process could push at the same time, and a draft PUT or DELETE during a push was overwritten by the push's write-back. A stale PUT after a push brought back `new-*` keys that were already created, and the next push created them twice.
*Avoid:* hold a Postgres advisory lock for the whole run (`pg_try_advisory_xact_lock` in a transaction the run owns, so it is freed when the task ends or the process dies) and refuse other writers with 409 while it is held. Keys a run has used up are recorded (`next`) and refused when they come back. Run the steps in `tokio::spawn` and await the handle, so a dropped request does not stop a run half way; log what will happen before the first step and the result after, and record a failed save instead of returning with `?`.

**A dropped sqlx transaction kept the push lock after the answer.** (Channel Setup lock, 11-11)
`lock` took `pg_try_advisory_xact_lock` in a sqlx transaction, and every handler let it drop on return. sqlx only queues the ROLLBACK on drop; it runs when the pooled connection is returned in a spawned task, so the lock outlived the response. The next PUT or Push that came in first got "a push is running" (about 1 in 60 runs of `draft_is_saved_discarded_and_pushed_only_when_safe`).
*Avoid:* a transaction that holds an advisory lock which others try-lock ends with an awaited `rollback` or `commit` on every path before the answer, early returns included (`release` in `dashboard/src/channels.rs`; the whole push runs in one task that releases at the end). Drop is only the fallback for a cancelled request or a dead process. Checked the other `pg_advisory_xact_lock` users (`bot/settings`, `bot/tickets`, `dashboard/store.rs`, `core/cases.rs`): they wait for the lock instead of trying it, so a late ROLLBACK only delays the next writer by a moment and nothing is refused.

**No row lock across Discord calls.** (Raider List review, 11-11)
The Raider List lift held `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` on its action row while it unbanned, so a slow Discord held the lock for the length of the HTTP timeout, and a second run waited instead of finding the work done. Root cause: the lock was used to make the case and the notice happen once, which a claim does without waiting.
*Avoid:* read without a lock, make the Discord call (it must be safe to repeat: the unban checks the ban is still the list's), then claim with `DELETE … ` and act on `rows_affected == 1`; only the claimer writes the case and the log. A lift that gives up after its last try posts "Raider List removal failed" to the mod log (`raiders::lift_gave_up`). Searched for the same pattern: `appeals` decide locks one appeal row across its unban (a person's click, left as is), and `backup::track_created` locks the newest backup across one Discord GET for its read-modify-write (left as is: it needs the lock and the GET is short).

**A transferred server kept its old owner's trust.** (11-13)
Ban Sync groups servers by their Discord owner, so a server someone set up and then transferred to you joined your group with sharing on and could ban people in all your servers; the DM said it had left its group. Its Extra Owners and Trusted Admins (set by the old owner) also kept full control. Root cause: an owner change only moved the server between groups, it never asked whether the new owner wants what came with it. *Avoid:* on an owner change (`bansync::on_owner`, GuildCreate and GuildUpdate) switch `ban_sync.enabled` off and remove EO/TA in one transaction with the `guild_owners` update and a config version, store what was removed (`owner_handovers`, migration 3920), and let only the new Discord owner keep it (Overview row "Ownership changed", `ops::keep_staff`). Keeping never turns Ban Sync on; the new owner does that with 2FA, and it then groups with their own servers. The dashboard memoizes the Discord owner for 30 s, so for that long it still named the old owner, who could switch Ban Sync on or add himself as an Extra Owner (S-h3 1): `api::is_discord_owner`, which every owner-only write and `level_of` use, also refuses when `guild_owners` names someone else or the user is the `owner_handovers.old_owner`. Round 2 (11-13, S-h3 and R-h3): EO/TA were not all of it. Permits the previous owner set stayed on, so his alt with an owner, full-immunity or dashboard-edit permit kept its power; the card gave back all staff with one click and showed only counts; a rollback to a version from before the change wrote the old EO/TA, permit roles and `ban_sync.enabled = true` back; `keep_staff` took row locks before the config lock (the reverse of `on_owner`, a deadlock); and `on_removed` deleted the `guild_owners` row, so an owner change while the bot was out looked like a first sight. *Avoid:* `on_owner` also deletes the roles of permits with `owner`/`dashboard_edit` or the `all` whitelist and stores them (`owner_handovers.paused_roles`, migration 3921), and names the weaker permits that stayed on (`other_permits`), which the card and the Ban Sync page list (`ops::weaker_permits`). It records a handover row on every owner change. Overview shows one row per paused person or role, with name and id and the previous owner marked, each with its own Keep, plus Dismiss. `api::rollback` keeps EO, TA, the roles of strong permits and Ban Sync off for a version older than `owner_handovers.at` and says so (`held`). Every dashboard writer of the permit tables takes the config lock first (`store::write_permits`, `keep_staff`, `claim_key`). `guild_owners` rows stay when the bot leaves; groups skip servers in `guild_left`, and the next GuildCreate compares with the kept owner. Round 3 (S-h3 and R-h3 round 2): the pause list named only `owner`, `dashboard_edit` and `all`, so a permit with `antinuke`, `quarantine` or a `wl_*` flag, and the `antinuke.quarantine_whitelist` user ids, kept their exemptions; and the rollback hold-back looked at the old version alone, so a permit made weak before the change got its guarded perms back. *Avoid:* one rule in core, `permits::guarded`: every permit is guarded except perms outside `GUARDED_PERMS` with only `WEAK_FLAGS` (filter and report flags), so a new flag is guarded by default; `on_owner` and the dashboard's rollback both use it. `on_owner` also clears the quarantine whitelist into `owner_handovers.quarantine_whitelist` (migration 3923), kept per person on Overview. A rollback never makes a role guarded again: a permit guarded in the old version keeps its current roles when it is guarded now, keeps its current perms when it is weak now, and comes back without roles when it is gone. Its Ban Sync copy cancel ran after the commit, so a failure there left the old group's copies pending and the retry found the owner recorded and did nothing (R-h3 round 3, 2): the cancel is in the transaction, and after the commit only cache drops and best-effort DMs run. A failed `on_owner` was not retried, so the new owner had no Owner level until the next guild event (R-h3 round 2, 3). *Avoid:* the events call `bansync::owner_seen`, which queues `owner_check` on failure (the job reads the owner from Discord and retries on the job lease). The dashboard checks the memoized owner against a Discord answer at most 2 s old (`Discord::guild_owner_fresh`, a lookup error counts as not the owner): when Discord confirms the user but `guild_owners` still names the previous owner, the user keeps Owner level and a retry is queued, but the owner-only writes (Ban Sync, Extra Owners, Keep, purge) wait until the bot has paused the previous owner's staff. Round 4: a retry dropped after repeated failures was queued again on every dashboard request, which filled the log (R-h3 round 3); `owner_state` queues at most one `owner_check` per server and hour (per dashboard process). And while that reset waited, the real owner could not even remove the previous owner's Extra Owners (S-h3 round 3): `put_permits` allows an Extra Owners change that only removes people when Discord confirms the user (`owner_state == Some(false)`), never one that adds.

**Ban Sync's hourly cap was counted outside the row's transaction.** (Ban Sync, count-then-insert sweep 11-11)
`share_job` and `import_job` each counted the users shared in the last hour, then wrote rows. A share and an owner-started import running at once both saw room and went past the 30 per hour cap.
*Avoid:* `offer` counts and writes in one transaction under `pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtextextended('ban_sync_cap:' || source, 0))`, and says whether it hit the cap. Send the rest stays outside the cap on purpose (the owner's choice). The general rule is "A count checked a cap, then a second statement inserted" (#183).

**A button read its state, acted, then marked it done.** (Raider List, full-codebase check 11-11)
The Raider List alert's Ban/Kick/Quarantine read `action = 'alert'`, ran the punishment and only then set the row. Two staff pressing at once both passed the read: two cases, two DMs, two mod logs, or a ban and a kick.
*Avoid:* claim first with `UPDATE ... WHERE action = 'alert'` and act only on `rows_affected == 1`; put the row back when nothing was done. Searched: Ban Sync's Ban here already claims first; appeals decide claims after the unban, but an unban is safe to repeat and the case and log come after the claim, so it was left as is.

**A staff button skipped the panic lock and the rate limit.** (review, 11-12)
The Raider List alert's Allow button wrote the allow straight away, while Ban, Kick and Quarantine go through `commands::apply`/`modcmds::run`, which call `gated`. During panic mode's mod lock, or past the command rate limit, a hacked staff account could still let listed raiders in.
*Avoid:* every button or command that acts for staff calls `commands::gated` with the permit it acts under before it changes anything, also when it only lifts or allows.

**Advisory locks on raw ids share one key space.** (Raider List review, 11-11)
`report` locked the reporting owner's user id and then `add` locked the reported user's id, both as plain `pg_advisory_xact_lock(id)`. Owner A reporting B while owner B reports A took the same two keys in opposite order and Postgres killed one as a deadlock.
*Avoid:* give each kind of lock its own key, `pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtextextended('<kind>:' || id, 0))` (as `raider_user:`, `raider_owner:`, `channel_drafts:` do). Searched: the other raw locks (`cases::create`, ticket numbers, config saves) all take one guild id per transaction, so they cannot cross; left as is.

**"It is gone, so an earlier try of mine removed it" needs proof.** (Raider List review, 11-11)
The lift took a missing ban to mean an earlier try had unbanned, and wrote an unban case in the bot's name even when staff had unbanned by a path the list never saw.
*Avoid:* record the intent (`raider_actions.unbanning = true`) just before the Discord call, and only a retry that finds that mark writes the missed case.

**A count checked a cap, then a second statement inserted.** (full check, 11-11)
`token_create` counted live MCP tokens and inserted in a separate statement, so parallel requests at 9 live tokens all saw room (the test made up to 20 against a cap of 10). `propose` (open MCP changes, each one a DM to the owner) and message reports (10 per reporter an hour; each interaction is its own task) had the same gap. A single `INSERT … SELECT … WHERE (SELECT count(*)) < n` does not help under READ COMMITTED: parallel statements do not see each other's uncommitted rows.
*Avoid:* count and insert in one transaction under `pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtextextended('<kind>:' || key, 0))` (`mcp_tokens:` and `mcp_writes:` are global, `reports:` is per guild and reporter), taken right before the insert and never held across a Discord call. A unique index is enough when it already lets only one win (`appeals_one_open`). Searched every `count(*)`, `exists(` and `.len >=` that gates an insert: the appeals daily limit is covered by its unique open index; the Raider List report cap, ticket numbers and config saves were already locked; the Ban Sync hourly cap is counted inside sequential jobs (an owner's import can overlap a share job by a few, left as is); the in-memory caps (rescue claims, verification remakes, 2FA fails) check and push under one Mutex.

**A ticket was marked closed before its log and channel delete, and a half-opened one blocked the member.** (Tickets 2.0 phase A, T-03/T-04, 11-15)
`staff::close` set `closed_at` first, so a restart before the delete left a live channel that no longer counted as a ticket (no close, no relay), and a second close returned silently. `dm::create` took the number with `channel_id = NULL`; a restart before the channel was made left that row open forever, and the unique open-ticket index then refused every new ticket of that member in that server.
*Avoid:* tickets have a `status` (`creating`, `open`, `closing`, `closed`, migration 3930; no CHECK against `closed_at` yet, so an image rollback can still close). A close claims `open` → `closing` and queues `ticket_close_check` in the same transaction, treats a 404 on the channel delete as done, and then sets `closed`; the check closes a ticket still `closing` whose channel is gone and opens any other one again (staff told), since a check that failed out would leave it `closing`. `on_channel_delete` leaves a `closing` ticket to its close. `claim_number` removes the member's own `creating` row older than 10 minutes, and a create whose row was removed meanwhile deletes its channel. A member DM that finds its ticket channel gone (deleted while the bot was offline, so no delete event) closes that ticket and asks for the message again. `closeall` takes only `open` tickets. Not covered yet: a channel made just before a restart stays as an empty orphan (phase C).

**A quarantine row was read as "still contained".** (review, 11-12)
A nuke run decided "already quarantined, only revert the late entries" from the row alone. The row stays when a Quarantine Whitelist user gives the roles back by hand, with Quarantine Hold off, or when the hold's undo fails, so a nuker who was released and nuked again was only reverted: no new quarantine, case or owner alert. A later quarantine also kept the old saved roles (`ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`), so a mod demoted since got the old Mod role back on release.
*Avoid:* decide from Discord whether the stored state still holds. `still_contained` needs the row and the member holding only what `quarantine_roles` leaves (`held`), and for a bot its managed role cleared; a member who left falls back to the row. A new real quarantine saves today's roles and restarts `created_at` only when the member holds a role quarantine would take and joined before the row was written: a rejoin with autoroles, a member with no roles, or one missing a recreated Quarantine role keeps the valid list (round 2). A failed read in `still_contained` counts as not contained. The failure undo (`restore_row`) is a compare-and-set on the row version `save_row` wrote (`xmin`, new on every write, so even an identical write meanwhile counts; `xmax = 0` tells an insert from an update), so a quarantine written meanwhile is never deleted or downgraded; a re-applied one (rejoin, sticky roles: `Kind::Again`) keeps the saved list, and a failed role change puts the old row back (`restore_row`). Searched: `dehoist`, `timeout_roles`, `sticky::due`, `commands` and `info` still treat any row as held, which only holds back more.

**A failed side step skipped the protection after it.** (review, 11-12)
`guard_perms` undid the change with `?` before quarantining, so when the undo failed (role above the bot, a 403) the actor was not quarantined. The hold did the same. The nuke report sent its log with `?` before the owner DM, so a nuker who hid the log channel from the bot also kept the owner from hearing of the nuke.
*Avoid:* an undo, a log or any other side step logs its error and lets the protection (contain, alert) run. The same pattern in `panic.rs` `start` and the other `?` under D13 was fixed by g2a (see the `panic::note` D13 note above).

**The quarantine overwrite hid the verification channel.** (review, 11-12)
`enforce_quarantine` (on ChannelCreate and ChannelUpdate), `t!setup` step 5 and the dashboard's Auto Setup and score Fix denied the Quarantine role View Channel in every channel, the verification channel too. With "quarantine on join" a joiner then could not see the Verify button and was kicked at the time limit. The ChannelCreate of a channel the bot just made can arrive before the bot saved it as the verification channel.
*Avoid:* the verification channel gets its own quarantine overwrite, not a skip: allow exactly View Channel and Read Message History (an extra allow such as Manage Messages breaks the shape), deny the rest of `QUARANTINE_DENY` plus Use Application Commands and Use External Apps (`brim_antinuke::verification_shape`). Skipping it (round 1) left a real quarantine free to send, react, make threads and invites there, never repaired and hidden from the score, and the channel id is an Editor setting. Every writer uses the shape: `ensure_channel`, the remake after a delete (which replaces the copied quarantine overwrite), `t!setup` step 5, `enforce_quarantine` (which also repairs a channel D55 already hit), the dashboard's Setup Channel, `quarantine_channels` and Channel Setup's push, and the score checks for it. Setup Channel also puts the shape back once the channel is saved (`reshape_verification`): with verification still off, the bot denies a new unsaved channel, and before round 3 nothing repaired it when verification was switched on later. A shaped channel that is not the saved one counts as a new verification channel only while verification is on and no live channel is saved (a saved id that 404s counts as none); `enforce_quarantine` takes `verify::CHANNEL_LOCK` only for that check, because the bot saves the channel it made under the lock and its ChannelCreate can come first.

**A write to the permit tables without a config version came back with a rollback.** (11-12)
`statics 10 ?remove` deleted a Trusted Admin straight from the table, and the rescue claim added an Extra Owner the same way. Every config version keeps a `permits` copy, so a rollback rewrote the removed TA, a Permits page opened before the removal saved it back (the version check passed, nothing had bumped it), and History's "Diff vs current" compared two stale copies. Root cause: one piece of state stored in two places (tables and the version copy), with writers that updated only one.
*Avoid:* every write to `extra_owners`, `trusted_admins`, `permits` or `permit_roles` runs in a config-version transaction: the dashboard's `store::commit` and the bot's `settings::save` always take the copy from the live tables (`permits::snapshot`), and a table-only change appends a version (`store::bump`, the bot's `locked` + `append`). Read permits you write back after any Discord call (Auto Setup). Take the config lock before the first permit-table write (`store::write_permits` does): the bot's TA edit locks first and then writes, so a dashboard path that wrote rows first and locked in `commit` deadlocked with it (review, 11-12). Searched: put_permits, rollback, import and Auto Setup already wrote inside a version; the MCP rollback preview already read live permits.

**A cache filled after an await kept an older value than the NOTIFY that cleared it.** (11-12)
`App::cfg` read the newest config, awaited, then inserted it. A save whose NOTIFY removed the cache entry between the read and the insert left the older config cached until the next save. *Avoid:* the config cache has a generation that every forget moves, and a load caches only under the generation it started with (`CfgCache`). Any lazy cache with an external invalidation needs the same.

**A save after a Discord call lost the race to an unrelated save.** (11-12) `set_bot_actions` read the config, and after the AutoMod rule was already made in Discord, committed with that version; any save in between answered 409 and the rule had no bot actions. *Avoid:* a config write that follows a side effect reads the version under the config lock in its own transaction (`store::locked`), so it cannot conflict. Keep Discord calls outside that lock.

**A slot freed only by the next write locked the page.** (bot profile review, 11-15)
A profile change holds a `reserved` row so one runs at a time, and a row left by a restart (an Update restarts the dashboard) was freed only when the next change came in. The page greyed its buttons while any row was reserved, so no change could ever come in. *Avoid:* free stale claims on every path that reads them (`profile::sweep` in both the page load and the write), and finish a claim with `where status = 'reserved'` so a late answer cannot overwrite what the sweep wrote.

**An idle timer fed only by gateway events counts the bot's downtime as quiet.** (Tickets 2.0 auto-close)
`last_activity_at` is set when the bot sees a message, and Discord does not replay messages sent while the bot was offline. The first sweep after a restart would close a ticket the member wrote in an hour ago.
*Avoid:* before an idle action, read the channel's `last_message_id` from Discord (a snowflake holds its time) and skip the action when it is newer than the cutoff.

**A retried job counted its own attempt again.** (verification timeout, 11-17)
The verification deadline counted a time out before the kick or ban ran. When the removal failed, the job ran again and counted again, so one real time out could reach the 3 that bans, and each failed ban left an unban job behind. A wave of joiners that never verify could also fill the server's invite cap with retry invites.
*Avoid:* count an attempt only after the action it counts has worked; remove a job queued for an action that then fails; cap anything a joiner can make the bot create (retry invites: 20 per server per hour, none during a raid or lock, none with verification off). Keep such counts in the database (`verify_timeouts`), not in memory: the bot restarts several times a day, so an in-memory count rarely reaches its limit. A delayed job about a member must check the member is still in the same stay: the verification deadline skips a member who joined less than the time limit ago (a job from an earlier join after a quick rejoin). A timed unban must lift only its own ban: the verification time-out unban checks the ban reason first, so a ban staff put on top in Discord stays.

**A try limit was checked in one statement and counted in another.** (security audit, 11-16)
MCP confirm compared the DM code in one UPDATE and counted a wrong code in a second one, so many parallel confirms all reached the compare before the count hit 3: about N guesses per change instead of 3. Root cause: check-then-act across two statements. *Avoid:* the compare and the count are one UPDATE with `attempts < MAX_TRIES` in its WHERE, so the row lock queues parallel guesses and no code is compared past the cap; a right code costs no try. The cap was per change, and a refused change frees its slot, so a token could loop propose and guess without end (each change DMs the owner): a token that sends 9 wrong codes in 24 hours (three changes' worth, counted by `attempts`, so a change left to expire still counts) is now revoked with its open changes closed (`revoke_if_guessing`), the claim UPDATE also needs a live token (a confirm already past the token check cannot run), and the owner gets a DM; the owner makes a new token to use MCP again (owner decision 11-16). A first draft counted refused changes: two misses and an expiry escaped it. Searched: the dashboard 2FA (`auth::attempt`, one mutex), the rescue claim (counted before the check under one mutex) and the job queue are already atomic.

**A second code was free to guess, it shared the dashboard's lockout, and a cut-off change was stored as failed.** (security review, 11-17)
After a right DM code, a wrong authenticator code put the MCP change back to pending without counting a try, so one DM code could be replayed with new authenticator guesses until the owner's 2FA locked, and that lockout was the one dashboard login uses: an AI client could lock the owner out of the dashboard. The change ran inside the request, so when the MCP server gave up after 30 s the handler could be cut off halfway, and the 15-minute sweep then wrote `failed` for a change that may have run; the AI only saw "dashboard unreachable" and would propose it again. Root cause: each guard counted only the first factor, and the outcome was written by the request that the client could drop. *Avoid:* every wrong code on a change counts a try, whichever factor (`reopen(true)` in `mcp::confirm`), and also counts towards the token's 24-hour revoke; MCP authenticator misses have their own lockout in `TotpAttempts` (`mcp_fails`) with the replay guard shared; the change runs in its own task and writes its outcome there (a first draft wrote it after awaiting the task, in the request the client drops: review 11-17); every status write after a claim needs `status = 'running'`; a cut-off or erroring run is `unknown`, and a confirm of a closed change says what became of it. Searched: `auth::attempt` has only these two callers; the bot's hourly sweep (`welcome.rs`) wrote `failed` the same way and now writes `unknown`. The DM names the server with its id first (`server_label`): a name is set by the server's staff and could copy another server's name and id, and `plain` also strips control, bidi and zero-width characters that can reorder or hide text.

**One fixed MCP token read and wrote everything, and the AI saw more than it needed.** (security review, 11-17)
`MCP_TOKEN` in `.env` was shared by every AI client, could only be changed on the server, and its holder read every server with no trace staff could see; the OAuth login got the write tools too; the AI read NSFW scan rows and member ids and names in log lines. Root cause: access was granted per installation, not per client, and reads were not logged. *Avoid:* every MCP login is a dashboard token (`tbmcp_`, checked by hash through `mcp_token_name`, revoked on the dashboard and refused at once) or an OAuth login that only reads (no write tools listed, a write call refused); writes forward the caller's own token, so the dashboard checks the same login. Each read of a server writes `mcp_reads` before the answer goes out, and a read the log cannot record is refused. The MCP role writes only that insert, in an explicit `SET TRANSACTION READ WRITE`, since its default is read-only; the grant names only `guild_id, tool, via` (with its own `id` or `at` it could block or bury the real rows: security pass 11-18), only tools that take a `guild_id` log it, and `jobs` without one logs every server it listed. `mcp_token_name` is SECURITY DEFINER with `public.mcp_tokens` and `pg_temp` last, so a temp table cannot shadow it; a token of the wrong shape is refused before any query. A Raider List verify acts like a confirm, so it alerts too (review 11-18). `redact` masks member ids and names and keeps server, channel, role and message ids. The owner gets a security alert DM (a queued `raider_notify` job, so it outlives an update's restart) on Raider List confirms, updates and token changes (owner decision 11-17).

## 3. Automod

**A short "gg" from seven members made a crowd.** (audit, 11-17)
Every short text's full heat, base weight included, went into the shared crowd pool, so with the default settings seven members who each typed "gg" within about a second were all deleted, struck and timed out. *Avoid:* a text under 10 characters (after `cure` and the alphanumeric filter) with no link and no mention never joins a crowd pool (`Crowd::MIN_POOL_CHARS`, owner decision 11-17); it still heats its author. The crowd defaults are unchanged.

**An unread level, whitelist or strike row left the scam or spam message posted.** (audit, 11-17)
`check_message` used `?` on the whitelist reads and on the level read (a member GET per tripped message, in the bucket the bans use), and `strike_member` on the strike read and write, all before the delete. *Avoid:* the level is read once per message from the message's own member roles (`author_level` through `App::cached_level`, a member fetch only without a member, none for a webhook), and the words, links, automod, crowd and trip checks all use it; the first fix moved only the trip read, so every raider's blacklisted link still made a member GET for the whitelist (review, 11-17). The level is kept in the level cache, so `punish_crowd`'s `whitelisted` reads hit it. A failed read deletes the message, punishes nobody and returns the error; `strike_member` deletes the tracked messages when the strike read or write fails.

**The raid ID-farm check was quadratic under the raid lock.** (audit, 11-17)
`flagged` searched a `Vec` of farm ids per joiner and `farm_ids` compared every bucket with every other bucket, about 25 million steps per join for a farm of 5000, under `app.raids`, which every server shares. *Avoid:* `farm_ids` returns a `HashSet` and counts each bucket's margin with sorted buckets and prefix sums. The first fix scanned every bucket when the margin spanned more buckets than there were, which nothing caps (`id_margin_ms` of a year at Hour granularity), so it stayed quadratic (review, 11-17). Work under a shared lock stays near linear in its input, whatever a setting says.

**A crowd trip deleted the owner's post, and that post counted towards the crowd.** (audit, 11-17)
Every message went into `Crowd::on_text` before the immunity check, so the owner and two members posting one link made a crowd of three; `punish_crowd` deleted every post and only skipped the strike. *Avoid:* immune and `automod`-whitelisted authors never join a crowd (`crowd_exempt` in `check_message`, from the message's own member roles: a member fetch per message would queue behind the punishments in a raid; a failed lookup counts them in).

**One spammer's own heat made the next member who said the same short text a crowd member.** (audit, 11-17)
The crowd pool added every post's heat with no cap per member, and "tripped within the window" took any text. *Avoid:* each member's share of a crowd pool is capped at half the max (as `ChannelHeat::shares`), and only a long text or link joins a crowd that tripped in the window.

**A trailing dot in a link host skipped every domain check.** (audit, 11-17)
`evil.com.` is `evil.com` to a browser, but missed the phishing, NSFW, blacklisted domain and shortener checks. *Avoid:* `filters::hosts` and `sites::host_of` trim trailing dots; `SiteList::parse` trims them from entries too.

**A downloaded domain list could hold an entry that hits normal chat.** (audit, 11-17)
The phishing and NSFW lists come from a third-party repo with no guard, so an entry like `discord.com` or `co.uk` would act on normal links in every server. *Avoid:* `SiteList::parse` refuses and logs `too_broad` entries: a fixed list of sites chat links to (and their subdomains), bare shared hosting suffixes, and two-label country suffixes.

**The scam-link filter hit Discord's own image links.** (audit, 11-17)
`is_nitro_steam_scam` did not list `discordapp.net` (`media.discordapp.net`, copied image links), `discord.gift`, `discord.new` or `discordstatus.com` as official, so the host check ("contains discord, not official") flagged them. Each hit is `malicious` (full heat, not skipped by the automod permit) and counts as Raider List scam evidence. *Avoid:* a brand-host check lists every official domain of that brand. The lure words still match inside other words on purpose ("freenitro").

**Spam similarity was slow under the lock every server shares.** (audit, 11-17)
`check_message` holds `app.heat` while `heat` compares a message with up to 5 earlier ones (500x500 Levenshtein each), so a long-message spam raid slowed automod in every server. Moving the comparison out of the lock was tried and dropped: two messages of one member run in parallel, and each must see the other in the history, or a burst of copies is missed. *Avoid:* keep the read, score and remember under one lock; make the score cheap instead. `best_similarity` returns 1.0 for an identical text and skips a comparison whose length gap already caps it below the best so far.

**A normal join paid two Discord calls for Raider List evidence.** (audit, 11-17)
`join_raid` always calls `raiders::raid_flagged`, and `add_evidence` looked the server up (guild with counts, the bot's member) before it checked the list, also when nobody was flagged. Those calls ran before verification. *Avoid:* `add_evidence` returns at once for an empty list. A raid still pays them once per flagged batch.

**A Discord AutoMod rule edited by someone without a permit still drove Brim's punishments.** (live test 11-13, the owner's permits-only rule)
Brim runs its own actions (timeout, kick, ban) on hits of a native rule. Discord lets anyone with Manage Server change that rule's keywords, so a member with a Discord permission but no permit could make Brim punish innocent messages. *Avoid:* `native::on_rule_audit` reads the audit entry of every rule create or update (live and replayed, anti-nuke on or off). A change by someone who is not Owner, EO, TA or a `dashboard_edit` permit puts the rule in `automod_rule_untrusted` (migration 3925), and `on_native_hit` skips it until a trusted change. A failed permit lookup counts as untrusted, it runs once per entry inside the D20 inflight guard, and the newest audit id per rule wins, so a replayed or slower older trusted entry cannot clear a newer untrusted one (code review 11-13). Hits that come between the gateway's rule update and its audit entry wait up to 30 s (`native::rule_changed`). The bot's own entries keep the verdict, since its keyword sync rewrites only part of a rule; a dashboard edit marks the rule trusted itself (`lockdown::trust_rule`). A rollback drops an old `ban_sync` section (security review 11-13). Searched: the bot's own keyword rule is synced back by `automod::on_rule_change`; no other Discord-side setting feeds a Brim action.

**The audit-log guard for AutoMod rules misses changes it never sees.** (security check of PR #194, plan permits-only-2 item 1)
`automod_rule_untrusted` is filled only from audit entries. A bot without View Audit Log, more than 1000 entries while it was down, or a change made before the deploy leaves no verdict, so the rule still drives Brim's actions. *Avoid:* the bot acts only on a rule state a trusted person approved (`automod_rule_approved`, migration 3926). `discord_api::rule_fingerprint` hashes everything that decides hits (lists sorted, missing fields read as empty), so the bot's twilight model and Discord's JSON give one hash (test `twilight_and_discord_json_give_one_fingerprint`). Approvals come from a dashboard create, edit or switch (`discord_api::approve_rule` in `lockdown.rs`, with the rule Discord returns; a switch approves only if nothing but `enabled` changed since the stored approval, else the Editor is told to review and save it), a trusted actor's audit entry (`native::on_rule_audit` fetches the rule), and the bot's keyword sync (actor = bot). The bot keeps each rule's latest fingerprint from gateway create and update events and a fetch of the guild's rules on a miss; a failed fetch takes no action. At GuildCreate, while the guild has no done marker, rules without an approval are approved once (`approved_by` 0, the rule-0 row marks the guild done) unless `automod_rule_untrusted` holds them as untrusted. A not-approved hit logs once per rule and fingerprint. The audit guard and its 30 s hold stay as a second layer and to name who changed the rule.

**The first-start rule approval ran at the first hit and stopped on a log failure.** (review of permits-only-2, 11-14)
`first_start` ran from the first native hit, so a rule changed between deploy and that hit was approved in its changed state, and a failed approval log (`?`) skipped the guild's done marker, so the next fetch approved again. *Avoid:* `native::on_guild_create` runs the fetch and the one-time approval while the marker row is missing; a hit only fills the cache. Log failures are warnings, never `?` before a marker or a state write (as in `drop_bots`). A guild that adds the bot later, or comes back after the purge deleted its marker, gets its rules approved once at that GuildCreate (owner decision, test `a_guild_back_after_the_purge_gets_its_rules_approved_once`).

**The keyword sync approved a rule it only partly wrote.** (owner decision, 11-14)
`sync_keyword_rule` sent only the keywords, regex, action and switch, and the bot approved the rule Discord returned. An exempt role or channel or an allow list added in Discord by someone without a permit was so kept and approved by the next sync. *Avoid:* the sync owns every field the fingerprint covers: the full `trigger_metadata` (allow list empty), `exempt_roles` from the roles of permits whose whitelist has `words` or `all` (`automod::word_exempt_roles`: only roles that exist and may carry a permit, at most 20), and `exempt_channels` empty (the word filter has no channel exemption). Only then is approving what Discord returns right. `on_rule_change` compares the whole rule (`rule_matches` folds keyword case, then compares fingerprints), so any other change in Discord is synced back. Permit saves write a config version, whose NOTIFY runs the sync. Discord's exempt roles exempt every holder of the role, while Brim's own filter reads only the member's top permit; the local filter still counts every word (`synced_words_still_count_locally`).

**A trusted audit entry approved a later change.** (review of permits-only-2, 11-14)
`on_rule_audit` cleared the hold (`seen_audit`) before it fetched the rule, and approved whatever the fetch returned, also when a newer gateway update had already changed it. *Avoid:* the approval is written before `seen_audit`, and only when no gateway update of the rule came more than `LATER_MS` (5 s, for gateway delay and clock skew) after the entry's snowflake time; that later change waits for its own entry. A failed fetch is logged in the AutoMod log and approves nothing.

**The rule cache outlived a new gateway session.** (review of permits-only-2, 11-14)
`RULES` and `FILLED` were filled once per process. A new session (not a resume) can miss rule create and update events, so a held fingerprint could be old. *Avoid:* GuildCreate clears the guild's `FILLED` flag and `RULES` entries (under the `FILL` lock), and the next hit or the first-start fetch reads them again.

**Three throwaway servers are three owners.** (Raider List review, 11-11)
Strong evidence from 3 different server owners listed a user by itself. Anyone can make 3 Discord accounts, give each a new server and invite Brim, then get any user listed and banned in every server that enforces the list. Root cause: "different owners" was taken as "independent witnesses" without asking what an owner costs.
*Avoid:* evidence keeps its source server's member count and the time the bot joined it (`Detail.members`, `Detail.bot_joined`), and only servers with `RAIDER_MIN_MEMBERS` members that had the bot for `RAIDER_MIN_DAYS` count; a bot account never lists by itself; and an automatic listing only alerts until the bot owner verifies it (`UNVERIFIED_ALERT_ONLY`). One owner's raid or spam detection makes no entry at all until a second owner flagged the user. security review-11-17: one person can still own three qualifying servers, so `RAIDER_AUTO_OWNERS` is 5 (owner decision). Searched for other counts of sources: Ban Sync counts the users a source shares (a rate limit, not a vote), and the dashboard's owner counts only sort the review queue.

**Filters only saw plain text.** (#101)
Links in `[x](https://..)`, `||spoilers||` or `**bold**`, lookalike letters, zero-width characters, spaced letters (`f u c k`), forwards, polls, user-installed app replies, display names and voice-channel prefixes all got past the filters.
*Avoid:* filter on `filters::urls` (finds links anywhere) and `filters::word_forms` (cured text). When Discord adds a new place for user text, run it through the instant filters too.

**decancer breaks `String += &String`.** (#101)
Its `AddAssign` impl makes `s += &other` ambiguous once decancer is in scope, and the code stops compiling.
*Avoid:* use `push_str`.

**Exemptions that were too wide or too narrow.** (#100)
Whitelisted channels and roles still got base message heat, other bots' slash-command replies counted as webhook spam, and saying "gg" twice in a quiet channel gave a timeout.
*Avoid:* every new heat source checks the whitelist, and quiet-channel heat counts once per user. Add a test with both spammers and normal chatters in one channel.

**A bot rename reads as a new name.** (auto-dehoist, 11-11)
The post-join username filter dedupes on username + global name + nick. Its Rename action changes the nick, so the echo update looked like a new bad name and the bot would rename on every update; a dehoist nick on a member the filter already punished would punish them again.
*Avoid:* for Rename the dedupe key leaves out the nick, and a clean nick counts as done (`joingate::post_join_key`). Auto-dehoist skips a name the filter matched, and its output always starts with a letter or digit, so dehoisting twice changes nothing.

**The verify button judged "suspicious" on other names than Join Gate.** (spammer-flag PR, 11-11)
Join Gate checks the cured username and display name; the verification Start button checked only the raw username. A member caught by the username filter at join (lookalike letters or the display name) was then let through without a captcha in "suspicious only" mode.
*Avoid:* build the name for `is_suspicious` with `joingate::names`. Discord's spammer flag has the same gap: only the join frame carries it, so the Start button asks Discord (`Api::public_flags`) when the flag would turn a Grant into a challenge, and a failed lookup means a captcha.

**Personal heat has no ceiling, so a pool can be filled by one member.** (#187, 11-12)
Removing auto slowmode's member count was argued with "heat punishes one member at 100% first". But `HeatState::apply` never caps: one 20-mention message is 315% alone, and the pool is fed before the punishment gates (grace window, automod whitelist, failed timeouts, strikes off). One member could slow a channel, or every channel.
*Avoid:* a shared pool caps each member's share at max heat (`ChannelHeat.shares`), and its trigger is above one share (`SLOW_PCT` from 200). The pool starts over when max heat or decay changes, or old heat outweighs the new caps. Before relying on "X stops it first", check that X has a ceiling and runs before the thing it guards.

**Some content arrives after the post.** (NSFW image scan, 11-12)
Discord adds link previews and GIF previews in a `MESSAGE_UPDATE` without `edited_timestamp`, and `events.rs` skipped those, so an image filter that only looked at new posts would miss every linked image.
*Avoid:* a filter on images or embeds also reads preview updates (only that filter, not the whole automod run), and is idempotent per message (a unique key), because the same message arrives again as an update, an edit and through the catch-up sweep.

**Text and images in places the filters did not read.** (overlap deep dive g2b, 11-12)
The link, invite, scam and word filters read only content, forwarded content and polls, so a webhook or user-installed app could post a phishing link in an embed or a link button with empty content. The NSFW scan, image heat and QR check skipped forwarded images, and a user-installed app's reply had its attachments dropped because it runs as an edit. Thread and reaction names were checked on raw text, and thread renames not at all. Root cause: each filter collected its own input, and a new carrier was added to some of them only.
*Avoid:* the instant filters read `filter_text` plus `app_text` (embed text and URLs, link buttons and text blocks of webhook and app posts, skipping Discord's previews of links already in the content; a poster's embed is always `rich`, so it is never taken for a preview); heat still counts only `filter_text`, so a feed's long embed is no spam. Images come from `automod::attachments` (own plus forwarded). Forwards add their `rich` embeds and components (a forward does not say who wrote the original). Files can be new when `automod::files_can_be_new`: a member's post, or any webhook or app message, since those (a deferred app reply too) can add files in an edit. A setting that exempts a source from automod (Monitor Webhooks) also stops the image scan, its row and its hit log. Names go through `filters::word_forms` like messages. A thread rename deletes the thread only when the audit log shows its owner renamed it to that name within the last minute (`activity::owner_renamed`); otherwise it is only logged: a `ThreadUpdate` names no actor and also comes for a lock, tags or pins. An edit of a webhook or app message reads only files not seen before (`automod::Seen`, one per check, keyed by (message, attachment) because a forward keeps the attachment ids). A file is marked only once its scan row is saved or its QR image decoded, so a dropped or stopped job is read again. Every webhook or app update (not Brim's own) goes through `on_edit` when its filtered text (content plus `app_text`) or its file list is new for that message (`automod::new_text`, a bounded map keyed by a per-process random hash, since a fixed-key hash can be collided to pass a bad edit), so an image-only fill gets its scan and QR read; otherwise only the preview image scan runs; a LOADING mark misses button updates and races the create. A server-installed app's reply stays exempt from punishment, but its image hit is logged under `interaction_metadata.user`, and the raider hold checks that user too. A message caused by a button click (`automod::clicked`, user- or server-installed app) punishes nobody: the clicker did not write it, so the filters only delete and log it, and the raider hold and the NSFW credit skip it. The NSFW "has new files" check counts only files the scan would queue (`nsfw::scannable`). A flagged rename is logged once per (thread, name).

**An exemption checked after the count, or not on every path.** (overlap deep dive g2b, 11-12)
Native AutoMod scopes counted a whitelisted member's hits and checked the whitelist only when acting, so their hits filled a server scope for the next member. A bad emoji name in a reaction, and a bad thread name, timed out an automod-permit holder, while the same word in a message is exempt.
*Avoid:* check the exemption before anything is counted or deleted, and use the same exemption on every path of one filter (`activity::words_exempt` = words or automod whitelist, as messages get). The mention lockdown counted whitelisted members' pings in full the same way (h4): it now counts only pings the mention, @everyone and automod whitelists do not cover.

**A failed step returned before the next protective one.** (overlap deep dive g2b, 11-12)
A failed strike timeout returned before the strike-cap lockdown, and a failed case did the same. Native rule actions and reaction or thread heat timeouts failed with only a trace line. *Avoid:* the same as "A failed log must not stop protection": keep the error, run the remaining protective steps, return it last, and report a failed action with `action_failed`. Every path that records a strike runs `automod::strike_cap_lock` (messages, reactions and threads, and a warn threshold's timeout). Searched: `filter_action` stops after a failed ban, but nothing protective follows there.

**A done marker written before the action.** (overlap deep dive g2b, 11-12)
The NSFW scan row was saved as a hit before automod acted. A failed action left the row, and the unique key then made every later pass (preview update, edit, sweep) skip the message for good. *Avoid:* a marker that makes later passes skip work stands only once the work succeeded. Mark the errors that come after the punishment landed (`automod::Punished`; a saved strike counts as landed) and free it on every other error, so a brief DB error before the punishment is retried, while a failed case, log, lockdown or strike timeout after it never punishes twice. Free the whole unit a pass checks: the sweep skips a message with any row, so every row of the message goes, and its attachment ids are forgotten.

**An early return in front of an unrelated protection.** (overlap deep dive g2b, 11-12)
Heat panic's raider hold sat after "automod off", so with automod off raiders posted freely. The catch channel's test mode returned "handled", so automod skipped every message there. A restore job with no `joined_at` and a `?` on the member lookup gave main roles to a rejoined unverified member, and retried for a member who left. *Avoid:* before an early return, check that everything below it belongs to the switch or case it tests. A delayed role add carries its membership and treats 404 as done (`http_err::member`), and skips a member whose roles are held for verification (`sticky::pending`). A job that gives roles back to a quarantined member adds them to the quarantine's `saved_roles` instead of dropping them, so the release returns them.

**An exemption that covered more than it was for.** (overlap deep dive h4, 11-13)
The automod permit (which Auto Setup gives staff) and a webhook whose bot owner is on the anti-nuke whitelist (PluralKit, Tupperbox) returned before every filter, so a hacked mod or a relayed post could send a scam link, an NSFW link or image, or an invite untouched. The catch channel let any permit holder write. Root cause: an exemption meant for spam heat sat in front of the whole punishment path.
*Avoid:* say what an exemption is for and cut there, and check which levels a helper covers: `App::whitelisted` is true for Owner, Extra Owners and Trusted Admins too, and the first fix punished them; they stay exempt (`Level::immune`). A custom permit keeps only the scam, malicious and NSFW parts (`automod::UNEXEMPT`), acted on whatever their heat weight, and its exempt spam takes no punishment claim (the claim comes after the permit check); an exempt webhook still has any instant-filter hit (`INSTANT`) deleted and logged; the catch channel exempts only Owner, Extra Owners and Trusted Admins (`Level::immune`). Searched: native rule hits and reaction/thread names keep the permit exemption (those are word and spam filters).

**A burst guard that also softened a different punishment.** (overlap deep dive h4, 11-13)
The 10 s grace claim after a heat punishment turned a scam or NSFW hit whose filter says Ban into a plain delete, and the crowd's similarity part tied with the scam part and won the "biggest part" pick (`max_by` keeps the last of equal values). Root cause: a guard against punishing one burst twice was applied to every punishment, and a tie went to whatever was pushed last.
A first fix let instant hits skip the window, which multiplied a burst of scam messages into one warn or ban per message, and a per-message "done" marker checked before acting and set after let two passes over one message both punish, and blocked a Ban after a strike on the same message. *Avoid:* rank punishments (delete < warn < timeout/strike < kick < ban) and, within the window per member (`HeatState::claim_rank`) and per (message, author) (`automod::ACTED`), act only on a stronger one; claim before acting, under the lock, and give the claim back on an error that is not `Punished`, only while the stored claim is still this pass's own (compare and restore), so a stronger claim another pass made meanwhile stays. A tie in `top_part` goes to the first part, and `heat` adds every instant part (blacklisted words too) before the spam parts.

**Handing a filter to Discord dropped what did not fit.** (overlap deep dive h4, 11-13)
"Use Discord AutoMod for words" switched the local word filter off for messages, while the synced rule silently left out words over 60 characters, words past 1000 and regex past 10 or 260 characters, and a failed sync was only a trace line. Two later tries to skip only the words Discord held still failed open: Discord's rule never sees webhook, app, forward or poll text, exempts admins, and can be edited or deleted in Discord, and an in-memory "synced" flag does not survive a restart. *Avoid:* a message that reached the bot was not blocked by Discord, so the local filter always reads every word (with lookalikes); the Discord rule is extra, never a reason to read less. The sync still runs on each save, alerts the automod log when it fails, and syncs the rule back when someone changes or deletes it in Discord (once a minute at most). Thread and reaction names use the same local list, so "Migrate words" only turns the sync on and keeps the lists. The same root cause hit reactions and threads: with the auto timeouts off, `activity::heat_up` skipped the strike and the strike-cap lockdown that messages still record.

**Two settings that together meant "do less".** (overlap deep dive h4, 11-13)
A filter action Timeout without its own duration hands the length to the strikes; with the strikes' auto timeouts off that became a delete only. *Avoid:* when a setting defers to another system, check what happens when that system is off. `automod::own_action` uses the strike timeout length then.

**Unrolling a short link visited the attacker's server.** (overlap deep dive h4, 11-13)
The shortener lookup followed redirects, so the target's server saw the bot's address (not behind Cloudflare), could answer the bot differently from members, and could hold the check for up to 12 s. It also ran only with invites or crawl on, while bad sites and NSFW links read its result. *Avoid:* a HEAD to a known shortener host with redirects off, read `Location` only, and ask again only while it points at another shortener (3 hops at most), within 12 s for the whole message; the final host is never contacted (`Sites::unshorten`). A client that does not build means no unrolling, never a default client that follows redirects. Run it for every filter that reads the target host.

**One shared queue let one server starve the others.** (overlap deep dive h4, 11-13)
The NSFW scan's lanes were FIFO across all servers, and a full backlog dropped the oldest job of any server. *Avoid:* a queue shared by servers takes turns per server (`Scanner::take` serves the server served longest ago) and drops or moves from the server with the most jobs (`most_jobs`, for a full backlog and a full live lane alike). The per-member cap (4 live images) was already in.
**A DM into the server skipped the server's protections.** (11-13)
Ticket DMs are relayed into a staff channel, but they were never read by automod, and new tickets opened during a raid or lockdown and without any limit, so a raid could flood staff with scam links and slurs through the bot. Root cause: the filters hang off guild `MessageCreate`, and a DM has no guild.
*Avoid:* text that the bot carries into a server goes through that server's filters. `tickets::dm::filtered` runs the word (local list, Discord's AutoMod never sees a DM), scam, phishing and invite filters before a ticket opens or a message is relayed; new tickets wait during a raid or lock (`lockdown::raid_or_lock`, shared with auto-dehoist) and are capped at 3 per member and 20 per server per hour. A quarantined member opens a ticket only when given no reason (automatic quarantines, or staff with an empty reason; owner decision, round 2), since tickets are then the only way they learn why; a staff quarantine with a reason gets a short refusal (`tickets::dm::quarantined_with_reason`, counting only cases since the current quarantine row began, so an old lifted case does not block): the staff intro says "Quarantined", and a quarantine DM without a reason says they can DM Brim while tickets are on (`reply::reach_staff`). Automatic quarantines (anti-nuke, rate limits, the Raider List, a rejoin) sent no DM at all; they now queue a `quarantine_dm` job (`reply::queue_quarantine_dm`), so the DM never holds back the containment or the revert, and queue none during a join or voice raid, for a bot, or for a join quarantine. The DM is also skipped during a lock or raid and is sent at most once per member per 24 h (a `quarantine_dm_cooldown` marker job), so a re-quarantine loop cannot spam a member. Not covered: shortener lookups and attachments.

**A raid outcome was assumed, not checked.** (11-13, round 2)
`joingate::on_join` skipped the Join Gate and verification once the raid's action *would* remove the joiner, so a raid ban that Discord refused left the joiner in the server with no quarantine, and a raid set to Kick cancelled a Join Gate Ban, so the raider could rejoin at once. *Avoid:* decide on what a punishment did, not what it was meant to do: `punish_or_retry` returns whether the member was removed, and the gate still bans after a raid kick.

**A judgement on role permissions read the live roles during a role lock.** (11-13, round 2)
A role lock strips permissions from roles, so a saved Administrator role looked harmless and `t!verify` could free it. *Avoid:* judge a role by its unlocked permissions (`lockdown::unlocked_role_perms`, the oldest lock snapshot wins over the live role). The rejoin grace for an autorole bot  covers any number of bot changes in its 60 s, but only roles with no dangerous permission and no permit, read live (a failed lookup or a role Discord does not list counts as not plain; the cached guild info missed a role created a moment before); anything else quarantines the bot as before. A single-use grace (round 2) brought back the false positive for a second autorole or level bot.

**A startup catch-up read one member per row.** (R2, 11-13, round 2)
`requarantine_missed` did a member GET for each `quarantines` row at every GuildCreate, in the bucket that kicks and role edits share, and rows from members who left pile up. *Avoid:* read members in pages (`GET /guilds/{g}/members?limit=1000`, its own bucket), and put the check off while a raid or lock is on, in one `requarantine_missed` job per guild (`jobs::replace`; `enqueue` started a chain per GuildCreate) and for 30 minutes at most, then run anyway (a GuildCreate takes the first wait from the pending job, so a re-identify does not restart the 30 minutes; `lockdown::Deferred`). The job is in `jobs::HEAVY`: it pages members and would hold back unbans and raid retries in the serial light worker. A failed run queues the job too, and runs take a per-guild lock, so a restart's job next to GuildCreate never quarantines or logs one rejoin twice (the second run sees the first's action_log rows). It compares Discord's join time with a DB time only with a 30 s margin, since the two clocks differ.

**Another bot's role was judged per call, and only by its id.** (11-13, round 2)
A bot giving two verification roles in two calls made each look like proof the member was verified already, and a bot role with View Channel on the verified-only channels skipped verification just the same. *Avoid:* note a bot's grants, wait 1.5 s, and judge one member at a time (`verify::given`); take back any added role that opens what a verification role opens and @everyone lacks (View Channel, or Send Messages where @everyone only reads, by base permission or overwrite) or holds Administrator. Round 3: only overwrites were checked, so the usual setup (@everyone without View at server level, the verified role with it) let any "Member" autorole verify. A bot's member overwrite opening such a channel is removed, and GuildCreate reads the audit log for grants made while Brim was down (`given::catch_up`). Every role change used to cost a user and a member read in the member bucket: a per-guild candidate set (60 s), a cached bot flag and a remembered verified member now come first. Round 4: a cache that skips a security check must be cleared by the events that change its answer, and must not be fed by the actor it guards against. The candidate set is dropped in `invalidate_guild` and on role and channel audit events; a member counts as verified only from a verification role no bot gave just now (`on_overwrite` noted any role, so a bot giving the role and an overwrite together made its later grants skip for 10 minutes), and an un-verify forgets them. `catch_up` reads removals too (Brim's own take-back costs no member read), skips a member that fails, waits out a raid or lock and retries a failed audit read in a heavy job (`verify_catch_up`), and logs when it hits its 500-entry cap. A human was exempt for being human, so Discord's Manage Roles alone could verify anyone; under the owner's 13:26 rule (exemptions only from permits and Owner/EO/TA) a verification role given by a human without a verify or quarantine permit is taken back too, logged, nobody punished (owner decision, 13:53), live and in `catch_up` for grants made while Brim was down. Round 5: a cache filled by slow reads must not outlive an invalidation that landed during the reads (a per-guild generation in `GATES`; a gate read across a forget is not stored); Brim's own lock edits only shorten the gate to 3 s and its own slowmode keeps it, so a lock over every channel does not rebuild it per joiner, while another actor's edit still drops it at once. A permit-less human cannot give an unverified member a channel-opening role either, and a failed permit lookup takes the role back (fail closed, staff told). `catch_up` retries a run that skipped an actor or member, takes a per-guild lock, runs beside the heavy worker (spawned, like `requarantine_missed`, so neither holds back `panic_start` or `nuke_revert`), and tells staff about its 500-entry cap only after a downtime it may have cut (`bot_seen`, written every minute). Round 6: a gateway event names no actor, so softening every one let an attacker's edit live 3 s on the old gate; `invalidate_guild` forgets again; only a gateway event about a role or channel Brim marked just before editing it softens (`given::mark_own(gid, id)` in lock, unlock and panic; scoped to the object since a guild-wide mark let anyone open the window by triggering auto slowmode, which needs no mark), and the audit arm drops the gate before anti-nuke runs. A read across a soften is stored with the short life. During a channel lock the gate judges the lock snapshot's saved overwrites (`lockdown::unlocked_channels`), so the Send gate holds while the lock lasts; panic's saved @everyone overwrites are put back the same way (`panic::unexempted`). A self-retrying job counts its failed tries in the payload and gives up after 5 with a staff log; a spawned job's row is finished only on Ok, so the lease and attempt limit apply to errors. A failed lookup or removal is tried once more, then staff hear of it. The hold steps aside only where it acts (a join quarantine, or Quarantine Hold on); a Quarantine Whitelist actor is judged here. Not covered: a channel opened only by two roles together, a member overwrite given while Brim was down, grants older than the newest 500 role changes.

**Renaming the bot would have made a second Discord AutoMod rule.** (Rename to Brim, 2026-11-20)
The bot finds its own keyword rule in Discord by its name ("TommyBot keywords"). With only the name changed, the next sync finds no rule, makes a new one in every server, and the old rule keeps blocking the old word list.
*Avoid:* anything the bot finds by a name it gave (Discord AutoMod rules, channels, roles, webhooks) also matches the old name after a rename (`discord_api::is_keyword_rule`), and the sync writes the new name. Prefer a stored id over a name. Old exported files keep their internal ids (message kit `tommybot-messages`) so they still import.

## 4. Parsing and limits

**The message cache grew with the number of servers.** (audit, 11-17)
The cache had a 16 MB text budget per server, no total, and did not count map and queue memory, and it kept the text of servers with no message log. 300 servers could hold about 5 GB. *Avoid:* an in-memory store per server also has a total cap (`cache::MAX_TOTAL_BYTES`, 512 MB; over it the biggest server loses its oldest first), counts its per-entry overhead (`OVERHEAD`), and holds data only where a feature reads it (`Logger::handle` caches only while a message edit or delete log is set, and an edit while it is off drops the cached copy, which would be stale when the log is on again).

**Parsed words lost their typed form or their limit.** (audit, 11-17)
`note remove 12345m` was read as a duration and `012345` as the number 12345, so those note ids could not be removed. `sanitize` could cure a 32-character name to more than 32 (㎏ -> kg), which Discord refuses. `COMMAND -h` sent a help embed with no cooldown for members without a permit. *Avoid:* an id that is free text takes the raw word (`modcmds::word_after`); a cured name is cut to 32 characters after curing; every reply path for members without a permit passes `cooled_down`.
**A member without a permit could make the bot search for and answer hundreds of names.** (audit, 11-17)
`commands::run` looked up each typed name and replied once per miss before any permit check, and automod waits for that loop. *Avoid:* the invoker's permit perm is checked before any lookup, at most `MAX_NAMES` (10) names are looked up, and the missed names get one reply.
**MCP `logs_tail` filtered before it masked, and read whole files.** (audit, 11-17)
`contains` matched the raw line and `redact` ran after it, so `contains: "postgres://user:a"` told a masked password apart one character at a time. Each hourly file was also read whole, and log files rotate by time only. *Avoid:* mask first, then filter (`tools::tail`); read at most the last 64 MB of a file (`read_end`) and say in the answer that older lines were not read. That notice is a line too, so the stop test is `>= n` plus a truncate (review, 11-17).
**A new range clamped stored values the old code allowed, and the dashboard named the wrong used value.** (audit, 11-17)
The first webhook multiplier range was 1-10, so a stored 0.5 (less heat for webhooks, allowed by `heat_ok`) read as 1.0 at once. `verification.time_limit_ms` and `filters.nsfw_threshold` are clamped on read but were not in `limits::CLAMPED_ON_READ`, so `stored_outside` showed a stored 5000 without "(used as 10000)". *Avoid:* a range's lower end is not above the lowest value the old validation took; a path clamped on read goes into `CLAMPED_ON_READ`.

**A stored prefix with a space fell back to `t!`.** (audit, 11-17)
The first fix read any prefix that failed `prefix_ok` as `t!`, but a stored `"tb "` worked: the bot strips it and splits the rest by whitespace. The API also refused it on every save of the section. *Avoid:* only a blank stored prefix reads as `t!`; `api::validate` checks the prefix only when it changed.

**Numbers that switched their feature off at 0 had no range, and one range was never checked.** (audit, 11-17)
