A verification time limit of 0 kicked every joiner at once, a strike expiry or reoffend window of 0 reset every strike to 1, `auto_lockdown.strikes` 0 locked the server on anyone's first strike, `panic.duration_secs` above `i64::MAX` wrapped and ended panic at once, `warn_base_points` 0 made warns add nothing, and a huge webhook multiplier tripped every webhook message. Import, MCP and the API took them, since `validate` only parses the type. `filters.nsfw_threshold` had a range only in `for_path`'s match arm, which the form shows but `check` never walks. *Avoid:* a number at a fixed path goes into `limits::FIXED` (save, import, MCP, prefix panel and `stored_outside` all use it), a float into `float_for_path` and `ranged_paths`; a value an old config can hold outside the range is also clamped on read (`VerifyCfg::time_limit_ms`, `HeatCfg::clamped`). `stored_outside` lists every section that has a range (backups and verification were missing).

**An empty prefix made every message a command.** (audit, 11-17)
The prefix panel needs 1-32 bytes, but the dashboard API, import and MCP saved `""` or `" !"`. With `""`, `strip_prefix` matched every message, so members got answers to "help" or "ban". *Avoid:* `sections::prefix_ok` (1-32 characters, no whitespace) in `api::validate` and the panel, and `MiscCfg` reads a blank stored prefix as `t!`.

**The Join Gate spammer card took the Rename action.** (audit, 11-17)
`spammer` had no `no_rename`, so a flagged spammer only got a new nickname, and the test loop listed six filters, not seven. *Avoid:* every account filter except the username filter has `#[serde(deserialize_with = "no_rename")]`, and the test lists every filter.

**The backups section had no range check.** (audit, 11-17)
`max_images` had no upper bound (automatic backups have no other cleanup), and an `interval_hours` above `i32::MAX` wrapped to a negative interval, so a backup ran every tick. *Avoid:* `limits::FIXED` has `backups.max_images` (1-50) and `backups.interval_hours` (1-168), and `due` converts with `try_from` instead of `as i32`. A number a section saves gets a `FIXED` range.
**Ticket snippets and the blacklist had no count cap.** (review, owner decision 11-17)
Holders of the weak Manage Tickets permit could add snippets and blacklist entries without end, and each add writes a full config version. *Avoid:* one rule in core (`sections::tickets_cap_problem`, `MAX_SNIPPETS` 100, `MAX_BLOCKED` 1000), run by the bot's ticket commands on the locked config inside `settings::save` (so two adds at once cannot both pass) and by the dashboard's `validate` (save, import, rollback). Only a new entry is refused: lists stored above the cap keep working and can be edited or shortened. The dashboard shows "N of cap" from the schema (`cap`), not a UI copy.

**A huge duration wrapped negative.** (audit, 11-17)
`duration::parse` took any value that fit an i64, so `/ban duration:106751991167300d` made `now + d` wrap and the ban ended at once. *Avoid:* the shared parser caps every duration at a year (`duration::MAX_SECS`, the filter action duration's cap; the 28-day timeout cap would refuse the renewed long timeouts), and `too_long` lets each caller say so (`duration::TOO_LONG`) instead of reading the word as a name. A lock keeps its own 30-day cap. Refusals also name the missing permit ("you lack the lockdown permit"), and the ticket blacklist refuses role and channel mentions (`user_ids`).
**A saved message could hold control characters.** (audit, 11-17)
`embeds::limits` checked lengths and shapes but not the characters, so U+0001 (the preview's own placeholder marker) could be saved through the API or MCP and used to forge preview HTML. *Avoid:* `limits` refuses any C0 control character or DEL other than `\n`, `\r` and `\t` in every text of the message and names the path (`embeds/1/author/name`).

**A member's text closed the log's code block or spoiler.** (audit, 11-17)
`diff` broke a fence with `replace("```", ...)`, which leaves a real fence in a run of 4, 5 or 7 backticks, so an edited or deleted message could close its diff block and write fake lines (a forged "Responsible") into the staff log. The report logs had the same bug with `replace("||", ...)` and a run of three `|`. *Avoid:* break every character of the delimiter (`` ` `` and `|`), not the delimiter string; the test tries runs of 3 to 8 backticks.

**A ticket member could write a staff line into the transcript.** (audit, 11-17)
`history` put the message text into `[time] Name (Role): text` lines without touching newlines, so a DM holding `\n[2026-11-03 09:10:00] Mod (Staff): ...` became a staff line in the stored transcript and on the dashboard page. *Avoid:* `tickets::staff::line` indents every continuation line, and the dashboard parses only lines that start with `[`.

**A log embed could pass 6000 characters, and a change to a new permission showed an empty "Changes".** (audit, 11-17)
`Log::build` did not count the author line toward Discord's 6000 total, so a report with a long author and a full description was refused and the log lost. `perm_diff` used `from_bits_truncate`, so a change to only bits twilight does not know (bit 48) gave no names under the heading. *Avoid:* every text part of an embed counts toward the total; `perm_names` names unknown bits as `Bit N`.

**A lock could run for years.** (audit, 11-17)
A lock duration had no upper bound, so a typo (`999999d`) queued an unlock far past any use. *Avoid:* `lock_marked` caps the duration at 30 days (`MAX_LOCK_SECS`).

**Id 0 parsed as a target.** (audit, 11-17)
The prefix parser and `info` accepted `0` and `<@0>` as ids, which then reached Discord as a request for a user that cannot exist. *Avoid:* both parsers drop id 0. A test covers users, roles and channels.

**`info` showed a member's moderation details to everyone.** (audit, 11-17)
`user_info` printed cases, notes and flags for any caller. *Avoid:* for a caller with no permit asking about someone else, it shows only account age, "In Server" and the join date (owner decision, 11-17).

**A warn whose auto action failed said nothing.** (audit, 11-17)
`apply_dm` dropped the error from `apply_warn_actions`, so the reply said the warn worked while the timeout or kick it should have caused never happened. *Avoid:* the error is logged and the reply adds "Warn saved, but the auto action failed".

**`/warn` gave 1 point, the chat command the server's base points.** (audit, 11-17)
The slash parser filled a missing `points` option with 1, while `t!warn`, confirmations, reports and automod use `misc.warn_base_points` (default 5), so the same warn reached the auto-punish thresholds at different times. *Avoid:* an optional value that has a server setting stays `None` until the call site reads the setting. Searched: every other warn path already uses the setting.

**Byte slicing panics on non-ASCII.** (e7edf56)
Duration parsing sliced off the last character by byte and panicked on input like `5å`. Converting large values to milliseconds overflowed.
*Avoid:* split on `char` boundaries (`chars`, `char_indices`), use checked or saturating maths. Never `&s[..n]` on user input.

**`Id::new` panics on 0.** (voice raid pings, 11-12)
Join Raid read its Warned Roles (strings in the config) with `r.parse.ok.map(Id::new)`, so a stored `"0"` (an import or a hand edit) panicked the join task before the raid log and the punishments. Root cause: a parsed number is not a valid snowflake.
*Avoid:* turn parsed or stored numbers into ids with `Id::new_checked` and skip `None`. Both raid triggers now read the roles through `joingate::warned_roles`. Searched: `verify::guild_of` parses the guild from a button's custom id the same way (the bot writes those ids), now also `new_checked`; the rest of the `Id::new` calls take ids Discord or the database already gave.

**Limits drifted between places.** (#134)
The prefix panels, the dashboard and the save path each had their own bounds, and an out-of-range value was changed silently.
*Avoid:* all bounds live in `core::limits` and are used everywhere. A value out of range is refused with a message.

**Heat max and decay had no range outside the dashboard.** (auto slowmode review, 11-11)
Only the dashboard's `heat_ok` checked them, and it let decay 0 through. The `heat` prefix panel took any number (`4 1000` saved a decay of 10, so `(1 - 10)^t` made every heat NaN; `3 0` made every message trip), and the bot used the values as read. Decay 0 meant heat never cooled, so an auto slowmode channel was never calm. Panic mode's `heat_decay` had no range either.
*Avoid:* `HEAT_MAX` and `HEAT_DECAY` live in `automod::heat` and go through `core::limits::float_for_path` (dashboard, import and MCP saves, the prefix panel's `Kind::Num` rows), and the bot clamps when it reads (`HeatCfg::clamped` in `AutomodCfg`'s deserializer, the panic decay in `add_heat`). A number the bot divides by, raises to a power or waits on gets a range in `limits` and a clamp on read.

**A URL checked by prefix lets look-alike hosts through.** (MCP security review, 2026-11-10)
The MCP OAuth registration took any `https://` redirect and checked localhost with `starts_with("http://localhost")`, which `http://localhost.evil.com` also passes. A stranger could register their own site, send the owner a login link and get the code, and with it a token for everything the MCP reads.
*Avoid:* parse the URL (`reqwest::Url`) and compare scheme and host exactly against an allow list. OAuth redirects go only to the exact callbacks in `MCP_OAUTH_REDIRECTS` or the owner's own computer.

**A refresh token that issues a fresh one with a full lifetime never expires.** (MCP security review, 2026-11-10)
Each MCP refresh gave a new 30-day refresh token, so a stolen one could be renewed forever and only rotating `MCP_OAUTH_SECRET` stopped it.
*Avoid:* carry the login time (`auth`) through every refresh and cap the new token at login time plus the limit.

**A zero quietly switched a protection off.** (11-12) Follow-up (11-13): `panic.executors` 0 stays "off" on purpose, and its dashboard field now says "(0 = off)". Zeros stored before the limits are left as they are; Overview's security score lists them (`limits::stored_outside`, row "Settings outside their range") so the owner sees which protection is off.
Strike cap, multiplier, strike durations, mention lock count and window, the mention hour cap (0 stays allowed: any mention trips), panic window and heat trigger, Join Raid count, span and ID matches, ban purge days and warn points had ranges only in the prefix panels, so the dashboard, import and MCP could save `panic.window_secs = 0` or `mention_window_ms = 0` and the feature never fired. *Avoid:* the ranges live in `core::limits` (`FIXED`, `for_path`), the prefix panels use the same constants, and rollback runs the same `validate`, `limits::check` and `web_mode_error` as a save (it skipped them). `panic.executors` stays out: 0 means off. Values saved before stay as they are (no clamp on read yet).

**The dashboard validated a section with a smaller type than the bot reads.** (11-12) `verification` was checked as `VerifyCfg`, but the bot parses `VerificationCfg` (also `channel`, `roles`), so a bad role id was saved and the bot read the whole section as default (off). *Avoid:* a section type both sides parse lives in `brim_core::sections` and the dashboard validates with that exact type.

**A range check refused a whole rollback.** (11-13) Rollback ran the save's `limits::check`, so one old value outside today's range (`joinraid.count` 800) blocked the rollback a cleanup after a hacked admin needs. *Avoid:* a rollback calls `limits::clamp` first, which moves a number to the nearest end of its range and returns `section.path: old -> new`; the rollback logs and returns those (`clamped`, shown in the toast). Saves and imports still refuse, so a user typing a value learns the range. A non-number is still refused.

**A lower limit makes old values look broken.** (#191, 11-13)
Auto slowmode's maximum dropped from 6 hours to 2 minutes. A server with an older 21600 got the Overview warning "Settings outside their range", which says a 0 value switches the protection off, although the bot reads that card clamped and the value still works at 120.
*Avoid:* `limits::stored_outside` marks values the bot clamps on read (`CLAMPED_ON_READ`) with `(used as N)`. When a card gets a `clamped` on read, add its prefix there; when a range shrinks, check what Overview and the form say about older values.

**Image decoding had a per-message limit but no global one.** (security audit, 11-16)
The QR check read up to 3 images per message on blocking threads, with no cap across messages, so a flood of image posts could run hundreds of 128 MB decodes at once. *Avoid:* a global semaphore (`qr::DECODE`, 2 slots) taken after the download (a slow host holds up no other server) and moved into the blocking task. A flood of images in one server can still slow QR checks in others; that flood is the heat system's job (owner decision 11-16), and skipping the check when busy would be a bypass. A deleted message kept its place in that queue (owner decision 11-16): MessageDelete, bulk deletes and channel or thread deletes now mark it (`qr::deleted`, before any await in `events::run`), and no download or decode starts for it; a decode already running finishes and its codes still count, so a scam deleted at once is still acted on. Searched: the profile image decode already has one; the NSFW scan runs in a fixed set of workers.

**A full process-wide map was cleared whole.** (security review-11-17, t-cap-map-clear)
Heat, ping windows, reaction-spam windows, scam-hit counts, live captchas, the spammer-flag cache, trigger cooldowns, anti-nuke heat, fight and take-back counts, and the "already acted on this message" sets were cleared whole past their cap, so a flood in one server wiped every other server's detection state (heat restarted, captchas being solved went invalid, a message could be punished twice). *Avoid:* past the cap, after dropping idle entries, `caps::drop_biggest` drops only the server (or rule) holding the most; maps keyed by message id drop their older half (`caps::newer_half`; by an id that is a snowflake, never a 0 placeholder). A restart counts the size this hour's log file already has. Caches that only cost a lookup (levels, thread parents) may still clear whole.

**A feed item without an id got a new random id at every read.** (feeds F1, 11-20, found while building)
The feed parser makes a random UUID for an RSS item that has no `<guid>`, so the seen list never matched it and the item would have been posted again at every check. *Avoid:* `feed::parse` sets its own id generator (the item's first link, else its title); a test checks that an item with no guid has its link as its id.

**A fetch of an address staff typed is a request into the internal network.** (feeds F1, 11-20, by design)
A feed address comes from staff, so a plain client would fetch `https://169.254.169.254/` or a name that resolves to 10.x. *Avoid:* `feed::client` resolves through `PublicOnly` (each DNS answer checked by `is_public`), has no proxy (a proxy resolves the name itself, past that check), allows https on port 443 only, no IP literals and no user name in the address, checks every redirect hop with the same rules (`feed::safe`, at most 3), and stops at 10 s and 2 MB. Errors name the cause in plain words, never the inner address.

**A feed poller lost, doubled and stuck items.** (feeds F1, 11-20, code review)
The poller saved the ETag before it handled the feeds, so a failed post was hidden by the next 304; a database write after Discord took the post failed the post, so the item went out again; a 400 from Discord broke the feed and the same item failed at every Check now; Check now on a paused feed posted; a good check never turned a broken feed on; a click during a check was wiped. *Avoid:* conditional-request headers are saved only when every feed on that address finished; bookkeeping after an outside side effect (a post, a DM) never fails that action; an item Discord refuses with 400 is remembered (skipped) and the feed still breaks; a paused feed only does a test post; a request flag is cleared only if it is still the value the run answered. `is_public` also refuses `::/96`, site-local `fec0::/10`, `64:ff9b:1::/48` and Teredo `2001::/32` (security review). Automod first trusted every message from a Brim webhook, but anyone with the Discord permission Manage Webhooks can read its token and post past automod: now it skips only the message ids Brim got back from its own sends (`feeds::sent_by_us`, which waits up to 5 s while a send in that channel is under way), and checks every other post and every edit (security review).

**Hourly log files had no size cap.** (security review-11-17, t-lg-disk)
A storm of logged errors could grow one hour's file to gigabytes on the volume Postgres shares. *Avoid:* `log::Capped` stops each hourly file at 100 MB with one notice line (stdout, capped by Docker, still has every line); 24 files keep the total bounded.

**An entry's link fields were never filled, and an empty one made Discord refuse the message.** (feeds F4, 11-20, found while building)
`botmsg::render` fills text only, never `url` or `icon_url` (so member text cannot become a link), so an edited feed entry with `{image}` as its picture or `{link}` as its title link sent the literal placeholder; and a template link whose variable was empty (a GitHub event with no page) sent `"url": ""`, which Discord refuses for the whole message (the feed then stops). *Avoid:* feed entries fill their link fields with the feed's link variables only (`fill_links`; the sender checked them as web links), and anything that is then not an http(s) link is emptied; `render` drops every empty embed, author, footer, image and thumbnail link for all entries.

## 5. Database and migrations

- **One flag held "staff switched it off" and "waits for its open time".** (forms review, 2026-11-23) The open job set `forms.open` back on at `opens_at`, also for a form staff had switched off after they set the time. *Avoid:* a job that turns a thing on checks that a person did not turn it off; `forms::update` drops `opens_at` when the form is saved off, so the job finds no match.
- **An "on" default for a switch that acts on members would start removing roles unseen.** (owner decision on Remove dangerous roles, 11-23) Config sections use `#[serde(default)]` with the struct's `Default`, so a new default reaches every saved config that lacks the key at the next bot start, and every server with no saved config at once (`GuildCfg::default`). For `misc.enforce_permits` that takes dangerous roles from staff without a permit, also on a just-added server (the first permit sync marks it migrated even with no permits). *Avoid:* the bot default stays off; "on by default" lives where a person confirms: the wizard card presets the switch on (`Card::preset`), Overview shows a row with a Fix while it is not saved on, and every save that turns it on asks first with the live list from `/enforce-permits` (`form::ask_enforce`). That list uses the bot's rule (`antinuke::enforce_permits`): not a bot, no level in `permits::holders`, a role below Brim that is not managed and holds `DANGEROUS_PERMS` with a Role lock's saved bits added. Earlier defaults (`always_dm`, migration 2300; the verification fail action, 4000) used a migration; use one only where the change cannot take something from members.
- **A lookup by Discord message id had no index.** (builder report on the undo buttons, 11-19) `message_sends` is read by `posted_id` on every press of a saved message's menu or button (`msgmenu`, the sticky cleanup), but only `embed_id`, `guild_id` and `webhook_id` were indexed, so each press read the whole Sent log of every server. **Rule:** a table read from an interaction gets an index on the id the interaction carries, in the migration that adds the read (4039 adds `message_sends_posted`).
- **A wrong "left" mark would have purged a server the bot is still in.** (security review, 11-18) `on_ready` marks every server missing from READY as left, and the hourly purge deleted its data 30 days later without asking Discord. One shard's READY lists every server, but a sharded bot's READY does not. *Avoid:* `welcome::purge_removed` asks Discord (GET guild) about every marked server each hour: an answer clears the mark and keeps the data, 404 or 403 with code 50001 purges once due, any other error (a Cloudflare 403, a 5xx) waits an hour (test: `a_wrongly_marked_server_is_not_purged`).
**A config read error skipped an audit entry's guards and count.** (audit, 11-17)
`on_audit_as` used `app.cfg(..)?`, and the cache is empty after each save, `relink` and a listener reconnect. The entry was marked handled with no count and no `action_log` row. *Avoid:* `App::cfg_or_last` falls back to the last config loaded (kept through `forget`, and set only by a read that is not stale), else after one retry returns Err with the defaults: the guards then undo with nobody punished, and the action is saved with `counted` 0 (`log_uncounted`) so `/revert` can undo it, and staff are told. The nuke run and its later passes use the same fallback.
**A restore into a new Postgres stopped on the MCP role.** (audit, 11-17)
`pg_dump` writes the grants to `tommybot_mcp` but not the role or its `ALTER ROLE ... SET`, so `restore-test.sh` (`ON_ERROR_STOP=1`) always failed, and a real restore into a new server lost the read-only setting; the migration that makes the role is already recorded and never runs again. *Avoid:* anything a migration makes outside the schema (roles, role settings) is made again by `db::set_mcp_password` on every start with `MCP_DB_PASSWORD` set (settings and connection limit too), and a restore creates the role first. restore-test.sh runs the exact DEPLOY.md restore (schema replaced in one transaction) and checks the role's grants.

**A ticket lookup ran for every message in any category.** (audit, 11-17)
`ticket_here` queried `tickets` for every plain message in a channel with a parent, also on servers without panels. *Avoid:* return before the query when the channel is not in the ticket category and the server has no panels.
**Member lookups on scheduled_jobs scanned the table, and fake report ids collided.** (audit, 11-17)
Every member update with "Remove Main Roles on TO" on ran a `payload->>'user'` DELETE with no index. Native AutoMod reports used seconds times 1000 plus a random byte as the message id, so blocks in one second hit the unique key and were lost. *Avoid:* migration 4006 indexes `(guild_id, kind, payload->>'user')`; a made-up key uses milliseconds plus 20 random bits.

**Hourly prunes and the anti-nuke seed scanned whole tables, and the first prune deleted months in one statement.** (audit, 11-17)
The `action_log` and `mod_actions` prunes and `seed_windows` (before the gateway) filter on `created_at` alone, but both indexes lead with `guild_id`. Unban and appeal checks filter `action_log` by guild and target, the dashboard bell `appeals` by user: no index either. *Avoid:* a query that filters on a column no index leads with gets one (migration 4009, plain `CREATE INDEX` because sqlx runs each migration in a transaction); a prune of a large table deletes in batches (`welcome::prune_batched`, 10,000 rows a statement).

**Rows pruned only on their next use outlived the promised time.** (audit, 11-17)
`verify_ips` was pruned only when someone verified on the web and `verify_timeouts` only on the next write for the same server, so a quiet server kept user ids past the 30 days and 1 day PRIVACY.md states. *Avoid:* every retention PRIVACY.md promises has a global prune in `welcome::purge_left`'s hourly list.

**Stored rows outlived the one use they had, and a "salted" hash could be reversed.** (security review, 11-19)
`verify_ips` was kept 30 days while the alt check reads only the last day, and its hash was SHA-256 of the OAuth client secret, the server and the IP: the secret sits in .env and in the MCP container, and all IPv4 addresses can be tried, so anyone with the secret and the rows got the addresses back. Every NSFW scan, clean or not, kept the member, channel, message and the model's scores. Moderation records (cases, notes, reports, appeals, tickets) and config history had no time limit at all. Root cause: each table's retention was picked from the feature, not from what the reading query needs, and a pseudonym was keyed with a secret that has other uses. *Avoid:* keep a row only as long as the longest window its readers use, and store only the columns those readers need (a clean image needs its hash, not who posted it); key a hash that must not be reversed with a key no other service, file or dump holds (`verify::ip_hash` makes one at start and keeps it in memory); a record type with no natural end gets a per-server limit (`retention` section, `bot::retention`), and a sweep never deletes an open item, a running timed action, the current config or the row a number sequence is read from (`MAX(case_no)`, `MAX(number)`).

**A data delete kept a member's live warn and then cleared its points anyway.** (security pass on the case undo fix, 11-19; owner decision 11-19: "Warns stay until they expire")
`PLACES` keeps a warn that still counts, but `ANONYMIZE` then set `user_id = 0` on every case the delete left, and `warn_points` reads by `user_id`, so an approved `/mydata delete` wiped the member's warn points at once. Nothing removed the kept warn later either. Root cause: "kept" was written once for the delete and not for the step that strips the person from what is left, and a row kept "until it ends" had no one to end it. *Avoid:* every step of a person-level delete uses the same keep condition (`CASE_KEEP` and the same live test in `ANONYMIZE`); a row kept until it ends names what removes it then: `persondata::sweep_kept` runs hourly from the scheduler and finishes done delete requests for rows made before the request, never later ones: an ended warn, a handled report or appeal, a closed ticket (owner decision 11-19). It reads every done request, with no time window: a window of one warn life would leave a warn that a restored backup brings back (review); migration 4040 adds the indexes by person it needs. Searched: tickets lose the person only when closed, and the "Not NSFW" rows are the server's.

**A first draft of the data delete removed the person's dashboard 2FA, open items and the newest case.** (security pass, 11-19)
With the authenticator row gone, whoever holds the Discord account could enrol a new one by DM code and pass 2FA; deleting the newest case let its number be given to another member. Root cause: the delete list was built from "every table with the user id", not from what each row is for. *Avoid:* a person-level delete (`core::persondata`) never deletes a credential, an open item (report, appeal, ticket), a running timed action or the row a number sequence is read from; those are kept or lose the person (`ANONYMIZE`). An export leaves out other people's ids and text that can name them (report reasons, which servers reported a raider).

**PRIVACY.md said staff see what their Discord permissions allow.** (security review, 11-19)
Only permits give access to Brim data; Discord permissions give none. *Avoid:* the notice uses the same words as the code's rules ("permit", "Discord permission"), and is read again when access rules change.

**One server whose purge failed stopped the purge of every server after it.** (audit, 11-17)
`db::purge_left` used `?` in its loop, so a lock timeout on one server kept the others' data each hour. *Avoid:* a loop over independent items logs a failed one and goes on.

**Case and ticket numbers took the config lock.** (audit, 11-17)
`cases::create` and `tickets::claim_number` locked `pg_advisory_xact_lock(guild_id)`, the same key as the config saves (`settings::locked`, dashboard `store.rs`), so numbering and config saves queued behind each other, and a numbering call inside a config transaction would deadlock. *Avoid:* the bare guild id is only the config lock; every other lock uses its own key, `hashtextextended('name:' || id, 0)`.

**Removed Raider List entries and their evidence were never deleted.** (audit, 11-17)
`expire` deleted rejected and old pending entries but not `removed` ones, so a removed entry kept its evidence (reporter ids and notes) for good, while PRIVACY.md said evidence goes with its entry. *Avoid:* every status has an end; a removed entry goes after 30 days once no server has a list action left to lift (the lift reads the row).

**PRIVACY.md promised retention the code does not do.** (audit, 11-17)
The new Message Builder row said an unused avatar goes "after 1 day", but `msghooks::gc` runs only when that server uploads or deletes, and the Yes/No row said 10 seconds where `confirm.rs` deletes rows older than 70 seconds. *Avoid:* a retention line names the trigger and the age the DELETE uses (read the query, not the constant's name).

**PRIVACY.md missed tables and said message text is never stored.** (audit, 11-17)
Reports (300 characters of the message), appeals, raid log, staff lists, sticky roles, lock updates, Message Builder, MCP and update records had no row, `guild_owners` said "deleted on leave" (it is the 30-day purge) and optional third parties (Cloudflare, the AI connector, off-site dumps) were not named. *Avoid:* a new table with user ids or user text gets its row, and a new outside service its line under "Optional services", in the same change.

**A 5-second job scanned the whole cases table.** (audit, 11-17)
`jobs::expire_cases` runs on every scheduler tick (5 s) and had no index for `active AND expires_at < now`, so each tick read every server's full case history. *Avoid:* a job that runs often gets a partial index that matches its WHERE (migration 4004, `cases_expiring`).

**Tables with personal data had no cleanup.** (audit, 11-17)
Expired `dash_session` rows (with the Discord access token) and every `action_log` row stayed forever, while PRIVACY.md promised sessions expire and did not list `action_log`. *Avoid:* a new table that stores user ids gets a retention line in PRIVACY.md and, if it is time-limited, a DELETE in the hourly list in `welcome::purge_left`. Sessions now go when expired, `action_log` after 90 days (owner decision 11-17), except Brim's own `quarantine` rows of a live quarantine, which `joingate::missed` reads to spot rejoins. An action older than 90 days can no longer be undone from the dashboard.

**Two PRs used the same migration number.** (#64/#65)
Git merged them without a conflict, then sqlx failed every test with "duplicate key _sqlx_migrations_pkey".
*Avoid:* parallel work gets its own number range (see the handover notes). Before merging several PRs, `ls crates/core/migrations` and look for duplicate prefixes.

**Old migrations are frozen.** (#112)
sqlx stores a checksum, so editing an applied migration breaks every existing database.
*Avoid:* never edit an old migration, not even a comment. Write a new one.

**A column that replaces another goes stale after a rollback.** (Message Builder phase 1, migration 3980, review)
The new `embeds.message` replaces `embed`, and the new code still writes `embed` so the previous image works after a rollback. But the previous image edits only `embed`, so after a roll forward the readers took the old `message` and the edit was lost. *Avoid:* a trigger clears the new column when an update changes only the old one (`embeds_stale_message`), and readers fall back to the old column (`embeds::MSG`). Drop the trigger with the old column.

**A default change must not overwrite user choices.** (#133, migration 2200)
*Avoid:* a migration that changes a default only moves rows that still hold the old default (600 → 60) and leaves other values alone. Test it from an empty DB and from the old schema with data.

**A new "last activity" column backfilled from an old time makes a new idle rule act at once.** (Tickets 2.0, migration 3932)
Backfilling `last_activity_at` from `opened_at` would have let auto-close shut every old open ticket the first time a server turned it on, though staff wrote in them yesterday.
*Avoid:* when a column starts tracking something that was not tracked before, backfill live rows with `now` (unknown counts as fresh), and only finished rows with the old time.

**A new migration file was not picked up.** (MCP views, migration 2800)
`sqlx::migrate!` embeds the migrations at compile time, and adding a file does not make cargo rebuild `brim-core`. Tests then failed with "relation mcp_... does not exist".
*Avoid:* after adding a migration, `touch crates/core/src/db.rs` before testing. CI builds clean and is not affected. The same happened after `git merge` brought in a changed `RaidCfg` (MCP PR 4): two schema tests failed against the old build until `touch crates/automod/src/join.rs`. If a test fails right after a merge on code you did not touch, touch the changed crate's file and run it again before you debug.

**A table stored personal data that PRIVACY.md did not list.** (VPN block, 11-12)
`verify_ips` (salted IP hashes from web verification, kept 30 days) had no row in PRIVACY.md.
*Avoid:* a migration that stores anything about a user adds its row to PRIVACY.md (what, why, how long) in the same PR.

**Parallel worktrees shared one target and one DB.** (11-01)
Cargo mixed crates from different worktrees, and one branch's migration broke the others' tests. Four targets (about 7 GB each) filled the disk.
*Avoid:* one `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` and one database per worktree (`su postgres -c "createdb X"`; `createdb -h localhost` hangs on a password prompt). Delete finished targets.

**Saving a note without a change was stored as an edit.** (11-11)
`notes::edit` always wrote a `note_edits` row and both callers logged a Modify, so pressing Save twice gave a "x → x" history line and a log.
*Avoid:* an edit that changes nothing writes nothing (`old.text <> $3` in the SQL) and the callers skip the log when `old == text`.

**A row was found by its id alone, without its server.** (security review, 2026-11-18)
Many statements read or changed a row only by a global id (ticket channel, appeal, report, sticky, message rule, webhook, slowmode channel, raid code, confirm nonce). Every id came from a server-scoped step, so nothing leaked, but one later change (an id from a button, job payload or event that is not checked) would let one server touch another server's row. Root cause: the server was checked once, earlier and often in another function, and the statement trusted the id after that.
*Avoid:* a statement keyed by an id that arrives from outside the function (a parameter, job payload, button id or event) also says `guild_id = $n`; pass the server down (event `guild_id`, the job's `guild_id`, the handler's path). Not needed when the same function just read, locked or inserted that row with its server, or for sweeps over all servers (retention, the job queue, the message-rule tick).

**Any database session can NOTIFY, also a read-only one.** (security review, 11-18)
Postgres has no grant for NOTIFY, and `default_transaction_read_only` does not stop it, so the MCP role (or any other) could send `config_changed` and make the bot redo each guild's save work (rule sync, ticket category, Raider List scan). *Avoid:* the listener always drops the cached copy (cheap, and a purge notice names no version), but runs the save work only for a version that is saved and newer than the last it handled (seeded at start). Compare version numbers, not `created_at`: that is the transaction's start, so a later version can carry an earlier time. Treat a notice as a hint to read the database, never as the change itself. A new role that must not write rows gets no INSERT grant; a channel it must not use needs a check on the listening side.

- **A person's data deletion rule that lived only in the code.** (Case proofs security pass, 2026-11-19) The proofs and edit history of a case the deletion keeps (the newest, a running timed action) were deleted by two statements in `persondata::ANONYMIZE`. An older image (after a rollback) runs its own list, which does not know the new tables, so the member's message copy stayed. *Avoid:* a table that hangs on another table's row gets its deletion rule in the migration (ON DELETE CASCADE, or a trigger such as `case_forget_person`), so every image obeys it.
- **The copy trigger for an old column only added.** (same) The trigger that copies `cases.proof` written by an older image into `case_proofs` fired only for a new value, so a proof the older image cleared or replaced came back after the next update. *Avoid:* a trigger that mirrors an old column handles replace and clear as well as set, and has a test for each.

- **The dashboard's database role could write what only the bot may set.** (Case proofs security pass, 2026-11-19) The role had INSERT and UPDATE on every table (one grant on all tables). No API route misused it, but SQL run as the dashboard could set `case_proofs.verified` (the bot's own copy of a member's message), change `reports.content` (the bot copies it as verified proof when a moderator acts on the report), point a case at another member (`cases.user_id`, `kind`), and rewrite history and log rows. *Avoid:* a table the bot writes and the dashboard only reads, marks or adds lines to is narrowed in `db::set_dashboard_role`: revoke the table-level INSERT and UPDATE and grant only the columns the dashboard's routes change (`cases`: reason, active, proof; `reports`: status, handled_by, handled_at; `case_proofs`: INSERT of the staff columns), and write the staff statement with only those columns. Before narrowing, grep every dashboard and core statement on the table, row locks too: `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` needs an UPDATE right on at least one column (the proof removal locked a `case_proofs` row and would have answered "permission denied"). `dashboard_role_has_rows_only` connects as the role, checks each refusal by its error code (42501) and runs what the dashboard does do. The dashboard's own unit tests and the browser test connect as the owner, so they do not see a missing right: run the browser test once with the dashboard connected as `tommybot_dashboard` after a grant change.

**Any role could switch the erasure log off for its own deletes.** (PR B security pass, fixed in PR D, 11-21)
`note_erasure` and `log_erasure` skipped logging whenever the setting `brim.erasing` (before: `tommybot.erasing`) was `on`, and any session may set a custom setting itself, so the dashboard's role could `SET LOCAL brim.erasing = on` and delete Extra Owners, permits, a rescue key or 2FA without a log line; a later restore brought them back. Root cause: a setting says nothing about who set it. *Avoid:* a skip that only the owner may grant needs a proof only the owner can write. Migration 4056: `purge_guild` and `restore_erasures` call `brim_owner.erasing_on`, which writes the transaction id (and its start time, so a row restored from another database cannot match) into `brim_owner.erasing`, a schema no other role may use (not `public`: an older image grants every table there again); `brim_owner.erasing(guild)` checks flag AND row, and the row names the purged server (NULL only for a restore): the dashboard may call `purge_guild` itself, and the log triggers are deferred to commit, so a purge of an unused server id in the same transaction would otherwise have hidden its other deletes (review of PR D). Postgres 16 already refuses a role that is no superuser to keep the flag as its own `ALTER ROLE ... SET` default, but a role can keep other defaults (the MCP role could turn its read-only default off): `db::set_dashboard_role` and `set_mcp_password` clear them (`RESET ALL`, also per database) at every start. Searched: no other function reads a session setting for a decision.

**Role names written into SQL broke with the rename.** (PR D, 11-21)
Migrations 4035 and 4043 compared `current_user` with the literal `'tommybot_dashboard'`, and every MCP grant named `tommybot_mcp`, so a server on the Brim names (`brim_dashboard`, `brim_mcp`) would have lost the dashboard's row limits and the MCP role all its views. Root cause: the role name was a constant in SQL, the code and the compose file at once. *Avoid:* the code uses the Brim names only, and `db::connect` stops at start on a login under the old names (2026-11-22, before any migration or role change), the bot grants the MCP views at start (`set_mcp_password`), and SQL that must know the dashboard's role names both (migration 4056). A new check on the dashboard's role in SQL names both names.

**The dashboard role's limits were gone after a rollback.** (2026-11-19)
`db::set_dashboard_role` narrows the role at every bot start, but an older bot image runs its own list (`GRANT SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE ON ALL TABLES`, no revokes), so a rollback silently gave the role every row right again. Root cause: a limit that only the newest code sets does not survive older code; grants are state that every image rewrites. *Avoid:* a limit that must hold on every image lives in a migration (migrations stay, an older image runs no down step): migration 4043 `dashboard_role_limit` row triggers refuse (42501) for `current_user = 'tommybot_dashboard'` what the column grants refuse. A trigger checks values, not the columns a statement names (the #357 image names every `case_proofs` column with `verified = false`). A grant that leaves a column out also refuses a chosen value for it: the staff proof's `id` and `added_at` must be the defaults (a chosen `id` would take the sequence's next values and block the bot's verified proofs). A trigger function that guards a role sets `search_path = pg_catalog, pg_temp`, or the caller puts its own `pg_temp` operators in front of the check. The same holds for history and log lines (`case_edits`, `note_edits`, `mod_actions`, `raider_evidence`): "no UPDATE" alone still let the role DELETE a line and INSERT it again with the same id and any text or time, which is a rewrite. Append-only means INSERT on the named columns only (no `id`, no time) and no DELETE; a case's or note's delete removes its lines through the foreign key, which runs as the owner. A new dashboard write to one of these tables needs its column in BOTH the grant and the trigger's argument list; `dashboard_role_has_rows_only` grants everything back as an older image does and runs the refusals again against real rows (a row trigger does not fire for `WHERE false`).

**Two migrations with the same number stopped every start.** (2026-11-20)
Two PRs merged one after the other each added a `4049_*.sql` (Brim panel text and permit role missing). sqlx applies both versions as 4049, so the second insert into `_sqlx_migrations` failed on a fresh database, and a database that had one of them refused to start ("previously applied but has been modified"). Root cause: each branch picked "the next free number" on its own, and nothing checked the list after a merge. *Avoid:* a test fails when two files share a number (`db::tests::migration_numbers_are_unique`); the coordinator gives each builder its number, and a builder checks `crates/core/migrations` again after rebasing on main. Renumbered the panel text one to 4051 (neither 4049 was on a live database yet).

**A per-member path must hold for 100,000 members.** (giveaways, 2026-11-23)
A giveaway can get 100,000 entries, and a button press can come from 10,000 members in a minute. *Avoid:* a press is one small indexed write (unique on giveaway + user, so a double press is a no-op) and a private answer, never a message edit; the shown count is refreshed by a job at most once a minute (`giveaways::queue_refresh` also skips the database for 60 s per server). Never load all rows to pick from them: the database draws (`ORDER BY -ln(1 - random()) / weight LIMIT n`, a weighted sample without repeats), and only the picked candidates are checked with Discord, with a cap on the checks. A change of rules does not re-check every entry with Discord; the draw re-checks the winners. Every query on a per-press path needs an index on its `WHERE` (migration 4061 adds `raid_log (guild_id, user_id)` for this). The load test `a_giveaway_with_100000_entries` measures it.

**A saved draw was never announced after a crash.** (giveaways review, 2026-11-23)
The end job saved the winners, then posted them and queued the DMs. A restart between the two left winners nobody was told about, and the retry saw the draw as done. *Avoid:* the save of a result queues its follow-up jobs (announcement, one DM per winner) in the same transaction (`giveaways::save_draw`). A DM job checks that the member is still a winner, and DM jobs run spawned (`scheduler::SPAWNED`), so 100 DMs do not hold back unbans. A cancel drops the end job only when it changed the row, so it never takes the retry from a running draw.

## 6. Dashboard

**One dashboard 2FA step-up unlocked the 2FA-gated actions in every server.** (security review, 11-17)
`dash_session.step_up_until` had no server, so `require(.., true)` and the bot's `fresh_2fa` (whitelist save, adding a Trusted Admin, main roles, backup clear, delete and load) took a step-up done anywhere, from any session of the user. *Avoid:* the step-up stores the server it was done for (`step_up_guild`, migration 4012): the prompt sends the server of the page it opened on, `Sess::stepped_up_for(guild)` and `fresh_2fa(app, user, gid)` accept it there only, and `/api/me` returns `step_up_guild` for the pages that check first (`stepped_up_here`). Account pages (profile, update, MCP tokens, Raider List review) keep `stepped_up`. An MCP confirm's code counts for the change's server. Test fixtures that set `step_up_until` also set `step_up_guild`.

**A Message Builder rule kept running after its saver lost the level to make it.** (security review, 11-17)
A schedule, trigger or sticky message is saved by an Owner, Extra Owner or Trusted Admin, but the runner never asked again. *Avoid:* `msgrules::saver_may_run` reads the level of `updated_by` before every schedule run, trigger answer and sticky repost (`sticky_msgs` for stickies); below Admin it turns the rule off with the reason in `issue` (shown on the card) and logs once (the update matches `enabled`, so a second run changes nothing). A save by someone with the level sets `updated_by` and `issue = null`, which confirms it. An unread level fails the run and pauses nothing. No migration.

A Trusted Admin could hand a permit role with the anti-nuke or quarantine whitelist (or View Mod Stats) to an alt in Discord, in a server whose permits are not listed by member: `guard_permit_roles` let every immune actor through, while the dashboard's `keep_exemptions` already kept those permits Owner/EO only. *Avoid:* the bot twin asks the actor's level; Owner and Extra Owner pass, a Trusted Admin's grant of such a role (`owner_only_roles`) is taken back and logged, nobody punished; this includes roles with dangerous permissions, which `guard_perms` spares for a Trusted Admin. A bot path is never weaker than its dashboard twin either.

A quarantined member's level always reads as no permit (`top_permit` skips anyone in `quarantines`), so "is this held member staff" by `resolve` was always no: a permit holder could release, or give roles to, quarantined staff whose permit came only from roles. *Avoid:* `antinuke::held_staff` reads the saved roles' permits (or the member's own permit rows once migrated) and dangerous permissions; `unquarantine`, the quarantine hold and the Raider List's release on removal all use it. Below Extra Owner, the hold's `quarantine` flag and whitelist spare the actor only for a held member who is not staff.

A Trusted Admin could give the quarantine role itself dangerous permissions or a channel override, which reached every quarantined member, staff too, around the staff release rule. *Avoid:* `guard_perms` asks for Owner or Extra Owner when the role (or role override) is the quarantine role (owner decision 2026-11-18); a Trusted Admin's change is undone and logged, never punished.

An anti-nuke revert of a role removal (`Inverse::AddRoles`) gave every removed role back, even Administrator to a member quarantined since, and one deleted role stopped the rest. *Avoid:* the bot and the dashboard give back no role with dangerous permissions or a permit and nothing to a quarantined member (those are left to staff, as the step text says), and try every role before they report the last error. The dashboard also reads the role lock snapshots, since a lock strips the very bits that make a role dangerous.

A schedule's claim moves `next_at` before the send, so an error after it (an unread saver level) lost a one-time schedule without a word. *Avoid:* an unread level after the claim sets the rule's `issue` with the reason; nothing is paused.

**Only the browser checked the typed server name before a delete.** (privacy, 11-17)
`purge` checked Owner level, 2FA and the physical owner, but a direct call skipped the name PRIVACY.md promises. *Avoid:* a confirmation the notice or the UI names (a typed name, a code) is checked on the server too, after the access checks so a refused user still gets 403.

**The rescue authenticator secret sat in plain text next to the key's hash.** (privacy, 11-17)
A database read alone gave the second factor of a rescue claim. *Avoid:* a secret that only the key holder needs is sealed with the key (`ops::seal`, opened at claim time); the row keeps only the hash. Older rows stay plain until the owner makes a new key.

**Log lines served to the AI connector kept login codes, state and session ids.** (privacy, 11-17)
The dashboard's request spans logged the full URI, so `/auth/callback?code=...&state=...` and member search text went into the files `logs_tail` reads, and `redact` masked only tokens and URLs. *Avoid:* request spans log the path, never the query; a new secret kind that can reach a log line gets a pattern in `mcp::tools::SECRETS` and a case in `masks_secrets`.

**A rescue claim from outside the server used up the key for nothing.** (rescue, 11-18; replaced 11-20)
The claim made the claimer an Extra Owner, which `api::level_of` counts only for a member, so 11-18 added a member check. Owner decision 2026-11-20: a lost account must be recoverable from a new account that nobody let in, so the member rule is gone and the claim moves the Owner level itself (next entry). *Avoid:* a grant that only counts under a condition is checked against that condition before anything single-use is spent; here the grant (bot owner) has no member condition, and `let_in` unbans the account and makes a one-use invite.

**Owner level came from Discord ownership alone, so a lost owner account could not be replaced.** (rescue, owner decision 2026-11-20)
A rescue claim now moves Brim's Owner level: `rescue_owners` (migration 4048) names the claimer and the Discord owner at claim time, and `permits::bot_owner` gives the Owner level to the claimer. The Discord owner account then has no level (paused) until the claimer gives it back (`rescue_give_back`). A first version ended the claim when Discord ownership moved: a thief holding the old account handed the server to an alt and had the bot back at once (security pass). Now the claim moves with Discord ownership (`on_owner` updates `discord_owner`) and ends only on a give-back or when ownership lands on the claimer (owner decision 2026-11-20; a sale needs a give-back first). *Avoid:* never decide trust from `GuildInfo::owner` or Discord's `owner_id` directly: levels go through `permits::resolve` (which maps), `level_members`/`member_roles`/`unentitled_roles` map in SQL, the bot uses `App::bot_owner` for the owner's DMs and owner skips, the dashboard `api::bot_owner`/`owner_state`. Discord facts (the owner cannot be acted on, has every permission: `target_above_bot`, `can_invite`, the rank in `hierarchy`) keep the Discord owner. `guild_owners` holds the bot owner, so a claim and a give-back reach `on_owner` (queued `owner_check`) as a handover: the previous holder's Extra Owners, Trusted Admins and strong permits are paused, with Keep on Overview. Since anyone can claim, a server without Brim answers like a wrong key and is not counted; the code tries still count on the key row only. The column is `server_id`, so `purge_guild` (data deletion, or the purge after the bot left) keeps the row and cannot give the bot back. A give-back right after a claim merged nothing, so the second handover overwrote the first and the paused owner's Extra Owners were lost: `on_owner` now appends the previous lists when the bot goes back to the owner the last change paused. `owner_check` inserts skip only a job that is not leased: a running one may have read the state before the claim. Searched: every `owner_id`, `.owner` and `guild_owner(` in bot, core and dashboard. shortcut: the erasure log does not record a give-back, so a database restore from before it brings the claim back; add `rescue_owners` to migration 4035's lists (and `restore_erasures`) if that matters.

**A rescue code checked without using up the key could be replayed.** (Secure my account, 2026-11-20)
The rescue claim needed no replay guard because a right code deletes the key. The Secure my account press checks the same key and code but keeps the key, so the same code would have worked again within its 30 s (and in a claim). *Avoid:* any second use of a secret that is not used up stores the last code step in the write itself (`rescue_key.last_step`, migration 4050, `where last_step is null or last_step < $step`), and every path that checks the code (claim and press) refuses a step at or below it and counts it as a wrong code on the shared `code_fails`. The press also refuses a key made before the server's last change of owner, even inside the 7 days a claim still allows. Searched: the dashboard 2FA (`TotpAttempts.last_step`) and the MCP confirm already guard replays. Found in the security pass: the first draft counted presses in the rescue claim's per-user limit, and a claim needs no step-up, so whoever held only the Discord account could send 6 claims every 10 minutes and keep the owner's button refused; the press has its own limit (`PRESSES`). A new Rescue Key clears `last_step`. The alert's link is checked (https, parses, 512 characters at most), because Discord refuses the whole DM for a bad button link and the bot does not retry a 4xx.

**The rescue key outlived an ownership change for good.** (audit, 11-17, owner decision 11-17)
`claim_key` never looked at `owner_handovers`, so a previous owner could claim their key and become Extra Owner in the new owner's server at any time. A first fix was keyed on the one `owner_handovers` row per guild, whose `at` every change moves: a second change brought an expired key back, or gave an old key 7 more days. *Avoid:* a key made before the last handover (`owner_handovers.at > rescue_key.created_at`) works for `RESCUE_GRACE_DAYS` (7) and is then refused (`permits::rescue_expired_sql`, built from the constant; one SQL for the claim, the prompt and the bot's job). `on_owner` deletes, in its transaction, a key made before the previous handover, so only the handing-over owner's key gets the 7 days, once, and sets `at = clock_timestamp` after the config lock. `rescue_regenerate` checks the owner and the 7 days and inserts under the same lock, and refuses inside the 7 days with the date. Then the physical owner gets a full-page prompt (`page_access` `rescue_prompt`, `RescuePrompt`; a new key refetches the access) and a DM from the `rescue_expired` job that `on_owner` queues. Tested: claim, prompt and regenerate inside and after the 7 days, a key made after the handover, and a second handover (bot `a_second_owner_change_...`). No migration.

**Dashboard twins of bot actions were weaker: appeal blacklist and pinging messages.** (audit, 11-17)
The appeal blacklist add and remove routes needed only an Editor, with no `appeals` permit and no panic mod lock, while the bot's Deny + Blacklist button needs both. Send and Edit of a built message checked only the webhook case, so an Editor could send a Trusted Admin's @everyone message again and again. Appeal answers went into the staff log as raw markdown (masked links, mentions). *Avoid:* a route uses its bot twin's checks (`reviewer` and `mod_lock_guard`; the page access follows the route); Send and Edit refuse below Admin when the allowed mentions name anyone (`pings_members`: `parse`, `users` or `roles` not empty), the same bar as saving; user text in an embed goes through `msgvars::escape`.

**Error texts named the wrong cause, and two config log lines had no version.** (audit, 11-17)
`users_only` told an owner to "try again" for an id Discord does not know (`Ok(None)`), a failed `/users/@me` at login showed raw JSON, and every 2FA setup DM failure said "allow DMs". The Discord AutoMod rule routes and the word migration committed a version but logged without it. A second tab turning Tickets on made a second category and log channel. *Avoid:* match each failure kind (404 user, Discord 403 on a DM, others) to its own text; a route that commits a version logs it (`audit_version`); a setup that makes Discord objects before its commit holds a try-lock (`tickets_setup:`) and reads the version again under it.
**The MCP tokens page said "No tokens yet." when the list did not load.** (audit, 11-17)
The owner could decide there was nothing to revoke while live tokens existed. *Avoid:* a list keeps a `failed` signal from its load, and the empty fallback shows "Could not load ..." with Retry when it is set.

**Dashboard mod actions skipped panic mode's mod command lock.** (audit, 11-17)
Appeal deny, dismiss and deny + blacklist, case edits, note add, edit, delete and sweep, and report dismiss and reopen ran during the lock, while their bot twins go through `commands::gated`. Only the appeal accept checked it. *Avoid:* every dashboard route whose bot twin runs `gated` calls `appeals::mod_lock_guard` (Owner, Extra Owner and Trusted Admin pass). The command rate limit of `gated` is still not mirrored (no dashboard rate limits yet).

**Reopening or dismissing a report on the dashboard erased its action.** (audit, 11-17)
`report_status` set `action = null` and cleared `handled_by` with no trace, so a ban made from a report vanished from its record. *Avoid:* an `actioned` report keeps its action and handler (the route refuses it), and a dismiss or reopen writes a `mod_actions` row (`report_dismiss`, `report_reopen`).

**"Delete server data" deleted what a lock, panic mode or quarantine gives back.** (audit, 11-17)
`purge_guild` empties `lock_snapshots`, `quarantines.saved_roles` and the `panic_end` and revert jobs, and `guild_owners`, while the bot stays in the server. *Avoid:* `api::purge` refuses while any `lock_snapshots` row exists (also one that is unlocking), while `recovery::no_backup_now` is true, or while a staff or automatic quarantine (not `on_join`) exists, and writes the checked owner back into `guild_owners`, so the next transfer still runs the D47 reset.

**The MCP DM showed `"pings": null` for a save that keeps @everyone.** (audit, 11-17)
`Save.pings` reads a JSON null as missing (keep the stored pings), but the preview filled in the stored value only for a missing key. *Avoid:* the preview treats a null like a missing key wherever the route does; check how serde reads `null` for each `Option` field the DM shows.

**An MCP change stuck in `running` kept its body.** (audit, 11-17)
A restart between the claim and the final update left the row `running` with its body, and expired bodies were only cleared on the next MCP call. *Avoid:* `mcp::service` and the bot's hourly prune (`welcome::purge_left`) close `running` rows older than 15 minutes and expire pending ones, both with `body = null`.

**A thread passed the built-message channel check.** (audit, 11-17)
`embeds::bot_channel` compared a thread's parent only with the ticket category, so a schedule or sticky could post in a thread of a log, appeal or verification channel, or of a ticket channel. *Avoid:* pass `embeds::parents` (the parent and, for a thread, its parent) and compare each with the bot's own channels and the ticket category.

**A schedule edit kept `waiting_once`.** (audit, 11-17)
The dashboard save reset `due_messages` and `issue` but not `waiting_once`, so after a "once" run waited out a pause, the first run of the new schedule was skipped with a wrong reason. *Avoid:* a save resets every run-state column the bot's enable path resets.

**Login and captcha failures showed raw JSON or "internal error".** (audit, 11-17)
A cancel on Discord's consent screen (no `code`), a second login tab (state mismatch) and a reused code answered with a deserializer message, JSON or a 500, and a Turnstile outage showed "internal error". *Avoid:* `auth::callback` answers these with a short page and a login link (`login_failed`); `verify::turnstile_ok` maps a send or parse failure to "The captcha check could not be reached".

**Auto Setup deleted the server's own channels and roles that had a matching name.** (audit, 11-17)
`drop_named` deleted every text channel named like a log channel (`#mod-logs`, `#reports`, `#appeals`, ...) or `#verify-here` that the config did not use, and `quarantine_role` deleted every unused role named Quarantine, before it made a new one. The code cannot tell a leftover of an earlier Brim setup from a channel or role the server made itself, so years of messages, or another system's quarantine, could go, and anti-nuke ignores the bot's own deletes. *Avoid:* a setup never deletes a channel or role it did not make in this run. It takes over the oldest unused one with that name (`oldest_unused`, owner decision 11-17): a log channel is moved into its category and its overwrites are replaced in full by the category's current ones, `#verify-here` gets the overwrites a new one would, and their messages stay (only adding denies, as the first version did, left an old allow or Manage Permissions reading the logs); a Quarantine role must pass the same `unfit` check as a picked one (not @everyone, not managed, below the bot), no member may hold it (`ops::held_roles`; a held one would lock its members out with no quarantine row, case or release), and it is stripped of its permissions. Other same-named ones are left alone. Searched: every `Method::DELETE` in the dashboard; the rest delete only what the user picked (messages, rules, webhooks, inverses of a nuke).

**Auto Setup lost a save made during its Discord calls.** (audit, 11-17)
Auto Setup decided "no custom permits yet" from its first read and then replaced `permits.custom` with the two default permits, so permits saved during its 10 or more seconds of Discord calls were gone. A save in that time also made its final `commit` return 409 after the roles and channels were made, and the level roles were written with no config version. *Avoid:* decide from the read in the final transaction (`no_custom`), write the level roles together with a `bump`, under the config lock and only for levels nobody changed since the first read (`new_levels`, so an Owner's change made meanwhile is kept), and put the setup's changed fields onto the newest version under the config lock (`put_changes`) instead of refusing the whole run.

**Message Builder Send and Delete had weaker checks than Edit and Save.** (audit, 11-17)
Send (`publish`) did not refuse Brim's log, ticket, verification and appeal channels, which edit, the schedule and trigger rules and the Builder's new channel refuse, so an Editor could post a fake log line as the bot. Delete needed only Editor, with no `emergency_gate`, so an Editor could delete a message that pings, sends as a webhook or runs by itself (which only a Trusted Admin may save), with its rules and every copy, also during a lockdown. *Avoid:* Send asks `bot_channel` like edit; Delete reads the row `for update`, calls `emergency_gate` and the same `trusted_only` check as save. The same class as "A second way to change something had a weaker check than the main way".

**A new automated send went out as "manual" and skipped every per-minute cap.** (Message Builder tags, security pass, 11-18)
`/tag` runs called `send_copy` with no rule and no answer, so `send_copy` filed them as `manual` (cap 0), and only the per-member limit held them back: ten permit holders could post 60 messages a minute. *Avoid:* every path that sends without a person at the dashboard has a server-wide cap. `/tag` checks it in the bot's queue insert (10 a minute per server), next to the per-member and per-channel limits.

**Lockdown Apply Fix applied fixes the user never saw.** (audit, 11-17)
`fix` ran every automatic fix the check found at click time, so a role or channel change after the page loaded could add a role-permission change nobody reviewed. *Avoid:* the page sends the ids of the automatic fixes it showed (`shown`), and `chosen` refuses with 409 when the check now finds a different set.

**Overview read the member list once per row.** (audit, 11-17)
`members_without` (up to 20 pages of members) ran once for each of the two rows with the same arguments, and the Channel Setup draft check read the guild, roles and channels again. *Avoid:* read the members once per request, and pass the score's `Live` to `channels::overview_check` (`world_of`).

**Auto Setup stays at Trusted Admin level.** (audit, 11-17) It sets `antinuke.quarantine_role`, and `lockdown.main_roles` while Monitor Public Roles is on, which Save puts behind the Owner bar; this is on purpose (owner decision 11-15: "if Auto Setup may do it, a Trusted Admin may"). *Avoid:* do not raise Auto Setup or its score fixes to Owner without asking owner decision.

**"Setup channel" left no config-change log line and posted a second panel.** (audit, 11-17)
`verify_channel` committed a version without `log_change`, and posted the verify panel on every press, also into the channel that was already set. *Avoid:* every route that commits a version calls `log_change` with it; the panel is posted only into a new or taken-over channel.

**Dashboard fixes and Channel Setup pushes saved a locked state as the newest backup.** (audit, 11-17)
The security-score fixes (`ops::backup`) and the Channel Setup push inserted a backup with no state check, while `backup::save`, backup create and delete refuse during a lock or panic mode. The newest backup is anti-nuke's restore source, so a later restore put the locked layout back. *Avoid:* these backups are skipped while `recovery::no_backup_now` is true (a lock, panic mode, or a nuke revert's guard job), and the log says "no backup" (`recovery::backup_named`).

**Dashboard lock buttons skipped the lock permits.** (audit, 11-17)
Lock, unlock and lock update on the dashboard needed only Editor, so a member could lock a server type or lift a lock that chat would refuse them, also during panic mode's mod command lock. *Avoid:* below Trusted Admin, `may_lock` asks the same permits as chat (`lockdown` or `lock_updates`, plus `missing` for each kind, which is shared in `automod::lockdown`) and refuses while `mod_lock` is on (owner decision, 11-17). Not mirrored: the per-permit command rate limit of `gated`, because the dashboard has no rate limits yet.

**Seeing a ticket channel was enough to talk as staff, and any Viewer could read transcripts.** (Tickets 2.0 phase A, T-01/T-02, 11-15)
A plain message in a ticket channel was relayed to the member by DM before any permit check (only the command branch checked), so anyone a wrong overwrite let into the channel could DM the member as staff. The transcript route needed only Viewer and served any bot file named `ticket_log_` in any channel of the server.
*Avoid:* every relay path checks the same live rule (`tickets::commands::allowed`: a Manage Tickets permit; admin commands Owner/EO/TA). Access roles are removed (owner decision 11-15, permits only): any holder of a plain Discord role got ticket staff power, and nothing guarded who gave that role. The default Admins and Moderators permits get Manage Tickets (Auto Setup, migration 3931). The transcript route needs `api::staff(.., &["tickets"])`, a channel the server's config ever named as its ticket log, a `cdn.discordapp.com` URL before and after redirects, and at most 25 MB, also while reading. Searched: every other Viewer route returns config or status, not message content; MCP has no ticket tool. Limit: config history keeps 200 versions, so a log channel older than that is refused until transcripts are recorded per ticket (phase B).

**Saving a Discord AutoMod rule turned off all its Brim actions.** (live test, 11-15)
`set_bot_actions` took `&mut config["discord_automod"][key]` for `actions`, `scopes` and `locks`. serde_json's index inserts `null` for a missing key, so a rule with no mention scope or lock saved `"scopes": null, "locks": null`. The bot cannot read a map from `null`, put the whole section back to defaults, and every Brim action on Discord AutoMod rules in that server stopped. Found when the dashboard approved an untrusted rule and the timeout never came. *Avoid:* never take `&mut v[key]` on JSON just to read or remove: use `get_mut` and index only to write a value. The lenient map readers (`lenient::map`, `map_of_lists`) read `null` as empty, so servers that already stored a `null` recover. Searched: the other `&mut config[..][..]` writes (`quarantine_whitelist`, the word migration) set a value first.

**A refused Discord login token gave a 500.** (live test, 11-15)
When Discord refused a session's OAuth token (the person removed the app's authorization, or the token expired), `/api/guilds` and the verify page's submit answered 500 "internal error", and the dashboard showed "Could not load servers" with no way back except logging out by hand. Root cause: `Discord::user_guilds` turned every HTTP error into the generic internal error. *Avoid:* every call that uses the session's own token goes through `auth::user_guilds`, which deletes the session and answers 401 on a Discord 401; the UI and the verify page send a 401 to `/auth/login`. Searched: `user_guilds` (server list, verify) is the only call with the session's token after login; `me` runs once in the callback with a token Discord has just issued.

See [crates/dashboard/ui/PITFALLS.md](crates/dashboard/ui/PITFALLS.md) for the two worst ones (#143): a `>` in a `view!` attribute ends the tag, and reading a signal after its page is gone crashes the whole dashboard. Also:

- **List closures that call `.get`** rebuild every child on each change and kill CSS transitions (#99). Read per element with a `Memo` and `class:`.
- **The UI crate is not in the cargo workspace.** `cargo clippy` at the root does not check it. Run `cargo clippy --target wasm32-unknown-unknown -- -D warnings` in `crates/dashboard/ui`.
- **The debug wasm is about 100 MB** and makes the page close silently in Playwright. Use `trunk build --release` for screenshots and the e2e sweep.
- **A refused save vanished with the toast** (8d72d50). An error the user must fix stays up until it is fixed or clicked away.
- **Auto Setup's steps read their state from the toast text** (#151). The toast closes itself after 3 s, so "Running" and "Done" vanished and the steps never stayed green. *Avoid:* keep a run's state in its own signal and show the result in the page; the toast only announces it.
- **Check access before you look at the body.** `put_section` and `import` validated the input before the level check, so someone without access learned the shape of a section from the error. Call `require` first, then validate.
- **A section struct without `#[serde(default)]` breaks the first save.** `RaidCfg` had required fields, so turning Join Raid on for a server that never saved it was refused ("missing field `suspicious_only`"). A config struct with a `Default` impl gets `#[serde(default)]` on the struct.
- **Pages everyone may open still need greyed write buttons.** Lockdown was `Full` for viewers, and History's Roll back was enabled for everyone; the server refused, but the buttons looked usable The same held for Overview's Fix buttons and system switches.
- **A number field without a schema default must be optional in the config struct.** The form saves an emptied field as its default, or `null` without one. Join Raid's `id_margin_ms` is a plain `i64`, so emptying it made every save fail. `schema::tests::number_fields_without_default_accept_null` checks every field, and `one_field_alone_deserializes` checks every section loads with one field set. Stepping an optional field back to 0 empties it: Join Raid's ID flag at 0 would flag every joiner in a raid. Give a page whose main action is a write `need(level)` in `access::page`, or disable the button with `Access::rank`.
- **A user choice kept only in the DOM is lost on reload.** The light/dark toggle only set `data-theme`, so a reload went back to dark. A choice the user makes in the UI is saved (here in `localStorage`, read again in `main`).
- **Every bug from the deep test of 2026-11-10** is listed with its cause in `tests/e2e/dashboard-deep-test-2026-11-10.md` of the archived old repository thomkr01/TommyBot; the UI ones (hover contrast, double page load, copy button, null number fields, `.row label` on the Import button) are in the UI file, sections 3 to 7.
- **`tests/e2e/deep.js`** checks every write route for seven access levels, every page as every level, the owner's number fields, toggles, save and cancel, the server switcher, 2FA, Auto Setup, toasts, theme, mobile and hover contrast.
- **The backup view sorted channels by category id, not position.** `(parent or own id, kind, position)` put categories (and loose channels) in snowflake order, so the tree did not match Discord, and matching children by category name broke when two categories share a name. *Avoid:* sort by `(voice, position, id)` and group children by `parent_id` (sent as a string: snowflakes lose precision as JS numbers).
- **A dropdown option's `selected=` stops working once the user has picked** (#158). The attribute only sets the default; after a pick the browser keeps the user's choice. Cancel reset the setting and the preview, but the dropdown still showed the old pick. *Avoid:* use `prop:selected=` (the live property) on every `<option>`, and `prop:value`/`prop:checked` on inputs. Fixed in all 16 options in `form.rs`, `embeds.rs` and `lockdown.rs`; no plain `selected=`/`checked=`/`value=` bindings are left. The e2e sweep picks a channel on Logging, presses Cancel and checks the dropdown; it waits for the page to load first, because a pick made before the config arrives is overwritten by it.
- **Role names from Discord already start with `@` for @everyone** (#158). The dashboard added its own `@` and showed `@@everyone`. *Avoid:* trim a leading `@` before adding one. Fixed in `form::items` (every role list and picker) and the `md.rs` role mentions; `recovery.rs` and the wizard's ticket pings already did. The bot itself never writes a role name after `@`.
- **The toast looked like two pieces.** A coloured `border-left` on a rounded box curves at the corners, and the absolutely placed progress strip starts inside that border, so the two met as a hook. *Avoid:* one colour mark per box. Put strips and edges inside an `overflow:hidden` box with `left:0; right:0` and no border on that side. Also `cargo fmt` in `crates/dashboard/ui` reformats unrelated files; revert them before committing.

- **Editing or deleting a case left no trace.** (Mod stats, 11-11) Anyone with Manage Cases could rewrite or close a case and a trusted admin could delete one, and nothing recorded who did it, so a staff member could hide their own history. `case_delete` also answered OK for a case that did not exist. *Avoid:* a change that can erase moderation history writes an append-only row (`cases::record`, kind `case_edit`/`case_delete`) in the same transaction, and a delete of nothing answers 404. Searched for other erasing routes: note edits already keep history (`note_edits`), report and appeal status changes keep `handled_by`/`decided_by`; the Revert Center and purge are owner/admin actions already logged.
- **An Admin-only check let every trusted admin through a permit-gated page.** (Mod stats, 11-11) `api::staff` and `access::page`'s `has` treat Admin as holding every permit, which is wrong for a permit meant to be owner-granted (View Mod Stats). *Avoid:* such a permit gets its own check (`access::mod_stats`) placed before `has`, `page_access` fetches permit perms for trusted admins too, and `keep_exemptions` stops non-owners from changing who holds it on save, rollback and import.
- **A missing `enabled` key was read as on.** (UI review, 11-11) Overview's switches guessed their own defaults and showed Anti Nuke and Join Raid on for a server that never saved them, while the bot, the security score and the pages read them as off. The settings pages only ringed cards red when `enabled` was literally `false`, so a system that was never switched on showed no "Not in use" ring. *Avoid:* read a missing switch as the bot does (the schema default, off for every system); never keep a second copy of a default in the UI.
- **The 2FA check ran before the DM code.** (MCP writes, PR 4, caught in review) `confirm` checked the TOTP code first, so anyone holding an MCP token could send wrong TOTP codes and lock the owner out of dashboard 2FA for 10 minutes. *Avoid:* when a request carries several secrets, check the one the caller must already hold (the DM code) first, and touch shared lockout counters only after it passes.
- **An action ran something other than what was reviewed.** (Channel Setup review, 11-11) Push ran the draft stored at that moment, which another editor (or a late save) could have changed after the preview. *Avoid:* every reply carries a version of what it shows (`updated_at`), the action sends it back, and a mismatch is refused with 409.
- **An approval showed less than it approved.** (Security review, 11-11) The MCP code DM cut the change at 1500 characters, so the end of a long change was approved unseen. The same class: `embed_post`'s DM showed only `{repost}` and not the embed it posts, a rollback's preview diffed the permits copy in the last config version instead of the live tables (a rescue-claimed Extra Owner was removed without showing), and the embed could be edited between the DM and the code. A `target` on an action without `{t}` in its path was never checked but went into the DM's inline code, so backticks and a fake block could reshape it (found in an edge-case check of #169). *Avoid:* an approval shows all of what runs or refuses (propose says "split it"); drop input an action does not use before it reaches the approval; preview what the action really reads (the stored embed, live permits); store a hash of what was shown and refuse at confirm (`stale`) when a fresh preview differs (migration 3100 `preview_hash`).
- **A check before a slow call did not hold for the write after it.** (Security review, 11-11, after #180) 2FA enrollment checked "not confirmed", sent the DM, then upserted the secret with no condition; a confirm during the DM had its secret replaced while staying confirmed, so a stolen session could end up holding the owner's 2FA. Confirm had the mirror race: it marked confirmed whatever secret was stored by then. *Avoid:* put the precondition in the write itself (`do update ... where not confirmed`, `update ... where secret = <the one checked>`) and treat 0 rows as refused.
