Searched the other upserts: the rescue-key regenerate replaces on purpose, the rest guard nothing.
- **A host allow list let any page on that host catch an OAuth code.** (Security review, 11-11) MCP OAuth took any path on a known client host, and a client id signed under that rule stays valid for 10 years. *Avoid:* compare host and path exactly (`REDIRECTS`), no query or port, and check the redirect again at `/authorize`, not only at registration.
- **An empty list switched an authorization rule off.** (Channel Setup review, 11-11) "Only owners may hide a channel from staff" compared against the staff roles; a server without roles on the Admins and Moderators permits had none, so nothing ever counted as hidden and anyone could do it. *Avoid:* decide what an empty list means for the rule (here: every newly limited channel hides it from staff) and say in the refusal how to fix it.
- **A filter meant for display also weakened the check.** (Channel Setup review, 11-11) Member overwrites were left out of the issue list so they would not make rows pulse, which also let a dangerous member overwrite through Push. *Avoid:* filter for display where it is shown; the list that decides keeps every row.
- **A picker's filter is not validation.** (Channel Setup review, 11-11) The UI left the quarantine and main roles out of the role picker, but the server took any role id, and the UI's own list still offered roles the server would refuse. *Avoid:* the server validates with the same rule (`Ctx::pickable`) and sends the allowed ids, and the UI offers only those.
- **The same rule written twice drifts.** (Raider List review, 11-11) The dashboard's Report button had its own copy of the bot's report checks and missed some: permit holders, a quarantined moderator's saved roles, a server that opted out, and putting a rejected entry back to pending. *Avoid:* a rule both processes enforce lives once in `brim_core` (`raiders::report`, `raiders::not_collectable`, `raiders::allow`); each side only looks up what only it can reach (Discord) and passes it in. Searched: the dashboard's staff and permit checks already call `brim_core::permits`; the appeal rules live only in the dashboard. The permit rule had drifted the same way (11-13): the bot used the member's highest-ranked permit only, the dashboard the union of all their permits, so a lower permit's Dashboard Edit or Ban counted on the dashboard but not in the bot. Both now read `permits::top_permit` (ties: the oldest permit).
- **A body extractor answers before the handler's own check.** (MCP writes, PR 4) The "not through the tunnel" check sat inside the handler, after `Json(..)`, so a request without a JSON body got 415 instead of 404 and the route showed it exists. *Avoid:* a check that must refuse before anything else is a `route_layer` middleware, not the first line of the handler.

- **Writing into Discord JSON by position panicked after the decision was saved.** (full check, 11-11) Appeals `close_log` added the Decision field with `embeds[0]["fields"].as_array_mut`. Writing through `v[0]` panics when `v` is not an array or is empty, and writing through `v["k"]` panics when `v` is a string, number, bool or array. A log message with no embed panicked after `decide` had committed, so the member never got the verdict DM. *Avoid:* to change JSON from outside (Discord replies, stored documents, user input), use `get_mut(i)` / `as_object_mut` / `entry(..)` and do nothing on a wrong shape; reading with `v["x"]` never panics. Side work like `close_log` must not be able to stop what comes after it. Searched every write by index or key in `crates/*/src` and the UI: config writes are safe because `put_section` and import only store objects that parse into their typed sections. The UI embed editor's `set_at` also panicked on a stored `"image": "url"` (the server check only reads `/image/url`) and now replaces a non-object parent with `{}`. The Decision field itself could pass Discord's 1024 limit ("Denied and blacklisted by <@id>" plus a 1000-character reply), which made Discord refuse the edit and left the buttons on the log; both the dashboard and the bot's button path now cut it to 1024 and skip a 26th field.

- **The config-change log read the new config.** (11-12) A save that cleared or moved `logging.config_changes` was announced only in the new channel, so clearing it hid itself. *Avoid:* a caller that saved a version passes it to `log_change`, which then also posts to that version's predecessor's channel. Events that saved nothing post to the current channel only, or moving the log would not stop them reaching the old one (review, 11-12).
- **The lockdown check looked only at main-role overwrites.** (11-12) An allow on @everyone, another role or a member beats the lock too. *Avoid:* `tshoot` reports every overwrite that allows writing, except bots' managed roles, the bot itself and ticket channels; only main-role and @everyone ones get the automatic fix, since a staff channel's allow is often on purpose. Its Apply Fix then also took the write allow off @everyone in every channel (11-13), which closes a channel everyone is meant to write in, and the bot cannot undo it. *Avoid:* an @everyone allow beside set main roles is listed with its fix marked `opt_in`; Apply Fix runs it only for the ids the user ticked (`picked`). Main-role allows are fixed as before, and with no main roles set @everyone is the main role.
- **A dashboard action skipped panic mode's mod command lock.** (11-12) Accepting an appeal unbanned during panic mode while the bot refused `t!unban`. *Avoid:* a dashboard path that does what a mod command does checks the lock (`appeals::mod_lock`, from the pending `panic_end` job) and lets only owners, Extra Owners and Trusted Admins through, like `commands::gated`. `panic::start` saves that job (without unlock) before its slow category calls, so the lock is visible from the start, rewrites its payload in place after each opened category (`end_payload`), so a crash leaves a job that closes what was opened, and deletes it when the start is undone. The rate limit was the other half (11-13): the dashboard lifted with its own Discord client, so `commands::gated` never counted it and a hacked reviewer could unban without limit. The accept is now a bot job (`appeal_accept` -> `appeals::accept_job`), which runs the log button's own `decide`: permit, panic lock, rate limit (a breach quarantines), lift, undo, verdict. The dashboard keeps its checks only to answer early, and a refusal is posted in the appeal log. *Avoid:* a dashboard write that is a mod command runs as a bot job through the command's own code, not a copy of it.
- **"Loaded" meant any IP list.** (11-12) `IpLists::loaded` was true with only the Tor list, so VPN and hosting addresses passed silently. *Avoid:* the refresh warns for each enabled list that is still empty.
- **The NSFW live lane had no per-member cap.** (11-12) One member's flood filled the shared live lane. *Avoid:* at most 4 images per member wait live (`PER_MEMBER_LIVE`); the rest go to the backlog.
- **A bot path had a lower bar than its dashboard twin.** (D67, 11-13) `t!backups load` and `clear` needed only the Custom backups permit while the dashboard asked Admin + 2FA, and `load` ran during a lock (it put back the locked permissions). The dashboard's `backups` section and `lockdown.main_roles` were Editor level although they steer anti-nuke's revert, panic's restore and what the monitors guard. *Avoid:* a change that steers anti-nuke takes anti-nuke's bar (Owner/EO + fresh 2FA) on every path: the bot command (`commands::backup_cmd`), the bot settings panel (`main_roles` while a monitor is on), `put_section`, rollback and import (`api::main_roles_bar`). A restore refuses during a lock or panic in `backup::load`, which the dashboard's job goes through too. Deleting one backup is a clear done in steps (S-h3 2, ten deletes empty the store), so `t!backups delete`, `/backups delete` and the dashboard's delete have the same bar, and they and `clear` refuse during a lock or panic (`backup::locked_or_panic`, `recovery::busy`). The Backups settings form is read-only below Owner.

**A deleted main role made "Move to main roles" a dead end.** (owner decision, 11-15)
The Security score fix refused to run with "a main role no longer exists" when one saved main role was deleted in Discord, without naming the role or a way forward. Root cause: saved role lists keep ids of deleted roles (nothing cleans them), and the fix required every id to exist. *Avoid:* skip saved roles that no longer exist and act on the rest; when none is left, say where to pick them again (Lockdown > Main roles). Searched: the other dashboard fixes and the lockdown plan already work from the live role list.

**A second way to change something had a weaker check than the main way.** (security audit, 11-16)
Four cases of one pattern. (1) Chat config edits (`t!` settings rows) took any typed channel or role id, while the dashboard's `check_ids` refuses ids of other servers, so a log route could point at another server's channel. `t!logging` (ticket transcripts) and `t!pingrole` had the same gap. (2) The hold whitelist flag `quarantine` on a permit keeps members out of the quarantine hold, the same effect as the Owner-only quarantine whitelist, but `antinuke_exempt` and the Permits page lock left it out, so a Trusted Admin could grant it. (3) Backup create needed only Editor level and the backups permit perm, worked during a lock or panic, and deleted backups past Max Images, while backup delete needs Owner or Extra Owner, 2FA and no lock or panic: ten creates were a clear. Root cause: a rule was put on the path that was built first, not on the state it protects. *Avoid:* when you add or review a path that changes some state, list every other path to the same state (dashboard, chat, slash, MCP, jobs) and give it the same check. Now: chat `resolve` and the two ticket commands check ids against this server (removing takes an id as given); `quarantine` is in `antinuke_exempt` and the UI `owner_only`; backup create is Owner or Extra Owner (owner decision 11-16, no 2FA) on the dashboard and in chat, `backup::save` refuses a person's backup during a lock, panic or nuke (also when the queued job runs), and only automatic backups delete the oldest. `t!whitelist` now takes a role or webhook only from this server too, and `backup::save` checks after its snapshot, since panic can start during the reads. Later the same day: automatic backups also skip a lock (its overwrites would become the restore source), `save` refuses every backup during a lock, panic or nuke, and a rollback runs `check_ids` and `check_held_roles` on every section it changes (an old version could name another server's channel). Searched: MCP writes (run as the bot owner through the same routes), Auto Setup (gives no whitelist flags).
- **A Message Builder access refusal answered 400.** (Audit 11-17, e2e matrix, 2026-11-17) The "pings members" and "sends as a webhook" refusals for staff below Trusted Admin were `BadRequest`, so the access matrix could not tell them from bad input. **Rule:** a refusal because of who the caller is answers 403 (`ApiError::Forbidden`); 400 is only for input that is wrong for everyone. The matrix in `tests/e2e/deep.js` now runs in CI, and any answer other than 401/403 to a refused user fails it.

- **A server error said only "internal error".** (owner decision, 11-18) Fix All showed "Staff cannot use their channels (internal error)"; the cause (Discord 404 on a permission overwrite) was only in the dashboard log, with nothing that tied that log line to the toast. **Rule:** `ApiError::Internal` answers "Server error, code XXXXXXXX. The cause is in the dashboard log." (a Discord cause `internal_text` recognises: its plain text plus " (code XXXXXXXX)") and logs the full cause with `error_id=XXXXXXXX` inside the request span (method, path, server id), so `logs_tail` (dashboard, contains the code) finds the line. The code is random per error and carries nothing internal. Errors with a cause the user can act on keep their own text (`BadRequest`, `Forbidden`, `TooMany`, `Busy`). A log field named `code=` after `?`/`&`, or a JSON `"code"`, is masked by `logs_tail`, so the field is `error_id`.

- **A dashboard change was recorded only in a Discord channel.** (security review, owner decision 11-18) `log_change` posted the line to the config-changes log channel and kept nothing, so a server with no such channel (or a deleted one) had no record of who changed what. *Avoid:* `log_change` takes who made the change (`by`, 0 for Brim itself) and stores every line in `dashboard_audit` before it posts (migration 4031, 90 days, removed with the server's data); the dashboard role may insert but not update those rows. A new record of who did what is stored first and posted second.

- **A Tickets save refused "<id> is not a channel or role of this server" for an id the page had loaded.** (owner decision, 11-19) The bot clears `tickets.category` when its category is deleted. A page loaded before that still held the old id; `put_section` ran `check_ids` (id not in the stored config, not in the server) before the version check, so the stale page got a refusal instead of "reload". *Avoid:* `put_section` answers `Conflict` before any other check when `expected_version` is not the newest; every section page goes through it, so no page can write an id the stored config dropped. A check that compares new with stored values runs only after the version is current.

**Sessions, public links and the edge trusted too much (security review, 2026-11-18).**
- A session lasted 7 days whether used or not, kept the same id through a 2FA step-up, and stored the Discord OAuth token as plain text. *Avoid:* `dash_session.last_seen_at` (migration 4032) ends a login after 24 hours without use (refreshed at most every 5 minutes); a step-up moves the session to a new id and leaves the old id 30 s without the step-up (`totp_verify`), so a copied cookie never gains it; the token is stored with `auth::seal_token` (key stream from the client secret and the session id). The cookie is always Secure: the dashboard refuses to start with an `http://` URL other than localhost.
- `CF-Connecting-IP` was believed from any sender: a container or a host process could pass web verification's alt-account and VPN checks with a made-up address. *Avoid:* `auth::edge_only` keeps the header only on a connection from the `cloudflared` container (DNS name, looked up again after 30 s); new dashboard code that needs the visitor's address reads that header after this layer. The MCP server reads it raw, for its login rate limit only (a forger inside the compose network gains nothing more there).
- The rate limiter keyed only on the `sid` cookie, so no cookie or a random one per request was never limited. *Avoid:* every route also takes from a per-address bucket (4x the session budget), `brim_core::ratelimit`; the MCP login routes have their own (`limit_login`).
- Rescue claims: 5 per user in memory only, a key without an authenticator worked alone, and every failed claim posted a log line. *Avoid:* wrong codes count on the key row (10 an hour, then the key waits; a wrong key cannot raise it, so nobody without the key can lock the owner out: Owner decision 2026-11-18, no per-server lockout); keys without a secret are refused and the owner is asked for a new one; failed claims post once per server per hour with the count of the hour before (`claim_failed`).
- Viewers read every holder of Extra Owner, Trusted Admin and each permit, also in history diffs, and could export it. *Avoid:* `api::hide_holders` on every route that returns permits to a Viewer (`get_config`, `diff_versions`), and the Overview score leaves out the ownership-change rows for Viewers (they name paused and kept holders); check any new route that names permit holders. Export is for Editor and up. Session and address buckets share one map, so their keys carry a prefix (`sid:`, `ip:`): a cookie `sid=ip:<address>` drained another address's bucket (security pass).
- The MCP login limit (first 20 at once) failed `crates/mcp/tests/oauth.rs`: test requests carry no `CF-Connecting-IP`, so they all share one bucket, and the local run's output filter hid the failure. *Avoid:* a new limit on a route with tests is checked against the number of requests those tests send; read the whole `test result` list of every crate, not the first failure only.
- Raid links never expired and uploaded pictures were served by their hash from any server. *Avoid:* raid pages and `raid_log` rows end after 30 days (hourly sweep); a new upload gets a random link (`media.token`), older uploads keep their hash link so posted messages keep their picture.
- **A saved log or command reply without `{text}` dropped the bot's own text.** (All embeds editable, 2026-11-20) `{text}` was only an important variable (red warning, save allowed), and `events::frame_over` laid the saved frame over the log as it was, so a frame with no `{text}`, or with it in a second embed, content or a link, sent only the frame: the log details or the command answer were lost without a notice. *Avoid:* every entry whose template has `{text}` gets it as `Def::required` (with `Def::what`, the editor's "what Brim puts there"); `botmsg::missing_text` looks for it only where every sender shows it (the first embed's title, description, fields, footer and author text, since `bot_embed` and frames send the first embed only). The dashboard save and kit import refuse such a copy (`botmsgs::problem`), a backup load leaves it out (`msgbackup::bot_messages_to_load`), and `botmsg::render` sends the default for a stored copy without it, so copies saved before the check, or brought back by Undo, can never drop the text. The UI notice (`ui/src/botmsgs.rs` `missing`) repeats the rule for display only. Searched: every `botmsg::render` caller (`reply::bot_embed`, `dm_msg`, `frame_embeds`, `stored` sends in `reply.rs`, dashboard `discord.rs` and the test DM), every `bot_messages` insert (save, undo, kit import, backup load); MCP has no bot message write.
- **Short replies were fixed sentences in code, outside the Bot messages page.** (All embeds editable, 2026-11-20) Verification side replies and `/tag` answers were `Ok("...".into)` strings and `format!`, so no server could reword them. *Avoid:* a short reply is an entry `reply.<area>.<name>` (one message = one entry, default = today's sentence as message text, values as named variables) and every sender calls `reply::say(app, gid, key, vars)`, which returns text, embeds and `edited`; channel sends use `commands::say_said`, interaction replies put the same text and embeds in the response. A command's reply goes through that command's `cmd.<command>` frame only while the server has no edited version of the entry, so an earlier frame keeps working and an edited entry is never framed twice (`commands::send_reply(.., frame)`). `botmsg::uses_stored` is the one test of "is the edited version used" for `render` and `say`. A test scans the source for `"reply.` ids (unknown key = panic at send time) and fails on an entry no code sends. A member-typed value (a tag name) is only a variable: it is filled in one pass, never searched for more variables. Searched: every `Step::Say`, `verify::setup` caller (`commands.rs`, `ensure_channel`, `adopt`), every string `tags::command` returned. Still fixed text, for later packages: the owner/Extra Owner verify answers, the DM-failed, "Check your DMs" and "Verification done." lines (`verify.rs`), the dashboard's own tag result text, `Slash commands are disabled` in `slash.rs` and `general.rs` (same words as `reply.tag.slash_off`, to merge into one entry in P7). Not live tested.
- **Ticket side replies were `error("...")` embeds in code.** (All embeds editable, 2026-11-20) Panel button answers, staff relay errors, the closing notice and the DM-ticket refusals were literals, so a server could not reword them. *Avoid:* each is an entry `reply.ticket.<name>` (table `TICKET_REPLIES` in `botmsg.rs`: one embed, red for an error, blue for a notice, default = the old sentence), sent with `tickets::say_key` (channel) or `reply::say` (interaction answer, `panel::answer`). A refusal that is passed up as `&'static str` (`gate`, `open`, `refusal`) now carries the entry key, and the caller says it. Only the words changed: every permit, blacklist, pause, lockdown, quarantine and cap check runs as before and picks the same key. `workflow::summary` returns `None` for a non-ticket channel and the caller says `reply.ticket.not_ticket` (one entry for the two old copies). Panel ("this server") and DM ("that server") refusals stay separate entries: they talk to the member from different places. Ticket text not converted yet has an owner package in the plan ( section 12 on tommybot-data). Searched: every `error(`/`info(` in `tickets/*.rs`, converted or listed in that section. Not live tested.
- **Lock notices and short lock replies were literals in `lockdown.rs`.** (All embeds editable, 2026-11-20) The public "The server is locked" notice, the "Lockdown Updates" post and the lock command's short answers (no permit, nothing is locked, update posted, already covered, nothing to unlock) could not be reworded. *Avoid:* entries `reply.lock.*` (`botmsg::lock_replies`); the two notices are embeds whose `{text}` is the list of updates the bot builds (`lockdown::updates_text`, empty until staff send one, so `{text}` is required but may render empty), the rest are message text. `lockdown::update` returns `reply::Said` (prefix: `commands::say_said`, slash: text + embeds), `fail_key` builds the "Lock Result:" reply from an entry: an edited version with an embed replaces the result embed, one with text only goes in the usual embed. The permit checks and `commands::gated` are unchanged; only the text they return is an entry. The notice default is byte-equal to the old one (test `lock_notice_numbers_updates_past_the_dropped_ones`). Lock result, loading and overview embeds and `LOCK_TYPES` are command answers, owned by later packages (plan section 13). Searched: every `locked_embed`, `fail(` and `lockdown::update` caller. Not live tested.
- **The confirm prompt, Undo button and menu answers were literals.** (All embeds editable, 2026-11-20) "Do you want to proceed?", its Working/Cancelled/timed out/answered lines, the Undo refusals (no case, button does not fit, case no longer holds the member, need a permit, another moderator acting, something went wrong) and the Message Builder menu's "Slow down" and "no longer in use" could not be reworded. *Avoid:* entries `reply.confirm.*`, `reply.undo.*`, `reply.menu.*` (`botmsg::confirm_and_button_replies`). The prompt is an embed whose required `{text}` is the target list the bot builds (`{seconds}` optional); the rest is message text sent with `reply::say` as content plus embeds. A sentence that goes inside a bigger result ("Command failed", "Failed to ban", the lost ticket permit in `closeall`) uses `reply::say_line`, which falls back to the default sentence when an edited version has no text, so a result line is never empty. Code-built text that is not an entry (`modcmds::run` results, `cmdcfg::button` refusals) becomes a `Said` with `From<String>`. Only the words changed: in `undo::handle` the no-permit stop still comes before any case lookup and before `Acting::claim`, and `press` keeps the command settings check, the `not_current` check for permit holders and `modcmds::run`; the click-time `tickets` permit re-check in `confirm.rs` is unchanged (DB test `a_press_needs_the_permit_of_its_command` passes). The prompt default is byte-equal to the old one (test `ten_second_question`). Searched: every string literal in `confirm.rs`, `undo.rs`, `msgmenu.rs`; left as is: the Undo reason "Undo of case #N" (a case reason) and the Action Log line on the mod log (moderation log details, package D1). Not live tested.

**The bot update alert went out titled "Raider List".** (owner decision, 11-20)
The dashboard queued every bot owner DM as a `raider_notify` job, and the bot sent each one in the editable `dm.raider_notify` entry. So the update alert and the MCP token alerts carried the Raider List title, and anyone who edited that entry could blank or rewrite them. Root cause: one job kind (made for Raider List notices) was reused for a security alert without its own message. *Avoid:* the update alert and the MCP token made/revoked alerts are fixed text in code (`raiders::alert_owner`, `fixed: "update"` or `"mcp"` in the job), each in its own embed ("Bot update", "MCP token") with the mention and user ID of who did it. They are never editable entries and not on the Bot messages page (owner decision 11-20, for security). Later "all embeds editable" packages must keep it that way. The job still carries `text`, so a bot build without `fixed` sends it in the old entry: the running bot sends the alert before it restarts, so the one update that installs this change still shows the old title once. Searched: by the owner's choice the Raider List confirm/verify alerts stay in `dm.raider_notify`, and the panic, join raid and voice raid alerts stay in the editable `dm.owner_alert`.
- **Report, case, note, whitelist and catch channel replies were literals.** (All embeds editable, 2026-11-20) The Report Message refusals and its "flagged and sent to the staff team" embed, the report button answers, the case and note replies of `cases`/`proof`/`note` (no case, reason updated, closed, proofs full, note not found, updated, removed, DM notices), the whitelist command's short refusals and the catch channel warning could not be reworded. *Avoid:* entries `reply.report.*`, `reply.case.*`, `reply.note.*`, `reply.whitelist.*`, `reply.catch.warning` (`botmsg::report_case_replies`). Report answers are `Said` (`reports::tell` sends text and embeds, ephemeral); mod command results take the entry's text with `reply::say_line` (`modcmds::say`), so they still go through the command frame. One sentence, one entry: `reply.undo.no_case` is merged into `reply.case.no_case`, a stored edit of the old key is no longer used (it existed for one hour). The limits in the note, reason and proof replies are written in the default; a test ties them to `NOTE_MIN`, `NOTE_MAX` and `CASE_TEXT_MAX`. Two traps: (1) the Close case button knows its press worked by comparing the reply with `modcmds::closed_text`, so that is now async and renders the same entry for the same server; never compare a reply with a hard-coded sentence. (2) The catch channel skips posting when its warning is already there, found by the embed title; an edited version may change the title, so the check also takes the edited title or text, and the old "Catch channel" title still counts (edits apply only from then on). The whitelist command is a security command (`cmd.whitelist` is locked), so its replies are locked entries until the security package  opens them with its guards. Only the words changed: the report permit, `gated`, `cmdcfg::button`, log channel and `permits::check` steps, the whitelist 2FA and permit checks run as before. Left for later packages: the report and AutoMod report logs, their Action Log lines and the User Info embeds, the case summary `reply::case_text`, the note card and the proof list, the Interactive Whitelist prompt and result embeds, "Unknown case type" and the whitelist usage line, the Report Message form title (a form, not a message). Not live tested.
- **Ticket command results, staff notices and the paused DM were literals.** (All embeds editable P6b, 2026-11-20) Claim, unclaim, reassign, priority, ticket type and note results, the notices everyone in the ticket sees ("claimed this ticket", "no longer claimed", "now handled by", type set or changed), the blacklist, snippet, ping role, logging, toggle, greeting/closing, enable/disable, `ticketsetup`/`category` results, the `closeall` result and the "Ticket Creation Disabled" DM could not be reworded. *Avoid:* entries `reply.ticket.*` (more rows in `botmsg::TICKET_REPLIES`, one embed each, red for a refusal), `reply.closeall.*` (text lines of the confirm result) and `ticket.paused` (`{reason}`, server footer and time added by code like the other `ticket.*` embeds). `workflow` results are a `Line` (entry key and values) made where the result is known and said in the server's words later; `Ok(None)` means nothing more to say (the dead "The claim was removed." / "The ticket was reassigned." texts were never shown and are gone). A sentence the code used to build from parts is one entry per case (`command_only_on`/`_off`, `note_no_log_deleted`/`_kept`, `closeall.partly_anon`), never a variable holding English words. The ticket log lines (`workflow::log_update`) keep their own text, so an edited notice never changes the log. Left for P7: the ticket permit refusals, "not set up yet", the panel ticket relay refusal, the usage lines of `reply`, `snippetadd` and `reassign` and the `tickets_cap_problem` cap text (shared with the dashboard save). Left for P9: the DM type pick's "This ticket is closed." when no server is known. Not live tested.
- **Errors, usage lines and permit refusals were literals.** (All embeds editable, 2026-11-20) "You lack the ... permit", the ticket, settings, invite, appeal, NSFW scan and Raider List refusals, every "Usage: ..." line, the parsers' wrong use texts ("missing user", unknown case type, lock types, too long duration), "Unknown command", "Slash commands are disabled" (now one entry for tags, general and moderation slash commands) and the ticket snippet and blacklist caps could not be reworded. *Avoid:* entries in `botmsg::error_replies` (`reply.error.*`, `reply.settings.*`, `reply.invite.*`, `reply.appeal.*`, `reply.nsfw.*`, `reply.raider.*`) and `reply.ticket.*` rows; a parser keeps returning its text and the send point puts it in `reply.error.wrong_use` with `reply::say_wrong` (`reply::UNKNOWN_COMMAND` gets its own entry); a usage line goes in `{text}` of `reply.error.usage` or `reply.ticket.usage`. Mistake found while building: `build` marks `{text}` required only when the first embed shows it, so a message-text entry with `{text}` got no required placeholder and an edited version without it would send the reply without the usage line. A message-text entry sets `required` and `what` itself (as feed posts do), and the scan test checks it.
- **Security texts could not be reworded, and a long author name made Discord refuse a whole message.** (All embeds editable, 2026-11-20) The anti-nuke, panic and join raid logs and commands, the whitelist command and replies, and the owner security, invite back, rescue key, MCP decision and 2FA code DMs were locked. *Avoid:* they are entries with `Def::security` (group "Security"): only Owner and Extra Owners save, revert, undo or import them (`botmsgs::may_edit`), Revert all leaves them, and each change is an audit line with the old and new version (`change_line`). A required placeholder of a security text counts only as plain text in the first embed's title or description, within its first 10 lines (`botmsg::fact_places`, `visible`: not in a spoiler, a link target, `<...>` or a code block's language tag; a footer, author or field does not count), and a security edit cannot hold control, zero-width or text direction characters (`hides_facts`); the logs need `{title}` and `{text}`, the rescue DM `{name}` and `{page}`, the 2FA DM `{code}`. `render` sends the default when an edited version's own words push a fact past Discord's limits (`keeps_facts` looks only in those same places, so a spare copy of the fact in a footer spoiler does not count; a value too long for the default too keeps the edit). Review found: the first draft took a placeholder anywhere `shown` looks and searched every string at send time, so `{title}` in the author line under a calm title, or the fact cut from the description but kept in a footer spoiler, passed; and `frame_over` dropped a frame's author for every log and command. The 2FA and MCP decision DMs stay per server (owner decision 2026-11-20); the 2FA DM is sent with no server, so a server's edit of it never shows. The dashboard's `Discord::dm` reads a bot-wide entry's copy under guild 0 even when the DM names a server. Mistake found while building: `botmsg::limit` cut title, description, footer and fields but not the author name, so an edited version with an author over 256 characters made Discord refuse the DM or reply outright; it is cut to 256 now, for every entry.
- **DMs with no server were literals, and the data request DM was locked.** (All embeds editable, 2026-11-20) The DM ticket commands (usage lines, confirmation on/off, "No server found", the server menu with its option lines and placeholder, "request expired", "cancelled", "wait a few seconds", "message was deleted", the DM type pick's "This ticket is closed.") are sent before a server is chosen, so no server's edited version can apply, and the data request DM could not be edited at all. *Avoid:* these are bot-wide entries `reply.dm.*` (`botmsg::outside_replies`, group "Outside a server (bot owner)"), listed in `botmsg::BOT_WIDE`, one copy under guild 0 that only the bot owner changes; senders call `reply::say_wide` (`tickets::say_dm` for a channel, `say_wide_line` for the text inside the menu). A test ties the group to `BOT_WIDE`, so a new outside entry cannot miss the list. The two outside DMs that state facts about the recipient (`dm.data_request`: what was shown or deleted, `dm.raider_notify`) are `Def::strict`: the P8 fact rules apply (`{text}` as plain text in the first embed's title or description, no hiding characters, the default is sent when an edit loses it), without the Security chip or Owner/EO rule, because the bot owner edits them. Searched: every `error(`/`info(`/`emb(` in `tickets/dm.rs`, every `dm_msg` and `frame_embeds` caller, `msgbackup` (skips guild 0 entries), kit import (skips outside entries). Only the words changed. Left (not P9): the dashboard shows an outside entry as editable to a server Admin, who gets the server's refusal on save. Not live tested.
- **A security text changed without a 2FA code, and a backup load replaced security texts without a log line.** (Security text 2FA, 2026-11-20) P8 gave security texts the Owner bar but no step-up, so a stolen Owner session (or an MCP call) could reword an anti-nuke log; and `msgbackup::load` swapped every `bot_messages` row with only "loaded backup" in the log. *Avoid:* `botmsgs::may_edit` asks `require(..., step_up = true)` for a security text, so save, revert and undo all need it; the kit import checks it separately (it does not use `may_edit`) when the applied part holds a security text, a preview needs none. Every new route that writes `bot_messages` must go through `may_edit` or make the same check. The backup load already needs 2FA (dashboard `recovery::load`, command `fresh_2fa`); it now writes one `dashboard_audit` line per security text whose copy changed (`botmsg::change_line`, shared with the dashboard), under the user who asked for the load, in the load's transaction. The bot does not post these lines to the config change log channel; the Backup Loaded server log covers the load. Not live tested.

- **Moderation logs and command answers built their facts in code, and a fields-only log had no place to put a frame.** (All embeds editable, 2026-11-20) The case log (`Case`, `Type`, `Moderator`, `Target`, `Duration`, `Reason`, and a purge's channel, counts and filter), case edits and proof logs, note logs, "Case Deleted", "Old Cases Removed", the result embed of a punishment command ("Ban result:"), the short result for several members and the short case line could only be framed around one `{text}`; the case log has no text at all, so no frame could change it. *Avoid:* the 13 keys in `botmsg::FACTS` (`log.mod_command.*`, `reply.mod.*`) are the whole message with named placeholders (`{case}`, `{kind}`, `{member}`, `{moderator}`, `{duration}`, `{reason}`, `{changes}`, `{when}` ...), `Def::details`; they are `Def::strict` (P8 fact rules, default sent when an edit loses a fact, old and new version in the audit line) but not Security. A log names its entry with `Log::entry` (the case log has no title to find it by) and its values with `Log::var` (a later value replaces an earlier one: a purge fills what the case log left empty); `events::framed_with` lays an entry of facts over the log as a whole (fields replace), a frame still adds its fields. The code builds each log itself until a server edits the entry, and a test lays the default template beside every builder (`mod_texts_tests`), so the two cannot drift. A copy made before (a frame around `{text}`) keeps working: `{text}` then holds every fact (`missing_text`, the editor's `missing`). Facts may stay in fields where the default has them (`fact_strings`). `render` now drops a field whose value is empty (Discord refuses the whole message for one): a case without a duration shows no Duration field. Answers: `ModTexts::load` reads the three `reply.mod.*` copies where a command's answer is made, `result_embeds_with`/`summary_with` use them; the text each command builds for `Applied.text` stays code, because the result embed and the confirm path read it back (`Case #N: kind <@id>` first line, then notes). Mistake found while building: a variable an entry lists but a sender forgets stays as literal `{name}` in the log; the case log sets every purge variable to empty. By design: an edited answer is still wrapped by the command's own `cmd.<command>` frame (its own entry on the same page, shown to the server as a separate edit), as a log entry of facts sits inside the group frame; the short replies that become one whole entry (`say_said`) are the exception because nothing else wraps them. Left: the "Add Reason" pointer embed under a result without a reason, and the purge note posted in the channel, keep code text. Not live tested.
- **Server event logs from the audit log built their facts in code, and the values never reached the sender.** (All embeds editable part 1, 2026-11-21) Every audit-built log (member, role, channel, server, invite, webhook, emoji, sticker, event, thread, pin) could only be framed around `{text}`, and `render::audit` returned a finished `Embed`, so a log's `Log::var` values were lost on the way through `Entry` and `LogMessage`; `send_event_log` dropped them the same way. *Avoid:* the types in `botmsg::AUDIT_LOGS` are entries of facts (`is_facts`, like `FACTS`) with one template (`audit_entries`): `{by}`, `{channel}`, `{target}`, `{when}`, `{reason}` and `{changes}` (the changes, the "Not Shown" line and the extra information as one block, which `render::audit` cuts from the lines after the reason). `Entry` and `LogMessage` carry `vars`, `events::send_entry` passes them to `framed_with`, and every builder sets every placeholder, empty ones too. A test in `render.rs` lays the default beside the built log for an overwrite, an update with a reason and no executor, a webhook and a prune. A variable name means the same in every area: `channel` and `changes` got general editor names. Review found: an entry of facts counted an old frame only when `{text}` was plain text in the title or description, so a frame from before with `{text}` in its footer, author or a field was skipped without a word; `missing_text` and `keeps_facts` now accept `{text}` wherever the old frame rules did (D1 entries too). An empty audit reason is no reason (the template drops a bare label). Left for D2 part 2: the gateway logs (message edit, delete and bulk delete, member join and leave, voice, polls, publish, event subscribe, boost level, automatic thread archive, member renamed, invite used, user name and avatar). Not live tested.
- **The gateway and bot-made event logs built their facts in code, and two senders dropped a log's values.** (All embeds editable part 2, 2026-11-21) Message edit, delete and bulk delete, poll, publish, member join and leave, voice, event sign-up, boost level, automatic thread archive, Member Renamed, Timeout Ended and the user name and avatar logs (Invite Used came with D3) could only be framed around `{text}`. *Avoid:* they are entries of facts listed in `botmsg::EVENT_LOGS` (group, title, lines: `Label|value` or a line as written); `event_entries` builds each template, takes every placeholder a line shows as a variable, and every one shown outside a link target, except `{when}`, as required. A placeholder that is not in `FACT_VARS` panics when the list is built, so a typo fails every test. Builders use `Log::fact(label, var, value)`, which leaves an empty line out and still sets the variable. The logger's builders return a `Log`, not an `Embed`, so `Entry`, `HeldLeave` and `HeldArchive` keep the values until `LogMessage` (`Log::parts`). In tests, `Logger::handle`, `flush` and `handle_voice` check every log of facts they make against its default (`reads_as_default`), so a new line in a builder that the template lacks fails the test that makes that log. Mistakes found while building: `send_log_rows` and `send_log_pinging` built the embed and passed no values (as `send_event_log` did before part 1), so an edited entry sent through it would have shown `{name}`; and a line whose only value is an empty code span (`` `{case}` ``) was kept as a bare label, which `fill_text` now drops. Not live tested.

- **Automod, Discord AutoMod rule, Join Gate and verification logs built their facts in code.** (All embeds editable, 2026-11-21) The filter and spam logs, the rule approval logs, Invite Used, the Join Gate log and the verification logs could only be framed around one `{text}`, and the logs whose title holds a name (`*alex has been warned!*`, `alex's Verification Result:`) or that share one title (five different "Verification" logs, two "Rule Not Approved" logs) all fell into one entry, so one edit had to fit texts that say different things. *Avoid:* each is its own entry of facts in `botmsg::FACTS` (`botmsg::d3_entries`), same pattern as D1: the builder names its entry with `Log::entry` when the title cannot, sets every placeholder with `Log::var` (empty ones too), and `d3_texts_tests` lays every default beside the builder. A sentence that differs per call (`taken_log`) became one entry per sentence (`given::BOT_TAKEN`, `OFFLINE_TAKEN`, `CHANNEL_TAKEN`), not a `{what}` placeholder, so the words stay editable. The message content in the filter logs is in a spoiler, so `{content}` cannot be a required fact there (the P8 rules refuse a fact in a spoiler); the member, filter and channel are. Left for D4 (security groups): the panic "Server Locked" log in `automod.rs`, the join raid logs in `joingate.rs`, `users_only.rs` and `verify/deleted.rs` (anti-nuke log). Not in any package yet: "NSFW Image Found" (`nsfw.rs`) and "Webhooks Deleted" (`webhooks.rs`). Mistake found in review: the "could not read the rule" log has its own entry but the same title as "Rule Not Approved", so `framed_with` fell back to that entry's edit and sent the wrong explanation without "Changed by"; a log with its own entry now skips a typed entry of facts (a typed frame, like the old "Verification" one, still applies). Each `FACT_VARS` name is one meaning for all entries: the Join Gate refusal is `{refused}` because `{failed}` is D1's count. Same as D1 by design: an old frame that kept `{text}` only in a footer, field or spoiler is now judged by the fact rules and the default is sent. Not live tested.
- **The general command answers built their facts in code, and a page turn dropped the command's saved frame.** (All embeds editable, 2026-11-21) The answers of `info` (user not in the server, member, staff view), `roleinfo`, `serverinfo` (facts page and role pages), `about` (three pages), `ping` and `invites` (stats, leaderboard, who invited, status) could only be framed around `{text}`. The page buttons (`settings::handle`, kinds `about`, `ginfo`, `cmds`, `cases.*`, `tkblock`, `tksnip` and the settings panels) rebuilt the page without the `cmd.<command>` frame, so an edited frame vanished on the second page. *Avoid:* each answer is an entry of facts in `botmsg::ANSWERS` (`answer_entries`, one embed per message: a page is its own entry); the code passes values only, through `reply::answer`, which fills every placeholder the answer did not set with empty, so a variant's missing lines go and no raw `{name}` reaches Discord. One entry holds every variant of its answer because a line whose placeholders are all empty and that is left a bare `**Label:**` is dropped; a line of free words with an empty placeholder keeps its words (an edit should put an optional value behind a label). Values in a field carry their own code style (an empty field must be blank to be dropped); values in a line do not. `answer_texts_tests` keeps the former builders as the reference and compares every default with them, moves every placeholder of every entry and checks a copy that loses the required fact sends the default. A page turn now lays the command's frame like the first page (`settings::page_command`). Titles that held a name use the name's placeholder (`{member.username}'s Info:`), not `{title}`. Left: the plain refusals of `info` and `invites` ("Nothing found", "Only members with a permit can see ...", the link maker's refusals) stay code text; the words inside the flag blocks (`Spam`, `Managed`, `No Avatar`) are part of one value. Not live tested.

- **Lockdown, auto slowmode, backup and scheduled action logs and the lock command answers built their words in code.** (All embeds editable, 2026-11-21) Server Locked/Unlocked, Unlock Incomplete, the two "Scheduled Action Failed" logs (startup check and job give-up, one title), Scheduled Action Dropped, Backup Created/Loaded/Not Loaded, the four auto slowmode logs, and the lock answers ("Brim Lockdown:" loading and result, channel locked/unlocked, "Lock Result:", "Active lockdown modes:") could only be framed or not edited at all. *Avoid:* logs are entries of facts as in D1 (`botmsg::d5_entries`); the startup check gets its own entry so it cannot take the job give-up's edit. The answers are entries of facts too (`botmsg::lock_answers`, keys `reply.lock.*`): each builder returns its embed with its entry and values (`lockdown::Entry`), and `lockdown::own` swaps in the server's edited version only when there is one (`reply::say(..).edited`), so the code keeps building the default and `d5_texts_tests` lays each default beside it. The compact text of a lock answer (`Reply.text`) stays code, like `Applied.text` in D1. A short reply sentence that a server edited with an embed still replaces the whole "Lock Result:" embed (P4 rule); without one the "Lock Result:" entry frames it. Not live tested.

- **Ticket, report, appeal and catch channel logs built their facts in code, and the dashboard posted its logs without any edited version.** (All embeds editable, 2026-11-21) New Ticket, Ticket Closed, the five "Ticket Updated" sentences (claimed, claim removed, given to someone, type changed, type set: one title, one entry each), the message report log (no title, its headline is the author line), the report-channel "Discord AutoMod Report", "Appeal Not Accepted", the appeal log the dashboard posts, and the two catch channel logs could only be framed around `{text}`, and the appeal log and the dashboard's "Lockdown Update" ignored a server's edit completely, because `Discord::post_log` sent `log.build` as it was. *Avoid:* entries of facts as in D1-D5 (`botmsg::d7_entries`, test `d7_texts_tests`, the dashboard's in `appeals.rs`); `events::send_private` (ticket logs) now takes a `Log` and passes its entry and values, as `send_log` does; `framed_log` does the same for the report logs. `over`, `bot_embed` and `parts` moved to `tommybot_logging::overlay` so the dashboard lays an edit over its logs the same way (`discord::framed`: own entry, then the group; the copy's fields are added, so the appellant's answers stay). Link fields are never filled from placeholders, so a copy's author line keeps the log's icon (the reported member's avatar) unless the copy sets one. Footers, the ticket closer's author line and the report thumbnail stay as the code builds them unless an edit sets its own. Fixed on the way: a Ticket Closed "Changes" list whose first line was over 1024 bytes made an empty field, which Discord refuses for the whole log; the field is now left out. Left: the dashboard's "Configuration Changed" log has no entry at all (settings, D8 or later); the permit sync logs go to the anti-nuke log and wait for D4's security pass. Not live tested.
- **Security logs built their facts in code, and an entry of facts with `{text}` let a frame skip every fact rule.** (All embeds editable, 2026-11-21) The anti-nuke, panic, join raid, permit sync, verification channel, log channel, NSFW and webhook spam logs (about 50 texts, many sharing the title "Anti Nuke") could only be framed around `{title}` and `{text}`. *Avoid:* one entry per text in `botmsg::SECURITY_LOGS` (group Security, so the Owner/EO, 2FA and backup audit rules of P8 and #416 apply), every placeholder shown is required and the title stays `{title}`. Senders call `overlay::security_log(title, key, values)`, which renders the default, so the sent log and the default cannot drift; the complex builders (`antinuke::report_log`, `panic::report`, `joingate::trigger_log`) name their entry with `Log::entry` and `fact`/`var`. `d4_texts_tests` keeps each old builder beside the new one. `over` now fills any placeholder a log did not set with empty, so a forgotten value never reaches Discord as `{name}`. A default over 10 lines (the nuke report) may keep its facts up to its own length (`fact_places`). Mistake found in the security pass: the first draft offered `{text}` on these entries "for old frames", as D3 do; `is_frame` then took any copy showing `{text}` as an old frame and skipped `hides_facts` and every required fact, so `||{text}||` under a calm title passed. Root cause: the frame exception is meant for copies saved before an entry had facts, and a security entry of facts never had such copies. Rule: a security entry of facts never offers `{text}` (a test checks every one). Second mistake: NSFW and webhook spam logs are security entries in the ordinary automod group, and `framed_with` fell back to the `log.auto_mod` frame any Admin may edit; a security log now never takes a non-security group's frame. Main was not affected by either (no security entry was an entry of facts before D4). Old copies saved on the NSFW and webhook spam entries before D4 lack the required facts, so the default is sent. Not live tested.
- **The security command panels could only be framed, and the whitelist menu and result skipped every edit.** (All embeds editable, security command answers, 2026-11-21) `t!antinuke`, `t!ancaps`, `t!anpanic`, `t!antinukerestore` and `t!joinraid` built their rows in code, so the `cmd.<command>` frame could only add text around them; the whitelist menu and its saved result were posted straight with `create_message` and the interaction answer, so not even `cmd.whitelist` reached them. *Avoid:* each panel is one entry of facts in `botmsg::PANELS` (`reply.panel.<command>`, group Security, so Owner/EO, 2FA and the audit line apply), and the whitelist answers are `reply.whitelist.prompt` and `reply.whitelist.saved`. Every value and every row number is a required placeholder: the number is its own placeholder named as the code (`{3}` fills `[3]`, `{4a}` fills `[A]`), because people type it to change the setting. The on or off sign after Status (`{toggle}`) is required too, so an edit cannot leave a fixed one. No `{text}` (the D4 rule). A panel may put all its rows in one list: `fact_places` counts as many lines as the panel's default has in all. `settings::panel_vars` fills the entry from the same rows `panel` reads, and `panel_texts_tests` keeps `PANELS` equal to `settings::maps` and each default equal to `panel`; a new row in a security panel needs its line in `PANELS` too (the test fails otherwise). The `cmd.<command>` frame still lies over the panel as before. A reply a command sends itself, outside the reply helpers, gets no edit at all: send every answer through an entry or `command_reply`. Mistakes found in the security pass: the first draft offered `{prefix}` for the footer, but a server's prefix may be `||` or three backticks, and the save check reads the template before it is filled, so `{prefix}{status}{prefix}` would have hidden a fact in a spoiler; a security entry offers no placeholder whose value a server sets freely, and the footer gets the prefix after filling (`settings::with_prefix`). Second: `limit` cut only the description when an embed passed Discord's 6000 total, so an edited copy of fields only (a panel, the whitelist result) was refused by Discord and nothing was posted; it now drops the last fields until it fits (and a security text then sends its default). Searched: every security entry's placeholders for server-set text (only `{prefix}` here), every `create_message` in `whitelist.rs` and `settings`. Not live tested.
- **The other settings panels and the reply after a setting changed were still code text.** (All embeds editable, 2026-11-21) `t!statics`, `t!heat`, `t!hfilters`, `t!joingate`, `t!verification`, `t!misc` and `t!raiderlist` built their rows in code, and "✅ [7B] Name: value" was a `format!`, so only the `cmd.<command>` frame reached them. *Avoid:* the panels are entries of facts in `botmsg::SETTINGS_PANELS` (group Settings panels, same rules as `PANELS`: every row number, value and the on or off sign required), and the changed reply is `reply.settings.changed` with `{row}` and `{value}` required. A security panel's setting (join raid), a setting only the Owner and Extra Owners may change (misc, the main roles) and the Trusted Admins line use the Security copy `reply.settings.changed_security` (`settings::changed_key`), so a Trusted Admin cannot reword the answer to such a change (found in review: the first draft gave misc the general copy). The Security copy is sent without any `cmd.<command>` frame (`send_said`), because the Trusted Admins line answers `t!statics`, and `cmd.statics` is not a security text: a Trusted Admin could have put the list in a spoiler there (found in the security pass). A paged panel (`t!hfilters`) is filled whole and then cut into pages of its cards (`settings::page_of`); a page turn uses the same entry, never `panel`, or the first page and the next ones would differ. The whole filled panel goes through `limit`, so a paged panel must stay under Discord's 6000 in all (the test compares every page with `panel`, which fails if fields were dropped). The misc panel's Prefix row is the one panel value a server sets freely (`{prefix}`); it is not a security text, so it stays. Not live tested.
- **The settings error replies were code text.** (All embeds editable, 2026-11-21) The refusals of `t!<panel>` changes (not a switch, not a list, not a valid value, out of range, takes one of, not a role or channel of this server, nothing found, ambiguous, no such row, "Not saved: ..."), the 2FA replies, the main roles and Trusted Admins refusals and "Only the person who opened this panel can use it" were `format!` strings. *Avoid:* `settings::edit_for`, `resolve` and `no_such_row` return a `Refusal` (entry plus values), never a sentence; `Refusal::say` sends the server's copy and `Refusal::text` is the default (tests compare it with the old text for every row). The entries are in `botmsg::SETTINGS_ERRORS` (each with a Security copy `<key>_security`, chosen by `settings::changed_key` as for the changed reply, or, when no row was found, by whether the panel has any such row) and `botmsg::SECURITY_ERRORS` (always Security). What went wrong is a key variable (`{row}`, `{typed}`, `{min}`/`{max}`, `{options}`, `{reason}`), no entry offers `{text}`. A Security copy is sent without the `cmd.<command>` frame (`send_said`). `settings::save` keeps its `String` refusal for its other callers; the settings command uses `save_with`, whose "Not saved" also becomes an entry. `needs_2fa` is one entry for the settings, whitelist and backups replies (`needs_2fa_line` where the reply is only text). The bot-not-user refusals (`users_only::refuse`) are security entries too, one per list. Not live tested.
- **A security refusal went through a command frame a Trusted Admin may edit.** (All embeds editable security pass, 2026-11-21) The backups command's owner-only, 2FA and "not while locked or in panic" refusals and the revert command's owner refusals were plain strings sent through `cmd.backups` / `cmd.revert`, ordinary frames a Trusted Admin can edit: `||{text}||` plus a fake Confirm 2FA link would have hidden the real refusal from the owner. *Root cause:* the frame decision looked only at `Said::edited`, not at whether the text is a security text, and the slash path framed every answer. *Avoid:* such refusals are Security entries (`botmsg::SECURITY_ERRORS`), and `reply::Said::security` (set from the entry's group) keeps them out of every `cmd.<command>` frame: prefix `commands::say_said`, slash `run`'s `frame` flag (`slash::unframed`), settings `send_said`. A copy a Trusted Admin saved of `cmd.backups` no longer reaches them. A command answer that must not be framed returns a `Said`, never a `String`. Searched: every owner-only, 2FA and permit refusal in the bot. The load-while-locked refusal is checked in `backup_cmd` as well (`reply.backups.locked_load`), so `backup::load`'s own string is only for its other callers. Left for a decision: the backups load/delete/clear results and the permit and panic-lock refusals (`gated`) still use the command frame; the held-staff release refusal goes through the moderation result entry; `t!tshoot lockdown` and the Raider List report owner-only refusals are ordinary entries. Not live tested.
- **The dashboard's Configuration Changed log had no Bot messages entry.** (All embeds editable, 2026-11-22) `api::log_change` posted it with `post_log`, which knows no entries, so no server could edit it, and the general command answers (help, dashboard, invite, config), the invite link replies and the report User Info embeds were still built in code. *Root cause:* the log is not a `LogKind`, so the per-kind frames in `botmsg::logs` never covered it, and the D packages listed areas, not every sender. *Avoid:* a dashboard log goes through `discord::framed` with its own entry (`log.config_changes`) and `post_embed_with`; a command answer is an entry rendered with `reply::answer` or `reply::say`, never a `Log` or `format!` built in the handler. A refusal helper returns an entry key and its values (`invite_cmds::Reply`), and tests render the default to compare with the old text. Searched: every `Log::new` and `post_log` in the bot and dashboard; the rest have a log type or group frame. Not live tested.

**A public endpoint takes GitHub and GitLab events from the whole internet.** (feeds F4, 11-20, by design)
`POST /hooks/feed/<id>` needs no login, so anyone can call it with any body. *Avoid:* only the address limit comes before the signature (a per-feed limit there let anyone without the secret use up a feed's turns, and GitHub does not retry a refused delivery; security review), the per-feed limit after it; the body stops at 1 MB, and the signature is checked over the raw body before anything is parsed (GitHub: HMAC-SHA256 with `verify_slice`; GitLab: the token, compared in constant time). Unknown, paused or stopped feeds, a wrong or missing signature all answer a bare 404, so nobody learns which feeds exist. The secret is 32 random bytes, never in a list, a log or an error, shown once (add and "Make new secret", which kills the old one at once). A repeated delivery id, or the same signed body under a new id (the signature does not cover the id), is ignored for 7 days (`feed_events` unique keys). The bot posts queued events every 5 s, but a failure for now counts once a minute, so a short Discord outage does not stop the feed after 25 s (security review). GitLab's confidential issues and notes never post (a private issue must not reach a Discord channel). Event text is untrusted: the bot runs the same checks as for RSS items (scam, NSFW, the server's invite, link and word filters) and allows only the ping role as mention. GitHub's default webhook content type is a form: it is refused with a 400 that says to pick application/json, after the signature.

**A route read data that its page hides.** (security review 2026-11-22)
The Appeal Blacklist page is hidden without the `appeals` permit perm (`access::page`), but its list route needed only Viewer, so any Viewer could read the user ids by the API. Root cause: the page rule and the route check are written apart, and nothing compares them. *Avoid:* a route needs at least what its page needs (`api::staff` with the same permit perm). Searched: every other route of a hidden page (cases, notes, reports, raid and join gate logs, mod stats, raider matches, rescue, bot profile) already asks the same permit perm or level. Test: `the_blacklist_needs_the_appeals_permit_perm`.

**The 2FA lockouts lived in memory.** (security review 2026-11-22)
The 5 wrong codes in 10 minutes, the code replay guard, and the 5 tries in 10 minutes for a rescue claim and for Secure my account were kept in the dashboard process. A restart (an update, a crash) reset them, and a code used just before the restart worked again. *Avoid:* a security counter lives in the database: `dash_fails` (counted by `auth::tries` under an advisory lock, so parallel tries can not pass the limit) and `dash_totp.last_step` (migration 4058). The rescue key's own miss counter was already in the database. Test: `totp_locks_after_five_misses_and_refuses_replay`.

**The dashboard had no script rule (Content-Security-Policy).** (security review 2026-11-22)
Only `frame-ancestors` was set. The UI writes message previews and emoji as raw HTML, so one escape gap would run a script with the viewer's session, the owner's too. *Avoid:* every response gets `script-src 'self' 'wasm-unsafe-eval'` plus the hash of each inline script of the built `index.html` (`script_csp`, made at start). A page the server makes itself (verify, raid report) gives its scripts a new nonce (`nonce_csp`) and has no inline event handler (`onclick=`): set the handler from the script. A new inline script in `index.html` needs nothing more; a new script host must go in the rule.

**The verify page's button stayed dead after a lost connection, and the raid page was wider than a phone.** (bug hunt 2026-11-22)
The verify script caught a bad answer but not a failed `fetch`: with no connection the click threw, the button stayed disabled and no text said why, so the member had to reload. A 32-character name with no space ran out of the raid report page at 375 px. Root cause: the pages the server makes itself were tested only on the good path and at desktop width. *Avoid:* a script on such a page wraps `fetch` in `try`, shows a message and frees the button; the page body has `overflow-wrap:anywhere`. Searched: only the verify page and the raid report page are made by the server with a script or a list.

**Logout left the `sid` cookie in the browser.** (bug hunt 2026-11-22)
`jar.remove(Cookie::from("sid"))` sends `sid=; Max-Age=0` with no path, so the browser applied it to `/auth` and kept the cookie set with `Path=/`. The same at the login callback for `oauth_state` and `login_next`, and at Secure my account. The session row was deleted, so no access remained. Root cause: a removal cookie must carry the same path as the cookie it removes. *Avoid:* remove with `jar.remove(auth::cookie(name, String::new(), st.secure))`, the same builder that set it; a test checks the `Set-Cookie` of a removal.

## 7. Build and deploy

**Every push to a PR branch ran the whole CI twice.** (owner decision, 11-20)
`ci.yml` had `on: push:` with no branch filter plus `pull_request:`, so one commit on a PR branch started every job (check, supply-chain, dashboard-ui, e2e, about 20 minutes for e2e) once for each trigger. Root cause: an unfiltered `push` trigger next to `pull_request`. *Avoid:* `push` is limited to `branches: [main]` (the Images workflow still follows main's CI run); the PR gets its four required checks from `pull_request` under the same job names. A new workflow with both triggers needs the same filter. A branch with no PR gets no CI.

**The updater trusted the digests in a queued row.** (security review, 11-18)
Only the dashboard checked GitHub's signed build record before it queued an `update_runs` row, and the dashboard's own database role can insert such a row, so a dashboard or database break-in could have the updater (the one container with the Docker socket) install any image in the repository. Root cause: a check made by the writer of a request was trusted by its reader. *Avoid:* the side that acts checks again itself: `updater.sh` `attested` asks GitHub for the record of each digest and matches image name, digest, repository, workflow and `main` in SQL (psql is the only JSON tool there, the answer goes in with `\set` from a file). It fails closed: no answer or no record installs nothing. `ATTEST_URL` exists only for the e2e test's stub. The updater image changes only with the server command, so a change to `updater.sh` needs that command (the Update page warns).

**The hardening block covered only the two services that hold the bot token.** (security review review, 11-18)
`x-hardening` (`cap_drop: [ALL]`, `no-new-privileges`) from #312 was added to bot and dashboard only, so the MCP server, Postgres and the tunnel kept Docker's default capabilities. Root cause: the change was scoped to the threat it answered, not to every service. *Avoid:* every new service gets `*hardening`. Postgres needs `CHOWN, DAC_OVERRIDE, FOWNER, SETGID, SETUID` back for its entrypoint (without `DAC_OVERRIDE` it cannot read its own data folder at start; tried on a real daemon). The updater gets only `no-new-privileges`: its Docker socket is root on the host anyway, and dropping capabilities could break the dump into a `./pre-update` owned by another user. A change to `docker-compose.yml` reaches the server only with the server command.

**A compose secret from `.env` cannot go into a `read_only` container.** (security review, 11-18)
Compose copies a secret with `environment:` into the container at start, and refuses a read-only one ("`file` is the sole supported option"). A `file:` secret is a bind mount, which the updater cannot recreate: it runs compose with the project at `/project`, a path the host does not have. *Avoid:* the bot token is an `environment:` secret and the bot and dashboard are not `read_only` (they drop all capabilities instead). Try a compose change on a real Docker daemon (`dockerd` runs in the cloud container) before you write it into DEPLOY.md. A variable set in the shell wins over `.env`, also for secrets.

**An update could not be undone with its migrations, and a restore could half drop the database.** (audit, 11-17)
The updater took no dump before the new image ran its migrations, so a rollback brought back only the old images (without, for example, the column migration 3989 dropped). The DEPLOY.md restore piped into psql with no stop on error and no transaction, after the dump's DROPs. With a pinned tag the update's `up -d` kept the old container, whose restart count failed the health check. *Avoid:* the updater dumps with the server's own `pg_dump` (a newer pg_dump writes settings an older server refuses) into `./pre-update`, after the pull and the same-version check, so a run that changes nothing never rotates out the dump from before a migration (review, 11-17). It stops before any restart when `/backups` is not a mount (new image, old compose file), when free space is under twice the database, or when the dump fails or passes its timeout (run inside the container, so it stops pg_dump itself). A restore checks the file (`gunzip -t`), drops and makes `public` again, and runs in `--single-transaction` with `ON_ERROR_STOP=1`; the bot starts only after it succeeded (`&&`). Every `up -d` the updater health checks uses `--force-recreate`.

**The Update page showed an older build as the newest.** (owner decision, 11-18, after deploying 9e3270b)
"Running: 9e3270b / Newest: 15484ff / up to date". The build list asked GitHub for `status=success` Images runs, and GitHub's filtered list left out the finished, green 9e3270b run for over 15 minutes (the unfiltered list had it). Until then the page could neither show nor install the new build. *Avoid:* never trust GitHub's `status=` filter for something that must be current: list the runs unfiltered and check `conclusion == "success"` in our code (`update::runs_to_builds`, at most the 10 GHCR keeps). When the page is up to date on a commit the list does not have yet, "Newest" shows the running commit, never an older one.

**A failed cleanup of old image versions blocked every new image.** (Images runs after 15484ff, 11-18)
The `delete-package-versions` step in each image job failed with "delete version API failed. Package not found" after it had deleted one or more old versions (most likely a child manifest GHCR had already removed with its index, left from the builds before `provenance: false`). The updater job failed, the matrix cancelled the other three, `latest` was skipped, and the Update button had nothing new to install for hours. *Avoid:* housekeeping steps in the Images workflow run with `continue-on-error: true`; only build, push, attest and the `latest` move may fail a run. After a merge, check the Images run's jobs, not only that `latest` has not moved yet.

**The new updater's first heartbeat failed on the old schema.** (owner decision, VPS deploy of #282, 11-18)
The updater waits only for Postgres to be healthy, not for the bot, which runs the migrations, so its first heartbeat wrote `updater_seen.sha` before migration 4014 had added it (`ERROR: column "sha" ... does not exist`, once, then fine 30 s later). *Avoid:* a container that does not run the migrations itself waits at start until the tables and columns it writes exist (`updater.sh` polls `select ... limit 0`), and `tests/e2e/updater.sh` starts the updater before the schema and fails on any ERROR line.

**A Postgres healthcheck passed before Postgres was up.** (e2e run of #282, 11-18)
On a fresh volume the postgres image first runs a temporary server that listens only on its socket, runs the init scripts, then stops it and starts the real one. `pg_isready` over the socket passed on the temporary server, so the next `psql` hit the gap (`connection to server on socket ... failed`). *Avoid:* every Postgres healthcheck (CI services, `docker-compose.yml`, e2e compose files) uses `pg_isready -h 127.0.0.1`; the temporary server does not listen on TCP.

**The approved-build Update had four holes.** (security review review, 11-17)
(1) An attestation looked up by digest alone also matched the same bytes copied into another package (the updater's image under `tommybot-bot`, same revision label). (2) A stopped MCP container (`ps -a`) was started by an update and failed its health check, so every update rolled back. (3) A build whose dashboard still queued untargeted runs could be installed, and then no update from the page worked again. (4) The Images `gate` used a job-level `if`, so a run whose jobs were all skipped (a failed CI, or a fork's pull request from a branch named main) could count as a successful build in the dashboard's list. *Avoid:* `update::attests` reads the in-toto statement: subject name `ghcr.io/thomkr01/tommybot-<img>` with the digest, and the workflow `images.yml` on `refs/heads/main` of this repository (under its old name `thomkr01/TommyBot` or its new name `thomkr01/Brim`, exact strings: GitHub records the name as spelled at build time). The updater adds mcp only when it runs (`ps -q --status running`), and refuses a dashboard image without `org.brim.update=digest` or `org.tommybot.update=digest` (images carry both, since an updater from before the rename reads only the old one). Workflow conditions that decide what counts as published are steps that cancel the run, never a job-level `if`. The updater checks `target_sha` and digests by regex in SQL (Postgres `$` is end of string; `grep -x` matches any line). An old updater's heartbeat keeps a stale `sha`, so it is read only with a fresh `sha_at`.

**Every move of main became an installable build.** (owner decision, 11-21)
Each push to `main` built four images (about 35 minutes) and the Update page offered each one, so the owner saw many half-step versions and no named version. *Avoid:* only a release is built and offered: a tag `vMAJOR.MINOR.PATCH` on a commit of `main`. Keep these rules when you change it. (1) `images.yml` stays a `workflow_run` of CI (filter `branches: ['v[0-9]*']`; for a tag push the CI run's `head_branch` is the tag name). A `workflow_run` run always uses the file as it is on `main`, so its signed build record names `images.yml` on `refs/heads/main`, which `update::attests` and `updater.sh` ask for. A `push: tags` trigger would run the tagged commit's own copy, which a branch commit can change. (2) The gate checks, as steps that cancel the run: CI passed on a push of this repository by its owner, and its `e2e` job passed (e2e runs for tags, on request and on a push that changed the dashboard or the browser test; a skipped job counts as passed, so the gate reads the job's own conclusion) (write access alone could tag an old commit as the highest), the tag is `v` plus three numbers, the tag (peeled for an annotated tag) names the commit CI checked, and `compare/<sha>...<main's sha>` is `ahead` or `identical` (main's sha from `ls-remote refs/heads/main`, never the name `main`: a tag named `main` would win). (3) `latest` moves only when the release is higher (`sort -V`) than the version label of the current `:latest`, so a re-run or a late older release never moves it back; it is not compared with the tags, since a higher tag that never built would hold it back for good. It is copied from the `:<version>` tag, not `:<sha>`, which two releases on one commit share. The package cleanup keeps 15 versions, more than the 10 releases listed, since a re-run adds a version. (4) The release goes into the image as the label `org.opencontainers.image.version`, which is part of the signed digest; the updater refuses an image without a release label, so an attested build of `main` from before releases is not installed. (5) The dashboard lists only runs titled `Release <version> at <sha>`, sorted by version, and looks up digests by the version tag, since two releases on one commit share the sha tag. (6) `image-cache.yml` builds the release while CI runs on the tag, only to fill the build cache: no push, no login, no attestation, only `contents: read`. It checks the parts of the gate known before CI ends (owner, tag, on main), so the shared cache holds only the owner's code from main; Images still runs its whole gate before it publishes. An updater or Update page from before this does not know releases: the server command is needed once, after the first release is out.

**The Update button kept installing an older version.** (owner decision, 11-17)
Four merges' CI runs finished out of order, and the oldest commit (#264) finished last. The `images` concurrency group runs one at a time in the order CI *finished*, and a waiting run is replaced by the next one, so the old commit was built last and moved `:latest` back. The Update page then read the newest successful Images run's `head_sha`, which for a `workflow_run` run is main's tip when it was queued, not the commit it built: it showed the newest commit as published, and each update pulled the old image and said "Updated". *Avoid:* the Images `gate` job builds only when its commit is still main's tip, and otherwise cancels its own run (cancelled, not skipped, since a skipped job counts as success). Each image builds only its `:<sha>` tag, in its own concurrency group, so a newer commit's build cancels an older one. The `latest` job checks the tip again and then moves all four `:latest` tags together, in a group that is never cancelled, so the images never point at different commits. The run title is `Release <version> at <sha>` (it was `Images for <sha>` before releases), and `update::published` reads the release and commit from it. The updater refuses a download that is the version already running. Never read the built commit from a `workflow_run` run's `head_sha`.

**Images were pulled by a moving tag.** (audit, 11-17)
`rust:1-bookworm`, `debian:bookworm-slim`, `docker:28-cli` and the backup script's `rclone/rclone` (`latest`) changed under the same name, so two builds or two backups of one commit could run different code. *Avoid:* name an exact version that is at least two weeks old (`rclone/rclone:1.75.2`). Postgres keeps its major tag (`postgres:16`): an exact minor pin can be older than the one a server already runs, and the `-alpine` variant would change the collation library under the data volume. The rclone pin is moved by hand.

**Dependabot would offer a Postgres major upgrade.** (audit, 11-17)
The new docker-compose ecosystem proposes the newest `postgres` tag, so a merged bump plus `docker compose up -d` starts Postgres 18 on the version-16 data volume, which refuses to start. *Avoid:* the docker-compose entry ignores `postgres` `version-update:semver-major`; a major upgrade is a planned pg_upgrade.

**Images were published for commits that failed CI.** (audit, 11-17)
`images.yml` ran on every push to main on its own, so a commit that failed clippy or tests became `:latest`, and the Update page offered it. "Keep 3 versions" also counted the untagged attestation manifests, so fewer real builds were left to roll back to. *Avoid:* `images.yml` runs on `workflow_run` of CI on main and publishes only when that run was a push and succeeded, from its `head_sha`. It stays a separate workflow. A job-level concurrency group on the matrix needs `${{ matrix.target }}` in its name: one shared group makes the 4 legs cancel each other. Its name stays too, since deployed dashboards read `images.yml` runs (`update::LATEST_URL`). The package cleanup keeps 10 versions.

**CI ran unpinned upstream code with the default token scope.** (audit, 11-17)
`pipx install graphifyy` and `npm i playwright` took the newest release on every run, and only `images.yml` set `permissions:`. *Avoid:* pin every installed tool to an exact version, and give each workflow `permissions: contents: read` (a job that pushes images asks for `packages: write` itself).

**The UI build took new dependency versions without a commit.** (audit, 11-17)
The UI crate's `Cargo.lock` was gitignored and the Dockerfile ran `cargo generate-lockfile`, so a bad upstream patch release could change or break the dashboard. `cloudflared:latest` had the same problem. *Avoid:* `crates/dashboard/ui/Cargo.lock` is committed; the Dockerfile runs `cargo fetch --locked`, CI runs the UI checks `--locked`, and the release build is `--locked`. Third-party images in compose name a version. After a UI dependency change, commit the new lockfile.

**An update could stay "running" forever.** (audit, 11-17)
