`updater.sh` has no `set -e` (its loop must survive), so when Postgres was briefly unreachable while `finish` ran, the row stayed `running`, the dashboard polled forever and hid "Update now" until the updater restarted. *Avoid:* runs happen one at a time inside the loop, so the loop fails any row still `running` every 30 s with an error that says the end could not be recorded.

**The backup was world-readable and ran `.env` as shell code.** (audit, 11-17)
`backup.sh` wrote the dump (2FA secrets, session tokens) with mode 644, sourced `.env` (a value with spaces, `&` or `$(` broke the script or ran code), left `*.tmp` files after a failed dump, and `rclone delete` removed every file older than 30 days on the remote, not only dumps. *Avoid:* `umask 077`; read only `BACKUP_DIR`, `RCLONE_REMOTE` and `RCLONE_CONFIG_DIR` from `.env` as text; a trap removes the temp file; the remote delete has `--include '*.sql.gz'`.

**GitHub Actions were pinned by tag.** (audit, 11-17)
Every `uses:` named a tag (`@v7`, `@stable`), which its owner can move to other code; `images.yml` can push the images the updater installs. *Avoid:* `uses:` names a full commit SHA with the tag as a comment (Dependabot updates both). `dtolnay/rust-toolchain` then needs `toolchain: stable`, since it reads the toolchain from its ref name.

**Docs and config said what the code does not do.** (audit, 11-17)
Cargo.toml named a licence with no LICENSE file, compose did not pass `PHISHING_LIST_URL`, `NSFW_LIST_URL` and `NSFW_THREADS`, `METRICS_ADDR` did nothing under Docker, the CHANGELOG named Postgres 18 for the database, an unused workspace dependency stayed, and the invite feature carried the name of another Discord bot (now Invite Tracking). *Avoid:* a new env variable goes into `.env.example` and, if the container reads it, `docker-compose.yml`; a feature name is checked against existing bot products first.

**Two image builds could finish out of order.** (audit, 11-17)
Two quick merges started two Images runs; if the older one finished last, `:latest` pointed at older code, and the Update button could install it on a database a newer migration had already changed. *Avoid:* runs going one at a time was not enough, because CI can finish out of order (see "The Update button kept installing an older version"): only main's tip is built and published. An older commit's `:<sha>` tag may never be built; the updater's rollback uses its local `:rollback` tag, so that is fine.

**Trunk's prebuilt wasm-bindgen needs a newer glibc.** (#123)
On the arm64 VPS `trunk build` failed with `GLIBC_2.38 not found`.
*Avoid:* the Dockerfile builds `wasm-bindgen-cli` itself at the version the UI lockfile resolves to. Don't hardcode the version: read it from the committed UI lockfile (the `wasm-bindgen` entry of `crates/dashboard/ui/Cargo.lock`, read before the UI code is copied in, so the build cache keeps this step).

**containerd deletes an untagged image.** (#128)
Rollback by image id failed because the old image was already gone.
*Avoid:* the updater keeps the previous version under a `:rollback` tag.

**A `git pull` in the old folder would start the bot on an empty database.** (PR D, 11-21)
With `name: brim` in docker-compose.yml, a server set up before the rename (`~/tommybot`, project `tommybot`) that pulls the new file gets new, empty volumes `brim_*`: the bot would start in every server with no settings, permits or whitelist. The Update page also told such a server to run `git pull`. *Avoid:* the compose file needs `BRIM_NAMES=1` in `.env` (`x-names`), so the old folder refuses to start (`git checkout HEAD@{1}` goes back); since 2026-11-22 the bot and the dashboard stop at start under the old names, so no Update page runs there. The move itself copies the volume and renames database and roles in the copy, so the old volumes stay as the way back.

**The updater does not update itself.** (#128)
Changes to `docker-compose.yml` or the updater need `git pull && docker compose up -d` on the VPS once.

**No logs without `RUST_LOG=info`.** (#92)
The bot prints nothing useful at the default level. `live.sh` and compose set it.

**A new RustSec advisory turns the `audit` job red.** (#115)
Fix with `cargo update -p <crate>`. A new dependency can also pull a crate back to an older, vulnerable version: tract 0.21 pinned `time` below 0.3.42 (#188). After adding a dependency, diff `Cargo.lock` for downgrades. Ignore an advisory in `.cargo/audit.toml` only with a reason, as for RUSTSEC-2023-0071 (rsa only via the unused sqlx-mysql).

**Kept image tags could not be pulled.** (security review, 2026-11-17) Each build pushed an index plus an untagged attestation manifest (3 package versions), and the cleanup keeps the newest 10 versions by date, so it deleted the image manifest under a tag it kept: `tommybot-bot:0d89861` returned 404 for its image while the tag still showed. *Avoid:* `images.yml` builds with `provenance: false` (one manifest, one version per build), the signed provenance goes to GitHub's attestation store (`attest-build-provenance`, not pushed to the package), and `imagetools create --prefer-index=false` so `:latest` is the same manifest as the sha tag. Before relying on an old tag, pull it or HEAD its manifest, not only the tag list.

**The patched twilight-model was outside every check.** (security review, 2026-11-17) `vendor/twilight-model` is a path crate, so Cargo.lock has no checksum for it and cargo audit skips it; nothing showed whether its bytes were still the upstream release plus our one change. *Avoid:* `scripts/check-vendor.sh` (CI supply-chain job) rebuilds it from the crates.io release in `vendor/twilight-model.lock` plus `vendor/twilight-model.patch` and fails on any other difference, and CI runs `cargo audit -f vendor/twilight-model.lock`. On a twilight upgrade, update both files. A cargo audit that only ran when Cargo files changed, in its own workflow, did not stop an image either: it is now a CI job, and Images publish only after CI passed.

**DEPLOY.md named a menu that does not exist.** (DEPLOY 6.4) It said **MCP** in the top bar; the link is **MCP tokens** in the account menu, so the owner could not find it. *Avoid:* when a guide names a button or menu, check its label and place in `crates/dashboard/ui/src/main.rs` (header) or the page itself. The rename missed "new MCP page" in CHANGELOG.md (found in the docs round after #166): grep every .md, CHANGELOG and README included, for the old label.

**A header was trusted behind any proxy.** (11-13) Web verification reads the visitor's IP from `CF-Connecting-IP`, which only Cloudflare's tunnel sets for us; behind another proxy, or with the port open, a visitor sends their own and skips the alt and VPN checks. *Avoid:* DEPLOY.md states that only Cloudflare Tunnel is supported for web verification. A new header-based check names the one deployment it trusts.

**A rollback reported success without checking the old version.** (11-12) After a failed update the updater restarted the old images and wrote "running again" without a health check, so an old binary crash-looping on a newer migration was reported as fine. *Avoid:* the rollback recreates the containers (`--force-recreate`, so the restart count starts at 0), waits on the same `settled` check as the update and reports `failed` when the old version does not start. `tests/e2e/updater.sh` covers it with a marker file standing in for the migration. The old version must also run on the newer schema and config (11-13): `db::connect` sets `set_ignore_missing(true)` (so migrations must stay additive: never drop or rename what an older binary reads), and a key or enum value an older binary does not know drops only that entry (`brim_logging::lenient::{map, list}` on anti-nuke `limits`, logging `routes`/`events`/custom `events`, Raider List `categories`, Discord AutoMod `bot_actions`), never the whole section (anti-nuke off). A section that still fails falls back to defaults and DMs the owner once a day (`reply::fallback_alert`). A DM alone was missed with DMs closed, while the dashboard kept showing the section on (S-h3 6): the alert also posts to the config-changes (or fallback) log channel, read from the raw config since `logging` may be the failed section, only when @everyone cannot read that channel (`events::public_with`, the log path's check without a config read, R-h3 round 2, 4); it posts nothing when `lockdown` (the main roles that check needs) is itself unreadable, and only to a channel of that server (R-h3 round 3, 1), and the dashboard names such sections (`api::unread_sections`) in a red Overview row, a banner on the page and an off Quick Systems switch. *Avoid:* every enum-keyed map or enum list in a config section uses the lenient readers; a single enum field still fails its section, which is then announced. Writes still refuse such an entry (`sections::unreadable_entry` in the dashboard's `validate` and the bot's `check_section`), so only data a newer version wrote is ever skipped. Round 2 (R-h3 2, 5; S-h3 5): that check looked at the whole section, and the UI sends the stored section back, so after a downgrade every save of a section holding a newer entry was refused; it also checked only keys, so a bad value under a known key (`limits.Ban.enabled = "no"`, a route that is no id) was saved and then silently dropped; and `lenient::map` over the AutoMod `actions` dropped a whole rule for one unknown action. *Avoid:* `unreadable_entry(section, new, stored)` checks keys and values, and refuses only an entry that is not stored as it is (the rule `limits::check` uses with `old`); list items count as stored when the stored list holds them. A map of lists uses `lenient::map_of_lists`, which drops one item, not the entry.

**A new migration was missing from the test binary.** (11-18)
`sqlx::migrate!` embeds `crates/core/migrations` at compile time, and core had no build script, so after adding a migration an incremental build kept the old list and the tests ran without it. *Avoid:* `crates/core/build.rs` reruns on a change in `migrations`.

**Each release compiled every Rust library again.** (CI speed look, 2026-11-21)
The builder stage started with `COPY . .`, so any change in the code threw away the layer that held the compiled libraries, and each image build took 8 to 10 minutes. *Avoid:* the Dockerfile copies only the manifests, the lock file and `vendor/` first, builds the libraries against empty stand-in crates, and then deletes every `brim*` artifact before the real code is copied, so no stand-in output can reach an image. A new workspace crate needs its own `COPY crates/<name>/Cargo.toml` line and a stand-in in that step; without it the build stops with a manifest error.

**The early image build ran beside the real one and saved nothing.** (v1.1.0 release, 2026-11-22)
CI on the tag ended in 7 minutes, the Image cache builds in 8 to 11.5, so Images started while the cache was still empty and compiled everything again. *Avoid:* each Images build job first waits (at most 20 minutes, and only waits) for the Image cache build job of the same release and target to end. The gate decides before that step; the wait never changes what is published.

## 8. Tests

**Two tests waited 30 seconds on a database that never answers.** (CI speed look, 2026-11-21)
`owner_gets_a_token_others_do_not` (MCP) and `icon_revert_clears_a_new_icon_and_refuses_a_bad_hash` (dashboard) build their state with `connect_lazy("postgres://x@127.0.0.1:1/x")`. A path that reads the database then waits for sqlx's default acquire timeout of 30 seconds before it fails, so each push spent about a minute on nothing. *Avoid:* a pool that must never connect gets `PgPoolOptions::new().acquire_timeout(200 ms)`. Searched: all four such pools (dashboard `main.rs`, `ops.rs`, `sections.rs`, MCP `tests/oauth.rs`) now have it.

**A role setup that never committed passed every local run.** (PR D, 11-21)
`set_mcp_password` opened a transaction and wrote the password outside it, with no commit, so the role it made was rolled back; locally the role was still there from earlier runs, because roles belong to the whole Postgres server, not to the dropped test database. CI (a new server each run) failed. *Avoid:* test role setup on a fresh Postgres server (`docker run postgres:16`), not only a fresh database, and run every statement of a transaction through `&mut *tx` up to `commit`.

**A DB test overflowed the 2 MB test thread stack.** (2026-11-20, after #397 and #398)
`verify_lifts_only_a_join_quarantine_and_admin_roles_need_staff` awaited `modcmds::run` many times inline; in a debug build its own async frames took about 1.7 MB, and a small growth in the code it calls tipped it over, which aborts the whole test binary. Not a recursion, and not the live bot (spawned tasks keep their futures on the heap). *Avoid:* a test that awaits a big command future (`modcmds::run`, `on_audit`) in a helper or loop boxes it: `Box::pin(..).await`. Check with `RUST_MIN_STACK=1048576` when a test grows.

- **The e2e sweep read the sidebar links before they rendered.** (e2e flake, 2026-11-19) `dashboard.js` waited for more than 10 `#/` links of any kind; the header's links met that before the sidebar's module links existed, so `links[0]` was undefined or the first page had no guild links. It passed on a re-run. *Avoid:* wait for the exact element the next line reads (here a `#/<id>/module/` link), never a count of something broader or a fixed sleep; the page itself was right.
- **Two DB tests shared a guild id and one purged the other's rows.** (case undo fix, 2026-11-19) A new test in `cases.rs` took `5_100_000_000 + pid`, the base `db.rs` already uses for a `purge_guild` test, so in about one run of four its cases vanished mid-test and an assert saw `None`. **Rule:** before a DB test picks a guild id base, search the crate for that number; a test that passes alone and fails in the full run has a neighbour writing its rows. The same holds for the offset of a shared helper: in `core/src/giveaways.rs` two tests passed `db(7)`, and one deleted the other's giveaway in about one run of two (giveaway extras, 2026-11-23). List the offsets in use (`grep -o 'db([0-9]*)'`) before you take one.
- **Dashboard tests shared a guild id base and failed with a duplicate key.** (#368 CI, 2026-11-19) `g3_handler_tests::setup(base)` uses `base + pid`; four bases were used by two or three tests of the same process (`9_884`, `9_893`, `9_894`), so tests running in parallel wrote the same `guild_config` row (`guild_config_pkey` violation). The one in `api.rs` came with the Tickets stale-page test. **Rule:** a new `setup(base)` takes a base no other call uses; `grep -rhoE 'setup\(\s*[0-9_]{10,}' crates/dashboard/src | sort | uniq -d` must print nothing. Searched: every `setup(` call in the dashboard crate; the four duplicates got their own bases.
- **A changed MCP tool description reached the AI model unseen.** (security review, 11-18) AI clients take new tool text without asking the user again, and nothing in CI compared it, so a harmful line in a description could pass review. *Avoid:* `crates/mcp/tests/definitions.rs` pins the instructions and every tool definition in `definitions.json`; a change fails CI until the reviewed file is updated with `UPDATE_DEFINITIONS=1`.
- **A mock let a timeout succeed while member edits failed.** (audit, 11-17) The timeout arm came before the `fail_member_edit` arm, so a test of a failed timeout passed against a mock kinder than Discord. *Avoid:* a failure flag arm comes before every arm for the same route.
**The DB tests skipped green and ran on any database.** (audit, 11-17)
Without `DATABASE_URL` about 500 tests passed without running, and with a production URL they deleted rows and changed the MCP role's password. *Avoid:* every DB test gets its URL from `db::test_url`: it panics in CI when the URL is missing, and refuses a database whose name does not end in `_test` unless `BRIM_TEST_DB=1` (CI sets it). `tests/e2e/run.sh` has the same check.

**Tests that could not fail.** (audit, 11-17)
A native AutoMod test never approved its rule, so every hit stopped before the whitelist it tested; two tests asserted only "nothing happened"; a core test checked a copy of antinuke SQL that had drifted; the OAuth test allowed a refresh to move `exp`; two tests shared `T::new(601)`; and one ordered two handlers with a 100 ms sleep. *Avoid:* an "it did not act" assert has a control in the same test that does act; never test a copy of production SQL; assert the exact value of a security limit; each `T::new` number is unique; wait for the event (a mock call), not a fixed time.

**The dashboard access matrix ran only by hand, and a wrong refusal only made a note.** (audit, 11-17)
`deep.js`'s matrix ran with `DEEP=1` only, and a refused user who got 400/404/500 was a NOTE, so a route that validates before it checks access passed. *Avoid:* `run.sh` always runs `DEEP_ONLY=matrix`; a refused user must get 401 or 403, and a 5xx for an allowed user fails.

- **Defaults are off on new servers.** Heat filters and anti-nuke start off, so a live scenario must turn on what it tests (#92, #93).
- **Bots are exempt from a lot.** Webhooks owned by Brim or by a verified bot are exempt, bot-authored commands are ignored and bots cannot press buttons. Coordinated spam, confirm buttons, appeals, reports, Join Gate and DM tickets need a second human account (#93, #142).
- **The live test must not use the hosted bot's token or server.** (11-11) The cloud env had the VPS bot's `DISCORD_TOKEN` and its only guild as `TEST_GUILD_ID`, so `live.sh` and the VPS bot would both react to every attack and the result says nothing. *Avoid:* a separate test bot (own Discord application) on a separate test server, or stop the VPS bot (`docker compose stop bot`) for the run.
- **The live script shares the bot's token** and its rate limits. Cache channel and role lookups and wait longer instead of polling hard (#142).
- **Make scenarios clean up after themselves** so they can run again. The old nuke scenario kicked the attacker bot every run (#142).
- **Cloud env vars are read at session start.** A variable added during a session needs a new session.
- **Two DB tests used the same guild id.** (MCP writes, PR 4; again in the race review, where `6_100_000_000` was already used twice in the bot crate) Both took `9_300_000_000 + pid`, ran at the same time in one process and saw each other's config versions, so `history_keeps_newest_200_versions` failed now and then. *Avoid:* give each DB test its own base (`grep -rn '_000_000_000 +' crates/` before picking one) and delete its rows at the end.
- **An e2e step waited for any row, not the one it made.** (11-11) The notes check waited for "a note is listed", which old notes from an earlier run already met, then the reload that put the new note on top swapped the card under the fill, so Save sent the old text. *Avoid:* wait for the exact value the step created.
- **An e2e step waited for a dropdown, not its options.** (11-11, PR #178 CI) The Logging Cancel check waited for the channel select, which shows with only "none" until the channel list loads, so it found no other option and crashed. *Avoid:* wait for the option the step will pick (`option:nth-child(2)`). Searched: the only other `selectOption` (log setup, by index) waits for its option already.
- **A mock was kinder than Discord, and a test passed only because of it.** (live test 11-13) The Ban Sync mock's ban PUT replaced the reason, but Discord keeps the first ban's reason (and writes no audit entry) when a banned user is banned again, so `a_synced_ban_replaces_a_shorter_local_temp_ban` passed while real Discord left the copy reading as the local temp ban. *Avoid:* when a live run shows Discord behaving differently, fix the mock first (the mock PUT, now in `test_support.rs`, keeps an existing reason; Ban Sync itself was removed on 11-13) and re-run the tests that touch it. The Ban Sync code fix is under "A second ban keeps the first ban's reason" (section 2).
- **A body-less DELETE was refused and a soft call hid it.** (live test 11-13) `live.py`'s `call` sends `Content-Type: application/json` on every request; `DELETE /guilds/{g}/bans/{u}` with no body got 400 (50109), and `soft=True` turned that into None, so cleanup left the user banned. *Avoid:* `call` sends `{}` on a DELETE without a body; a soft call is for an expected 404, not for every 4xx.
- **`tests/e2e/run.sh` needs root Postgres, trunk and Playwright**; the gateway is not covered by the Discord mock.
- **A global cleanup in one test deleted another test's rows halfway through its setup.** (PR #183 CI, 11-11) `raiders::expire` deletes every old rejected or pending entry in the database. Test 55 created its old entries first and their evidence after, so test 54's `expire` running in parallel could delete an entry before its evidence existed; that evidence was then orphaned and the count read 2 instead of 1. *Avoid:* in a test that calls a job working on the whole table, create the rows such a parallel run could act on last, so every state it can see is already complete. Searched: `expire` is the only global cleanup called from the raider tests (54 and 55).
- **Two tests ran the same global sweep at once and deadlocked.** (PR #368 CI, 2026-11-19) `persondata::sweep_kept` finishes every done delete request in one transaction: it deletes cases, then reports, appeals and tickets, then updates cases. The two datareq tests (978, 979) each called it, so one sweep held report rows the other wanted while waiting on case rows the other held (40P01). Production runs one sweep an hour, so only the tests collided. *Avoid:* tests that call a job working on the whole table and holding locks across statements take turns on one static `tokio::sync::Mutex` (`datareq::tests::SWEEP`). Searched: `sweep_kept` is called only by the hourly scheduler and these two tests.
- **Two test files gave `T::new` the same numbers.** (11-13) `lifecycle_tests` and `quarantine_tests` both used 70-81, and the number picks the guild ids, so two tests running at once shared a guild and each one's `cleanup` could delete the other's config mid-test. *Avoid:* `T::new(n)` numbers are unique across all test files (h2 tests use 150-199; the base is `pid * 10_000 + n * 10`, so `n` stays below 1000); grep `T::new(` before picking one.
- **A filter lived in one caller, not in the shared lookup.** (Q5 review, 11-14) Roles Discord hands out were dropped only in `App::level`, so `permits::resolve` and `top_permit` called directly (enforce permits, Raider List, dehoist, mod commands, voice raid, the dashboard's own permit lookup) still gave their permit. *Avoid:* put a rule about which roles count into `permits::top_permit` (here the `unpermittable_roles` table), which every reader goes through. Searched: every `permits::resolve` and `top_permit` caller.
- **A "sent once" set shared the size-cap clear with the counts.** (Q6 review, 11-14) The owner DM count map was cleared at 10 000 entries, and with it the record of who was DMed, so a busy server could DM the owner again. The key also held the role set, so one actor giving other roles counted again. *Avoid:* keep "already done" apart from a cache that gets cleared, and key it by what the message is about (guild, actor).
- **A rate gate dropped the last event.** (keyword rule, 11-14) `on_rule_change` ignored a change that came less than 60 s after the last sync, so a quick second edit in Discord stayed. *Avoid:* a gate that drops events schedules one run at the window's end (`keyword_rule_sync` job, deduplicated per guild).
- **A compare folded more than Discord folds.** (keyword rule, 11-14) `rule_matches` lowercased regex patterns too, but Discord keeps their case, so a changed `(?i)` or letter case was not seen as a change; repeats were not deduped either. *Avoid:* fold only what Discord folds (keywords), sort and dedupe lists before comparing.
- **`false` passed for a known bot flag.** (Q4 review, 11-14) Callers that had the member passed `bot = false` to `permits::resolve`, so a bot still listed as TA got TA trust there. *Avoid:* pass `member.user.bot` wherever the member is known; `false` only where only an id is known, with a comment.
- **A test row's level ignored what the call changes.** (deep test, 11-15) `deep.js` expected a Trusted Admin to roll back to History v1. v1 comes from `dashboard.js`, before `levels.sql` adds the TA and EO, so the rollback also changes both lists, and `api::rollback` correctly needs an owner for the TA list and the Discord owner for Extra Owners (the same rule as `put_permits`, #139). The row also let the owner's rollback wipe the TA, EO and permits for the later phases. *Avoid:* a matrix row's level is the strictest rule the call touches with the fixture's data; a row that changes fixture data undoes it (the rollback row runs `levels.sql` again).
- **Numbers with no range at all.** (deep test, 11-15) `panic.executors`, `joinraid.active_ms` and `automod.heat.similarity_floor` took any value, so 999999999 executors or a similarity above 1 switched the feature off without a word. *Avoid:* every number the bot acts on has a range in `core::limits` (`FIXED` or `float_for_path`, and `ranged_paths` for a new float); executors may be 0 (off). The bot clamps the similarity on read (`HeatCfg::clamped`), like heat max and decay. Round 2: the heat weights (`heat::WEIGHTS`) took any value, a negative one cooled a spammer; they now go from 0 to the max heat in the same section (`limits::float_range`, clamped on read too). A new weight goes in `WEIGHTS` and `weights_mut` (a test checks they match). A filter's own "Action duration" (`filters.<key>.duration_secs`) had no upper end; it is 0 (default) to 365 days (`FILTER_DURATION`).
- **A role carried a permit, so whoever handed out the role handed out the permit.** (owner decision 2026-11-15) Manage Roles, a reaction-role bot or a level bot could give anyone a permit, and every guard (View Mod Stats) had to judge the giver. *Avoid:* a permit belongs to a Discord user id (`permit_members`, one permit per person); its roles are Brim's to give. `permit_sync` (GuildCreate, job `permit_sync` after a dashboard save) pages every member, and only after a complete paging moves role holders to ids once per guild (`migrate_members`), forgets listed people who are gone, gives listed members their permit's roles and takes permit roles from everyone else, nobody punished (never managed, Discord-given or above-the-bot roles, never a quarantined member). Live, `guard_permit_roles` takes back a permit role given to anyone not listed, whoever gave it (Owner, EO and TA too), and `guard_permit_removal` gives a listed member's role back. A leaver loses permit, EO and TA (`permit_sync::on_leave`). Until a guild is migrated (`permits::members_migrated`) every lookup and guard reads the roles as before; new code that reads permits must go through `permits::top_permit`/`member_roles`, never `permit_roles` alone.
- **A permit by id outlived the quarantine and the leave.** (permit review, 2026-11-15) When roles carried permits, a quarantine (it strips the roles) and a leave took the permit with them. By user id, a quarantined holder and a user no longer in the server still resolved to their permit in `top_permit`, `App::level` and the dashboard's `permit_perms`. *Avoid:* `permits::top_permit` gives no permit to a user with a `quarantines` row (both paths); `App::level` and `permit_perms` give nothing to a non-member (a fetch error stays an error, see "guild_member errors"), as `level_of` does for EO/TA; a dashboard save refuses a new permit member who is not in the server. When trust moves from a role to an id, list every way losing the role used to remove the trust and keep each one.
- **A missing key wiped data.** (permit review, 2026-11-15) `write_permits` read a custom permit without `members` as "no members", so an old UI tab or an older MCP client that saved a permit cleared everyone listed in it. *Avoid:* a new list field on a saved object keeps its stored value when the key is missing or `null` (review round 2: `"members": null` still cleared them); only an explicit list, `[]` too, changes it.
- **A leaver's level was gone before the audit entry asked for it.** (owner decision 2026-11-15) A kick or ban outside Brim is judged by the victim's level, but MemberRemove can run `permits::forget_member` before the MemberKick or MemberBanAdd audit entry comes in, and `App::level` gives a non-member nothing either way, so every kicked permit holder read as "no permit". *Avoid:* `permit_sync::on_leave` keeps the level a listed leaver held for 10 minutes (`permit_sync::left_level`, in memory) before it forgets them; `antinuke::guard_rank` reads that, else `permits::resolve` by id (the entry came first, the rows are still there), and the live level only for a timeout. When code deletes trust on an event, check every later reader that judges the same event.
- **A loop breaker failed open.** (permit review round 2, 2026-11-15) After 5 reverts in 10 minutes for one (guild, actor, member), `fight_stopped` and `permit_fight_stopped` stopped every revert, so the bot that fought Brim won: the permit role stayed on, or stayed off, for the window. *Avoid:* a breaker on a security revert slows it down (here at most one revert a minute, `FIGHT_SLOW`), never stops it; the alert stays once per window.
- **A leave and a later ban were read as one event.** (permit review round 2, 2026-11-15) `left_level` kept a leaver's level for 10 minutes, so a member who left by themselves and was banned by id later looked like a kicked or banned permit holder, and the actor was punished. *Avoid:* keep the leave time (wall clock) with the level; a kick or ban entry uses it only when the entry's snowflake time is not more than 5 seconds after the leave, as a kick or ban causes the leave.
- **A bot-kept message took over messages staff posted.** (ticket button review, 2026-11-15) `public::sync_post` first edited every `ticket_panel_posts` row in #open-a-ticket, so a Publish (a chosen subset, or a menu) into that channel was rewritten to the bot's message on the next save, and with every panel off the old buttons stayed. *Avoid:* code that keeps its own message in sync touches only the rows it posted (`posted_by = app.me`), and removes its message when there is nothing left to show.
- **Two authenticator entries with one name.** (2FA step-up, owner decision 2026-11-15) The login 2FA and the rescue key QR both used the issuer "Brim", so the app showed two "Brim" entries. The owner typed the rescue code at step-up, got "code rejected" and then the lockout. *Avoid:* each secret a person scans gets its own issuer name ("Brim" and "Brim Rescue"), and the code prompt says which entry to use.
- **A new DM button answered after its REST work.** (DM ticket review, 2026-11-15) The member's ticket type menu in the DM first saved the type, posted in the ticket, logged it and updated the staff buttons, and only then answered Discord, so a slow API showed "This interaction failed" although the type was saved. *Avoid:* the same as entry 1: every new button handler defers first and edits the answer at the end, also when it copies a flow (`staff_button` kindset) that already does.
- **A new text field's default reached servers that never chose it.** (panel text review, 2026-11-15) The first draft gave `tickets.panel_text` the new default text through serde, so every server whose config has no such key, including restored versions and imports, would get that text added on top of its own panels. *Avoid:* a new field that changes what members see defaults to "nothing new" when missing; the new default is written together with the default it belongs to (`save_id` writes it with the default panel), and a migration moves only rows that are exactly the old default.
- **Security checks warned about a state the permit rules intend.** (Overview, owner decision 2026-11-16) After permits went per Discord user ID, "Staff permit members" still warned when nobody was listed in the Admins or Moderators permit, but people are added to a permit only when needed. "Automod whitelist" read only the Whitelist page lists and told the owner to add "staff roles", although staff skip the automod through their permit (Owner, EO and TA always; the Admins and Moderators permits by the `automod` whitelist flag Auto Setup gives them). The wizard setup score had the same two points and the wizard notes said Discord permissions whitelist a member and left out the permits that are whitelisted. *Avoid:* when a trust rule changes, search the security score (`security.rs`), the wizard score and texts (`wizard.rs`) and the Overview links (`pages.rs`) for checks built on the old rule. A check about staff reads the permits, never the Whitelist page lists or roles.
- **`v["key"].as_array_mut` added `"key": null` to a body sent to Discord.** (Message Builder 2b, 2026-11-16) serde_json's `IndexMut` inserts a missing key as null, so filling variables in an embed without fields sent `"fields": null`. **Rule:** read optional keys of JSON that leaves the process with `get_mut(..)`/`pointer_mut(..)`, never `v["key"]` on a `&mut`.
- **A value filled into a message pushed it past Discord's limits.** (Message Builder 2b, 2026-11-16) Variables and role mentions are longer or shorter than their tokens, so a text that passed the save check could be refused at send time, and `{guild.icon}` with no icon was sent as an image link. **Rule:** cut every filled text to its Discord limit after filling, cut member-chosen values to 100 characters, and leave out an image slot whose filled value is not an https link.
- **An edit in place sent the raw text.** (Message Builder 2b, 2026-11-16) The edit path of publish replaced the filled `content` with the saved text so it could clear old text, which posted `{guild.name}` as typed. **Rule:** only default a missing key; never overwrite a filled body with the saved one.
- **Old copies answered from the newest version.** (Message Builder 3, 2026-11-16) Menus on a copy posted before the Sent log (`mb:<embed>:<row>`) were answered from `embeds.published`, which every new Send overwrites, so an old copy gave the answers of a menu it does not show. **Rule:** a posted copy answers from its own stored body: by its send id, else by its Discord message id, and the newest version only when neither is stored.
- **One unreachable copy blocked the delete.** (Message Builder 3, 2026-11-16) Delete removed up to 101 Discord copies inside an open transaction and stopped at the first 403, so the copies before it were gone, the message stayed, and every retry failed the same way. **Rule:** commit the database change first, then remove the Discord side best effort and say what was left.
- **A Discord create in a request handler was not cancel-safe.** (Message Builder 3, 2026-11-16) "+ New channel" ran Channel Setup's create inside the handler, so a closed tab could leave a made channel with no log line and the push lock held until rollback; a failing step after the create (its position) read as "not made" and a retry made a second channel. **Rule:** run locked Discord work in `tokio::spawn` (like push), and once the thing exists, later step failures are warnings.
- **A failed permission lookup greyed usable channels.** (Message Builder 3, 2026-11-16) The channel picker used `member_roles(..).unwrap_or_default`, so a failed lookup of Brim's roles judged it by @everyone only and disabled channels it can post in. **Rule:** when an input to a permission check is missing, skip the check (no reason shown); never compute it from a default.
- **A reply was written into the message opened after it.** (Message Builder 3, 2026-11-16) `save` wrote `cur` after its awaits, so switching to another message mid-save put the first one's id and Sent state into the second. **Rule:** an async reply checks that the same item is still open (an open counter) before it writes page state.
- **New migration not in the e2e build.** (Message Builder 3, 2026-11-16) `sqlx::migrate!` embeds the folder at compile time and core has no build script, so an incremental build after adding a migration ran without it ("relation does not exist"). *Avoid:* touch `crates/core/src/db.rs` after adding a migration locally; CI builds clean.
- **A phase shipped without the plan's safety rules for it.** (Message Builder 3, 2026-11-16) PR #229 first went up without the plan's 4d.7 and 4d.8 rules (sends during panic, lockdown or a nuke case; no edits in log, ticket, verification or appeal channels; caps and banned categories for "+ New channel"), because the build read the phase line and not the safety sections. **Rule:** before a phase PR, grep the plan's safety sections (4, 4b, 4c, 4d, 4e) for every route and feature the phase touches, and list each rule as built or deferred in the PR.
- **"Allow @everyone" let a filled-in name ping everyone.** (Message Builder pings, 2026-11-16) `allowed_mentions.parse = ["everyone"]` applies to the text after variables are filled, so a server or display name containing "@everyone" pinged the whole server although the author never wrote it. **Rule:** build pings only from what the author's own template contains; add "everyone" only when the template itself has @everyone or @here.
- **A rollback's save kept a permission stamp it did not earn.** (Message Builder pings, 2026-11-16) `saved_level` said a Trusted Admin saved the message, but the previous image's save changes the text without touching that column, so an Editor's text could ping after the roll forward. **Rule:** a stamp that grants something is valid only together with a marker the older image cannot keep in step (`saved_at = updated_at`); any write by older code then voids it.
- **A picture showed in the preview but not in Discord.** (Message Builder, 2026-11-17, the owner's thumbnail from a site that answers Discord's fetch with 403) The browser loads a link that Discord's image proxy cannot, so the preview looked right and Discord silently showed nothing. The preview also drew the thumbnail top left (a `float` inside a flex column), not top right. **Rule:** after a send or edit, read Discord's answer: an image slot with width 0 was not loaded, so say which slot and why. The preview uses the same layout classes as the editor (`.embed.th` + `.thumbslot`), never its own copy.
- **A picture Discord showed was reported as not loaded.** (Message Builder, 2026-11-17, the owner's GitHub mascot links) Discord answers a send before its image proxy has fetched a link it has not seen before, so the answer had width 0 and the Sent log said "Discord could not load the big image" while Discord showed it a moment later (tested on Tommybot test server: width 0 at once, 1774 after a few seconds, bot and webhook alike). **Rule:** width 0 in the first answer is "not yet", not "failed": read the message again (after 2 s, then 3 s) before warning; keep the first answer only when it cannot be read back.
- **Two first sends made two webhooks in one channel.** (Message Builder 4, 2026-11-16) The webhook was looked up, then made, with no lock, so two sends at the same time each made one and the row kept only the last. **Rule:** "find or make" of an outside resource runs under a lock on its key (here `pg_advisory_xact_lock` per channel), and the made-again path names the stale id so a racing caller reuses the fresh one.
- **A gone webhook looked like a gone message.** (Message Builder 4, 2026-11-16) Discord answers 404 for both "Unknown Webhook" (10015) and "Unknown Message" (10008). Treating every 404 as "copy deleted" hid live copies from the Sent log, and Delete left them up. **Rule:** read Discord's error `code`, not only the status; delete a copy as the bot when its webhook is gone, and keep a webhook while copies it posted are still up.
- **Webhook messages lost their buttons.** (Message Builder 4, 2026-11-16) Discord ignores `components` on webhook execute and edit unless the request has `with_components=true`. **Rule:** every webhook execute and edit path sends `with_components=true`.
- **A global header layer overwrote a route's stricter header.** (Message Builder 4, 2026-11-16) `SetResponseHeaderLayer::overriding` for Content-Security-Policy replaced the `sandbox` policy that `/media` sets. **Rule:** global security headers use `if_not_present` when a route may set a stricter value of its own.
- **A webhook token in a logged URL.** (Message Builder 4, 2026-11-16) A webhook path holds its token, and reqwest errors print the URL. **Rule:** map request errors with `without_url` for any path that holds a secret.
- **Brim's own cleanups deleted its own Message Builder webhooks.** (Message Builder 4, 2026-11-17) The webhook spam cleanup deletes webhooks made in the last 10 minutes, and a backup restore with "delete webhooks" deletes every webhook not in the image. Both hit the Message Builder's webhooks, and then nobody could edit the copies they posted. **Rule:** a bulk delete of a Discord resource skips the ones Brim itself depends on (`msghooks::ours`). A restore that cannot read that list deletes none.
- **A record of "who did it" was lost to a race.** (Message Builder 4, 2026-11-17) The dashboard marked the copies as gone when Discord answered "Unknown Webhook" before the audit log entry came. The entry then found nothing to do and wrote no log. **Rule:** when two paths can notice the same event, the later one fills in what it adds (here `hook_gone_by` and the log) instead of returning early on "already done".
- **Saving a schedule vouched for text nobody checked.** (Message Builder 5, review, 2026-11-17) The first build stamped the message as saved by the Trusted Admin on every rule save, so after a rollback an Editor's text (saved by the older image) got back the pings and webhook a Trusted Admin had set. **Rule:** a schedule that is turned on vouches only for the version the page showed (`seen` = `embeds::VERSION`, refused on a mismatch or unsaved changes) and never for a save by an older image (`saved_at <> updated_at`); a rule that is off vouches for nothing.
- **A far date crashed the next-run math.** (Message Builder 5, review, 2026-11-17) `NaiveDate` parses a year like `+262142`, and `local + Duration` panics on overflow, reachable by any Viewer through the preview route. **Rule:** limit typed dates to a sane range when parsing, and use `checked_add_signed` in time math on user input.
- **The shortest repeat held per line, not per rule.** (Message Builder 5, review, 2026-11-17) Ten daily times one minute apart, or "every 15" plus "every 16", passed the 15-minute minimum. **Rule:** check the whole rule (`msgsched::too_close` over its next 400 runs) at save, and the runner also skips a clock run less than 15 minutes after the last one (`last_clock`).
- **A restore sent a schedule at once.** (Message Builder 5, review, 2026-11-17) Relinking a remade channel set `next_at = now`, which matched no line, so the runner picked any recent "once" line and could send it a second time. **Rule:** a rule switched back on gets its real next time (`msgsched::next_of`), and a "once" run is matched loosely only while it is marked as waiting out a pause (`waiting_once`).
- **A send-time check done only at save.** (Message Builder 5, review, 2026-11-17) The schedule's channel was checked against log, ticket, verification and appeal channels only when saved; a later config change made the runner post there. **Rule:** a check that guards a delayed action runs again right before the action.
- **A trigger answered a message automod removed.** (Message Builder 6, review, 2026-11-17) The first build queued trigger answers before `automod::on_message` ran, and the dashboard woke at once (NOTIFY), so a spam message with a trigger word got an answer before automod deleted it. **Rule:** the Message Builder hook runs after automod in `events.rs`, the dashboard checks the message, member, timeout and quarantine again before it answers, and a reply uses `fail_if_not_exists: true`.
- **The Sent log trim dropped answers still waiting to be deleted.** (Message Builder 6, review, 2026-11-17) Keeping the last 100 copies removed rows whose `delete_at` was still ahead, so "delete after" never deleted those answers. **Rule:** a row with pending work (`delete_at`) is never trimmed; trim only finished rows.
- **Regex cost had no per-server total.** (Message Builder 6, review, 2026-11-17) Each pattern had a size cap, but 50 rules of 10 patterns and 50 words, kept compiled for every server, let one server load the shared bot. **Rule:** one `RegexSet` per rule with `size_limit` and `dfa_size_limit`, and a server total (30 patterns, 300 words) checked at save under the server lock.
- **A batched counter lost counts.** (Message Builder 6, review, 2026-11-17) Entries were marked written before the write, and the size cap cleared counts that were never stored. **Rule:** mark a batch as written only after the write succeeds and only if the value did not change meanwhile; on overflow drop only stored entries, and read a missing count back from the database before counting on.
- **A JSON body that is not an object panicked a handler.** (Message Builder 6, review, 2026-11-17) `spec["channels"] = ..` on a `Value` the user sent panics when it is a number or a string. **Rule:** use `as_object_mut` (or a typed struct) before writing into JSON from a request.
- **A trigger answer skipped the rollback check that schedules have.** (Message Builder 6, 2026-11-17) Schedules refuse a message an older Brim saved after a rollback (no rights check there), but trigger answers sent it. **Rule:** every automated send (schedule, trigger, sticky) calls `msgrules::trusted` before it sends.
- **A sticky's repost pinged again each time.** (Message Builder 7, review, 2026-11-17) `send_copy` forced no pings only for trigger answers, so a sticky with @everyone pinged on every repost. **Rule:** every send that repeats by itself without a person pressing Send (trigger answers, sticky reposts) uses `allowed_mentions: {"parse": []}`.
- **A sticky's own new webhook counted as another bot.** (Message Builder 7, review, 2026-11-17) The bot's cached list of Brim webhooks did not know a webhook made by the first send, so the copy it posted moved the sticky down again, in a loop, until the bot streak paused it. **Rule:** a cache that decides "is this our own post" falls back to the database for an id it does not know (`message_webhooks`), and Brim's own user id never counts.
- **Two reposts of one channel mixed the sticky order.** (Message Builder 7, review, 2026-11-17) Two dashboards during an update could repost one channel at once. **Rule:** a repost holds `pg_try_advisory_lock` per channel on its own connection (released at the end, or with the connection if the process dies); a run that finds it taken goes back in the queue. A run caused only by bots reposts from the first sticky that follows bots and every one after it, so the order holds.
- **The webhook-deleted log gave one advice for every copy.** (Message Builder 7, 2026-11-17) The "Message Builder Webhook Deleted" log said "send the message again, then delete the old copy in Discord" for all copies of the webhook, but a sticky copy is replaced at its next repost and a trigger answer with a delete time is still deleted on time. **Rule:** advice in a log or notice matches what happens to each kind of copy (kept, timed, sticky); check every source of `message_sends` when one text covers them all. A trigger answer whose timed delete failed was forgotten too: the claim clears `delete_at` first, so a failed delete now writes a "delete it by hand" warning on its Sent log row. A sticky copy counts as sticky only while a sticky still holds it (`sticky_msgs.send_id`).
- **A sticky copy that could not be deleted was forgotten.** (Message Builder 7, review, 2026-11-17) Moving or switching off a sticky cleared its stored copy id before the delete, so a failed delete left a copy nobody tracked. **Rule:** a failed delete of a copy Brim posted is shown on the sticky (or in the answer) with "delete it by hand"; Brim only ever deletes copies by the id it stored.
- **A stored id was trusted at use because it was checked at save.** (security review, 2026-11-17) Log channels, the appeal channel, the dashboard's Lockdown Update post, the verification channel (anti-nuke invite back, verification retry invite), ticket categories and verification roles were checked to be in the server only when saved. An id saved before those checks (or written by any other path) could post one server's logs or appeal answers into another server, make an invite there, or count another server's role as safe. **Rule:** every send, invite, parent or role grant that uses a stored id checks at use that the object is in the same server: bot logs through `events::send` (error, no owner DM), dashboard logs through `Discord::post_log_with` (`private_channel_of`, which like the bot also refuses a channel a main role can read: it checked only @everyone before, follow-up review 2026-11-18), invites and verification roles against `guild_info`, categories by the fetched channel's `guild_id`. A role or channel missing from the server's lists is not used.
- **"The last server used" sent DMs to the wrong server's staff.** (security review, owner decision 2026-11-17) With confirmation off, a DM ticket message went to the last server the user wrote to, so a message with files meant for another server's staff landed in the wrong ticket. **Rule:** a DM goes straight on only when the user shares exactly one ticket server with Brim; with several, they pick the server for each message (`tickets::dm`). `ticket_users.last_guild` is no longer read or written.
- **A refused Member write left a channel hidden, and Overview then called it private.** (the test server, 2026-11-18) The standard set wrote each main role's View, then @everyone's deny, and counted a refused write but went on. A channel whose Member write failed (a 429 or a missing permission) then lost @everyone's View with no main role let in, which `standard_overwrites` reads as a private channel: the Overview row and the Fix skipped it from then on, while members could not see it. **Rule:** a deny that hides a channel from @everyone comes last and is skipped when any allow before it on that channel failed (as `info_access` does); test it with a refused role write.
- **A 429 retry waited too short and too long at once.** (the test server, 2026-11-18) `Discord::send` clamped each Retry-After to 10 s and tried 3 times, so a longer limit failed after 30 s anyway, and the Overview reload after a Fix hung, then showed a 500 with the old list. **Rule:** a retry waits the time Discord asks for, within a total budget (15 s); a longer wait returns the 429 at once, so the page says "try again in a minute". A best effort step (role order, catch channel) puts its failure in the step result, not only in the log, and keeps Discord's own reason for statuses that do not say it (a 400).
- **Auto Setup lost its "running" line.** (owner decision, 2026-11-18) The simpler Auto Setup page (11-17) dropped the `<Working />` line, so a one-minute run showed nothing until the result. **Rule:** every action that can take more than a few seconds shows the shared `Working` indicator while it runs.
- **A channel hidden from every member read as "private on purpose", so Overview and the Fix left it hidden.** (test server #general, 2026-11-18) Before #303 a run could write @everyone's View deny after a main role write failed (a 429). `standard_overwrites` called any channel with @everyone denied and no main role let in private, so the half-done channel never showed in Overview and no Fix or Auto Setup run repaired it. Administrator roles (bots) in Discord's access list made it look like someone was let in. **Rule (owner decision 2026-11-18):** a channel is public unless Channel Setup limited it or it is one of Brim's own layouts. Private means: @everyone denied, no main role let in, AND some overwrite other than a bot (managed) role allows View. Channel Setup's limited channels deny the main roles and always let the Brim member in (also on a channel that was hidden already, or a deleted staff role would leave it looking half done: review, 11-18); the Logs, Staff and Staff Chat categories let the Brim member in; a jail lets the quarantine role in; logs, verification, catch, Information and tickets are skipped by id. Read only real overwrites, never Discord's access list (Administrator shows there). Searched: `private_overwrites`, `is_public`, `private_channel_of` and the lockdown "private" flag mean "hidden from @everyone" for log channels and display only; they stay as they are.

**The domain blocklists held one string per domain and grew with every refresh.** (owner decision, 2026-11-18)
`SiteList` kept about 1.14 million `String`s in a `HashSet`: about 100 MB, and each 12-hour refresh left freed fragments in glibc's heap (measured 102 -> 178 -> 181 -> 213 MB over refreshes). Even a compact block grew to 110 MB without a trim, because glibc raises its mmap threshold after the first big free and then keeps big parse buffers in the heap. *Avoid:* a list of this size is one compact structure (`fst::Set`, built from sorted unique keys on `spawn_blocking`), never a collection of small allocations; a periodic reload that frees big buffers calls `malloc_trim(0)` after the swap (`sites::release_freed_memory`). Not an allocator swap (mimalloc grew more). Measured numbers: tommybot-data `bot-memory-2026-11-18/methods.md`. Searched: `IpList` (one `Vec` of ranges, small), the message cache (capped).
- **Channel Setup called every members' channel "Staff only".** (test server, owner decision 2026-11-18) `access_of` read "@everyone denied View" as limited, but with verification every members' channel denies @everyone and lets the main role in, so #general, the text and voice categories and the ticket category showed "Staff only", and the panel offered "Only these roles" for them. Editing such a channel would have sent a limited access and hidden it from members. Picking "Everyone" also cleared @everyone's View deny, which let unverified members in. **Rule:** limited means @everyone denied AND no main role let in (as `private_overwrites`); open with a main role is the standard set (main roles in, @everyone out). The row badge comes from the server (`tag_of`): none for a members' channel, "Unverified" for the verification channel, "Staff" for a staff-only one (the Owner and Trusted Admin level roles count as staff), a custom role's name, "Members read only" where the main roles cannot send (Information).
- **A loading message blinked instead of using `Working`.** (owner decision, 2026-11-18) The Update page's queued note had no moving indicator. **Rule:** a loading message or status line uses the shared `Working` component; only an item that is itself loading (placeholder bar, running step) may pulse, through the one `.skel, .checks li.run` rule in `style.css`.
- **Loading dots blinked or froze with system animations off.** (owner decision, 2026-11-18) `.working` swapped the glide for an opacity pulse under reduced motion. **Rule:** `Working` keeps its normal movement under reduced motion; only that component is exempt from the global reduced-motion rule.
- **Reduced-motion rules.** (owner decision, 2026-11-23) Animations are always on. There is no motion switch and no `data-motion`; a new rule must not stop animations for any setting. Only the hover lift may be removed.
- **The Wizard restarted at step 1 after a visit to another page.** (owner decision, 2026-11-18) The step and the Auto Setup picks lived only in signals of the open page. **Rule:** state a person builds up over several steps is kept per server in `localStorage` (`wizard:<guild>`), cleared at the last step; never put a secret there. Unsaved edits inside a step's own cards are still lost.
- **The Update page's time left and bar only moved with the server answers.** (owner decision, 2026-11-18) Both were worked out from the server's whole-second step time once per poll, so a slow answer froze them and the native `<progress>` jumped. **Rule:** a running timer counts on from the last answer with the page's own clock (250 ms tick) and the bar is a `.pbar` with a 1 s linear width transition.
- **Built-in DMs were plain text built in code, outside the Message Builder.** (owner decision, 2026-11-18) Each DM was a `format!` string sent as content, so nobody could restyle or reword it and a text DM never matched the embeds. **Rule:** every built-in message has a key and a default template in `logging/src/botmsg.rs` (Message Builder format, with the variables it may use); code sends it through `reply::dm_msg` (bot) or `Discord::dm` (dashboard) with `(variable, value)` pairs, never a `format!` as the whole message. A server's saved copy (`bot_messages`) wins unless the template is `locked` (2FA code, rescue key, anti-nuke and panic alerts, invite-back): a locked key must never read a stored copy. An unknown key panics, so a test scans the source for `"dm.` keys. An optional line (Moderator, Duration) is a template line with only its variable: a line whose variables are all empty is dropped. Embeds from the builder carry no `type`; `reply::parts` adds `rich`. Mentions in an embed do not ping; a DM already notifies its receiver. Searched: every `reply::dm*`, `create_private_channel`, `discord.dm` caller. Left on their own embeds for now: ticket relay and closed DMs, captcha DM, welcome DM to the adder, note card (phase 3).
`SiteList` kept about 1.14 million `String`s in a `HashSet`: about 100 MB, and each 12-hour refresh left freed fragments in glibc's heap (measured 102 -> 178 -> 181 -> 213 MB over refreshes). Even a compact block grew to 110 MB without a trim, because glibc raises its mmap threshold after the first big free and then keeps big parse buffers in the heap. *Avoid:* a list of this size is one compact structure (`fst::Set`, built from sorted unique keys on `spawn_blocking`), never a collection of small allocations; a periodic reload that frees big buffers calls `malloc_trim(0)` after the swap (`mem::release_freed_memory`). Not an allocator swap (mimalloc grew more). Measured numbers: tommybot-data `bot-memory-2026-11-18/methods.md`. Searched: `IpList` (one `Vec` of ranges, small), the message cache (capped).
- **A loading message blinked instead of using `Working`.** (owner decision, 2026-11-18) The Update page's queued note had no moving indicator. **Rule:** a loading message or status line uses the shared `Working` component; only an item that is itself loading (placeholder bar, running step) may pulse, through the one `.skel, .checks li.run` rule in `style.css`.
- **Loading dots blinked or froze with system animations off.** (owner decision, 2026-11-18) `.working` swapped the glide for an opacity pulse under reduced motion. **Rule:** `Working` keeps its normal movement under reduced motion; only that component is exempt from the global reduced-motion rule.

**The image-scan model held 103 MB, most of it freed heap and a second copy of its own bytes.** (owner decision, 2026-11-18) Loading the model left ~37 MB of freed buffers in glibc's heap, each scan ~20 MB more, and the 24 MB `include_bytes!` copy stayed resident although tract keeps its own weights. Measured 103 -> 43 MB with both released. *Avoid:* a one-off big allocation (model load, a scan, a list refresh) is followed by `mem::release_freed_memory`; embedded data copied at startup is released with `mem::drop_file_pages` (it discards clean file pages only; a later read reloads them, tests: `mem::tests`, `nsfw::tests::bundled_model_is_the_pinned_one...`). Not an allocator swap (mimalloc 133 MB, jemalloc 98 MB for the same load) and not unload-when-idle (26 MB more for ~150 ms on the next image), not a quantised or f16 model (8x slower, or does not run). Numbers: tommybot-data `optimise-2026-11-18/results.md`.
**The QR check decoded a reposted image again every time.** (owner decision, 2026-11-18) A 12 MP photo costs ~250 ms and ~100 MB of transient memory to decode; a spam flood of the same file paid it per post. *Avoid:* a result cache for pure per-file work is keyed by the SHA-256 of the bytes (never a fast hash: a crafted collision would let a scam image reuse a clean image's result), holds only results that depend on the bytes alone (the QR decode reads nothing from the server's settings, so it is valid for every server), and is bounded in count and age (`qr::CODES`: 2,000 entries, 1 hour, 16 KB of text per entry; tests: `qr::tests`). The scan results of the NSFW check already follow this (`Scanner::cache`).
**The updater image was the whole Postgres image, and Postgres ran with 100-connection defaults.** (owner decision, 2026-11-18) The updater only needs `psql`: its image was 594 MB on disk (amd64 build) instead of 183 MB. Postgres had `shared_buffers` 128 MB and `max_connections` 100 for a small database. *Avoid:* the updater copies `psql` and its libraries out of the pinned Postgres image onto plain Alpine of the same release (no package install; the `pgclient` stage in `Dockerfile`); a new `psql` library dependency shows as `not found` in `ldd /usr/local/bin/psql` inside the image, so check that after a Postgres image bump. `max_connections=40` in `docker-compose.yml` must stay above the worst case of all pools plus listeners (bot 10+2, dashboard 10+2, MCP 3, updater, dumps, admin about 33): add the new pool to the comment and the number when a service or a pool grows, or connections wait for the 30 s acquire timeout.
- **A deleted catch channel stayed set, and every save logged a failed warning post.** (test server, 2026-11-18) Nothing noticed a catch channel deleted in Discord: no Overview row and no fix, and `catch::post_notice` (run after each save) logged "404 Unknown Channel" each time. **Rule:** a stored channel Brim depends on gets an Overview row when it is gone (`catch_gone_check`), with a fix that makes it again in the shape Auto Setup makes (`sections::make_catch`, shared with the catch setup button); background posts into a gone channel return quietly and leave the telling to that row.
- **A colour set by hand must survive Auto Setup.** (Member role colour, owner decision 2026-11-18) Auto Setup and the Member role fix give the Member role green (`MEMBER_COLOUR`) when they make it, take one over, or find an earlier colourless one named Member (or the Custom name). **Rule:** only a role without a colour (0) gets one; a main role with another name keeps its look. The staff roles are Brim's layout and always get their colours.
- **A restore brought back deleted servers and revoked grants, and dumps left the server readable.** (security review, 2026-11-19) A restore replaced the whole database with the dump, so every server purged and every Extra Owner, Trusted Admin, permit holder, rescue key, dashboard 2FA and MCP token removed after the dump came back; the off-site copy was a plain gzip; its rclone image was pinned by tag only; the same rclone key could delete every off-site copy. **Rule:** a grant or server removal leaves an `erasure_log` line (migration 4035, deferred constraint triggers, so a delete-and-insert in one transaction such as a permits save logs nothing), and `scripts/restore.sh` carries the live log into the restored database and calls `restore_erasures`. A new table that holds a grant gets the trigger in that migration's list. Every daily dump, the 7 local copies too (owner decision 2026-11-19; the updater's `pre-update` dumps are not), is age-encrypted to `BACKUP_AGE_RECIPIENT` and the private key never sits on the server, so anything unattended that reads a dump (the monthly restore test) runs on the plain dump inside `backup.sh` before it is encrypted; the backup key has no delete right and the bucket's object lock keeps copies 30 days; images that scripts run come from `docker-compose.yml`, pinned by digest when they get a credential, so Dependabot updates them.
- **The backup scripts were not executable.** (security review, 2026-11-19) `backup.sh` and `restore-test.sh` were stored with mode 644, so the crontab lines in DEPLOY.md (`~/tommybot/scripts/backup.sh`) failed with "Permission denied" and no dump was made. **Rule:** a script DEPLOY.md runs by its path is committed with mode 755 (`git ls-files -s scripts` shows 100755).
- **The restore test left a plain copy of the database behind, and leaked rows into its log.** (Daily backup encryption, 2026-11-19) `restore-test.sh` removed its Postgres container with `docker rm -f`, but the image's data folder is an anonymous volume, so every run left the restored database (2FA secrets, session tokens, data deleted since) unencrypted under `/var/lib/docker/volumes`. Its psql errors went to the log with DETAIL and CONTEXT lines, which quote rows. A typo in `BACKUP_AGE_RECIPIENT` made `age` fail inside the dump pipe, so no copy at all was made. **Rule:** a throwaway container that holds data is removed with `docker rm -fv`; a log gets only psql's ERROR line; a key is checked (`age -r KEY < /dev/null`) before the dump, and a bad key falls back to the plain copy with a message.
- **A rename of variables missed a check on the old name's prefix.** (variable names, 2026-11-23) The Message Builder read the server from Discord only when a used variable began with `server.`; after `{server.name}` became `{guild.name}`, no send filled it and members saw the raw text. *Avoid:* such a check is one function beside the names (`msgvars::needs_guild`), never a copied string; a rename greps for the old prefix as a string (`"server."`), not only for the whole names. Variable names follow `botmsg::public_name` (Discord things keep Discord's API names, Brim's own are `function_name`); code passes values by code name, and `to_code`/`to_public` convert at the edge.
- **A bot message copy could keep a Message Builder variable that it never fills.** (Bot messages page, 2026-11-18) `botmsg::unknown_vars` only counted `{a_z}` names, so a saved copy with `{guild.name}` or `{role:1}` passed the save check, and the member got the raw text: the bot message templates fill only their own variables. **Rule:** a check that refuses unknown variables reads every `{...}` name the senders could treat as a variable (letters, digits, `_`, `.`, `:`); a test holds one of each kind. A built-in message also cannot hold buttons (code adds them), an embed time or `<t:+N>` (only the Message Builder sender fills those): `dashboard/src/botmsgs.rs` `problem` refuses them. Searched: `embeds::missing` (the builder's own check knows all its variables).

- **Two tests in one binary used the same test server id.** (security review test run, 2026-11-19) Test ids are `<base> + std::process::id`, so two tests that pick the same base get the same id in the same run, and in a parallel run one inserted `guild_config` version 1 while the other did (`duplicate key`, a random red check). Nine bases were used twice (dashboard `api.rs` 9_884_000_000, `mcp.rs`/`api.rs`, `ops.rs`/`api.rs`, `embeds.rs`/`update.rs`, `mcp.rs`/`channels.rs`; bot `lockdown.rs`/`tickets`, `antinuke.rs`/`slowmode.rs`, `antinuke.rs`/`joingate.rs`; core `sections.rs`/`tests/db.rs`). **Rule:** a new test base is grepped first (`grep -rn "<base>" crates`) and used once per crate.
- **A bot message copy with an empty footer or author made Discord refuse the whole message.** (Default bot messages phase 3, 2026-11-19) `botmsg::render` dropped an embed that was all empty and an empty description, but kept `{"footer": {"text": ""}}` or an author with no name; Discord answers 400 and the DM or ticket message is lost, and the ticket footer code saw "a footer" and left out the server footer. **Rule:** after filling, `render` removes every embed part whose text is empty (description, footer, author), so a sender only sees parts Discord accepts (test: `tickets::msgs::tests`). Searched: `render` is the only place that fills these templates.
- **A command word can mean two commands, so a reply frame found by word alone hit the wrong one.** (Command replies, 2026-11-19) `r` is unquarantine for moderators and reply in a ticket, `c` is purge and close; the ticket aliases are not in `general::CMDS`, so the lookup fell through to the moderation aliases and a ticket error was framed as `cmd.purge`. **Rule:** `reply::command_key` takes the family of the sender (`mod_first`): moderation senders use `prefix::alias`, ticket senders use `ticket_alias`, general senders `general::lookup`; a test holds `r`/`c` for both. A frame also goes on the first embed only and a reply over 5500 characters in total is sent as built (Discord counts all embeds together), and a result that edits an earlier message (lock) must be framed too or the frame vanishes. Confirm questions stay unframed so a frame without `{text}` cannot hide what is being confirmed. Searched: every `create_message`/`update_response` that answers a command.
- **A log group had one editable frame, so a server could not word one log type alone.** (owner decision, 2026-11-19: one entry per log type, as for command replies) `log.<group>` framed every log of the group. **Rule:** a log type with a fixed title has its own entry `log.<group>.<title slug>` (`botmsg::LOG_TYPES`); `events::framed` takes the group key and finds the type by the embed's title: the type's saved copy wins, else the group's (`log.<group>`, where every edit from before sits, so nothing is lost and no migration), else the log as built. A log whose title holds a name or number stays on the group entry. A new log type is one title in `LOG_TYPES` (its group's list) and a test checks the title is really sent: an audit title from its `LogEvent`, anything else as a literal in the source, so a typo cannot leave a dead entry. The group must be the kind the code sends that title with. Security groups (anti-nuke, panic, join raid) have no types, only the locked group entry. Kits and backups copy `bot_messages` by key and need no change. Searched: every `framed` caller (`send`, `send_private`, `reports`).
- **A push that made roles read the quarantine role as above the bot.** (Layout Guard L4 review, 2026-11-19) The Roles tab renumbers every role after the draft, new ones included, but the safety check still used Brim's live position, so with one new role the quarantine role right below the bot read as above it and every such push was refused. *Avoid:* check a renumbered role list with the bot position read from that same list (`roles::bot_top`), never with `Live::bot_pos`.
- **Settings the score only warns about passed the "serious problem" check.** (Layout Guard L4 security pass, 2026-11-19) The verification level and content filter are `Warn` rows, and `layout::blocking` keeps only `Bad` ones, so a push could set both to off and save that as the correct layout. *Avoid:* a change to a guarded setting compares it with live on its own (`roles::weaker`); a `Bad`-only filter is not a safety check for everything.
- **A role edit during a lock gave Send Messages back.** (Layout Guard L4 security pass, 2026-11-19) The Roles tab may set Send Messages; during a lock or panic mode that is the lock's, and Brim's own audit entries are not put back. *Avoid:* any editor that writes Send (channel overwrites or role permissions) reads `Ctx.locked` first (`roles::editable`).
- **A handler that awaited a whole mod command overflowed the test stack.** (review, 2026-11-19) `undo::handle` awaited `modcmds::run` inline; that future is large, a test thread has 2 MB, and the test died with "stack overflow" instead of a failed assert. The mock also answered 404 to every interaction reply, so a handler that returns its error stopped before it acted. **Rule:** a handler in the interaction chain boxes a large command future (`Box::pin(modcmds::run(..))`), which also keeps the gateway event future small; the mock in `test_support` takes interaction answers, follow-ups and edits.
- **An e2e step that fills a form right after `goto` can lose the fill** (Notes step, 2026-11-19). The page rebuilds its inputs when its data arrives, so the user ID was empty when Add note was pressed and `POST /notes` answered 400 ("user ID must be a number"); it failed on two PRs and passed on others. **Rule:** wait for `networkidle` after `goto` before filling, as for the dropdown pick above.
- **A per-command setting must hold at every way into the command.** (Per-command settings, review, 2026-11-19) A command has a prefix form with aliases (some only in `prefix::alias`, such as `slock`), short ticket words (`r`, `c`, `s`), a slash form whose subcommand can be another command (`/ban remove` is unban), and right-click entries (Kick, "Purge 100 From Here"). A gate on only one of them lets "who may use it" or "off" be passed through another. The Commands page also listed `/ban remove` under ban, so unban had no slash form. *Avoid:* `cmdcfg::prefix` (in `events`, before every prefix handler) and `cmdcfg::slash` (in `slash::handle`, before `run`) resolve every word and menu to its catalog command with `cmdcfg::canonical`; a new way into a command goes through one of the two. The gate only refuses: the command's own permit check still runs after it, so a setting can never give a command to someone. Safety commands (`core::cmdcfg::SAFETY`) ignore off, narrowing and cooldown both at save (`problem`) and at read (`of`), so a rollback or import cannot lock staff out. A server alias never replaces a built-in word (`cmdcfg::RESERVED`). The review found four more ways in, all fixed: a sibling word that runs another command (`hackban` bans, `warns` shows cases, `cases modify` edits a case, `info -server`) now also answers to that command's settings (`cmdcfg::also`); a button that runs a command (Warn, Timeout, Kick, Ban on a report; Ban, Kick, Quarantine on a Raider List alert; the undo buttons under a case) calls `cmdcfg::button`; `whitelist` in a ticket is unblock; and `RESERVED` now holds every word `commands::KNOWN` dispatches (`verifysetup` is in no catalog row, so an alias could take it). A new word, sub-form or button that runs a command gets a row in one of these. The cooldown map stops taking entries when it is full of running cooldowns, so members without a permit cannot grow it.
- **A test with many calls of one large async function overflowed the stack.** (Case proofs, 2026-11-19) `modcmds::run` holds every command's state in one future; two new arms made its debug frame large enough that an old test overflowed, and a new test with 11 call sites held 11 copies. *Avoid:* a new arm of a large `match` in an async fn goes into its own async fn called with `Box::pin`, and a test that calls such a function many times calls it through one closure that returns `Box::pin(async move { .. })`.
- **The browser test ran into the dashboard's own request limit.** (2026-11-19, seen after the case page steps of #357) A session gets 40 API requests at once and 20 more each second (`auth.rs` BURST, RATE). `tests/e2e/dashboard.js` opens pages back to back, and the new case steps added about 25 requests in under a second (6 page loads and changes, each with `/config`, `/access`, the case and a member lookup), so on a fast run the next page (Logging) got 429 for `/config` and `/schema`, showed no channel dropdown and the step timed out; a slower run passed. The page itself was not over the limit for a person, but it asked for the name of a member who left again after every change. *Avoid:* the test counts its own API requests and `page.goto` waits exactly until the limit has room for a page load (no fixed sleep, the limit stays as it is); new steps need nothing extra. A page that loads its data again after a change does not repeat lookups that gave no answer (`asked` in `CasePage`).
- **A log built outside the logger skipped the event route and the ignore lists.** (Logs to L5, 2026-11-19) `events::send_log` looks the channel up by kind only (`target(kind, None)`) and never reads `ignored_users` or `ignored_roles`, so a log of an event (the timeout end) would have gone to the kind's channel instead of the event's, and an ignored member would still be logged. The dashboard cannot send a framed log at all (its `post_log` knows no Bot messages entries), so a dashboard log (case delete) posted from there would not be editable. **Rule:** a bot log of one `LogEvent` goes through `events::send_event_log` (event route, custom types, ignore lists, frame); a dashboard or sweep log is queued as a job (`case_delete_log`, `old_cases_log`, like `case_log`) and sent by the bot with `send_log`. A new log type is one title in `botmsg::LOG_TYPES` that the code really sends (a test checks it); a new kind is also in `LOGS`, `LogKind::ignorable` (security-type kinds false) and the dashboard's `LOG_KINDS`. twilight reads only the audit change keys it knows, so AutoMod rule and app permission details arrive as `other` and the log says some settings are not shown. Searched: every `send_log*` caller and every `LogKind` match.
- **A noisy log type took the kind route or the general channel, so a vote or sign-up flooded a channel nobody chose for it.** (Logs to L11, 2026-11-19) `LogConfig::target` falls back from event to kind route to the general channel, which is right for most events but wrong for poll votes and event sign-ups: one vote is one message, and Auto Setup gave every event the shared `discord-logs` channel. **Rule:** an event that must stay off until a person picks a channel for it is listed in `LogEvent::quiet`; `target` then uses only its own event route or a custom type (no kind route, no general channel) and Auto Setup (`sections::log_channels`) skips it. The logger also builds nothing for it while it has no route (`Logger::vote`, `Logger::signup`). A new noisy event is added to `quiet`, not to a `matches!` of its own. Other rules from the same package: (1) a state that decides whether a gateway event is news (boost level, open threads, published messages, ended polls) lives in the `Logger`, in memory and bounded (`MAX_KEPT`, `MAX_THREADS`, `first_time`), and the first sight of a guild only records it (`Logger::seed` at `GuildCreate`), so a restart logs nothing false; (2) a thread archive is held for `HOLD` and dropped when an audit `ThreadUpdate` for the same thread arrived within it, since only an archive with no audit entry is Discord's own; (3) a stage audit entry's target is the stage instance, so `render::audit` leaves out the Target line and the Channel line names the stage; (4) an update of a message with no cached copy is never read as a publish (it would hide the edit log), so the cache also runs while only the publish log is on; (5) a bounded map evicts its oldest entry when full (a "stop inserting" cap leaves new scheduled events unnamed once old ones pile up); known limit: the new state events (boost level, thread open/archive) are applied after the config read, so two events of one thread or guild in the same instant can swap; (6) a poll's updates (vote counts) are never a "Message Edited" log; only the finalized one is, once. Searched: every `LogEvent::ALL` user (dashboard schema, Auto Setup, the botmsg title test), every `LogKind` match, `guild_of` in `events.rs`.

**A saved edit of a DM sent outside any server was never sent.** (owner decision, 11-20) The raider notice DM was saved on the Bot messages page, but it is sent with no server, so the copy lookup read nothing and sent the default.
