The data request DM had the same path, but it is locked. *Avoid:* an entry sent outside a server is listed in `botmsg::BOT_WIDE`: its one copy is stored under guild 0 (`botmsg::copy_guild`), and only the bot owner may save it (`botmsgs::may_edit`). Every sender with no server reads that copy: `reply::dm_msg` with `None`, and `reply::frame_embeds` (async, takes the app). Searched: the callers of `dm_msg(None, …)` and `frame_embeds` (raider notices in `raiders/review.rs` and `raiders/tasks.rs`, data request in `datareq.rs`). Not live tested.
- **The landing page flashed after login, and some actions had to be pressed again after the 2FA code.** (2026-11-20) The web app drew the landing page while it still waited for `/api/me`, so it showed between the login callback and the server list. The 2FA prompt resumed an action by clicking its control again, which fails for a request sent after an await (Overview Fix all when the step-up ran out mid-run, config import) and for a control that closes or changes before the code (import Apply, Channel Setup push, Delete server data, Load backup, panic mode). *Avoid:* see `crates/dashboard/ui/PITFALLS.md` ("The landing page showed..." and "After the 2FA code..."): wait for `/api/me` before choosing a view, and use `run_resumable` for those requests.
- **The Update page showed no change lines because it only knew merge commits.** (2026-11-20) `update::changes` listed a pull request only from a two-parent "Merge pull request #N" commit, but main has used squash merges since #335 ("Title (#N)", one parent), so after that every list was empty: "Newest" showed a hash only, and Install rows had no lines. **Rule:** `update::commit_change` reads both forms; a build's lines come from one compare of the oldest to the newest build, split at each build's commit (`changes_per_build`), so the list costs one GitHub call per new pair. The oldest listed build has no lines (its base is not in the list). A finished update shows the lines of the installed build, not of every build it skipped. The same package: the Servers page takes `banner`, `owner` and `permissions` from Discord's `/users/@me/guilds` (no new scope), lists servers where the person may add the bot (owner, Manage Server or Administrator) as `addable`, and "Add to server" is `/auth/invite?guild_id=<digits>`, the existing invite with that server preselected (same scopes and permissions; a non-numeric id is ignored).
- **Channel Setup showed the Channels grid under the Roles and Server tabs.** (2026-11-20) `.cs-grid` sets `display:grid`, which beats the browser's `[hidden]` rule, so `hidden=` on that element did nothing (the same held for any element with its own display rule, such as the role picker). **Rule:** `style.css` ends with `[hidden] { display:none !important; }`; a new class with a `display` value needs no extra rule for `hidden`. The browser test switches the tabs and checks that the grid is gone.
- **Spacing was off the 4 px grid in 233 places.** (2026-11-20, the owner's new design rule) Tokens such as `space-6`, `space-10` and `space-18` and raw 2, 3, 6, 10 or 14 px values were used for margin, padding and gap. **Rule:** every spacing is 4 px or a multiple of 4 (see `design/README.md`); `tools/ui-sizes.mjs` fails on a spacing that is off the grid, in `style.css` and `bundle.css`. A border or ring width uses `--stroke-2`, not a space token. A control whose inner offset changes (the toggle knob: track 44x24, knob 16 at 4 px) gets its size changed with it. Not covered by the lint: `em` spacing and positions set from Rust (`left`, `top` of popovers).
- **A log that needs the old value needs stored member data, and stored member data has five ways out that are easy to miss.** (Logs, 2026-11-19, a rule from the build, no bug shipped) Discord's member update carries only the new username, display name and avatar, so `profile_log` keeps the last seen ones in `profile_snapshot` (migration 4044). **Rule:** a new table with member data is wired, in the same PR, to all of: (1) written only while the feature that needs it is on (`profile_log::routed`: nothing is stored or read when neither log has a channel); (2) deleted when the member leaves (`on_leave`); (3) a retention delete in `retention::sweep` (here a fixed 90 days with no setting, owner decision 2026-11-20; a per-server limit would be a `RetentionCfg` field with a `delete_for` arm and a `limits::FIXED` range); (4) `persondata::PLACES`, so `/mydata` shows and deletes it and the dashboard counts it; (5) the dashboard role: a REVOKE in `db::set_dashboard_role`, the `dashboard_role_limit` trigger in the migration, and both lists in `dashboard_role_has_rows_only`. Also `PRIVACY.md`, the `/mydata delete` DM text and `T::cleanup`. `purge_guild` needs nothing: it finds every `guild_id` column. Never use `dashboard_role_limit` for DELETE: it returns NEW, which is NULL there, so the trigger would cancel every role's deletes. A member's name is shown in a code block (`Log::diff`), never as plain embed text, since a display name can hold a masked link. The 90 days are fixed, so a leave missed while the bot was offline is always caught by the retention. Events run in their own tasks, so the read of the old row takes `FOR UPDATE` (two updates at once would both log). The first sight of a member only stores, so the whole member list is read once a day, after a restart and within the hour after the logs are turned on (`profile_log::sweep`, last in the hourly loop, one request per 1,000 members in sequence): a quiet member's first change is then logged too, a missed leave is deleted (only after a complete paging), and the 90 days count from `seen_at`. A sweep over every server takes `only` for its test, as `retention::sweep` does (a test that sweeps all servers touches other tests' rows). Known limits: a change made while the bot was offline only updates the row, with no log; a server that turned the logs on less than an hour ago has no old values yet; the old avatar picture works only while Discord still serves that hash.
- **A ticket pinged and gave access to roles that no longer exist (`@unknown-role`).** (owner decision, VPS test 2026-11-20) Nothing removes a permit role row (`permit_roles`) when the Discord role is deleted or re-made, and the stored ids in `tickets.ping_roles` and a panel's `ping_roles` go stale the same way. `tickets::staff_roles` (channel overwrites, at creation and in the public-channel resync) and `panel::ping_staff_roles` (the staff ping of every ticket) read those rows as they were, so the intro showed two `@unknown-role` mentions beside `@Admins @Moderators`, and the overwrites carried dead ids. The dashboard's role picker lists only live roles, so a dead id stays in the saved value with no way to untick it (not changed: the picker's list can be stale, and dropping unlisted ids there could remove a valid new role; the bot skips dead ids instead). **Rule:** every place that mentions or grants access to a stored role id first drops ids the server no longer has (`tickets::live_roles` / `live_ids`, which keep the list when the role lookup fails); the same goes for a new role mention anywhere else. The DB rows stay: `relink` re-points them when Auto Setup or the Overview Fix remakes a role. Searched: every `staff_roles`, `ping_staff_roles`, `ping_roles` and `<@&` in `tickets/`. Role pings in other modules read their roles from config the dashboard validates at save, not from permit rows; not changed. Also (same PR): the ticket type is in the channel name (`name-type-number`, `tickets::kind_name`, set at creation for a panel type and in `workflow::set_kind` for every later change). Discord allows two renames per 10 minutes per channel: a refused or slow rename (5 s limit) is only logged and the type change stands. Nothing finds a ticket by its channel name (lookups use `channel_id`).
- **A role alone gave a permit before a guild's member migration.** (owner decision, 2026-11-20 decision card "Remove it now") `permits::top_permit` read `permit_roles` (a role holder had the permit) for every guild without a `permit_members_migrated` row, and four more readers had the same "unmigrated" branch (`antinuke::held_staff`, `raiders::not_collectable`, `automod::word_exempt_roles`, the Overview handover card). **Rule:** a permit belongs to user ids only (`permit_members`); `top_permit` and `resolve` take no roles, and `permit_roles` is only what the bot hands to listed members (and what guards and pings read). A new reader of "who holds a permit" reads `permit_members`, never a role. Kept on purpose: `migrate_members` still converts a guild's role holders to listed members once, at the first sync (GuildCreate), so a server loses nothing; the ownership handover in an unmigrated guild still deletes the strong permits' role rows, so the migration cannot give them back later (`owner::on_owner`). `enforce_permits` waits in a guild whose first permit sync has not run (nobody is listed yet, so taking roles then would lose the staff for good). Until that sync, staff who hold a permit role and are not Owner/EO/TA resolve to no permit (anti-nuke whitelist, held-staff check and the raider list see them as members); the sync is queued at GuildCreate and retried. Not done: some functions still carry a `roles` argument that nothing reads (`App::cached_level`, `raiders::collect`, `joingate::immune_by_roles`); removing it is a separate cleanup. Tests that gave a permit through a role now list the user in `permit_members`; `permits_resolve_levels` asserts a user listed nowhere has none.
- **Permit role rows outlived their Discord role.** (follow-up to the `@unknown-role` ticket ping, 2026-11-20) Seen on the VPS guild: Admins and Moderators each listed an old and a new role id, the old pair deleted. Auto Setup and the Overview Fix never make permit roles; a person deleting one by hand, or a restore that was not relinked, leaves the row. **Rule:** a row is not dropped when the role is deleted, because an anti-nuke restore makes the role again with a new id and `relink` needs the old row to point it there. `deadroles::sweep` (hourly, in `welcome::purge_left`) starts `permit_roles.missing_since` (migration 4049) when it sees the role missing, clears it when the role is back, and drops the row after `DEAD_ROLE_DAYS` (7, one constant). Readers skip missing roles at once (`tickets::live_roles`, also the `ticket config` view). A saved permits copy from the dashboard rewrites the rows and so restarts the clock (a server that saves permits every week never drops a dead row; harmless, readers skip it); `relink` clears `missing_since` when it points a row at the restored role, or a sweep that read the roles just before the restore could drop the fixed row. A backup loaded more than 7 days after the role was deleted finds no row to relink: the permit has no role until staff set one. Not done: stored config lists (`tickets.ping_roles`, panel `ping_roles`, feed `ping_role`) keep a dead id until edited, readers skip it; `level_roles` rows are not swept (the owner sync would have to make the role again first).
- **#open-a-ticket showed a bare "Open a ticket" title over a "New panel" button.** (owner decision, 2026-11-21) The panel message text was written only together with the default panels; a server that pressed Add panel got an empty text, and the Add button named the first panel "New panel". Root cause: a default kept in config is written once, on one path, so every other path gets nothing. **Rule:** a message's look is a Default messages entry (`ticket.panel`), so the default always applies until the server saves its own, on every path. The ticket greeting is the one config text that owner decision wanted on for every server: its serde and schema default is the default greeting and 4055 filled the empty ones; an empty greeting a server saves still sends none (the 2026-11-15 rule above still holds for other new fields). The first panel the Add button makes is labelled "Open a ticket". Migration 4055 moved each written panel text into an edited version of the entry, and the dashboard tells the bot (`pg_notify('ticket_panel')`) when Default messages change, so the posted message follows without a Tickets save.
- **A 2FA refusal showed a server state.** (Brim poses review, 2026-11-21) Pressing Verify in the "Confirm it's you" dialog before setting up an authenticator showed `totp not enrolled`; a wrong code showed `invalid code`, and a second setup showed `totp already enrolled`. `ApiError::Forbidden` sends its text as is, and the dialog shows it word for word. **Rule:** a refusal a person reads says what is wrong and what to do, in a sentence (`auth::WRONG_CODE`, `NOT_SET_UP`, `ALREADY_SET_UP`, `NOT_SET_UP_MCP`). Code that reacts to a refusal compares against the constant, never against a copied string (`mcp.rs` counts a guess with `auth::WRONG_CODE`). Only the text changed: which codes pass and the lockout are the same. Searched: every `ApiError::Forbidden` in `auth.rs`, `mcp.rs`, `ops.rs` (the Rescue Key refusal "invalid rescue key or code" already says what is wrong and is kept, since `secure.rs` compares it).

**The dashboard showed "Discord is limiting requests" on normal page loads and after "Fix all".** (the owner, 11-21) One page load starts several handlers at once (`/score`, `/score/fix`, `/discord`, `/lockdown/check`), and each fetched `GET /guilds/{id}/channels` and `/roles` fresh, so the same requests went out side by side. On a 429 they all waited the same Retry-After and retried together, so they hit the limit again and ran out of tries. Discord's `X-RateLimit-Remaining: 0` was ignored, and the 30 s memo (`memo_get`) and a few raw calls skipped the 429 handling. *Avoid:* every bot request goes through `Discord::send`, which waits when Discord said a path's bucket is used up or sent a 429 (shared per "METHOD /path" and for a global limit, 15 s in all). GETs through `call`, `get_json` and `memo_get` share one request when asked at once (`shared_get`) and reuse an answer for 2 s. Every write clears all reused answers and stops a GET that was under way from storing its answer, so data read after a fix is fresh. Tests: `discord::tests::parallel_gets_share_one_request_and_a_write_clears_them`, `a_used_up_bucket_makes_the_next_request_wait`. Not live tested.

**A feed address had to be the feed file itself.** (feed setup, 2026-11-21)
Staff paste a site's normal address, and the add failed with "this is not an RSS or Atom feed". *Avoid:* `feed::find` reads the page and takes the feed its `<link rel="alternate">` names. The found address goes through `check_url` and the same guarded client as a typed one (public names only, https on 443, redirect, time and size limits), one hop only.

**GitHub's public list of events leaves out most of each event.** (followed repositories, 2026-11-21)
A push in `/repos/<repo>/events` has no commits, and a pull request has no title, link or author. *Avoid:* `feedfollow::body` reads each part again from its own `url` (only `https://api.github.com/`), and a push from the compare API, so `feedhooks::github_post` gets the body the webhook sends. The list says `merged` and `created` where the webhook says `closed` and `submitted`. The hourly limit (60 without `GITHUB_TOKEN`) does not count toward stopping a feed, and an unchanged list (304) costs nothing.

**A feed log line showed "Feed: GitHub (``)".** (feeds, 2026-11-21)
A webhook feed stores no address (GitHub or GitLab calls Brim), and its repository name came only with the first event that posts, after the "Feed Connected" line was queued; `feed_label` printed the empty address in a code span. *Avoid:* the name is taken from every signed delivery, the ping too (`feedhooks::take`), and from the pasted link when the feed is added (`repo_of`, `gitlab_path`); `label_of` leaves out an empty address, and one equal to the name. The poster name of posts stays GitHub or GitLab. Test: `a_label_never_shows_an_empty_address`.

**Check now said nothing, and a new feed posted nothing to test with.** (feeds, 2026-11-21)
The first check only remembers items, and Check now on a webhook feed did nothing. *Avoid:* Send latest posts the newest item (website, YouTube) or the newest event of a switched-on kind (GitHub, followed or webhook: the public events list, at most 5 events read), even one posted before, under a new delivery key. One at a time per feed (refused while one waits), not counted as a failed check. GitLab webhook feeds and private repositories say how to test instead (GitLab's Test button, GitHub's Recent Deliveries > Redeliver).


## A module switch must reach every reader of its parts (2026-11-21)
- The `modules` section holds the master switches of Server Joins, Messages and Logging (a missing switch is on). The bot applies them in `GuildCfg::parse`: Server Joins off turns Join Gate, Join Raid (with voice raids), Verification and the Raider List off in memory, and Logging off makes `LogConfig::target` drop event logs. The stored part settings stay as they are.
- The first version missed readers that use the raw config: the Raider List SQL queries, the dashboard's web captcha, the Overview health list and the standard channel set's gate (`config_gate`), which would hide channels from @everyone while no verification ran. A part write that turned the module on again also fired on any save of a part that was already on.
- **Rule:** code that reads a part's `enabled` from raw JSON or SQL also reads `modules.<module>` (`brim_core::sections::module_of`). A write turns the module on only when it turns the part on from off, and then needs the `modules` bar (Trusted Admin with 2FA).
- The in-Discord settings panel (`joingate 1a ?on`) saved the part on but left Server Joins off, so nothing ran. Both writers now use `module_to_turn_on`, and the panel asks for the same bar (Owner, Extra Owner or Trusted Admin with a fresh 2FA) before it turns a module on.
- The first Moderation switch forced each moderation command off in the command settings. That also blocked the Raider List alert buttons (Server Joins) and the commands that lift an Anti Nuke or Auto Mod punishment (unban, untimeout, unwarn, the Undo buttons). **Rule:** the Moderation switch is one check in the command gates (`cmdcfg::module_off`), with a kept list (`KEPT` plus `SAFETY`). A button of another module uses `cmdcfg::button_settings`, which skips that check.

## Ticket type buttons (2026-11-22)
- A panel with the type switch on posts one button per ticket type: `tk2:open:<panel>:<typehash>` (menu option value `<panel>:<typehash>`, form id gets the hash as a sixth part). **Rule:** the bot takes the type only from a hash that matches a type in the current list and only while the panel still shows more than one button (`button_types`); anything else gets `form_changed`. Never read the type name from the id.
- `panel_message`, `types_problem` and `button_types` are the one source for the posted message, the save check and the dashboard preview. The preview is drawn from `POST .../tickets/preview` (the page keeps no copy). A change to the message changes the preview with it; test `the_preview_is_what_publish_posts`.
- A type name is the button label: at most 80 characters with the switch on. The save also refuses more than 25 buttons on the panel message.
- `{ticket_type}` is empty for a ticket with no type (a DM ticket has one once the member picks it before the channel); every `tags` caller passes the type or "".
- Migration 4059 renames only the exact untouched 4057 panel. A panel with any other setting keeps its name.

- **The appeal page could show what the punishment DM hid.** (appeal flow, 2026-11-22) A case reason of the bot's own (an automod filter, the Join Gate's minimum account age) is left out of the DM when the owner hides it, so showing "the newest case reason" on the appeal page would have told a raider the gate's rule. The server name went to anyone who knew the id. *Avoid:* the page shows only staff cases' reasons (`mod_id` is not the bot) and the server only to someone punished there. A Discord log message that more than one path edits (member additions, staff questions, the decision) is edited one at a time (`appeals::patch_log`), and an addition checks the appeal is still open, or it writes back an old copy without the Decision field.

- **One Discord 502 left the emoji set half replaced.** (emoji upload, 2026-11-23) `tools/emojis.mjs upload --replace` deleted an emoji and then created it, with a retry only for 429. One "502 Bad gateway" on a create stopped the run (and printed the whole HTML page) after some emojis were already new and one was deleted. *Avoid:* every Discord call in a tool that changes many things retries 429 (with `retry_after`), 502, 503, 504 and network errors with a backoff, and prints a short error line, not the body. Draw the new image first, then delete, then create. A failure of one item must not stop the run: count replaced, created and failed, and print the exact rerun command for the failed names. Not tested against Discord with an error.

- **A purge without a member was logged as a case against the moderator.** (mod log, 2026-11-23) A purge of a channel and a slowmode stored the moderator as the case's member (`user_id`), because the case needs a number there. The mod log showed the moderator as Target, the moderator's own case list and count held the purge, and the moderator could not change their own purge case ("it is about you"). *Avoid:* a case without a member stores `user_id = 0`, the value a data deletion already uses for "no person"; `cases::create` then logs no `target_id`. Every place that shows or filters a case's member uses the one rule in `brim_core::cases`: `target()` in code, `TARGET` and `HAS_TARGET` in SQL. They also cover older rows (a purge or slowmode with `user_id = mod_id`; a self-target is refused, so that pair always means no target). Used in: the mod log (`Target: None`), `t!cases`, the report member view, the dashboard Cases list, case page, edit guard and case delete log, and the MCP `cases`, `case` and case edit preview. A dashboard staff action on such a case records no target. Other kinds with no member (lock, lockdown) open no case.

- **A move of a job that the scheduler holds is lost.** (giveaway pause, 2026-11-23) The scheduler leases a job, runs it and then deletes it by id. An update of `run_at` on that leased row (a shift of the end time, the end of a pause) changes a row that is deleted a moment later, so the giveaway had no end job any more and never ended. *Avoid:* a move updates only unleased rows (`locked_until IS NULL OR locked_until < now()`) and queues a new job when it moved none. The job itself checks under the row lock that it is still due (`giveaways::end_due`): when the end moved later or the giveaway is paused, it waits and makes sure one other pending job exists.
- **A draw or an entry during an attack.** (giveaway pause, 2026-11-23) `paused_at` existed but nothing set it, so a raid, lockdown, panic mode or nuke did not stop entries or the draw. *Avoid:* the Enter press and the end job read the live state (`giveaways::attacked`: raid detector, panic run, open anti-nuke case, server locks; a lock of one channel does not count) and set the pause at once; a round every 15 s pauses and resumes. A restored or remade role or channel moves into running giveaways (`giveaways::relink`), so a remade blocked role still keeps its members out.
- **A backup must not bring back a giveaway row.** (giveaway backups, 2026-11-23) A first version saved running giveaways in the backup and brought back the rows that were gone. The only thing that deletes a row is the 90-day prune of ended giveaways, and manual backups are kept for ever, so a load would have brought back a giveaway that already drew, and drawn it again. *Avoid:* the rows stay in the database through a restore; only their channel and role ids move (`relink`). Panic and raids come back at start, but an open anti-nuke case is in memory only: the pause round also reads `nuke_incidents` before it resumes.
- **A winner could claim while the place was being drawn again.** (giveaway claims, 2026-11-23) The claim job read the expired place, then spent seconds on member checks; a Claim press in that time still saw an open deadline, said "You claimed", and the redraw then removed the place. *Avoid:* the job closes the claim in the same statement that reads it (`claim_due`, `claims_due`: `deadline = LEAST(deadline, now())`). Places that run out together are drawn again in one redraw (one post, not one per place). A new place number skips every place in use, also the ones handed out in the same save.
- **A winner role was taken away while a later win still held it.** (giveaway repeat, 2026-11-23) A member who won the same winner role in two giveaways lost it when the first role time ended, and a second win while they still had the role queued no removal at all (the role counted as "had before"). *Avoid:* a role removal that is due skips while another removal for the same member and role waits in the future (`giveaways::role_off_waits`), and a win for a member who has the role adds its own removal when one waits. The member keeps the role until the latest role end time. The job that runs is due, so it never counts itself; a test that calls the job must make its row due first.
- **A repeat that starts a giveaway must claim its run and make the giveaway in one transaction.** (giveaway plans, 2026-11-23) The message schedule stores the next run first and sends after, so a crash between the two loses a run. For a plan the giveaway is a database row, so the plan row lock (`FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`), the new giveaway (`giveaways::create_in`, under the per-server lock that counts the limit) and the next run are one transaction: two runners, a restart or a late tick start it once, and a crash starts it never or once, not twice. A run that is more than an hour late is skipped with the reason on the plan (the last run of a plan has 6 hours); a plan over the limit of running giveaways skips the run, it does not queue. "Start after the last giveaway ends" keeps `held` and looks again every minute: one indexed read per waiting plan.
- **A plan runs with the rights of who saved it last.** (giveaway plans, 2026-11-23) Like message schedules, the runner reads the saver's permit (`giveaways`, or Trusted Admin and up) before each run and stops the plan with the reason when it is gone; an unreadable level only skips the pass. A template and a plan never keep an entry code (only a hash is stored and it is salted with the giveaway id): a giveaway made from them has none, and the form asks for a new one.
- **A runner that looks at few plans per pass, and marks a plan late by the clock, loses runs under load.** (giveaway plans, 2026-11-23) A pass looked at 8 plans, one at a time, and a run was skipped when it was over an hour late; many plans due in the same minute could wait past that and be skipped only because the runner was busy. A pass now takes 200 plans in 8 lanes, and a run is late only by the time Brim was down (runner start minus the run time). 10,000 due plans start once in about 8 s.
- **A plan must not start a giveaway in a server under attack.** (giveaway plans, 2026-11-23) The attack state is in the bot's memory, the plan runner is in the dashboard. The bot's pause round (every 15 s) moves the plans due soon of an attacked server a minute on, with the reason on the plan; it starts once when the attack is over. Tests that call the pause round side by side use `pause_round`, not `sync_pauses`, whose one-round guard makes a second call do nothing.
- **A switch showed on while the server ran it off.** (giveaways, 2026-11-23) The schema (`crates/dashboard/src/schema.rs`) gave `giveaways.enabled` the default `true`, but `GiveawaysCfg::default` is off (owner decision). A server with no saved giveaways settings showed the switch on while Start stayed off. The `default` of a schema field must equal the Rust `Default`; a test now checks the giveaways switch, and every new `dv(.., true)` on an `enabled` field needs the same check against its Rust default.

## The preview of a default message missed the buttons Brim adds

Default messages can carry buttons that code adds (`giveaway.running`, `ticket.confirm`, link buttons under logs). The editor and the preview drew only the stored message. The bot now saves the real button rows with its samples (`previews.rs`, `added_buttons`, key to rows), and `added_buttons_belong_to_real_messages` fails when a key is not a message. Label and emoji come from the same builders and constants the bot posts with (`enter_row`, `claim_row`, `link_row`, `GO_MESSAGE`, `GO_POLL`, `GO_CHANNEL`).

**Rule:** a new button under a default message is added to that table in the same change. The preview of a message must never show an example that the bot cannot post (a running giveaway shows a count of winners).

## Giveaway card pictures (2026-11-23)

**A picture variable stayed as typed text in the link slot.** `fill_value` skips every `url` key, so only `fill_links` fills a link. A new link variable (`{sponsor_picture}`, `{banner}`) must be in the `LINKS` list of `fill_links` in `botmsg.rs`, and the message key must be one that calls it (`feed.*` and `giveaway.*`). An empty or non-web value then drops the slot, so Discord never gets an empty link.
**A failed look-up replaced a good cached value with an empty one.** The sponsor picture is kept in memory for hours. A Discord error (5xx, 429, timeout) must keep the old picture and ask again in a few minutes. Only a first look-up that fails stays empty. *Avoid:* never store the result of a failed fetch over a good cache entry.
**The giveaway cards show their facts as fields (2026-11-23).** The default running and ended messages keep Hosted by, Sponsored by and the requirements as lines under the title, and Ends, Entries and Winners as inline fields (the ended card adds a full-width Result field, `Ends` reads just "Ended", and `{ga_winner_count}` is the number asked for). A field with an empty value is dropped by `drop_blank_fields`, so a new fact needs its own field, never a line that can be empty next to a label. A server that saved its own copy keeps it; only the default changed. The place under the title is kept free for a host's own text later.

## A load test of the plan runner shared the database with other runners (2026-11-23)

**`many_due_plans_all_start_once` failed with 0 or a few started plans.** Two causes, both outside the plan code. (1) The test asked `due` for the oldest 200 plans of all servers and then kept its own; plans left by another test run filled the 200, so a lane saw none and stopped. (2) A dashboard server running on the same database took the due plans with its own runner and moved them a minute on ("Brim could not run this plan"), because it cannot reach Discord. *Avoid:* a test of a runner picks its plans in the query, by its own server range, never by filtering after a limit. Never run a dashboard server on the database the tests use: give the server its own database (`createdb`), and start it only with that one.


## One dropped request ended the whole access matrix and named nothing (e2e, 2026-11-23)

In CI run 45195154781 the browser sweep failed with `FAIL matrix: crashed: page.evaluate: TypeError: Failed to fetch`. The page tests passed, the server log held no error and no panic, and the same page code passed on the next commit. The log could not say which of the hundreds of calls was dropped, because one thrown error ended the whole matrix loop and only the crash text was kept. Whether it was the runner or a route that closes a connection without an answer could not be told.
**Rule:** a test loop that makes many calls catches the error of each call and fails with the call's own name (user, method, path), then goes on. A crash that names nothing is a finding nobody can follow up. The matrix in `tests/e2e/deep.js` now does this. If the same route drops again, it is a real fault in that route, not the runner.


## Stock emoji in bot messages (2026-11-23)

**The "Ticket Closed" DM showed a stock mailbox next to Brim's own emojis.** The title used `TICKET_CLOSE`, but the first line had the Unicode `📪` typed into the text. A typed Unicode emoji never turns into a Brim emoji, so it shows the system look. The same was true for the first line of every punishment DM, the verify and appeal DMs, the verify panel and captcha titles, the join raid and voice raid log titles, the Bot check logs, the lockdown update lines, the note title, and the dashboard's owner alerts (MCP token, Secure my account, update, "Not you?"). *Avoid:* a message Brim posts never has a typed emoji. Use a constant from `crates/logging/src/emoji.rs` (its fallback is the Unicode emoji, so tests and self-hosted bots still work) in a `format!` (`{{server}}` for a placeholder). The dashboard loads the ids too, so its owner alerts use the constants the same way. `emoji::fill` (`{e:name}` tags) drops an emoji that is not uploaded and has no fallback: use it only for server owners' own text. A new emoji needs its icon in `assets/icons` first. Left as they are on purpose: the sample values in the variable lists of the editor, and test strings. The giveaway Enter default is now the Brim ticket (`{e:giveaway_enter}`): a server owner's own button emoji stays, but the default is never a typed Unicode emoji.

The Update page showed "Newest: Could not reach GitHub right now." and an empty release list right after a release, and nothing in the log said why. Root cause: `update::get_json` turned every failure (no connection, the hourly limit of 60 unauthenticated calls per server address, a bad answer) into `None` with no log, and `builds` kept no list, so one failed refresh emptied the page. The limit is shared with the GitHub feeds and with the compare and pull request calls one page load makes. *Avoid:* a failed call is logged (WARN with the url and reason) and its reason goes to the page (`error`), the limit is named with its reset time; the last good release list stays on a failed refresh, and a failed refresh is not tried again for 30 s. `GITHUB_TOKEN` (public read only, already passed to the dashboard) is sent to api.github.com calls only, never to the registry. Searched: every GitHub call of the dashboard is in `update.rs` (`get_json`, attestation check) and `feedfollow.rs` (already names its limit); the attestation check returns its own error. "Running a78b863 / Updated to 2d21eaa" is not a fault: the last button update was 11-21, later versions came by the server command.


## An identity value from a failed lookup went into a commit (2026-11-23)

**A commit author email held an error text.** The email was read from a lookup that failed, and the failure text was stored as the value. It showed in the author and committer of a pushed commit, and it pointed to a tool. *Avoid:* set the author name and email as fixed literal values in the repo config, never from a command or an API call. Before every push, print `git log --format='%an <%ae> | %cn <%ce>'` for each commit to be pushed and read it. A wrong value in a pushed commit is not fixed by a new push: the commit stays public on its branch until the branch is deleted.
